<span class="c4 c14">A Note from the Author</span>

You'll probably notice that I've changed a few things.

Events happen at different times than they did in J.  K.  Rowling's
original books.  Some things happen differently, and some things don't
happen at all.  Don't let that confuse you.

This is intentional.

You might also notice that some of your favorite scenes from the
original books are missing entirely.  That's intentional, too.  I'm not
trying to rewrite the entire Harry Potter series, and I'm certainly not
trying to improve upon it.

At its heart, this is a story about two people, their relationship, and
how they change each other.  The larger events of the wizarding world
are still happening around them, but they're not necessarily the focus.
 Sometimes those events have been rearranged, abbreviated, or left out
entirely to make room for the story I actually wanted to tell.

This whole thing originally began as a random writing experiment.  I had
an idea, decided to see where it went, and never really expected anyone
else to care.

To my considerable surprise, people did.

The encouragement and enthusiasm I received inspired me to keep writing,
and what started as a little experiment eventually grew into something
much larger than I ever intended.

I hope you enjoy reading it as much as I've enjoyed writing it.

**A Note About the Artwork**

All illustrations in this book were generated using ChatGPT.

Why?  Because I'm writing fan fiction, giving it away for free, and
unfortunately do not possess the sort of disposable income required to
commission talented human artists to illustrate an entire novel.  I
would love to be able to do that someday.

Until then, I have a computer, an imagination, and an artificial
intelligence that has been remarkably patient with my increasingly
ridiculous artistic demands.

Please enjoy the pictures.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

A novel set in the **Catgirl Extended Universe (CEU)**  of J.K.
Rowling's Harry Potter.

The "Catgirl Extended Universe" (CEU) is an alternate continuity of
Harry Potter fan fiction in which events, character developments, and
changes to established lore carry forward from one story to another.

*The fun I had creating these stories with you made every moment
worthwhile, even if nobody else ever reads a single word.*

# <span class="c9"></span>

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# <span class="c9"></span>

# <span class="c9">Prologue</span>

## <span class="c12">The Looking Glass of Possibilities</span>

<div class="illustration">

![The Looking Glass of
Possibilites](Images/The%20Looking%20Glass%20of%20Possibilites.jpg)

</div>

Albus Dumbledore was bored.  This was an unusual state of affairs for
the Headmaster of Hogwarts School of Witchcraft and Wizardry, who
ordinarily had more than enough to occupy his time.  There were
disciplinary reports to review, letters from the Ministry to ignore, and
a particularly unpleasant dispute involving Peeves, three Hufflepuffs,
and a quantity of treacle that nobody had yet satisfactorily explained.

</p>

Dumbledore had decided that all of these matters could wait.

Instead, he sat behind his desk, examining an ancient magical artifact
known as *the Looking Glass of Possibilities.*

It was a curious object.  Unlike the Mirror of Erised, which showed
people what they most desired, the Looking Glass revealed worlds in
which history had unfolded differently.  Every choice, every accident,
every improbable coincidence had the potential to create an entirely
different reality.

Dumbledore found the possibilities fascinating.  He also found them
rather entertaining.

He peered into the glass.

A young Harry Potter appeared, sitting at the Slytherin table.
 Dumbledore watched for several moments.  Harry was wearing green and
silver robes and arguing with a young Draco Malfoy, his best friend,
about Quidditch.

"Boring." Dumbledore muttered.

He stirred his wand through the glass as though it were a particularly
stubborn cup of tea.

The image dissolved.  Another appeared.

Harry Potter and Hermione Granger stood beneath a flowering tree,
holding hands.

Dumbledore raised his eyebrows.  "Ah!  Now that's more like it."  

He watched for a moment longer.  "Frankly, that's how it should have
been."

He stirred the glass again.

This time, Harry Potter appeared in the arms of Draco Malfoy.  They were
kissing.  Passionately.  Dumbledore stared.  He blinked.  

"Oh, not *again* ."

Fawkes looked up from his perch.

"How many times must I watch these two fall in love?"

Fawkes gave a sharp, disapproving squawk.  Dumbledore turned angrily
toward the Phoenix.  "What??"

Fawkes squeaked loudly at him.

Dumbledore looked offended.  "CERTAINLY NOT!" he said, loudly.

The phoenix tilted his head at Dumbledore.

"Don't you give me that look!" Dumbledore scolded.  I have absolutely
nothing against two young men falling in love.  In fact, it can be a
beautiful story."

Fawkes just stared at Dumbledore.

"I've just seen it HUNDREDS of times in *The Looking Glass* .  One time,
they are secretly in love.  In another, they are married.  In yet
another, they start a bakery in Hogsmeade!"

Fawkes gave Dumbledore a soft trill in response.

"Yes, the bakery was charming.  That's not the point!"

Dumbledore gestured toward the Looking Glass.  "An infinite number of
possible realities, Fawkes!  Infinite!  Surely somewhere out there is
something a little more original!"

The phoenix regarded him skeptically.

Dumbledore sighed.  "Oh, don't look at me like that.  You know perfectly
well what I mean."

Dumbledore stirred the glass with his wand again.

There was Lucius Malfoy, standing in Madam Malkin's robe shop.  Beside
him stood a young girl.  She had long, platinum-blonde hair, an
aristocratic bearing, and the unmistakable expression of someone who had
already decided that most of the world was beneath her.

Dumbledore frowned.  "Well, well."  He examined the image more closely.

Lucius was speaking to the shopkeeper.  The little girl was examining a
set of expensive dress robes with obvious approval.  Lucius left for
some other errands.

The door opened again.  A small, dark-haired boy entered.

Harry Potter.

The girl turned.  Their eyes met.

Dumbledore leaned forward.  "Ohhhh."

Harry said something.  The girl responded.

Dumbledore began to smile.  "Oh, this is *interesting* ."  He settled
deeper into his chair.  "Lucius Malfoy has a daughter instead of a son."
 He stroked his beard thoughtfully.  "And young Harry Potter appears to
have made quite an impression."

In the glass, the girl tossed her hair and turned away.  Harry watched
her go, looking thoroughly bewildered.

Dumbledore chuckled.  "Now THIS...  This is not something I expected."
 He reached for a tin of lemon drops.

"Now then.  Let's see where this goes."  He popped a sweet into his
mouth.  The Looking Glass shimmered.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# <span class="c9"></span>

# <span class="c9">Year 1</span>

## <span class="c12">The Robe Shop</span>

<div class="illustration">

![The Robe Shop](Images/The%20Robe%20Shop.jpg)

</div>

Harry had been in Diagon Alley for less than an hour and had already
decided that the wizarding world was absolutely brilliant.

It was also completely mad.

There were shops selling cauldrons.  Shops selling owls.  Shops selling
things in jars that Harry had deliberately avoided looking at too
closely.  He had money (an astonishing amount of money, apparently) and
somewhere there was a school waiting for him where people did magic on
purpose instead of accidentally making people's clothing shrink.

And now he learned he needed robes.

Hagrid had left him at "Madam Malkin's Robes for All Occasions" while he
went to fetch something to eat.

"Won't be but a minute, Harry.  You get yerself measured up."

Harry had been slightly nervous about being left alone.  Then he'd gone
inside and seen the girl.  She was standing on a stool in front of a
mirror while a witch knelt beside her, pinning the hem of a black
Hogwarts robe.  She was about Harry's age.  Maybe a little taller,
although the stool made it difficult to tell.  Her hair was almost
white, long and perfectly straight, falling over her shoulders while
another attendant tried to keep it out of the way.

Harry had never seen hair quite like it.

"Not there."

The attendant stopped.

"Sorry, dear?"

"The pin."  The girl pointed impatiently.  "It's pulling."

"Oh.  Of course." The woman adjusted it.

The girl inspected herself in the mirror.  "No.  Now the other side is
uneven."

Harry frowned.  He wasn't entirely certain it was.

The attendant patiently adjusted it again.  "Better?"

The girl tilted her head.  "A little."

Harry decided almost immediately that she was horrible.

Then she turned slightly and Harry saw her properly.  He stared.  He
didn't mean to.  It just sort of happened.

She was...  Well.  Harry didn't really know.  "Pretty" seemed like the
wrong word somehow.  Aunt Petunia called actresses "pretty." Dudley
occasionally called girls on television "fit" when he thought his mother
couldn't hear.

Harry didn't know what he thought, but he definitely thought something.
 He just knew that the strange pale girl with the almost-white hair was
suddenly very difficult not to look at.

"Don't bunch it there." she said.

The attendant took a breath.  "I'm only trying to..."

"I know what you're trying to do.  You're doing it incorrectly."

Harry's fascination diminished slightly.  Definitely horrible.

Madam Malkin appeared from behind a curtain.  "Hogwarts too, dear?"

Harry nodded.  "Yes."

"First year?"

"Yeah."

"Stand up here, then."

A second stool had been placed several feet from the girl's.  Harry
climbed onto it.  A measuring tape immediately began moving around him
by itself.

Harry jumped.  Madam Malkin laughed.  "First time?"

Harry nodded.

"Don't worry.  It won't bite."

Harry watched the tape wrap itself around his chest.  This was
brilliant.

The girl looked at him.  Harry looked away.  A moment later, he looked
back.  She was still watching the mirror.  Harry stared at her hair
again.  Then her face.

She turned.  Harry snapped his eyes forward.  He looked sideways.  She
was staring directly at him.

"What?"

Harry nearly lost his balance.  "Nothing."

The girl's eyes narrowed.  "You were staring at me."

"No, I wasn't."

"You were."

"I was looking at the mirror."

"The mirror is behind me."

"Yeah."

"Which means you were looking at me."

Harry couldn't think of a response to that.  The girl turned fully
toward him.  She seemed prepared to say something unpleasant.  Her chin
lifted slightly.  "You really shouldn't..."

Harry looked at her.  She looked back.  Something strange happened to
her face.

The superior expression disappeared, but only for a second.  Her eyes
moved over Harry's face, stopped at his scar, then returned to his eyes.
 Then his hair.

Harry suddenly became extremely conscious of his hair.  He'd tried
flattening it that morning.  It hadn't helped.

The girl opened her mouth.  Nothing came out.

Harry waited.

"I..."  She blinked.

Harry had the peculiar impression that whatever she'd planned to say had
fallen straight out of her head.

She seemed to remember herself.  Her shoulders straightened.  "My name
is Dracena Malfoy."  The name was delivered with the air of someone who
expected it to mean something.

Harry waited.  Dracena waited.  Apparently he was supposed to react.  He
didn't.

Something resembling disappointment flickered across her face.
 "Malfoy." she repeated.

"Right."

"You haven't heard of my family?"

"No."

That seemed to astonish her.  "Really?"

"Really."

"Are you Muggle-born?"

Harry had heard Hagrid use the word *Muggle*  twenty minutes earlier.
 He wasn't entirely sure what it meant.

"I don't think so."

"You don't think so?"

"My parents were wizards."

Dracena relaxed slightly.  "Oh."

"Well, what's your name?"

"Harry."

"Harry what?"

"Potter."

This time Dracena really did stare.

Harry shifted uncomfortably.  "What?"

"Harry Potter?"

"Yeah."

" <span class="c2 c4">The</span>  Harry Potter?"

Harry sighed.  Apparently this was going to happen a lot.

"I suppose."

Dracena looked at his forehead again.  Harry's hair had fallen partly
over the scar.  She leaned slightly to one side, trying to see it.
 Harry leaned the other way.  She leaned farther.

Harry frowned.  "Could you not?"

Dracena immediately straightened.  "I wasn't doing anything."

"You were staring."

"I was looking at your hair."

Harry stared at her.  Dracena's cheeks turned slightly pink.  She seemed
to realize what she'd said.

"Your scar." she corrected quickly. "I was looking for your scar."

"Oh."

"Everyone knows about it."

"I didn't."

That stopped her again.

"You didn't know about your own scar?"

"I knew I had a scar.  Not that everyone knows about it?"

Dracena stared at him.

Harry was beginning to enjoy doing that to her.

"So you really don't know anything?"

"I know things."

"About our world."

Harry's enjoyment faded.  "Not much."

Dracena considered him with an expression Harry couldn't quite read.  It
wasn't contempt exactly.  If anything, she looked fascinated.

"That's very strange." She said.

"Thanks."

"I didn't mean..."

The shop door opened.  "Harry!"  Harry immediately recognized the
booming voice.  Hagrid ducked his enormous head through the doorway.
 "Yeh nearly done?"

Madam Malkin looked over.  "Just a few more minutes."

"Right!  Thought I'd grab us summat ter eat.  Yeh like pasties, Harry?"

"I don't know."

Hagrid laughed.  "Well, we'll find out!"  He disappeared again, the bell
over the door jingling violently behind him.

Harry smiled.  When he turned back, Dracena was staring at the door.

"What?"

She looked at Harry.  "What *was*  that?"

Harry's smile disappeared.  "That was Hagrid."

"Yes, I gathered it had a name."

Harry frowned.  Dracena didn't notice.

"Why do you have such a brutish servant?"

Harry's fascination with her suffered another considerable setback.
 "He's not my servant."

Dracena blinked.  "Then why is he carrying things for you?"

"Because he's helping me."

"Why?"

Harry stared at her.  "Because he's nice?"

Dracena looked genuinely puzzled by this explanation.

"He's the groundskeeper at Hogwarts."

Her expression went from puzzled to utterly bewildered.  "The
groundskeeper?"

"Yeah."

"At Hogwarts?"

"Yes."

"And he's accompanying you through Diagon Alley?"

"Yes."

"Why?"

Harry hesitated.  That was considerably harder to explain.  "Because he
came to get me."

Dracena stared.  "The Hogwarts groundskeeper came to collect you
personally?"

Harry suddenly felt defensive.  "Yeah."

"Why?"

"Because I didn't get my letters."

"What letters?"

"My Hogwarts letters."

"How could you not get them?"

"My aunt and uncle wouldn't let me read them."

Dracena's face changed.  "Why not?"

"I don't know.  They don't like magic."

She stared at him as though he'd just announced that his relatives
didn't believe in shoes.

"They're Muggles?"

"Yeah."

"But you said your parents were wizards."

"They were."

"Were?"

Harry's stomach tightened.  "They died."

Dracena stopped.  Harry had already learned that this was another thing
people reacted strangely to.

"Oh."  For once she didn't seem to know what to say.  "I'm sorry."

Harry looked at her.  There wasn't anything snobbish in her face now.
 Just an eleven-year-old girl who had realized she'd stumbled onto
something painful.

"Thanks."

Then Dracena's brow furrowed.  "So you've been living with Muggles who
hate magic, you didn't know Hogwarts existed, and they prevented you
from receiving your letters?"

"Pretty much."

"And Hogwarts sent the *groundskeeper* ?"

Harry shrugged.  "He's nice."

"That's not the point!"

Harry almost laughed.  "What's the point?"

"The point is..."

"All finished, dear."

Dracena looked down.

The attendant had stepped back from her robes.

"Oh."

Madam Malkin smiled pleasantly.  "If you'd just step down, we'll have
these wrapped for you."

Dracena stepped off the stool.

Harry watched.

He couldn't help it.

She caught him again.  This time she didn't accuse him of staring.
 Instead she smoothed the front of her dress and tried very hard to look
as though she hadn't noticed.  Madam Malkin handed her the package.

"There you are."

"Thank you."

It was the first time Harry had heard her thank anyone in the shop.  The
attendant looked mildly surprised too.

Dracena walked toward the door and looked back at Harry.

"Harry Potter."

"Dracena Malfoy."

She smiled.  It wasn't the superior little smile she'd worn earlier.
 This one seemed to escape before she could stop it.

"I suppose I'll see you at Hogwarts."

"Suppose so."

"Try to learn something before then."

Harry frowned.  "There it is."

"What?"

"You were almost nice."

Dracena looked offended.  "I *was*  nice."

"You were."  Harry smiled.  "Then you ruined it."

She glared at him.  Harry grinned.  Strangely, Dracena grinned back.

"Goodbye, Potter."

"Bye, Malfoy."

She opened the door.  Harry expected her to leave.  Instead she turned
around.

"Wait."

"What?"

"Why did the groundskeeper have to come and get you?"

Harry opened his mouth.

"Miss Malfoy."  Madam Malkin was still smiling pleasantly, but there was
something in the smile now that suggested she had reached the absolute
limit of her patience.  "Your robes are finished."

"Oh."

Dracena glanced toward the door, then back at Harry.

"But..."

"Your mother is waiting."

Dracena sighed.  "Fine."

She stepped outside.  The door closed.

Harry watched through the window as that pale head disappeared into the
crowd.  Madam Malkin's measuring tape tightened around his waist.

Harry jumped.

"Stand still, dear."

"Sorry."

Harry tried.

"Madam Malkin?"

"Yes, dear?"

"Are all Witches like that?"

She laughed.  Harry wasn't sure why.

He looked toward the window again.  Dracena was gone.  That was probably
for the best.

She was rude.  Spoiled.  Bossy.  She treated shop attendants like
servants.  She'd called Hagrid brutish.  She apparently thought everyone
ought to know who her family was.

Harry didn't like her.  He was quite certain of that.  A minute later,
the bell jingled and Hagrid returned carrying a paper bag large enough
to feed six people.

"All done, Harry?"

"Almost."

"Meet anyone?"

Harry looked toward the window one more time.  "Sort of."

"Nice?"

Harry thought about it.  "No."  Then he thought about white-blonde hair,
a momentary stutter, and the strange way she'd smiled at him before
leaving.  Harry frowned.  "Maybe."

Hagrid raised an eyebrow.

Harry shrugged.  "I dunno."

------------------------------------------------------------------------

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## <span class="c12">The Hogwarts Express</span>

<div class="illustration">

![The Hogwarts Express](Images/The%20Hogwarts%20Express.jpg)

</div>

By the time the train had properly left London, Harry had decided that
Ron Weasley was all right.

This was an enormous relief.  Harry hadn't had many friends before.
 Dudley had made sure of that.  Children at school had learned that
being friendly with Harry meant being unfriendly with Dudley, and Dudley
was considerably larger.

Ron didn't know any of this.  More importantly, Ron didn't seem to care.

He was lanky, red-haired, slightly awkward, and one of so many children
that his mother occasionally got their names wrong.  He knew enormous
amounts about the wizarding world that Harry didn't, but unlike the pale
girl in Madam Malkin's, Ron didn't seem to think Harry was strange for
not knowing them.

Mostly he seemed delighted to be the one explaining things.

"So they really don't move?"

Harry looked at the Chocolate Frog card.  "Photographs?"

"Yeah."

"No."  Harry replied.  "They don't."

Ron looked horrified.  "They just stand there?"

"Sometimes they smile."

"That's creepy."

Harry laughed.  "No, people walking out of photographs is creepy."

"That's normal."

"Not where I grew up."

Ron considered this.  "Huh." was all he managed to say.

They had been sharing sweets for perhaps twenty minutes when the
compartment door opened.  A girl with a great deal of brown hair stood
outside.  "Has either of you seen a toad?"

Harry looked at Ron.  Ron looked at Harry.

"No." Harry said.

"A boy named Neville has lost one."

"Oh."

The girl looked around the compartment as though the toad might be
hiding behind Harry's head.  "I'm Hermione Granger."

"Harry Potter."

Her eyes widened.  Harry braced himself.

"Oh."

She looked at his forehead, then, mercifully, she looked away again.
"I've read about you."

"Everyone keeps saying that."

Hermione seemed to realize she'd embarrassed him. "Sorry."

"It's all right." Harry said, looking at the floor.

Ron introduced himself.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Hermione stayed.  Harry wasn't entirely sure how that happened.  She
asked what they'd bought in Diagon Alley.  Ron mentioned spells.
 Hermione immediately informed them she'd already tried several simple
ones and had been practicing before school.

Ron rolled his eyes.  Harry asked what she'd managed to do.

Hermione brightened.  Within minutes she was explaining something about
wand movements while Ron argued that she couldn't possibly know whether
she'd done it properly because she'd never seen anyone else perform the
spell.

Harry listened to both of them.  It was comfortable.  Strangely
comfortable.  Nobody was trying to impress anyone.  

Harry liked it.  The compartment door opened again.

This time Harry looked up, and forgot what Ron had been saying, because
Dracena Malfoy stood in the doorway.  She had changed into her Hogwarts
robes.  Her pale hair fell over the black fabric in a way Harry thought
looked...

Well, he wasn't sure what it looked like.  He just stared.  Again.

Dracena stared back.  Again.

There was a moment when neither said anything.

Ron noticed.

Hermione definitely noticed.

Dracena recovered first.  "Potter."

Harry blinked.  "Malfoy."

The corner of her mouth rose.  "I thought that was you."

Two girls stood behind Dracena, along with a rather heavy boy Harry
didn't recognize.  They all looked at Harry with considerable curiosity.

Dracena, however, was looking only at him.  "I was wondering where you'd
got to."

"You were?"

She hesitated for a fraction of a second.  "Obviously."

Harry smiled.  Dracena's eyes narrowed.

"What?"

"Nothing."

"You're doing that thing again."

"What thing?"

"Looking at me."

"I'm looking at the door."

"I'm standing in the door."

"Right."

Ron snorted.

Dracena glanced at him.  Her expression changed.  "You're a Weasley."

Ron immediately stiffened.  "Yeah."

"I thought so."  There was something about the way she said it that
Harry didn't like.

Dracena looked at Hermione.  "And you are?"

"Hermione Granger."

Dracena waited.  Hermione waited back.

"Granger." Dracena repeated.

"Yes."

"What family?"

Hermione frowned.  "My family."

One of the girls behind Dracena giggled.

Hermione's face reddened.  Dracena looked confused rather than amused.

"Your parents.  Who are they?"

"They're dentists."

Dracena blinked.  "Dentists?"

"They look after people's teeth."

"I know what a dentist is."

"Oh."

Dracena's eyes narrowed.  Hermione said nothing.

"They're Muggles, then?"

"Yes."

Something clicked into place for Dracena.  "Ah."

Harry recognized that *ah* .  He didn't like that either.  

Dracena returned her attention to him. "Anyway, Potter, I came to find
you."  Harry felt an inexplicable little jump somewhere in his stomach.

"You did?"

"Yes."

She stepped into the compartment.  "I thought you might like to sit with
us."

Harry glanced at Ron and Hermione. "I'm all right here."

Dracena seemed not to have anticipated this answer.  "Well, yes.
 Obviously.  But you don't know anyone yet."

"I know them."  Ron smiled.  Hermione did too.

Dracena frowned.  "You met them today."

"I met you once."

"That's different."

"Why?"

Dracena opened her mouth, then stopped.  She tried to speak again.
 "Because I'm a Malfoy."

Harry stared.  "Right."

Dracena waited.  Harry still had no idea what he was supposed to do with
that information.

She sighed.  "Potter, you said yourself you don't know anything about
our world."

"Thanks."

"I don't mean it as an insult."

"It sounds like one."

Dracena's composure slipped.  "Would you stop being difficult for five
seconds?"

Harry grinned.  "There she is."

Dracena glared.  Then, despite herself, almost smiled.  She suppressed
it.

"I'm trying to help you."

"Help me with what?"

"Everything." She gestured vaguely toward the train, Hogwarts, and
seemed to imply the entire wizarding world beyond it.  "You don't know
the families.  You don't know how things work.  You don't know who
anyone is."

"That's true."

"Exactly."

Dracena brightened.

Harry had finally understood.

"There are people at Hogwarts you should know, Potter.  Important
people.  Good families.  People who could help you."

Harry glanced around. "Like you?"

Dracena smiled.  "Yes."

There was something so completely matter-of-fact about it that Harry
almost laughed.  Dracena wasn't joking.

"You're Harry Potter." she continued. "People are going to want things
from you.  You won't always know why they're being friendly.  You need
someone who understands these things."

That, Harry had to admit, sounded almost sensible.  "And you do?"

"I've been around these people my entire life."  She hesitated.  Then
said, quietly:  "I could show you."

Harry looked at her.  For a moment, all the superiority disappeared.
 There was just Dracena.  The strange girl from the robe shop.  She
really meant it.

Harry smiled.  "That's nice."

Dracena's face lit up.

"But I'm still staying here.  I'm learning a lot from Ron and Hermione."
 The light disappeared.

"Oh."

Harry immediately felt bad.  "I mean, you can stay too."

Dracena looked at Ron, then Hermione.

"No." she said, icily

Ron rolled his eyes.  Dracena ignored him.

"Potter, that's rather what I'm trying to explain."

"What?"

She lowered her voice slightly, as though she were about to share
something important.  "There are some wizarding families that are better
than others."

Ron went rigid.  Harry frowned.  "Better how?"  Harry asked, acidly.

Dracena seemed surprised by the question.  "Better."

"Why?"  Harry asked.

"Older.  More respected.  Better connected.  Families that have
contributed something."

Ron had gone red. "My family's old." Ron said, indignantly.

Dracena glanced at him.  "Yes.  It is."

The way she said it was somehow worse than if she'd disagreed.

Ron stood.  "What's that supposed to mean?"

"Sit down, Ron." Hermione said.

"I'm only telling Potter the truth."

"No." Harry said. "You're insulting my friends."

Dracena looked genuinely startled.  "Friends?"

"Yeah."

"You've known them for an hour."

"So?"

"So..."

Dracena faltered.

Harry waited.

Dracena looked at Ron, then at Hermione.  Then back at Harry.  Something
about this seemed to trouble her far more than Harry expected.

"Potter, I'm offering to help you."

"I know."

"You don't know what you're doing."

"Probably not."

"I do." Dracena said.

"Congratulations."

Dracena's eyes flashed.

Harry felt that strange little spark again.  Annoying her was kind of
fun.  He had absolutely no idea why.

Dracena drew herself up.  "You'd do considerably better at Hogwarts if
you learned to recognize a good opportunity when someone offers you
one."

"Maybe."  Harry smiled.  "But I don't think I need you picking my
friends." The words landed harder than he'd intended.

Dracena's face changed.  The confidence vanished.  Harry saw an
eleven-year-old girl who had offered something she genuinely expected
him to want and had just discovered that he didn't.  She looked hurt.

Harry's smile disappeared.  "I didn't mean..."

Then the mask slammed back into place.

"Fine."  Dracena lifted her chin.  "I was only trying to prevent you
from making an idiot of yourself."

Ron muttered something.

Dracena rounded on him.  "What was that, Weasley?"

"I said he's doing fine without your help."

"Nobody asked you."

"Nobody asked you either."

"Ron." Hermione warned.

Dracena looked back at Harry.  "Enjoy your company, Potter."

"I will."

"Wonderful."

"Brilliant."

"I hope you have a delightful journey."

"Having one so far."

Her eyes narrowed.

"You are incredibly irritating."

Harry grinned. "You came looking for me."

One of Dracena's friends made a tiny choking sound.  Dracena's cheeks
went pink.  "I did not."

"You just said you did."

"I happened to be walking past."

"You said, 'I came to find you.'"

"I was being polite."

"You're not very good at it." Harry said, smiling.

Ron burst out laughing.  Hermione covered her mouth, gasping.

Dracena looked furious.

Harry was having an excellent time.

"You know, Potter, I was beginning to think you were interesting."

"You've been staring at me since you opened the door."  

Dracena's mouth fell open.

Harry realized, a second too late, that he'd noticed because he'd been
staring at her too.  Dracena realized it at exactly the same moment.

They both went red.  Hermione looked between them.  Ron looked
completely lost.

Dracena recovered.  Barely.  "Your hair is ridiculous."

Harry touched it instinctively.

"Your hair looks like somebody poured flour over your head."  Harry
said, grinning.

Dracena gasped.

Ron howled.

"You...!"  Dracena pointed at Harry.  "You have absolutely no idea who
you're dealing with."

"Dracena Malfoy.  You told me twice."

"Goodbye, Potter."

"Bye, Malfoy."

She spun around, and stopped at the door.

Harry waited.  She glanced back for a moment.  Just for a moment.

Harry was still smiling.

Dracena tried very hard not to, and failed miserably.

She then became furious at herself, turning beet red.  She disappeared
down the corridor with her entourage.

The compartment went quiet.

Ron stared after her. "She's awful."

Harry was still looking at the doorway. "Yeah."

"Really awful."  Ron said.

"Yeah."  Harry said, distracted.

"Completely full of herself."

"Definitely."

Harry looked down at the Chocolate Frog in his hand, then back toward
the door.

Ron frowned at him. "What?"

"Nothing."

Hermione was staring at Harry.  Harry noticed.

"What?"

"You like her."

Harry nearly dropped the Chocolate Frog.  "What?"

Ron stared at Hermione.  "Are you mental?"

Hermione shrugged.  "He does."

"I do not!"

Hermione looked toward the corridor.   "She likes you too."

Ron looked positively appalled.  "She just spent five minutes insulting
us!"

"Yes." Hermione said, matter of factly.

"And him!"

"Yes."

"And then he insulted her!"

"Yes."

Ron stared.  "How does that mean they like each other?"

Hermione thought about it. "I don't know.  It's just obvious."  She
 frowned.  "I still think I'm right."

"You're not."

"All right."  She said it in a way that made Harry certain she didn't
believe him.

Ron shook his head.  "Malfoy."

Harry looked at the door again.  "She's not that bad."

Ron stared at him.  "She called my whole family rubbish!"

"She didn't actually say rubbish."

"Harry!"

"All right!  She's horrible."

Hermione smiled.  Harry caught it.

"What?"

"Nothing." she said, smiling.

"You've got that look."

"What look?"

"You've known me less than an hour.  You don't know my looks."

Harry considered that.  "Fair."

------------------------------------------------------------------------

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## <span class="c12">The Sorting Hat</span>

<div class="illustration">

![The The Sorting Hat](Images/The%20Sorting%20Hat.jpg)

</div>

"Malfoy, Dracena!"

Dracena rose from the bench, straightened her robes, and walked toward
the stool with the practiced composure of someone who had been preparing
for this moment since she could walk.  There was no question where she
belonged.  Her father had been a Slytherin.  Her mother had been a
Slytherin.  Generations of Malfoys had been Slytherins.

She sat down.  Professor McGonagall lowered the Sorting Hat onto her
head.

"Ahhh.  A Malfoy.  Well, this should be straightforward.  A long and
distinguished family history of..."

The voice stopped.  Dracena waited.

*"Oh."*

She frowned.

*"Oh, my."*

*"What?" Dracena asked.*

*"Well, now.  That's interesting."*

Dracena shifted impatiently on the stool.

" *Come on.  Get it over with and put me in Slytherin already.* "

*"Oh, you're a cheeky one, aren't you?"*

*"I know where I belong."*

*Yes, yes.  You certainly do.*

*"Then what's taking so long?"*

*Well, there's ambition.  Quite a lot of it, actually.  Cunning.  Pride.
 Resourcefulness.  A rather impressive sense of self-importance, if I
may say so.*

*"You may not."*

The Hat chuckled.

" *And there's the Malfoy temper.  Good heavens, there's a lot of that.*
"

*"Are you quite finished?"*

*"Not remotely."*

Dracena sighed.

*"You know, there's something rather Gryffindor about you."*

Dracena nearly fell off the stool.  "WHAT?"

Several students looked up.

" *Oh, don't look so horrified.  I didn't say I was going to put you
there.* "

*"Good!"*

*"But it's there.  Courage.  A rather stubborn sort of courage, too.
 The kind that doesn't know when to stop."*

*"I'm a Malfoy!"*

*Yes, I had gathered that from the name.*

*"And Malfoys belong in Slytherin!"*

*"Generally speaking, yes."*

*"Then PUT ME THERE!"*

The Hat began to laugh.  A deep, rolling laugh that seemed to shake the
ancient fabric around her ears.

Dracena sat rigidly on the stool. * "What is so funny?"*

*"Oh, nothing.  Nothing at all."*

"You're laughing at me!"

*"I'm afraid I am."*

"Well, stop it!"

That only made the Hat laugh harder.   *"Oh, my dear girl.  You are
going to be trouble."*

Dracena folded her arms.

*"I most certainly am not."*

*"Oh, you most certainly are."*

*"I'll have you know that my father..."*

*"Yes, yes.  Lucius Malfoy.  I remember him.  Very serious young man.
 Very certain of himself.  Rather like you, actually."*

*"Then you should know..."*

*"Oh, I know quite a lot about you, Dracena Malfoy."  * Something in the
Hat's voice changed.  It was still amused, but there was a curious
warmth beneath it.   *"You're clever.  You're proud.  You love your
family.  You know exactly who you are, and you're quite certain you know
who you're going to become."*

*"Of course I do."*

*"Yes.  That's what makes this so entertaining."*

Dracena frowned.   *"What does that mean?"*

*"Oh, you're going to change, my dear."*

*"I most certainly am not!"*

*"Oh, yes, you are."*

*"I'm going to be a Malfoy!"*

*"I rather expect you will."*

*"Then what are you talking about?"*

The Hat chuckled again.  " *Let's just say that you're going to learn
some very interesting things about yourself over the next few years."*

Dracena stared straight ahead.

*"And you're going to cause an extraordinary amount of trouble along the
way."*

*"I am NOT!"*

*"Oh, I do hope it's all for the better."*

*"Wait.  What?"*

*Well, there's only one place for you, of course.*

*"FINALLY!"*

The Hat drew a breath.

" **SLYTHERIN!** "

Applause erupted from the Slytherin table.

Dracena reached up to remove the Hat.  But just before she lifted it
from her head, the voice whispered one final time.

*"Have fun, Dracena Malfoy.  Oh, this is going to be marvelous."  * And
then the Hat began laughing again.

Dracena practically tore it off her head.  She marched toward the
Slytherin table, her cheeks burning.  Behind her, Professor McGonagall
picked up the Hat and regarded it with a faintly suspicious expression.
 For one brief moment, she could have sworn the ancient thing was still
chuckling.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

The hat fell over Harry's eyes.  For a moment, there was only darkness.
 Then a small voice spoke directly into his head.

*"Hmm."*

Harry nearly jumped.

*"Oh, don't do that.  You'll knock me off."*

*"Sorry."*

*"Interesting.  Very interesting."*

Harry waited.  The Hat seemed to be rummaging around inside his head.
 Harry wasn't sure he liked that.

*"Plenty of courage.  Oh, yes.  More than you know.  Loyalty, certainly.
 Rather a reckless streak.  That's going to cause trouble."*

*"What?"*

*"Nothing you need concern yourself with yet."*

The Hat seemed almost to chuckle inside his skull.

*"Gryffindor would suit you very well."*

Harry relaxed.

Then the Hat said:   *"But..."*

Harry immediately became nervous again. *"But what?"*

*"Oh, nothing."*

*"You can't say "but" and then say nothing."*

The Hat laughed.

*"Oh, you're going to be entertaining."*

Harry frowned beneath the brim.

*"What does that mean?"*

*"It means you are very certain about some things for a boy who learned
they existed literally five minutes ago."*

Harry thought indignantly that this wasn't fair.   *Life rarely is.  *

*"Stop doing that."*

*"Doing what?"*

*"Listening to what I'm thinking."*

The Hat was silent for a moment.

*"You do understand what I am, don't you?"*

Harry hadn't really considered that.   *"Never mind."*

" *You might consider Slytherin."*

Harry's stomach dropped.   *"WHAT?"*

Several students nearby glanced toward the stool.  Harry remembered the
pale boy he'd met earlier.  The stories Ron had told him.

Dark wizards.\
Bad people.\
And that blonde girl from Madam Malkin's...

Dracena.  She had said she expected to be in Slytherin.

Harry had liked her, at first.  Then she'd started talking about
Muggles.  And Hagrid.

*"No."*

The Hat sounded amused.   *"No?"*

*"Not Slytherin."*

*"Interesting."*

*"What's interesting?"*

*"You."*

*"Why?"*

The Hat didn't answer immediately.  Harry heard something that sounded
suspiciously like laughter.   *"Oh, my boy."*

*"What?"*

*"Nothing."*

*"You keep saying that!"*

*"Because explaining it would spoil all the fun."*

Harry frowned.

*"I don't want Slytherin."*

*"Yes, yes.  You've made that abundantly clear."*

The Hat shifted slightly on his head.

*"Still..."*

Harry groaned inwardly.   *"What now?"*

*"There are things Slytherin could teach you."*

*"I don't want to be a Slytherin!"*

*"I didn't say you were one."*

Harry stopped.  The Hat's voice became quieter.   *"That's something
children frequently misunderstand about this ceremony."*

*"What?"*

*"You think being placed somewhere means everything outside it must
belong to somebody else."*

*"I don't understand"*

*"You will."*

Something about the Hat's voice made him uncomfortable.

" *There are things you'll need that won't come naturally to you, Harry
Potter."*

Harry's heart jumped.  The Hat knew his name.  Of course it knew his
name.  It was rummaging around through his mind.

*"Patience." the Hat continued. "Restraint.  Cunning.  Knowing when not
to charge directly at the thing frightening you."*

Harry thought that sounded rather cowardly.  The Hat laughed again.

*"Oh, yes.  You are definitely going to be trouble."*

Harry scowled.   *"Can you just put me in Gryffindor?"*

*"I could."*

*"Please?"*

The hat stayed silent for a moment   *"Although..."*

Harry groaned.   *"WHAT?"*

*"Nothing, nothing."*

But Harry could practically feel the Hat smiling.

*"Just remember this conversation someday."*

*"Why?"*

*"Because one day, Harry Potter, I suspect you are going to discover
that Slytherin contains something you very desperately want."*

Harry immediately thought of Dracena Malfoy.  He had no idea why.  His
face became hot.  The Hat burst out laughing.  " *Oh, this is going to
be marvelous."*

*"Shut up!"*

*"As you wish."*

And then, suddenly, the voice wasn't inside Harry's head anymore.

The Hat shouted for the entire Great Hall to hear:   **"GRYFFINDOR!"**

The table to Harry's left exploded in cheers.  Harry pulled off the Hat
and hurried toward them.  Halfway there, for reasons he couldn't
explain, he looked toward the Slytherin table.

Dracena Malfoy was watching him.  She had already been Sorted.

Their eyes met.  Dracena made a face at him.  Harry made one back.

She smiled.  Harry smiled too.  Then he remembered they weren't supposed
to like each other.

He quickly turned away and sat beside Ron.  From the stool behind him
came the faintest rustle of ancient fabric.  Harry could have sworn it
sounded like laughter.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# <span class="c9"></span>

# <span class="c9">Year 2</span>

## <span class="c12">The Flying Lesson</span>

<div class="illustration">

![The Flying Lesson](Images/The%20Flying%20Lesson.jpg)

</div>

Harry Potter had never been particularly good at sports.

This was mostly because Dudley had made certain Harry was never
particularly good at anything.  At his old school, Harry had spent most
of his time avoiding games that involved being chased by large boys.  He
was quite good at running, although he had never considered this an
athletic accomplishment so much as a survival skill.

Consequently, when Madam Hooch announced that their first flying lesson
would involve riding broomsticks, Harry wasn't entirely certain what to
expect.  He knew wizards flew on broomsticks.  He'd seen pictures.  He'd
also seen pictures of dragons, and he sincerely hoped nobody expected
him to ride one of those.

The class assembled on the lawn outside Hogwarts.  A row of rather
battered broomsticks lay on the grass.  Harry examined the one nearest
him.  It looked like something Aunt Petunia might have used to sweep the
garden shed.

"Those are dreadful."

Harry didn't need to turn around.  "Morning, Malfoy."

Dracena Malfoy stood beside him, examining the school brooms with
obvious distaste.  Her platinum-blonde hair was tied back with a green
ribbon.  Her robes were immaculate, her shoes polished, and her
expression suggested that the entire lesson had been arranged
specifically to offend her.

"Honestly, Potter.  Look at them."

Harry looked.  "Yeah?"

"They're ancient."

"So?"

"So they're practically antiques."

Harry shrugged. "Maybe they're valuable."

Dracena stared at him.  "That's not how broomsticks work."

"How would I know?"

She sighed.  "Obviously."

Harry smiled.

Dracena narrowed her eyes.  "Don't."

"Don't what?"

"Look so pleased with yourself."

"I'm not."

"You are."

Harry grinned.  "Can't help it.  You're very entertaining."

Dracena's cheeks turned slightly pink.  She looked away.  "Idiot."

Ron appeared beside Harry.  "Don't mind her.  My dad says the Malfoys
probably think anything that costs less than a hundred Galleons is
firewood."

Dracena turned.  "Weasley."

"Malfoy."

"How delightful.  They've let you out of the charity ward."

Ron went red.

Harry sighed.  "Dracena."

She looked at him.  "What?"

"Must you?"

"Must I what?"

"Be horrible?"

Dracena looked genuinely surprised.  "I'm not being horrible."

Ron made an incredulous noise.

"I'm being accurate."

Harry shook his head.  "There's a difference."

Dracena considered that, then she smiled sweetly.  "Not always, Potter."

Before Harry could answer, Madam Hooch blew her whistle.  "Everyone
beside a broomstick!  Quickly!"

The students hurried into position.  Dracena selected a broom near the
end of the row.  She examined it, made a face, then nudged it with her
shoe.  "Disgusting."

Harry looked at his own.  It had several bent twigs and a handle that
appeared to have been repaired with something resembling glue.  He
decided not to complain.

"Now." Madam Hooch announced, "hold your right hand over your broom and
say 'Up!'"

"UP!" shouted the class.

Nothing much happened.

Ron's broom rolled over.

Neville's twitched.

Hermione's rose several inches, then fell back into the grass.

Harry looked down at the broom.  "Up."  The broom leapt into his hand.

He blinked.  "Oh."

Beside him, Ron stared.  "Blimey."

Harry looked at the broom, then at Ron. "What?"

"You did it."

Harry shrugged.  "Was I not supposed to?"

Across the lawn, Dracena was watching.  Her own broom had risen
obediently into her hand, although not quite as quickly.

She looked at Harry's, then at her own broom.  Her expression darkened.
 Harry noticed.

"What?"  Harry said, acidly.

"Nothing."

"You're staring."

"At the broom."

"Which I'm holding."  Harry grinned at her.

"Unfortunately."

Harry smiled.  Dracena turned away.   *Show-off.*

The irritating thing was that Potter hadn't even looked as though he
were trying.  He'd simply said *Up* , and the broom had practically
jumped into his arms.

Dracena had been flying since she was little.  Father had bought her a
beautiful little training broom when she was six.  She'd spent summers
racing around the gardens at the Manor, occasionally terrifying the
house-elves.

Potter, apparently, had never flown before.  And he'd just done that.
 It was absurd.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Madam Hooch demonstrated how to mount the brooms and grip them properly.
 "Now, when I blow my whistle, kick off gently.  Rise a few feet, hover,
and come straight back down."  She looked sternly around the class.
 "Nobody is to go flying off across the grounds.  Is that understood?"

Everyone nodded.  Neville looked terrified.

Harry glanced at him.  "You all right?"

Neville swallowed.  "Not really."

"Just do what she says."  Neville nodded.

Dracena watched the exchange.  Potter was always doing that, talking to
people who were frightened as though it were perfectly natural.  She
didn't understand it.

Madam Hooch raised her whistle. "On my signal..."

Neville kicked off too early, much too hard.  His broom shot upward.

"Neville!" Madam Hooch shouted.

Neville screamed.

The broom climbed.\
Ten feet.\
Fifteen.\
Twenty.

Harry stared.  "Blimey."

Neville was clinging desperately to the handle.  "HELP!"

The broom bucked.  Neville slipped.  For one horrible second he hung
from the handle by both hands.  Then he fell.

Harry winced.

Neville struck the ground with a sickening thump.

Madam Hooch ran toward him.  "Nobody move!"

Neville was crying.  His wrist hung at an unpleasant angle.  Madam Hooch
examined it.

"Broken.  Come along, dear.  Hospital wing."  She helped him to his
feet, then turned toward the class.  "Everyone is to remain on the
ground until I return.  Anyone who takes off will be out of Hogwarts
before they can say Quidditch."

Harry wondered exactly what "Quidditch" was.

She led Neville away.  The class stood in uncomfortable silence.
 Dracena noticed something glittering in the grass.  "Oh."  She bent
down.  A small glass ball lay near Neville's abandoned broom.  Inside
it, red smoke swirled.  Dracena picked it up.  "Well, look at this."

Ron turned.  "That's Neville's Remembrall."

Dracena examined it.  "Is it?"

"His gran sent it."

"How charming."

She held it up to the sunlight.  "Rather cheap-looking, isn't it?"

Harry sighed.  "Dracena."

She looked at him.  "What?"

"Give it back."

"To whom?"

"Neville."

"Neville isn't here."

"Then give it to me."

Dracena smiled.  "Oh, Potter."

Harry knew that smile.  He'd learned to distrust it.

"Don't."

"Don't what?"

"Whatever you're thinking."

Dracena looked offended.  "I'm not thinking anything."

"That's a first."

Ron laughed.

Dracena's eyes narrowed, then she looked at the broom in her hand.  An
idea occurred to her.  A rather delightful idea.  She smiled.  Harry
groaned.

"Oh, no."

"Oh, yes."

"Dracena."

"Catch me, Potter."

She kicked off.  The broom shot into the air.

Harry stared.  "You're joking."

Dracena climbed rapidly, laughing.  "Come and get it!"

Ron looked at Harry.  "She's mental."

Harry looked at his broom, then up at Dracena as she rose into the air.

He sighed.  "Apparently so am I."

He mounted, kicked off, and chased after her.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Harry had never felt anything like it.

The broom rose beneath him as though it understood exactly what he
wanted.

He leaned forward?  It accelerated.

He shifted his weight?  It turned.

He climbed, and the ground dropped away.

For one glorious moment, Harry forgot everything.

The Dursleys.\
School.\
Lessons.\
The fact that he was breaking Madam Hooch's instructions thirty seconds
after she'd given them.

He laughed.  Loudly.  Flying was brilliant.  Absolutely brilliant.

Above him, Dracena heard him.  She glanced down, as Potter was coming
toward her.

Fast.  Very fast.

Her eyes widened.   *Oh.  * She leaned forward, and her broom
accelerated.

Harry followed.

Dracena glanced back.

He was gaining.

*That's impossible.*

She'd been flying for years.

Potter had been flying for fifteen seconds.

"Give it back!" Harry shouted.

Dracena laughed.  "Come and take it!"

Harry climbed.

Dracena banked sharply left.

Harry followed.

She dove.

He dove.

She rose.

He rose.

Dracena's heart began hammering with excitement.

Potter was keeping up.  He was actually keeping up.  She'd never had
anyone her age do that before.  Her cousins were either too young or too
old, and the children Father invited to the Manor generally let her win.

Potter wasn't letting her win.  He was trying to beat her.    She liked
it.  She liked it enormously.

"You're going to get us both expelled!" Harry shouted.

Dracena looked back.  "Then stop chasing me!"

"Give Neville's Remembrall back!"

"Come and get it!"

"I am!"

"Not very successfully!"

Harry grinned.  He leaned forward, and the broom surged.

Dracena gasped.  Suddenly he was beside her.

"Hello."

She nearly fell off.  "Potter!"

"Malfoy."

"How did you..."

"Nice weather."

"You're impossible!"

"Been told."

Dracena swerved, and Harry followed.

She climbed.  He climbed after her

She dove.  He dove after her.  For several moments neither spoke, they
simply flew.  Harry forgot he was supposed to be annoyed.  They were
laughing.  Actually laughing.  Racing one another across the Hogwarts
grounds.  For a little while, it was just fun, then Dracena remembered
the Remembrall.  She remembered that she was supposed to win.

She pulled sharply upward, and Harry followed.  They hovered high above
the lawn.  Dracena held up the little glass ball.  "Well, Potter?"

Harry smiled.  "Well, Malfoy?"

"Still want it?"

"Obviously."

"Then come and get it."

Harry leaned forward.

Dracena pulled back.

"Ah-ah."

Harry stopped.

She smiled.  "You know, Potter, for someone who's supposed to be famous,
you're remarkably easy to manipulate."

Harry frowned.  "What?"

"All I had to do was pick up a silly little ball, and you came flying
after me."

"Because it's Neville's."

"Yes, yes.  Your poor little friend."

Harry's smile faded.

Dracena noticed, but she'd found her opening.  Dracena Malfoy did not
abandon an advantage once she'd found it.

"Honestly, Potter.  Do you always come running whenever someone needs
rescuing?"

"Only when someone is being horrible."

"Then you must be terribly busy."

"Mostly when you're around."

Dracena laughed.  "See?  There you go again."

Harry frowned. "What?"

"Following me."

Harry stared.

Dracena smiled triumphantly.  "Every time I do something, there you
are."

Harry opened his mouth.  Closed it.

Dracena's smile widened.  "Really, Potter.  People will start talking."

Harry went red.  "About what?"

"Oh, I don't know."  She tilted her head.  "Perhaps they'll think you
like chasing me."

Harry stared.  Dracena was enormously pleased with herself.

Harry smiled.  "Well, you keep running away."  Dracena blinked.  For one
terrible moment she couldn't think of anything to say.  Her cheeks
warmed.

"Only because you're so slow."

Harry laughed.

Dracena lifted the Remembrall.  "Now, I think we've established who's
the better flyer."

Harry looked incredulous. "We have?"

"Obviously."

"You haven't lost me yet."

"No."

She smiled sweetly.  "But I've got what you want."  Harry glanced at the
glass ball.

Dracena saw it.  Suddenly she knew exactly how to end the argument.  She
held the Remembrall over the empty air.

Harry's expression changed.  "Dracena."

She smiled.  "Goodbye, Potter."

She threw it.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Harry didn't think.  He simply moved.

The Remembrall fell.  Harry pushed his broom downward, hard.

The wind roared past his ears.  The ground rushed toward him.  Someone
screamed.  Harry barely heard it.

He could see the little glass ball.\
Falling.\
Falling.

He leaned farther forward.  The broom accelerated.  The lawn filled his
vision.

Ten feet.\
Five.\
Three.

Harry reached.  His fingers closed around the Remembrall.

He pulled up.  The broom responded instantly.  Harry skimmed across the
grass, so low his shoes brushed the tops of the blades.

Then he rose, stopped, and landed.

Silence.

Harry stared at the little glass ball in his hand.  He was breathing
hard.

Ron shouted.  "HARRY!"

The Gryffindors erupted.  Harry grinned.  He'd done it.  He'd actually
done it.

Above him, Dracena hovered motionless.  Her mouth was open.  She'd never
seen anyone fly like that.

Not her cousins.\
Not the older students.\
Not even some of the professional Quidditch players Father had taken her
to watch.

Potter had dived almost vertically, caught the ball, and pulled out of
the dive inches from the ground.

On a school broom.  On his first flight.

Dracena swallowed.

*Oh.*

*She looked down at him.  Harry was laughing.  Ron was slapping him on
the back.  Hermione looked horrified and relieved simultaneously.*

Dracena felt something unpleasant twist inside her.

Jealousy?  Certainly.  Potter had just done something extraordinary.
 Something she wasn't sure she could have done herself.

But there was something else too, a strange, warm feeling.

She'd watched him.\
She'd seen what he'd done.\
And some part of her was...  Proud?

No.  That was ridiculous.  Why would she be proud of Potter?

He'd just made her look like an amateur.

Dracena scowled.

Then she heard a voice.

"POTTER!"

Professor McGonagall stood at the edge of the lawn.

Harry froze.  Dracena smiled.

*Oh, excellent.  *

------------------------------------------------------------------------

"Professor!"

McGonagall looked furious. "Miss Malfoy."

Dracena adopted her most innocent expression.  "Professor, I'm so glad
you're here."

Harry closed his eyes.

*Here we go.*

"Potter has been flying without permission."

McGonagall stared at her.

Dracena continued.  "Madam Hooch expressly forbade us from leaving the
ground, and Potter completely disregarded her instructions."

Harry looked at her.

"Dracena!"

She ignored him.  "He was flying recklessly, Professor.  Very
dangerously .  I was quite concerned."

Ron made a strangled noise.

McGonagall said nothing.

Dracena pressed her advantage.  "I think he ought to be disciplined."

Harry stared.  "You started it!"

Dracena looked offended.  "I beg your pardon?"

"You stole Neville's Remembrall!"

"I found it."

"And flew away with it!"

"I was merely..."

"Miss Malfoy."  McGonagall's voice cut through the argument.

Dracena stopped.  "Yes, Professor?"

McGonagall looked at her for a very long moment. "I saw everything."

Dracena's smile vanished.  "Oh."

" **Everything** ."

Dracena swallowed.

McGonagall turned toward Harry. "Potter.  Come with me."  Harry's
stomach dropped.

"But Professor..."

"Now."

Harry looked at Ron.  Ron looked miserable.  Hermione shook her head.

Dracena stood very still.

Harry turned toward her.  "Thanks a lot, Malfoy."

She opened her mouth.  For some reason, she wanted to say she was sorry.
 "You should have followed the rules, Potter." was what came out,
instead.

Harry glared, then followed McGonagall toward the castle.

Dracena watched him go.  Her victory felt considerably less satisfying
than she'd expected.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Harry was certain he was about to be expelled.

McGonagall marched him through the corridors without speaking.

Harry tried explaining twice.  She ignored him.

Finally they reached a classroom.  McGonagall opened the door.

"Wood!"

A tall, broad-shouldered boy looked up. "Professor?"

"Oliver, I need a word."

Oliver Wood followed them into the corridor.  McGonagall looked at
Harry.  "Potter, this is Oliver Wood.  Captain of the Gryffindor
Quidditch team."

Harry blinked.  "Quidditch?"

Wood looked at him, then at McGonagall.  "Professor?"

"I believe I've found you a Seeker."

Wood stared.

Harry stared.

McGonagall continued. "Mr.  Potter has just performed a dive that I
would hesitate to attempt myself, and recovered a falling object inches
from the ground."

Wood's eyes widened.  "On a broom?"

Harry looked confused.  "Well, yes."

"What kind?"

Harry shrugged.  "One of the school ones."

Wood stared.  "You're joking."

"I assure you, Oliver, I am not."

Wood looked back at Harry.  "How long have you been flying?"

Harry hesitated.  "About five minutes?"

Wood made a peculiar noise.

McGonagall almost smiled.  "Precisely."

Wood grabbed Harry by the shoulders.  "Five minutes?"

Harry nodded.

Wood looked delighted.  "Professor, this is brilliant!"

McGonagall raised a finger.  "Provided Mr.  Potter understands that
flying without permission is not acceptable."

Harry nodded rapidly.  "Yes, Professor."

"And that attempting such maneuvers without supervision is
extraordinarily dangerous."

"Yes, Professor."

"And that he will not be repeating today's performance merely to impress
his classmates."

Harry went red.  "I wasn't trying to impress anyone!"

McGonagall raised an eyebrow.

Harry thought of Dracena, then looked at the floor.

Wood was grinning.  "Well, Harry, welcome to Quidditch."

Harry blinked.  "Really?"

"Really."

Wood looked toward McGonagall.

"That's two second-years, then."

Harry frowned.  "Two?"

Wood nodded.

"Slytherin's got a new Seeker too."

Harry felt a horrible suspicion forming.

Wood continued.

"Apparently she's extraordinary.  Been flying since she was little.
 Their captain's been bragging about her for weeks."

Harry closed his eyes. "No."

Wood looked puzzled.  "What?"

Harry sighed.  "Dracena Malfoy?"

Wood snapped his fingers. "That's her!"

Harry groaned, and Wood laughed.  "What's wrong?"

"Nothing."

"Know her?"

Harry considered.  "Unfortunately."

McGonagall looked amused.

Wood grinned.

"Well, this should be interesting.  Two of the youngest Seekers Hogwarts
has seen in ages."

Harry thought about Dracena.

Her smug smile.\
Her ridiculous hair.\
Her infuriating habit of treating everyone as though they were beneath
her.\
Then he thought about the way she'd flown.

Fast.\
Confident.\
Clever.

He smiled. "Yeah."

Wood noticed.

Harry looked up.  "Yeah, I think it will."

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Dracena found him two days later, practicing on the lawn with one of the
school's battered brooms.  Wood had given him some exercises.

Climb.\
Turn.\
Dive.\
Recover.

Harry was discovering that flying was considerably easier than almost
anything else he'd ever tried.  He was also discovering that he liked it
more than almost anything else he'd ever tried.

"Potter!"  Harry looked down.  Dracena stood beside the pitch.  He
descended.

"Malfoy."

She examined the broom.  Her expression became one of profound disgust.

"You're still using that?"

Harry looked at it. "Yeah."

"Why?"

"Because it flies."

Dracena stared. "Potter."

"What?"

"That's a school broom."

"Very observant."

"It's practically firewood."

Harry shrugged.  "Seems all right to me."

Dracena looked at him, then at the broom.

Harry waited.

"I hear Wood made you Seeker."

Harry grinned.  "Yeah."

Dracena lifted her chin. "Congratulations."

Harry blinked.  That was unexpected.  "Thanks."

She looked away.  "I suppose you weren't entirely dreadful."

Harry smiled. "Careful, Malfoy.  That almost sounded like a compliment."

Dracena flushed.  "Don't be ridiculous."

Harry laughed.  She glared in response

"Honestly, Potter, you can't possibly intend to compete on that thing."

Harry shrugged.  "Why not?"

"Because you'll lose."

Harry smiled.  "To you?"

"Obviously."

"Looking forward to it."

Dracena hesitated.  Something about the way he said it made her stomach
flutter.  She suppressed the feeling immediately.

"Well."  She straightened.  "Perhaps there's another possibility."

Harry raised an eyebrow. "Oh?"

Dracena smiled.  The one she used when she believed she was about to
offer someone something irresistible.  "You could play for Slytherin."

Harry stared.  "What?"

"Slytherin."

"I heard you."

"Well?"

Harry laughed.  "You're joking."

Dracena looked offended.  "Certainly not."

"I'm in Gryffindor."

"Yes, Potter, I'm aware."

"Then how would I play for Slytherin?"

Dracena waved a hand.  "It's not entirely unheard of for students to
play for other Houses."

Harry looked doubtful. "Really?"

"Well.  It's unusual."

"How unusual?"

"That isn't the point."

Harry grinned.  "Right."

Dracena sighed.  "Potter, I'm trying to offer you something."

Harry remembered the train, and smiled.  "Again?"

Dracena's cheeks warmed.  "Don't start."

Harry folded his arms.  "All right.  What?"

Dracena brightened.  "A proper broom."

Harry looked down at the battered school broom.  "What's wrong with this
one?"

Dracena stared.  "Everything."

"It flies."

"Barely."

"I seem to have done all right with it."

Dracena's expression tightened.  She remembered the dive.  The speed.
 The impossible recovery.

"Imagine what you could do with something better."

Harry considered. "Like what?"

Dracena smiled.  "A Nimbus 2000."

Harry blinked. "A what?"

Dracena stared.  "A Nimbus 2000."

"Is that good?"

She looked as though he'd asked whether water was wet.  "Potter."

"What?"

"It's one of the finest racing brooms available."

"Oh."

Harry looked at his own broom.

"Right."

She frowned.  "Well?"

"Well what?"

"Wouldn't you like one?"

Harry shrugged.  "I suppose."

Dracena smiled triumphantly.  "Exactly."

Harry looked suspicious.  "What's the catch?"

"No catch."

"Really?"

"Well, you'd have to join Slytherin's team."

Harry laughed.

Dracena scowled. "Stop laughing.  I'm serious."

Harry looked at her.  He realized she was.

"Dracena, why would you want me on your team?"

She opened her mouth, then stopped.  That was an excellent question.
 She knew perfectly well why.  Potter was talented.  Extraordinarily
talented.  Having him on Slytherin would practically guarantee victory.

It would also mean she'd get to practice with him.  \
Fly with him.\
Talk to him.\
Spend time with him.

Dracena's face warmed.   *Absolutely not.  * She lifted her chin.
 "Because you'd be useful."

Harry's smile faded.  "Useful?"

"Yes."

He looked at her.

Dracena continued.  "Father could arrange it.  He knows people.  He
could make certain you had the best equipment."

Harry considered, then shook his head.  "No, thanks."

Dracena blinked.  "What?"

"No."

"But..."

"I'm happy in Gryffindor."

"Potter, you're being ridiculous."

"Probably."

"You'd have a much better broom."

"Maybe."

"And better opportunities."

Harry sighed.  "Dracena, you know, you'd be a lot more fun if you
weren't such a snob."

Dracena stared at him. "I beg your pardon?"

"You're a snob."

"I am *not*  a snob!"

Harry raised an eyebrow.  "You just offered to buy me an expensive broom
because you think Gryffindor isn't good enough for me."

"That is not what I said!"

"You said I should associate with better people."

"Well, you should!"

Harry spread his hands.  "See?"

Dracena scowled.  "Potter, there is nothing wrong with having
standards."

"No.  But there's something wrong with thinking everyone who isn't like
you is beneath them."

Dracena opened her mouth, then lifted her chin. "Well, I suppose you
wouldn't understand.  You weren't raised among people of any particular
standing."

Harry winced. "See, there you go again."

"What?"

"Being a snob."

Dracena went red.  "I am merely pointing out that we come from very
different backgrounds."

"Yeah.  I noticed."

"And there's nothing wrong with being proud of one's family."

"Didn't say there was."

"Or one's heritage."

"Didn't say that either."

"Then what exactly *are*  you saying, Potter?"

Harry looked at her.  He thought about the robe shop.\
The train.\
The way she'd laughed while he chased her across the grounds.

For a moment he forgot to be annoyed.  "I'm saying you're actually
rather fun when you stop being such a petulant snob.

Dracena blinked.  "What?"

Harry shrugged.  "Flying with you was brilliant."

Her cheeks warmed.  "It was?"

"Yeah."  Harry smiled.  "Until you tried to smash Neville's Remembrall,
anyway."

Dracena looked away.  "Oh."

"I just wish you didn't have to be horrible to everyone else."

Dracena's expression tightened.  "I'm not horrible."

"You're pretty horrible to Ron."

"Well, Weasley..."

"And Hermione."

"Granger is..."

"Dracena."

She stopped.

Harry smiled.  "See?  You were doing it again."

Dracena glared.  "You are the most infuriating boy I've ever met."

"Thanks."

"That wasn't a compliment!"

"I know."

She folded her arms.  "Well, for your information, Potter, I find you
quite irritating myself."

Harry smiled faintly.  "Dracena.  Thanks, but no."

She stopped.

He looked at her.  "I don't really need people to buy my friendship."

Dracena froze.

"Why do you?"

"What?"  Dracena said, angrily

Harry hesitated.

"I just mean..."

"Are you suggesting I buy my friends?"

Harry shifted.  "Well, you keep offering me things."

Dracena's cheeks went red.  "I'm trying to help you!"

"I know."

"Clearly you don't!"

Harry frowned.  "Dracena..."

"My friends are my friends because they want to be!"

Harry looked at her.  "All right."

"They respect me!"

"All right."

"They..."

She stopped.

Because suddenly she wasn't entirely sure what she was going to say.

Pansy liked her.  Of course Pansy liked her.  They'd known each other
for years.  Their families knew one another.  They attended the same
parties.  They went on holidays together.  Pansy liked spending time at
the Manor.

Everyone liked spending time at the Manor.  It was a wonderful house.
 And Father knew everyone.

And...

Dracena felt something unpleasant move inside her.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Harry watched her.  He hadn't intended to upset her quite this much.  

"Look." he said, "I just don't want to leave my team."

Dracena looked up. "Then don't."  Her voice was icy.

Harry sighed.  "Dracena..."

"Enjoy your dreadful broom, Potter."

Harry looked down at it. "Thanks.  I will."

"You really don't know anything about the wizarding world."

Harry smiled. "Learning every day."

She glared.  "You're insufferable."

"Been told."

"You'll regret turning down that broom."

"Maybe."

"You'll lose."

"To you?"

Dracena lifted her chin.  "Of course."

Harry grinned.  "Then I'll see you on the pitch."

Harry shrugged.

He mounted his broom, kicked off, and resumed Wood's exercises.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Dracena returned to the Slytherin common room in a foul mood.

Pansy found her sitting beside the fire. "What's wrong?"

"Nothing."

Pansy sat beside her.  "Potter?"

Dracena glared.  "Why would you think that?"

Pansy smiled.  "Because you're making that face."

"What face?"

"The Potter face."

Dracena stared.

"I do not have a 'Potter face.'"

"Of course not."

Dracena folded her arms.

Pansy waited.

"He refused."

"Refused what?"

"My offer."

"What offer?"

Dracena sighed.  "I offered to arrange for him to join our Quidditch
team."

Pansy blinked. "Why?"

"Because he's talented."

Pansy stared.

Dracena frowned. "What?"

"You want Potter on our team?"

"He's an excellent flyer."

"He's a Gryffindor."

"I'm aware."

"He's Harry Potter."

"Yes, Pansy, I know his name."

"He's irritating!"

"Of course."

"Arrogant!"

"Naturally."

"And completely impossible!"

Pansy nodded.

Dracena glared.  "Stop agreeing with me like that."

Pansy smiled.

Dracena turned toward the fire.

After a while, she said quietly: "He thinks I buy my friends."

Pansy's smile disappeared.  "What?"

Dracena looked down.  "He said he doesn't need people to buy his
friendship."

Pansy frowned.  "That's horrible."

Dracena nodded.  "Yes."

"Potter's horrible."

"Yes."

"An absolute prat."

Dracena hesitated.  "Well, not always."

Pansy stared.

Dracena realized what she'd said.  She immediately scowled. "I mean,
he's still a prat."

"Of course."

Dracena stared into the fire and leaned back in her chair.  "He's going
to lose." she announced.

Pansy looked up. "Who?"

"Potter."

"Right."

Dracena smiled.  A small, private smile.  "He has absolutely no idea
what he's getting himself into."

------------------------------------------------------------------------

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## <span class="c12">The Dueling Club</span>

<div class="illustration">

![The Dueling Club](Images/The%20Dueling%20Club.jpg)

</div>

"You're going to lose, Potter."

Harry Potter smiled. "Morning, Malfoy."

Dracena Malfoy stood opposite him on the raised platform in the Great
Hall, her wand held delicately between two fingers.  She looked
extraordinarily pleased with herself.

Harry had learned to distrust that expression.

"Are you quite finished embarrassing yourself?" she asked.

Harry glanced around.  "Don't think I've started yet."

"Oh, you started the moment you agreed to duel me."

"Really?"

"Naturally."

Harry grinned.  "Funny.  I thought you were the one who asked."

Dracena lifted her chin.  "I thought you might benefit from some
instruction."

"Very generous."

"I do try."

"Must be exhausting."

Dracena smiled sweetly.  "Not nearly as exhausting as explaining things
to you, Potter."

Several students laughed.

Harry rolled his eyes. "Very funny."

"Thank you."

"Wasn't a compliment."

"I know.  I'm accustomed to receiving those from people who actually
know what they're talking about."

Harry laughed.  "All right, Malfoy."  He raised his wand. "Let's see
what you've got."

Dracena's smile widened.  "Oh, Potter."  She tilted her head.  "I
thought you'd never ask."

Professor Lockhart, who had been watching the exchange with obvious
delight, clapped his hands.  "Wonderful!  Wonderful!  A little
competitive spirit!"

Professor Snape stood beside him, looking considerably less delighted.
 "Perhaps." Snape said dryly, "we might proceed before the students
graduate."

Lockhart laughed.  "Quite right, Severus!"

He turned toward Harry and Dracena.

"Now, remember, this is a demonstration.  We are practicing defensive
spells, not attempting to injure one another."

Dracena smiled.  "Of course, Professor."

Harry looked at her.  "Why don't I believe you?"

"Because you're not terribly bright."

Harry grinned.  Dracena grinned back.  Evilly.

Snape raised his wand.  "On my signal."

Harry and Dracena bowed.  Dracena's bow was elegant.  Harry's was
slightly awkward.

She smiled again  "Honestly, Potter.  Even your bowing needs work."

Harry straightened.  "Shut up, Malfoy."

She laughed.  Snape sighed.  "Three."

Harry tightened his grip.

"Two."

Dracena raised her wand.

"One."

They moved.

" **Expelliarmus** !" Harry shouted.

" **Protego** !"  Dracena's shield flashed.  Harry's spell rebounded
harmlessly.

She smiled.  "Predictable."

" **Stupefy** !"

Dracena ducked.  "Too slow, Potter!"

"Then stop moving!"

"That rather defeats the purpose!"

She flicked her wand.  " **Rictusempra** !"

Harry twisted aside.  The spell struck the wall behind him.

He grinned.  "Missed!"

Dracena's eyes narrowed.

"Did I?"  She raised her wand again.  " **Serpensortia** !"

A jet of light burst from its tip.  Something enormous and dark struck
the platform.  A snake.  A large, black snake.  It raised its head and
hissed.  The students nearest the platform shrieked and stumbled
backward.

Harry stared.  "Blimey."

Dracena smiled triumphantly.  "Well, Potter?"

Harry looked at the snake.  "Bit dramatic, isn't it?"

"Naturally." She lifted her chin.  "Some of us appreciate spectacle."

Harry laughed, and Dracena made a terrible mistake.  She looked at him.

Harry was standing with his wand raised, his dark hair falling across
his forehead, his green eyes narrowed in concentration.  He was smiling.
 Dracena felt something peculiar happen in her stomach.

*Oh.  * She blinked.

Harry looked rather...

Well.

Not handsome, exactly.  That would be ridiculous.  But there was
something about the way he stood.  The way he held his wand.  The way he
looked at her as though she were someone worth fighting.

Someone worth challenging.

Someone worth...

*Stop that.  * Dracena shook her head, and lost control of the snake.

It turned, slithering toward the edge of the platform.  A frightened
Hufflepuff girl stumbled backward.  The snake raised its head and
hissed.

Harry saw it.  "Hey!"

He turned away from Dracena.  "Leave her alone!"

The snake hissed again, examining the Hufflepuff girl with snake-like
interest.

Harry frowned.  "I said leave her alone!" he yelled at the snake.

The snake froze and turned toward him. "Really?" The snake said,
insulted.  "I wasn't going to hurt her.  Honestly."

The snake lowered its head, then coiled quietly on the platform.

Harry stared for a moment.  "Did that snake..."

Nobody answered him.

Dracena stared at him.  Her mouth was slightly open.

Harry frowned.  "What?"

Then Lockhart exclaimed:  "Extraordinary!"

Harry turned.  Lockhart looked delighted.  "Parseltongue!"

Harry blinked.

"What's 'Parcel Tongue?'  Talking to packages?"  The previously
frightened Hufflepuff girl laughed a bit.

Snape's expression had become unreadable.  Dracena was still staring.
 Harry looked at her.

"Dracena?"  She blinked.  He smiled.  "Your snake's behaving now."

Dracena opened her mouth, but nothing came out.

Harry raised his wand.  " **Expelliarmus** !"

Dracena's wand flew from her hand, and clattered across the platform.
 She stared.

Harry grinned.  "Got you."

Dracena blinked, then her face went scarlet.  "That's not fair!"

Harry laughed. "What?"

"I was distracted!"

Harry smiled.  "Then don't get distracted next time."

Dracena glared.  "Potter!"

"Malfoy."

"You were talking to a snake!"

"Apparently.  I think it talked back."

"That's not normal!"

Harry considered.  "But conjuring one is?"

Several students laughed.  Dracena's cheeks burned.

Harry smiled.  "Good duel, though."

She stared, then looked away.  "Shut up, Potter."

Harry laughed.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# <span class="c9">Year 3</span>

## <span class="c12">The Argument</span>

<div class="illustration">

![The Argument](Images/The%20Argument.jpg)

</div>

Harry, Ron, and Hermione were sitting beneath a tree near the lake.  It
was an unexpected day off.  The weather was beautiful.

Ron was eating something.  Hermione was reading.  Harry was lying on his
back, watching clouds drift across the sky.

For once, everything was peaceful.

"Potter."

Harry closed his eyes.  "Of course."

Dracena Malfoy stood nearby with Pansy Parkinson.

Harry sat up.  "Malfoy."

Dracena smiled.  "Enjoying your afternoon?"

"Was."

She looked at Hermione.  "Still studying, Granger?"

Hermione glanced up.  "Yes."

Dracena smiled sweetly.  "How industrious."

Hermione returned to her book.

Dracena continued.  "I suppose when one hasn't the benefit of
generations of magical education, one must work terribly hard to
compensate."

Hermione stopped reading.

Harry sat up straighter.

Ron scowled.  "Oh, shut up, Malfoy."

Dracena looked at him.  "Weasley."

Harry sighed.  "Dracena."

She turned.  "What?"

"Must you?"

Dracena frowned.  "Must I what?"

"Be horrible?"

She looked offended.  "I'm not being horrible."

Harry raised an eyebrow.  "Really?"

"I'm merely observing that Granger has certain disadvantages."

Hermione slammed her book shut.  "Such as?"

Dracena looked at her.  "Your upbringing."

Hermione stared.  "My upbringing?"

"Yes."

Hermione smiled thinly.  "My parents are dentists."

Dracena blinked.  "How charming."

"They're rather good at it."

"I'm sure."

Harry stood.  "Dracena."

She looked at him.

He wasn't smiling.  "What's wrong with Hermione's parents?"

Dracena frowned.  "They're muggles.."

"Why does it matter?"

She sighed.  "Potter, you wouldn't understand."

Harry folded his arms.  "Try me."

Dracena lifted her chin.  "Magical families have traditions."

Harry nodded.  "Right."

"Histories."

"Right."

"Responsibilities."

Harry waited.  Dracena continued.

"My family has preserved magical knowledge for centuries."

Harry considered.  "That's nice."

Dracena frowned.  "Nice?"

"Yeah."

She stared.  "Potter, it's rather more than nice."

Harry shrugged.  "All right."

Dracena looked exasperated.

"Some of the oldest magical families have spent generations preserving
spells, artifacts, histories, and knowledge that would otherwise have
been lost."

Harry nodded.  "Fine."

"Fine?"

"Yeah."

Dracena stared.

Harry continued.  "But what does any of that have to do with Hermione?"

Dracena opened her mouth, but nothing came out.

Harry waited.

She frowned.  "Because she doesn't have that."

Harry looked at Hermione, then back at Dracena.

"So?"

Dracena stared.

"She's still brilliant."

"Yes, but..."

"She's top of nearly every class."

"I know."

"She's better at Charms than I am."

Dracena looked annoyed.  "Potter..."

"Better at Transfiguration."

"Yes..."

"Better at Potions."

Ron snorted.  

Harry smiled faintly.  " **Much**  better at Potions."

Hermione tried not to laugh.

Harry turned back.  "So what difference does it make?"

Dracena looked increasingly frustrated.  "Because heritage matters!"

Harry shrugged.  "Why?"

Dracena stared.  "Why!?"

"Yeah."

She looked at him as though he'd asked why the sky was blue.  "Because
it does!"

Harry sighed.  "That's not an answer."

Dracena went red.  "Potter, you have absolutely no appreciation for
tradition."

Harry folded his arms.  "Maybe I don't."

Dracena blinked.

He continued.  "Maybe I don't understand why a bunch of old families
spend so much time congratulating themselves for having old ancestors."

Dracena's expression changed.  Harry didn't notice.  "Honestly, half the
traditions I've heard about seem like excuses to treat people badly."

Dracena's face tightened.  "That's not fair."

Harry shrugged.  "Isn't it?"

"No.".  She stepped closer.  "Potter, you have no idea what you're
talking about."

Harry looked at her.  "Then explain it."

Dracena took a breath.  "My family has spent centuries preserving
magical knowledge."

Harry nodded.  "You said."

"Some spells are dangerous."

"Right."

"Some artifacts are dangerous."

"Right."

"Some knowledge shouldn't simply be handed to anyone who asks."

Harry frowned.  "Why not?"

"Because people can misuse it!"

Harry considered.  "All right."

Dracena blinked.

"That's fair."

She looked surprised.

Harry smiled slightly.  "But that still doesn't explain why Hermione's
parents matter."

Dracena stared.

Harry continued.  "She's not asking for dangerous spells."

"That's not the point!"

"Then what is?"

Dracena opened her mouth, but nothing came out again.  She looked like
her brain had skipped the tracks.

Harry waited.

She looked toward Hermione.  Hermione was watching quietly.

Harry continued.  "Why should Hermione's accomplishments count for less
because her parents are Muggles?"

Dracena swallowed.  "Because..."
