Harry waited.

She tried again.  "Because magical heritage..."

"Is important.  I know."

He looked at her.  "But why does that make Hermione less important?"

Dracena stared at him.

She was furious.  Harry was always doing this.  Always asking her
questions to things she knew, but couldn't explain.  Not that Potter
would listen anyway.  Or understand.  That was infuriating.

Because Potter was standing there looking at her.  Waiting.

...And she realized he was right.  Again.

Dracena's face burned.

Then she remembered what he'd said about her family.

*"A bunch of old families congratulating themselves for having old
ancestors."*

Her eyes stung.  That wasn't what her family was!  That wasn't what
their traditions meant!

Father had shown her ancient books.  Beautiful enchanted tapestries.
 Family records going back centuries.  Things people had preserved
through wars and disasters.  Things that would have vanished if nobody
had cared enough to keep them.  And there was Potter dismissing all of
it as though it were nothing.

As though her family were nothing.

Dracena looked at him.

Her voice was quiet.  "You really don't understand."

Harry frowned.  "Then tell me."

She swallowed.  "I just did."

Harry sighed.  "No.  You told me your family has a history.  You didn't
tell me why that makes Hermione inferior."

Dracena's eyes filled.  She blinked furiously.  Harry noticed.

His expression softened.  "Dracena..."

"Don't."

Harry stopped.

She lifted her chin.  "You're an idiot, Potter."

Harry said nothing.

Dracena waited.  Normally he'd say something back.\
Something clever.\
Something irritating.\
Something that would let her laugh and forget how badly he'd hurt her.

But he didn't.  He simply looked at her, and that was worse.

Much worse.

Dracena swallowed.  "Well."

She straightened.  "I suppose there's no point discussing this with
someone who knows absolutely nothing about the wizarding world."

Harry looked at her.  "Maybe."

She blinked.

"But I know Hermione."

Dracena stared at Harry

Harry smiled sadly.  "And I think that matters more."

Dracena's face crumpled for just a moment.

Then she turned.  "Come along, Pansy."

Pansy looked confused.  "Dracena?"

"Now!" she shouted

They walked away.  Dracena held herself perfectly straight.  Her head
high.  Her shoulders back.  Every inch the aristocratic young lady,
until they turned the corner.

Then she ran.

"Wait!" Pansy yelled after her.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Harry watched Dracena disappear.  He felt strangely miserable, but
couldn't understand why.  

Ron, however, looked delighted.  "Well, that shut her up."

Harry said nothing.

Hermione looked at him.  "Harry?"

He turned.  "Yeah?"

She smiled.  "Thank you."

Harry shrugged.  "For what?"

"For defending me."

Harry looked surprised.  "Of course."

Hermione smiled.  "You didn't have to."

Harry frowned.  "Yeah, I did."

She looked at him.

"I can't stand the way she talks about you."

Hermione nodded.  "Neither can I."

Ron snorted.  "She's horrible."

Harry sighed. "Yeah."

Hermione watched him, then smiled slightly.  "But you still like her."

Harry froze.  "What?"

Hermione's smile widened.  "You do."

Harry went red.  "I do not!"

Hermione grinned.  "I still think she likes you, too."

Ron stared.  "WHAT?"

Harry glared.  "Hermione!"

She laughed.

Harry pointed at her.  "Don't start!"

Ron looked horrified.  "Harry!"

Harry turned.  "What?"

"Malfoy?"

Harry covered his face.  "Oh, for heaven's sake."

Ron stared.  "Dracena Malfoy?"

Harry groaned.

Hermione laughed.

Ron looked as though he'd just discovered Harry had been secretly
raising a dragon in his bed.

"Harry!"

Harry dropped his hands.  "I DON'T LIKE HER!"

Hermione smiled sweetly.  "Of course not."

Ron shook his head.  "Blimey."

Harry stared at both of them, then sighed.

"You're both impossible."

Hermione laughed.

Ron grinned.

Harry sat down.

After a moment, he smiled too.

Because whatever else happened, he had Ron and Hermione.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Dracena Malfoy could not stop thinking about the argument she'd had with
Harry Potter.

*"Why should Hermione's accomplishments count for less because her
parents are Muggles?"*

It was an irritating question.  An extraordinarily irritating question.

Mostly because Potter had asked it.

Because he *was*  wrong.  Wasn't he?

Obviously.

He simply didn't understand.  He hadn't grown up surrounded by magic.
 He hadn't spent his childhood listening to stories about the great
wizarding families, learning their histories, studying the spells and
traditions they'd preserved for centuries.

He didn't understand what it meant to be a Malfoy.  He'd dismissed all
of it as a bunch of old people congratulating themselves for having old
ancestors.

Dracena still felt a little sting whenever she remembered that.

Still, the question scratched around in her head, like a pebble in her
shoe.

*"Why should Hermione's accomplishments count for less?"*

Dracena tried to ignore it, but she couldn't.

So she began watching Hermione Granger.  At first, she told herself she
was looking for mistakes.  For evidence that Granger wasn't quite as
clever as everyone believed.

Surely there was something.  Some weakness.  Some gap in her ability.
 Some indication that generations of magical education really did
matter.

In Charms, Hermione performed spells with extraordinary precision.

In Transfiguration, she mastered difficult transformations before almost
anyone else.

In Potions, she understood concepts Dracena had been taught since
childhood.  That was particularly irritating.  Dracena had grown up
surrounded by magic.  She'd had private tutors.  An enormous family
library.  Parents who could answer her questions.  People who'd been
teaching her about the wizarding world since before she was old enough
to hold a wand.

Granger had none of those things.  Her parents were dentists, for
heaven's sake.   *Dentists.  * Dracena wasn't entirely certain what
dentists did, although she understood it involved Muggles' teeth.

And yet somehow their daughter was among the best students at Hogwarts.

It was ridiculous.  It was infuriating.  Dracena was beginning to
suspect, it was also rather extraordinary.

One afternoon, during Charms, Professor Flitwick posed a particularly
difficult question about the limitations of permanent enchantments.

Dracena knew the answer, or at least, most of it.  She'd read about the
subject in one of Father's books.  She raised her hand.

So did Hermione.  Flitwick called on Hermione.

Of course he did.

Hermione answered.  And she kept answering.

Dracena slowly lowered her hand, because Hermione hadn't merely given
the correct answer.  She'd explained the underlying magical principles,
identified two important exceptions, and even mentioned an obscure
historical example Dracena had never heard of.

Flitwick positively beamed.  "Excellent, Miss Granger!  Absolutely
excellent!  Five points to Gryffindor!"

Hermione smiled.  Dracena stared at her.

*Oh, for Merlin's sake.*

Beside her, Pansy Parkinson made a disgusted noise.  "That stupid
Mudblood."

Dracena glanced at her.  Pansy scowled.  "Honestly.  Flitwick
practically falls over himself giving her points."

Dracena looked toward the front of the classroom.  Flitwick had already
moved on to another student.

She frowned.  Had he favored Hermione?  He'd asked a question.  Hermione
had answered it.  Correctly.  Rather brilliantly, in fact.  Five points
seemed perfectly reasonable.

Dracena looked back at Pansy.  "I don't think he was favoring her."

Pansy blinked.  "What?"

"Flitwick.  She answered the question."

Pansy rolled her eyes.  "Oh, honestly, Dracena."

She leaned closer.  "Of course she did.  She's always got her hand up,
hasn't she?  Desperate to prove she's just as good as the rest of us."

Dracena looked toward Hermione.  She was writing something in her notes.
 Harry leaned over and whispered something to her.  Hermione smiled,
then she whispered something back.

Harry laughed.

Dracena frowned.   *Stupid Potter.*

*Pansy continued her tirade.  "It's embarrassing, really.  All that
showing off.  You'd think someone would tell her that no matter how many
books she reads, she's still just a..."*

"Mudblood." Dracena finished quietly.

Pansy smiled.  "Exactly."

Dracena looked at Hermione again.  She thought about the question.  The
answer.  The five points.

All the things Hermione Granger had accomplished without the advantages
Dracena had enjoyed her entire life.

For the first time, the word sounded rather ugly.  Father used it
occasionally.  So did some of his friends.  It was simply something
people said.  But now, sitting beside Pansy, listening to her use it to
explain away an accomplishment neither of them could honestly criticize,
Dracena found herself strangely uncomfortable.

It hadn't made Hermione's answer any less brilliant.  It hadn't made
Flitwick's praise any less deserved.  It hadn't explained anything at
all.  It was just a nasty little word to throw at someone when you had
nothing better to say.

Dracena looked down at her notes.

She thought of Father.  Of the Malfoy family library.  Of the ancient
books and spells and histories she'd been taught to treasure.

She was proud of those things.  She would always be proud of them.  But
she'd never thought that being a Malfoy meant she ought to resent
someone else for being clever.

Quite the opposite.  Father had always insisted that excellence was
something to be admired.

Hermione Granger, irritating though she was, was undeniably excellent.

Dracena frowned.  Well, that's inconvenient.

Three rows ahead, Harry Potter was laughing at something Ron Weasley had
whispered.  Dracena glared at the back of his head.  " *Stupid Potter."
she said to herself.*

Somehow, once again, Harry Potter had managed to make her question
something she'd never thought to question before.  The worst part was
that he wasn't even near her to argue with.

Pansy was still talking.  "...and honestly, I don't know why they even
let people like that into Hogwarts.  My father says standards have been
slipping for years.  It's no wonder when they keep admitting every
little Mudblood who..."

"Pansy?"

Pansy turned.  "Yes?"

Dracena looked at her.  "Do shut up."

Pansy stared.  "What?"

Dracena returned to her notes.  "You heard me."

Pansy's mouth fell open.  "Dracena!"

"Honestly, Pansy.  You're giving me a headache."

Pansy looked scandalized.  "I was only saying..."

"I know what you were saying."  Dracena dipped her quill into the ink.
 "That's the problem."

Pansy stared at her.  For several seconds, neither spoke.

Then Pansy huffed and turned away.

Dracena continued writing.

*Granger.*

She paused, then continued taking notes.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## <span class="c12">The Lesson</span>

<div class="illustration">

![The Lesson](Images/The%20Lesson%201.jpg)

</div>

Neville Longbottom was beginning to regret signing up for extra credit.
 At the time, it had seemed like an excellent idea.

Professor Lupin was a good teacher.  Neville liked him.  Unlike some
professors, Lupin didn't seem to believe that being frightened or
getting something wrong was a personal failing.

Neville also desperately needed the extra credit.  His marks in Defense
Against the Dark Arts weren't terrible, but they weren't particularly
good either, and his grandmother had been rather emphatic about the
importance of improving them.

Unfortunately, Neville hadn't realized that the advanced class would
involve **actual**  Dementors.  He stared at the enormous wooden cabinet
at the front of the classroom.

Something inside it moved.  Neville swallowed.

"Oh, dear."

"You're not the only one thinking that."  Neville turned.  Harry Potter
was standing beside him.  Neville managed a weak smile.

"You're frightened too?"

Harry looked at the cabinet.  "Well, yeah."

Neville blinked, and Harry shrugged.

"Have you **seen**  those things?"

That made Neville feel slightly better.  Only slightly.

Hermione Granger was sitting at a desk, reading an enormous book she'd
brought with her.  She had already covered several pages in notes.
 Neville had no idea how she'd managed to take notes before the lesson
had even begun.

Standing near the window, looking thoroughly displeased with the entire
arrangement, was Dracena Malfoy.

She examined the enormous cabinet inside the classroom, then her eyes
traveled to the three Gryffindors.  "Oh, wonderful."

Harry looked over.  "Morning, Malfoy."

"Potter."

"Nice to see you too."

Dracena folded her arms.  "Of all the students in Hogwarts, I appear to
have been assigned to this class with the three of you."

Hermione looked up.  "You weren't assigned.  You volunteered."

Dracena gave her a cold look.  "Father insisted."

Harry smiled.  "Couldn't say no?"

Dracena's eyes narrowed.  "Some of us have parents who take our
education seriously, Potter."

Harry's smile faded.  Dracena noticed.  For a moment, she looked as
though she might apologize.  Instead, she lifted her chin.
 "Particularly when it concerns dangerous magic.  Old families have
enemies, you know."

Harry shrugged.  "Must be exhausting."

"It is."

"All those people not liking you."  Harry grinned, as Dracena glared at
him

Neville watched them with mild bewilderment.  They always did this.  He
wasn't entirely sure why.

Professor Lupin entered before Dracena could respond.  "Good morning,
everyone."

"Good morning, Professor." Hermione said brightly.

Lupin looked around.

"Four students.  Excellent."

His eyes fell on Dracena.  "Miss Malfoy.  I'm pleased to see Slytherin
represented."

Dracena lifted her chin.  "Father believes advanced defensive magic is
essential for anyone from a family of consequence."

Lupin regarded her for a moment. "I should think it essential for anyone
who might encounter danger, regardless of their family."

Dracena looked faintly annoyed.  "Naturally."

"Good.  Then we agree."

Harry coughed.  Dracena glared at him.

Lupin smiled.

"Now.  Dementors."

The room became very quiet.

Something moved inside the cabinet.  Neville's stomach tightened.  
Harry's wasn't  doing much better.  Lupin turned toward it.

"Before anyone becomes alarmed, I want to explain precisely what we're
doing today."  He tapped the cabinet with his wand.  Several glowing
symbols appeared across its surface.  One looked like letters that moved
when Harry tried to read them.  The second was a circle surrounding a
triquetra.  The third was a series of interconnected circles.  The last
looked like a feather of some kind of magical beast; perhaps a
hippogriff.  

"These are containment and resistance charms.  The Dementors inside have
been bound to a limited manifestation.  They cannot administer the Kiss,
make physical contact, or feed upon you beyond a carefully controlled
threshold."

Hermione raised her hand.  "Professor, Dementors aren't ordinarily
susceptible to that sort of binding."

"Quite right."  Lupin smiled.  "Which is why this is an advanced
lesson."  He gestured toward the glowing symbols.  "The charms are
experimental.  They've been developed specifically for supervised
defensive instruction.  What you encounter today will feel real, but the
danger has been greatly reduced."

Neville stared at the cabinet.  "Reduced?  Not Eliminated?"

Lupin looked at him.  "Yes, Neville.  The fear will be real.  The cold
will be real.  Some unpleasant memories may surface.  But I will be
here, and the containment will prevent anything worse."

Neville swallowed.  "Right."

Lupin's expression softened.  "That's why we're doing this one at a
time."

He turned toward the blackboard.

With a flick of his wand, words appeared:  EXPECTO PATRONUM

"The Patronus Charm is among the most difficult defensive spells you
will ever learn."

Hermione's hand shot up.  "Isn't it normally taught much later?"

"Yes."

"Then why are we learning it now?"

"Because I believe some students are capable of more than the curriculum
expects."  He looked at all four of them.  "...and because learning how
to face fear is valuable even when you don't succeed immediately."

Neville wished he'd chosen a different extra-credit class.  Perhaps
Herbology.  He was good at Herbology.  Most plants didn't suck happiness
out of people.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

"Neville, would you like to begin?"

Neville's heart sank.  "Me?"

"Yes."

Neville looked toward the cabinet, then toward the door, then back at
Professor Lupin.

"Do I have to?"

"No."

That surprised him.

Lupin pulled a chair closer.  "Sit down for a moment."  Neville obeyed,
and Professor Lupin sat opposite him.

"Tell me what you're afraid of."

Neville looked embarrassed.  "The Dementor."

"Of course.  Anything else?"

Neville hesitated.  "Getting it wrong."

Lupin nodded.  "Why?"

Neville looked down.  "Because everyone else will probably get it
right."

Behind him, Dracena made a faint noise.  Neville didn't look.

Lupin leaned forward.  "Neville, listen to me.  This is difficult magic.
 Very difficult.  I don't expect any of you to produce a fully formed
Patronus today."

Hermione looked mildly disappointed.  Lupin noticed and smiled.  "Even
you, Miss Granger."

Hermione blushed.

"Now." Lupin continued, "the charm requires a powerful positive thought.
 A happy memory, usually, but it can be anything.  Something that can
stand against the despair a Dementor creates."

Neville nodded.

"You must hold onto that memory.  Not merely remember what happened, but
remember how it felt."

"Right."

"Have you chosen one?"

Neville hesitated, then gently nodded.

"Good."  Lupin stood.  "Let's try."

Neville approached the cabinet.  His legs felt like jelly.  Harry gave
him a thumbs-up.  Hermione smiled encouragingly.  Dracena watched with
an expression of polite skepticism.

Lupin raised his wand.  "Ready?"

Neville wasn't.  "Yes, Professor."

The cabinet opened.  Cold spilled into the classroom.  Neville's breath
caught.  A dark, hooded figure emerged.  It floated several inches above
the floor.  The air thickened around it.  Neville felt the happiness
drain out of him.  Suddenly he was thinking about his grandmother.\
About how disappointed she'd be.\
About his parents.\
About all the things he couldn't do.

The Dementor approached.  Neville raised his wand.  "Expecto..."  

His voice cracked.  The Dementor came closer.

"Expecto Patronum!"  Nothing happened.  The cold deepened.  Neville
stumbled backward.

Lupin raised his wand.  The glowing symbols on the cabinet brightened
for a moment, and The Dementor recoiled.  The cabinet doors slammed
shut.  Warmth returned.

"That's Enough." Professor Lupin said.

Neville stood trembling.  "I'm sorry."

Lupin looked at him.  "For what?"

"I couldn't do it."

"Of course you couldn't."

Neville blinked.

Lupin smiled.  "It's your first attempt."

"But..."

"Neville, failure isn't something to be ashamed of."

Neville looked down.  Lupin continued.  "Failure teaches us things.
 Tell me, what did you learn?"

Neville thought.  He could still feel the cold.  He could still remember
the horrible sense that nothing good would ever happen again.

"I think..."

Lupin waited.

"I think I got stuck in my own head."

"How so?"

"I was thinking about how frightened I was."

Lupin nodded.

"And?"

"And then I was thinking about how I was going to fail."

"Yes."

"And then I couldn't remember the happy thing anymore."

Lupin smiled.  "Excellent."

Neville stared.  "Excellent?"

"Yes.  You've identified exactly what went wrong."

Neville considered that.

Lupin continued.  "Fear is not something to be ashamed of, Neville.
 It's a perfectly natural response to danger."

A short laugh came from the window.  Dracena had covered her mouth.  Not
quite quickly enough.

Everyone turned.  Harry glared.  Hermione frowned.  Neville's face
burned.  Lupin looked at Dracena.  "Something amusing, Miss Malfoy?"

Dracena straightened.  "Not at all, Professor."

"Then perhaps you'd care to explain?"

She hesitated.  "I merely thought that being afraid of something that
cannot actually hurt us was rather silly."

Harry's expression hardened. "He's trying, Malfoy."

Dracena looked away.  Lupin regarded her for a moment.  "Fear rarely
waits for our permission to be rational, Miss Malfoy."

Dracena said nothing.

"Something worth remembering when it's your turn."

Her cheeks reddened.

Lupin turned back to Neville.  "Now.  Shall we try again?"

Neville looked at the cabinet, then at Lupin.  He wasn't ready, but he
was going to do it anyway.

"All right."

Lupin smiled.

"Good."  He placed a hand on Neville's shoulder.  "Neville, you come
from a long line of powerful witches and wizards."

Neville looked up.

"Your family has produced people of extraordinary courage and ability."

Neville swallowed.  "I know."

"But you aren't required to become them."

Neville blinked.

"You have your own strengths.  I've seen them in class.  You pay
attention.  You listen.  You keep trying even when something frightens
you."

Neville looked down.  "That doesn't seem very impressive."

"It is to me."

Neville looked up again.

Lupin smiled.  "I know this is frightening.  But I also know you can do
this."

Neville took a breath.  "All right."

"Find your memory."  Neville closed his eyes.  He thought about a
morning in the countryside.

He'd been very young.  There was a meadow behind his grandmother's
house.  He remembered walking through it just after sunrise.  The grass
was wet.  The air smelled of flowers and the damp earth.  There were
little yellow flowers everywhere, and purple ones, and delicate white
blossoms whose names he'd later learned.

He remembered kneeling beside them, and examining their leaves.  He
remembered feeling the warmth of the rising sun on his face.  He'd
simply been Neville, and he'd been happy.

"Ready?" Lupin asked.

Neville opened his eyes.  "Yes." he lied.

The cabinet opened.  The Dementor emerged.  The cold returned.  Neville
felt the fear rise, but this time he held onto the meadow.

The flowers.\
The sunlight.\
The smell of wet grass.

He raised his wand.  " **Expecto Patronum** !"

Silver light burst from the tip.

A shimmering cloud of mist emerged all around him.  It spread across the
classroom, beautiful and luminous.  The Dementor stopped.  Then
retreated back into the safety of the cabinet.

Neville stared.  The silver mist swirled around him, then dissipated.

Lupin blinked.  "Oh."  He looked genuinely surprised.

Neville stood motionless.

"Did..."  He looked at Lupin.  "Did I do it?"  Lupin stared at the
cabinet, then at Neville, then he laughed heartily.

"Well!"

Neville looked worried.  "Was it wrong?"

"Technically?"  Lupin considered.  "Yes."

Neville's face fell.

"But!"  Lupin raised a finger. "It worked."

Neville blinked.  "It did?"

"It most certainly did."

Lupin looked delighted.  "That wasn't a fully formed Patronus.  Not even
close.  But it was powerful enough to repel a Dementor."

Hermione began applauding.  Harry joined her.  Neville went scarlet.

Lupin smiled broadly.  "Excellent work, Neville."

Neville looked down at his wand.

He'd done it.  Neville Longbottom felt rather proud of himself.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

#### <span class="c19"></span>

"Miss Granger?"

Hermione practically leapt from her chair.  "Yes, Professor!"

Harry grinned.

"Try not to explode anything."

Hermione glared.  "I won't."

Dracena muttered:  "Disappointing."

Hermione ignored her.

Lupin smiled.  "Have you chosen your memory?"

"Yes."

"Good.  Remember, the emotional strength of the memory matters more than
its complexity."

Hermione nodded, and She closed her eyes.  It wasn't a memory.  Not
exactly.  It was a vision.  She imagined a library, an enormous library
filled with books.  Shelves rising almost impossibly high, and warm
golden light spilling through tall windows.  A soft, well-worn leather
chair beside a fireplace.

The smell of paper.  Old bindings.  There were books everywhere.  Books
she'd never read.  Books she hadn't even heard of.  An entire lifetime
of things to learn.  Nobody telling her she was strange for wanting to
know them.

Hermione smiled.

Lupin opened the cabinet, and the Dementor emerged.

Hermione raised her wand.

" **Expecto Patronum** !"  Silver light erupted immediately.  It
gathered, twisting around Hermione.  Suddenly a small, shining otter
bounded across the classroom.

Harry laughed.  "Brilliant!"

The otter leapt toward the Dementor.  The creature recoiled.  The otter
circled it, almost playfully, driving it backward into the cabinet.

Lupin shut the doors and Hermione lowered her wand.

The otter vanished.

For a moment nobody spoke.  Lupin smiled.  "Extraordinary, Miss
Granger."

Hermione beamed.  Harry applauded.  Neville joined him enthusiastically.

Even Dracena looked impressed, although she quickly concealed it.

"An otter?" Harry asked.

Hermione smiled.  "My favorite animal."

"Hermione laughed.

Dracena rolled her eyes.  "How terribly predictable."

------------------------------------------------------------------------

"Mr.  Potter."

Harry stood.  "Right."

Lupin looked at him.  "Ready?"

Harry considered the cabinet.  "Probably not."

Lupin smiled.  "An excellent beginning."

Harry laughed nervously.

He'd been looking forward to this class.  He liked Professor Lupin, and
enjoyed learning practical magic.  He also desperately needed the extra
credit after his latest Potions marks.  Snape had written "DREADFUL"
across his last essay in letters large enough to be read from across the
classroom.  Harry had been particularly annoyed because he'd actually
tried.

Now, however, he was beginning to wonder whether there were easier ways
to improve his marks.

Lupin approached.  "Have you chosen a memory?"

Harry nodded.  He thought about Hagrid telling him he was a wizard,
about how wondrous at all felt as he discovered Hogwarts.  He thought
about his friends, about Ron and Hermione.

But then his thoughts drifted toward his parents.  He'd seen
photographs.  He'd heard stories.  He knew they had loved him.  He tried
to imagine them.

His mother smiling.\
His father laughing.\
A house where nobody called him a freak.\
Where nobody locked him in a cupboard under the stairs.

He thought about being where he belonged.

Harry swallowed.  "Ready."

The cabinet opened.  Cold swept across the room.  Harry raised his wand.

"Expecto Patronum!"

Nothing.

The Dementor approached.  Harry heard something.  A scream, then a
woman's voice.  His stomach dropped, and he fell to the floor.

Lupin slammed the cabinet shut.  Harry stumbled to his feet.

"Harry?"

Harry shook his head.  "I'm fine."

Lupin studied him.  "Are you sure?"

Harry nodded.

"Again."

The second attempt was worse.  The scream came sooner.  Harry nearly
dropped his wand.

Lupin stopped the exercise.  Harry stood breathing hard.  Hermione
looked worried.  Neville looked frightened for him.

Dracena was watching.  She wasn't smiling.

Lupin approached.

"Harry, you don't have to continue."

Harry shook his head.  "I want to."

"There's no shame in stopping."

"I know."

Lupin waited.

Harry looked at the cabinet, then at his wand.

He thought about his parents.    He tried to imagine something else.

He imagined his mother holding him.  He imagined his father lifting him
into the air.  Laughter.  A family.  Something he'd never really had the
chance to remember, but something he desperately wanted to believe had
existed.

Harry closed his eyes.  "All right."

Lupin hesitated, then nodded.

The cabinet opened.

The Dementor emerged.

The cold came.  Harry felt the scream beginning somewhere at the edge of
his mind.  His happy vision pushed it away, with the thought of his
mother smiling and his father laughing.

" **EXPECTO PATRONUM** !"

Silver light exploded from his wand.  It gathered in the air,and swirled
around the room.  It manifested slowly.  Four legs, then antlers.  A
magnificent stag stood in the silvery light.  The creature leapt
forward.

The Dementor recoiled.  The stag lowered its antlers and charged toward
it.  The cabinet doors slammed shut behind the fleeing creature.  The
stag stood in the classroom for another moment, shining brilliantly,
then faded.

Harry stared.  "Blimey."

Hermione was on her feet.  "Harry!"

Neville applauded.

Lupin looked almost overcome.  "Excellent, Harry."

Harry was still staring at the place where the stag had stood.

"That was..."  He swallowed.  "That was my dad, wasn't it?"

Lupin's expression softened.  "Your father was associated with a stag,
yes."

Harry looked down at his wand.  For a moment he couldn't speak.  Across
the room, Dracena watched him.  She'd seen Potter do extraordinary
things before.  She remembered him flying for the first time.  She
remembered him duelling.  Remembered how he'd charge into situations any
sensible person would avoid.

But this was different.  He'd failed.  Twice.  He'd looked genuinely
disturbed by the experience.

And he'd tried again, and he'd produced something magnificent.

Dracena felt a strange warmth in her chest.  She immediately suppressed
it.

"Show-off." she said, to herself.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

#### <span class="c19"></span>

"Miss Malfoy."  Dracena stood.

"Finally."

She'd spent the entire lesson watching three Gryffindors demonstrate
their abilities.

Longbottom had managed something.  Granger had produced a perfect
Patronus.  Potter had produced an enormous stag.  Dracena was not going
to be the only student who failed.

Absolutely not.

She approached Lupin.

He looked at her.  "Have you chosen a memory?"

"Naturally."

"Good."

Dracena lifted her chin.  "I've been practicing the incantation."

"I'm pleased to hear it."

She glanced toward Harry.  He smiled encouragingly.  Dracena immediately
looked away.

Idiot.  Stupid Potter.

Lupin raised his wand.

"Ready?"

"Of course."

Dracena closed her eyes.  She thought of Diagon Alley.  She'd been six.
 Father had taken her shopping, just the two of them.  He'd bought her a
beautiful little silver bracelet from a jeweller's shop, then a box of
chocolates.

Then he'd taken her to look at broomsticks.  She remembered holding his
hand.  Remembered how enormous he'd seemed.

How important.  How safe.  She'd asked for something terribly expensive.
 He'd laughed, then bought it anyway.  She remembered thinking that
Father could do anything; That nothing bad could happen while he was
there.

Dracena smiled.

"Ready."

The cabinet opened.  Cold swept across the classroom.  Dracena's smile
vanished.  The Dementor emerged, and she felt something horrible, a
sudden certainty that everything good would disappear.

That people would leave.  That she'd be alone.  That she would
disappoint everyone who mattered.

She raised her wand.

"Expecto...  uh...  Patronum!"

Nothing.  The Dementor approached, and Dracena felt her stomach twist.

Her father's smiling face vanished.  The memory collapsed.  She stumbled
backward.

Lupin shut the cabinet, and Dracena stood rigid.

Her face was pale.  Her hands trembled.  She hid them behind her back.

Lupin approached.  "Miss Malfoy?"

"I'm fine."

"That wasn't what I asked."

"I'm perfectly fine."

Lupin sighed.  "All right."

Dracena lifted her chin.  "I simply wasn't prepared."

"Of course."

"I'd like to try again."

Lupin studied her.  "First, tell me what happened."

Dracena stiffened.  "Nothing happened."

"Miss Malfoy."

She glared.  "My memory wasn't sufficiently strong."

"Perhaps."

"Then I'll choose another."

"Good."

Dracena looked away.

Lupin waited.

She said nothing.

He sighed.  He approached her, and crouched down next to her.  "Look,
Miss Malfoy, I don't know what your difficulty is."

Dracena's eyes flashed.  "I don't have a difficulty."

"Then stop treating every question as an accusation."

Harry looked down, trying to hear what they were saying.  Hermione
raised her eyebrows.  Neville stared at his shoes.

Dracena went red.

Lupin continued.  "I'm trying to help you."

"I don't need..."

"Yes, you do."

Lupin's voice softened.  "And that's perfectly all right."

Dracena stared.

He continued.  "You're capable of this.  I know you are."

She looked away.  "Clearly."

"Miss Malfoy."

She looked back.

"Listen."

Dracena sighed.

Lupin smiled faintly.  "If you can't hold onto a happy memory, try
something else."

"Such as?"

"Something that would make you happy."

Dracena frowned.

"That's what I was doing."

"Not necessarily something that happened."

She blinked.  "What?"

"Imagine something."

Dracena stared.  "Imagine?"

"Yes."

"Something that hasn't happened?"

"Precisely."

Dracena looked skeptical.  "That seems rather childish."

Lupin smiled.  "The best magic often is."

Harry snorted.  Dracena glared.

Lupin continued.  "Clear your mind.  Don't force a memory.  Let yourself
imagine something that would make you genuinely happy."

Dracena considered.  "Anything?"

"Anything."

She nodded.  "Fine."

Lupin stepped back.

Dracena closed her eyes.  She tried to imagine something pleasant.

The Manor.\
Christmas.\
Her mother.\
A beautiful dress.\
A new broom.

Nothing seemed right.

She cleared her mind again.

Then, quite without warning, she imagined  Harry Potter.

Dracena's eyes opened.  Oh, absolutely not.

She closed them again, and tried to think of something else.

Her father's study.\
A summer holiday.\
The gardens.

Harry Potter.  Stop that.

She tried harder.

She wasn't thinking about Harry arguing with her.  Or Harry laughing at
her.  Or Harry chasing her on a broom.

She was imagining something else.  A warm summer afternoon.  Hogsmeade.
 She was older.  Perhaps fifteen or sixteen.  Harry was walking beside
her.  He was holding her hand.

Dracena felt her cheeks warm.  "This is ridiculous." she thought to
herself, but she let the thought continue.

They were sitting together outside a newly built café at Hogsmede.
 Harry was smiling at her.  It was a smile that was only meant only for
her.  She was smiling too.

He leaned closer.  Dracena's heart jumped

He kissed her.  Just a little kiss.  Soft.  Warm.  And in the imagined
future, Dracena Malfoy did something she had never once done in real
life.  She stopped worrying about what anyone thought, and she kissed
him back.

She was happy.

Completely.\
Utterly.\
Ridiculously happy.

And then the Dementor came.  Dracena opened her eyes.

She raised her wand.  

**"EXPECTO PATRONUM** !"

<div class="illustration">

![The Lesson](Images/The%20Lesson%202.jpg)

</div>

The classroom exploded with silver light that overwhelmed everything.

A magnificent peacock burst from Dracena's wand, its feathers spreading
in a vast shimmering fan.

Silver light filled the room.  Professor Lupin brought his hand across
his eyes to shield himself from the bright silver light.

The creature cried out.  The sound was almost musical.

The Dementor recoiled.  The cabinet began shaking.

Lupin stared.  "What..."

The peacock spread its wings, and light poured from its feathers.

The containment charms flared, and failed.  The dementor screamed an
otherworldly, high pitched scream.

The dark shape of the dementor folded into itself, as it shrieked,
shrinking until it vanished into thin air.

The cabinet doors flew open.  Empty.

The classroom fell silent.

Silver feathers drifted through the air as the peacock vanished.

Dracena stood motionless.  Her wand was still raised.  Her hair had come
loose from its ribbon.  Her face was flushed, and she looked absolutely
astonished.

Harry stared.\
Hermione stared.\
Neville stared.

Lupin slowly lowered his wand.

For several seconds nobody spoke.

"Well..."  Lupin said.   He looked at the empty cabinet, then at
Dracena.

"Well, well."

Dracena swallowed.  "Professor?"

Lupin approached the cabinet.

He examined the broken, glowing symbols, then laughed in sheer
astonishment.

"Extraordinary."

Dracena blinked.  "Did I do it?"

Lupin turned.

"Miss Malfoy, you didn't merely do it."  He gestured toward the cabinet.

"You completely overwhelmed the containment.  And the Dementors
themselves.

Dracena looked alarmed.  "Did I kill them?"

"No.  Dementors don't die in quite the way ordinary creatures do.  The
manifestation has been forced back into the incorporeal plane from which
it was drawn."

He looked at her.  "I've never seen a Patronus do that."

Dracena stared.  "Never?"

"Never." he said, dumbfounded.

Harry grinned.  "Blimey, Malfoy."

Dracena turned.  Harry was looking at her with genuine admiration.

"That was brilliant."  Something inside Dracena's chest did a peculiar
little flip.

She immediately lifted her chin.  "Naturally."

Hermione laughed.  Neville applauded.

Lupin shook his head.  "Miss Malfoy, I don't believe I could have
produced a result quite like that myself."

Dracena's eyes widened, then she smiled, a tremendous smile.

"Well." She smoothed her robes.  "I suppose some of us are simply more
gifted."

Harry rolled his eyes.  "There she is."

Dracena smiled sweetly.  "Jealous, Potter?"

Harry laughed.  "A little, actually."

Dracena blinked.  She hadn't expected him to admit it.  "Good."

------------------------------------------------------------------------

The four students left Lupin's classroom together.

Neville was still looking at his wand.  "I can't believe I did it."

Hermione smiled.  "You were wonderful."

Neville blushed.  "It wasn't a proper Patronus."

"It repelled the Dementor." Harry said.

Neville looked up.

Harry grinned.  "That's what matters, isn't it?"

Neville smiled.

"Yeah."

Dracena walked slightly ahead of them.  Her head was held high.  She was
enormously pleased with herself.  She'd produced the strongest Patronus
in the class, possibly the strongest Patronus Lupin had ever seen.

Father would be delighted.  She could already imagine telling him.  She
might even get one of the new "Cumulonimbus 2020's" out of it

Then she remembered what she'd been thinking about.  Her smile faltered.
 Oh, bother.

Hermione turned toward Neville.  "What was your memory?"

Neville smiled shyly.  "A meadow."

Harry looked at him.  "A meadow?"

Neville nodded.  "Near my grandmother's house.  I used to go there in
the mornings.  There were flowers everywhere."

Hermione smiled.  "That's lovely."

Neville looked embarrassed.  "I suppose."

Harry shook his head.  "Don't be embarrassed.  It worked!"

Neville smiled, then he looked at Hermione.

"What about yours?"

Hermione brightened.  "A library."

Harry laughed.  "Of course."

Hermione glared.  "There's nothing wrong with liking books."

"Didn't say there was."

"It was beautiful.  Huge windows, comfortable chairs, shelves
everywhere...  Ron would have fallen asleep just hearing about it."

Harry grinned.  

Neville looked at Harry.

"What about you?"

Harry hesitated.  "My parents."

Hermione's expression softened.

Harry looked down.  "I imagined what it would've been like if they were
still alive."

Nobody spoke for a moment.

"That's a good one."  Neville said, quietly.

Harry smiled.  "Yeah."

Dracena was listening.  She felt a strange ache in her chest.

Potter had imagined his parents.  She'd imagined kissing Potter.
 Something about that seemed terribly unfair.

Hermione turned toward her.

"What about you, Dracena?"

Dracena froze.  "What?"

"Your Patronus.  What did you think about?"

"Oh."

Dracena's mind went completely blank.

Harry looked at her.  Neville looked at her.  Hermione waited.

Dracena swallowed.

She lifted her chin.

"I was thinking about returning home."

Harry blinked.  "Home?"

"Yes."

Dracena waved a hand.  "To the Manor."

Hermione frowned slightly.

Dracena continued.  "Where I have servants to attend to my needs, and I
don't have to concern myself with this ridiculous Dementor nonsense."

Harry stared.

Dracena smiled triumphantly.

An entirely reasonable explanation.

Then Harry said:  "You're lying."

Dracena's stomach dropped.  "What?"

Harry smiled.  "You're lying."

"I am not!"

"Yes, you are."

Dracena went red.  "How dare you!"

Harry shrugged.  "Why?"

"Why what?"

"Why are you lying?"

"I'm not!"

Hermione was watching with considerable interest.

Neville looked confused.

Harry grinned.  "Come on, Malfoy."

Dracena glared.  "Potter."

"Was it something embarrassing?"

"No!"

Harry's grin widened.  "Oh, it was."

"It was not!"

"Something silly?"

"No!"

"Something about your father?"

Dracena hesitated.

"No?"

"Potter!"  Dracena said, angrily

"Something about Quidditch?"

"No!"

"Something about winning the House Cup?"

"Absolutely not!"

Hermione chimed in, smiling widely. "You were thinking about Harry,
weren't you?"

Both Dracena and Harry stopped walking at the same moment

Her face went scarlet.  His face didn't do much better.

Hermione's eyebrows rose, and she grinned even more widely.

Neville looked between them.

Dracena stared at Harry, then Hermione.

For one terrible moment, she couldn't think.

Hermoine stared back, then her smile widened.

"Oh."

Dracena's heart stopped.

"Oh, what?"

Hermione grinned.

"It **was**  about Harry!"

Dracena nearly screamed.  "IT WAS NOT!"

Hermione covered her mouth, giggling.

Neville looked alarmed.

Harry was gobsmacked.

"What was he doing?"

Dracena stared.  "WHAT?"

"In your memory."

"It wasn't a memory!"

Hermione blinked.

Dracena realized her mistake.

Too late.

Hermione smiled and grinned evilly.

"Oh?"

Dracena wanted the ground to swallow her.

Hermione continued.  "So it was something you imagined."

Dracena closed her eyes.

Idiot.\
Idiot.\
Idiot.

Hermione was now smiling openly.

Harry looked delighted.

"What was he doing, Malfoy?"

Dracena lifted her chin.  "Being an idiot."

Harry laughed.  "That's not very happy."

"It is for me."

"Really?"

"Immensely."

Harry considered.  "Was I falling off my broom?"

"No."

"Getting detention?"

"No."

"Being eaten by a dragon?"

Dracena almost smiled.

"Yes." she lied.

Harry grinned.  "Come on."

"No."

"Tell me."

"No!"

"Please?"

Dracena looked at him.  That was unfair.  Potter wasn't supposed to say
please.  Especially not with that smile.  And those eyes.  She looked
away.

"I was imagining you being quiet."

Harry laughed.  "That's impossible."

"Precisely.  That's why it made me so happy."

Neville chuckled.

Hermione shook her head, smiling at the ground

"Well, whatever it was, it must've been something."

Dracena looked at him.  "What?"

"Your Patronus."  He smiled.  "That was the most powerful thing I've
ever seen."

Dracena froze.

Harry continued.  "Really.  It was amazing."

For a moment, she forgot to be embarrassed.   Harry was looking at her
with genuine admiration.

Dracena felt her face warm again.

"Oh."

Harry smiled.  "Well done, Malfoy."

She swallowed.  "Thank you, Potter."

They walked in silence for several steps.

"Still think you were imagining me falling off my broom."

Dracena rounded on him.  "POTTER!"

Harry burst out laughing.  Hermione joined him.  Even Neville smiled.

Dracena glared at all three of them.

Then, despite herself, she laughed too.

That evening, Dracena lay on her bed in the Slytherin dormitory.  She
stared at the canopy.

She'd done it.  She'd produced an extraordinary Patronus, A magnificent
peacock.

Professor Lupin had been astonished.  Potter had been impressed.  Father
would be proud.  Everything should have been wonderful.

Except...

Dracena closed her eyes.  She could still see it.

The imagined afternoon.\
The café.\
Harry's smile.\
His hand in hers.

The kiss.

She pulled her pillow over her face.  "Oh, for Merlin's sake."

She'd been thinking about Harry Potter.  Not merely thinking about him.
 Imagining going on a date with him.  Imagining kissing him, and somehow
that had produced the most powerful magic she'd ever performed.

Dracena groaned into her pillow.

This was a disaster.  A complete disaster.

Granger.

Hermione had been smiling.  That horrible, knowing smile.  Dracena
stared at the curtains around her bed.

"Oh, no."

She lay back down, and pulled the blanket over her head and made a
solemn decision.

She would never, under any circumstances, tell another living soul what
she'd been thinking about.

Especially Harry Potter.

Most especially Harry Potter.

She could not possibly tolerate him discovering that the happiest thing
she could imagine...

The thing that had filled her with so much joy that she'd driven an
entire Dementor out of existence...

Was Harry Potter kissing her.

Dracena buried her face in the pillow again.  "Stupid Potter."

And somewhere in Gryffindor Tower, Harry was telling Ron Weasley about
the extraordinary silver peacock Dracena had conjured.

He had absolutely no idea that he'd been responsible for it.

Which, Dracena decided, was the only good thing about the entire
afternoon.
