# <span class="c9"></span>

# <span class="c9">Year 4</span>

## <span class="c12">The Bureaucracy</span>

Neville Longbottom was angry.  This was unusual enough that Harry Potter
stopped walking.

Neville was sitting at one of the library's larger tables, surrounded by
parchment, books, and several official-looking documents bearing the
seal of the Ministry of Magic.  He was writing furiously.  Every few
seconds, he stopped, scratched something out, consulted another
document, and muttered under his breath.

Harry approached.  "All right, Neville?"

Neville didn't look up.  "Hello, Harry."

Harry glanced at the parchment. "That doesn't look like Herbology
homework."

Neville laughed.  It wasn't a particularly happy laugh.  "No."

Harry pulled out a chair.  "What is it?"

Neville put down his quill.  "Paperwork."

Harry looked at the enormous pile.  "Blimey.  What for?"

Neville hesitated, then sighed.  "St.  Mungo's."

Harry's expression changed.  "Your parents?"

Neville nodded.  "Gran's trying to get their treatment authorization
renewed."

Harry frowned.  "I thought they were already being treated."

"They are."  Neville rubbed his forehead.  "But the National Wizards
Health Service changed the forms again.  Gran sent in the old ones, and
they rejected them."

Harry stared.  "They rejected them?"

"Yes."

"Why?"

"Because they're the wrong forms."

Harry blinked.  "But they were the right forms when she sent them."

Neville nodded.  "Yes."

Harry considered this.  "That's stupid."

Neville laughed bitterly.  "Tell me about it."

He picked up another sheet.  "Now we have to submit a new application
for continued long-term restorative care.  They've suspended
authorization for some of the specialist treatments until it's
approved."

Harry stared.  "But your parents need those treatments."

"I know."  Neville's voice tightened.  "Gran's been trying to sort it
out for weeks."

Harry looked at the paperwork.  There were dozens of pages.  Some had
little boxes.  Some had enormous boxes.  Some seemed to require
information that was already written on other  forms.

One had been returned with a large red stamp:   **INSUFFICIENT
SUPPORTING DOCUMENTATION.**

Harry frowned.  "What documentation?"

Neville pointed.  "That one."

Harry looked.  "What's wrong with it?"

"Nothing."

"Nothing?"

Neville shook his head.  "They wanted the original."

Harry stared.  "Isn't that the original?"

"No.  That's the certified copy."

Harry blinked.  "What's the difference?"

Neville looked at him.  "Apparently, about six weeks and five galleons."

Harry sat down.  "Oh."

At the next table, Hermione looked up from her Arithmancy notes.
 "Neville?"

He turned.  "Yes?"

"Surely your caseworker can help?"

Neville stared.

Hermione continued.  "St.  Mungo's must have someone assigned to your
parents.  And your father was an Auror, wasn't he?  Surely the Ministry
has some sort of service for injured Aurors."

Neville put down his quill, very carefully.  "You'd think so."

Hermione frowned.  "What?"

"You'd think so, wouldn't you?"  His voice had become unusually sharp.

Harry looked at him.  Neville was almost never angry.  Not like this.
 Neville took a slow, but sharp breath.  "The Ministry cut the Auror
Rehabilitation Office's budget last year."

Hermione blinked.  "They did?"

Neville nodded.  "Gran says they reassigned half the staff."  He looked
down. "Our caseworker has nearly five hundred former Aurors and their
families to look after."

Harry stared.  "Five hundred?"

Neville nodded.  "Five hundred."

Hermione looked horrified.  "That's impossible."

Neville laughed.  "Apparently not."

He picked up a letter.  "His last owl took two months to answer."

Hermione frowned.  "Two months?"

"...and six days.  Yes."  Neville's voice began to shake.  "And when he
finally answered, he said he'd be happy to help, but we'd have to submit
the new forms first."

Harry stared.  "But that's what you need help with."

Neville looked at him.  "Yes!" His voice rose, exasperated.

Madam Pince glanced toward their table.

Neville lowered his voice.  "Yes, Harry.  That's exactly what we need
help with."

He looked down at the parchment.  "Gran's getting older.  She's not very
good with all this new paperwork.  And I thought..."  He swallowed.  "I
thought I could help."

Hermione's expression softened.

"Neville..."

"But I don't understand half of it!"  His voice cracked.  He gestured
toward the forms.  "Look at this!"

Harry leaned forward.  Neville pointed at a paragraph.

"'Provide documentation establishing continuing eligibility under
subsection seventeen of the Magical Health and Welfare Appropriations
Act.'"

He looked at Harry.  "What does that mean?"

Harry stared.  "I haven't a clue."

"Neither do I!"  Neville looked miserable.  "And if we get it wrong
again, they'll send it back again."

Hermione reached for the form.  "May I?"

Neville nodded.

She began reading.  Harry watched her expression change, from
concentration, to confusion, to irritation, then finally to outright
disbelief.

"This is dreadful."

Neville laughed.  "Yes."

Hermione looked up.  "Why are there three different places to list the
same medical history?"

Neville shrugged.  "I don't know."

"And why does this require an attending Healer's signature when the next
page requires a departmental certification of the same information?"

Neville looked at her.  "I don't know, Hermione."  he said, exasperated.

She frowned.  "And this section..."

"I DON'T KNOW!"  The library went silent.

Neville froze.  Hermione stared.  Harry looked at him.

Neville's face turned scarlet.  "I'm sorry."

Hermione shook her head.

"No, Neville, I'm sorry.  I wasn't..."

Neville looked down.  His hands were trembling.  "I'm just..."

He swallowed.  "I'm so tired of this.  I'm overwhelmed."

------------------------------------------------------------------------

<span class="c4 c14"></span>

<span class="c14">At the table behind them, Dracena Malfoy had been
listening.  She hadn't intended to.  She was supposed to be working on a
Transfiguration essay.  She had an open textbook, three sheets of
parchment, and a half-finished explanation of the limitations of
human-to-animal transformations.  Neville Longbottom had been getting
progressively louder.  The conversation had become rather difficult to
ignore.</span>

Dracena looked over her shoulder.  "Longbottom?"

Neville stiffened.  Harry turned.

"Malfoy."

Dracena ignored him.  "Why doesn't your grandmother simply speak to
someone at the Ministry?"

Neville stared.

Dracena continued.  "Surely she knows someone who can straighten this
out."

Neville looked at her.  For several seconds, he said nothing, then he
laughed.  It was a horrible laugh.

Dracena frowned.  "What?"

Neville shook his head.  "Of course."

Dracena blinked.  "Of course what?"

Neville looked at her.  "Of course you'd say that."

Dracena's expression hardened.  "What's that supposed to mean?"

Neville stood.  

Harry blinked.  "Neville?"

Neville ignored him.  He was looking directly at Dracena.  "Well, that's
easy for you, isn't it?"

Dracena frowned.  "Excuse me?"

"'Just talk to someone at the Ministry!'"  Neville laughed again.  "Why
didn't we think of that?"

Dracena stared.

Neville continued.

"'Just send an owl to someone important!  Have Father arrange a meeting!
 Perhaps invite one of the Ministers to tea!'"

Dracena's cheeks warmed.  "Longbottom..."

"That's how it works for you, isn't it?"

She straightened.  "I beg your pardon?"

Neville's voice shook.  "You can just ask someone to fix things!"

Dracena stared.  "Well, if there's been an administrative mistake..."

"THERE HASN'T BEEN A MISTAKE!"

Madam Pince looked up sharply.

Neville lowered his voice, but only slightly.  "That's the problem!"

He pointed at the paperwork.  "This is how it's supposed to work!"

Dracena said nothing.

Neville continued.  "Gran and I have to fill out all these forms.  We
have to wait for someone to read them.  We have to hope we haven't made
a mistake.  And if we have, we start all over again!"

He swallowed.  "But you wouldn't know anything about that, would you?"

Dracena's face tightened.  "Longbottom, I was only..."

"Of course not!"  Neville looked at her.  "You're Dracena Malfoy!"

Harry stood.  "Neville..."

"No, Harry."  Neville's eyes were wet.  "She should hear this."

Dracena went very still.

Neville continued.  "You might be able to walk into the Ministry and
have someone fix everything for you, Your Highness."

Dracena flinched.  "But the rest of us..."  His voice broke.  "The rest
of us ordinary people have to do things properly."

Dracena stared.

Neville laughed bitterly.  "There's no shortcut for us."

Dracena looked down at the paperwork.  She'd never really thought about
it.  Of course Father knew people at the Ministry.  He knew everyone.
 If there were ever a problem with something official, he sent an owl.
 Sometimes he visited someone.  Occasionally someone came to the Manor,
and then the problem went away, or got fixed.  That was simply how
things worked.

Wasn't it?

Harry spoke quietly.  "She shouldn't have to."  Harry was looking at the
forms.  "Neville's grandmother shouldn't have to know someone
important."

He looked at Hermione.  "Doesn't everyone pay into the Magical Health
Service?"

Hermione nodded.  "Yes.  It's funded through Ministry revenues and the
contributions taken from working witches and wizards."

Harry turned back to Neville.  "Then it should work."

Neville stared.

Harry continued.  "Your parents were Aurors.  They were hurt doing their
jobs.  They need care."  He gestured toward the forms.  "Why should you
have to spend your afternoon doing this?"

Neville looked down.

Harry's voice grew firmer.  "You should be doing your homework."

He looked toward Dracena.  "Or playing Quidditch.  Or doing whatever you
want."

Dracena said nothing.

Harry continued.  "Nobody should have to spend weeks begging the
Ministry for treatment they're already supposed to receive."

Hermione nodded.  "He's right."

Neville swallowed.

Dracena looked at him.  For the first time, she noticed something she'd
never really considered before.  Neville Longbottom wasn't simply a
clumsy boy who was bad at Potions.  He was a boy whose parents were in
hospital.\
Whose grandmother was raising him.\
Who was spending his afternoon fighting a bureaucracy because nobody
else had time to help.

She had just suggested he ask someone important to make the problem
disappear as though it were obvious.\
As though everyone could do that.

Dracena felt suddenly ashamed.  "Neville?"

He looked at her.

She hesitated.  "Perhaps I could..."

Neville's expression changed.  "Do what?"

Dracena blinked.  "I was only going to..."

"Do what, Princess?"

Harry frowned.  "Neville."

Neville ignored him. "Take pity on us?"

Dracena recoiled.  "No!"

Neville laughed.  "Why not?"

She stared.

Neville stepped closer.  "Why would you want to help my family?"

Dracena opened her mouth.  Nothing came out.

Neville's voice shook.  "My parents fought against yours."

Dracena went pale.  "Longbottom..."

"They fought Voldemort!"

Several students gasped.  Neville didn't care.

"And your family supported him!"

Dracena stared.

Neville continued.  "My parents were tortured until they couldn't even
recognize their own son!"  His voice broke.  "And now I have to sit here
filling out forms so they can keep getting treatment!"

Dracena looked horrified.

Neville's eyes filled.  "So what are you going to do?"  He laughed
bitterly.  "Ask your father to put in a good word?"

Dracena swallowed.  "That's not..."

"Or perhaps he'll make a donation!"

Her face went white.

Neville continued.  "Maybe we can have a nice little plaque at St.
 Mungo's thanking the Malfoys for their generosity!"

"Neville!"

Harry's voice cut through the room.

Neville stopped.

Harry was standing beside him.  He looked serious.  "That's enough."

Neville stared.

Harry continued.  "She's trying to help."

Neville looked at Dracena.  She was standing motionless.  Her eyes were
bright.  She looked like a girl who'd just been slapped.

Neville swallowed,then looked away.

"I'm sorry."

Dracena said nothing.

Neville looked at Harry.  "I'm sorry."

He sat down, very slowly and covered his face.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

"Neville?"

He looked up.

Hermione smiled gently.  "Let me help."

Neville shook his head.  "Hermione, you've got your own work."

She waved a hand.  "I've nearly finished."

Harry looked at her enormous stack of books.  He doubted that very much.

Hermione pulled the forms closer.  "Now.  Let's see what we can do."

Neville smiled weakly.  "Thanks."

Harry sat down.

"Anything I can do?"

Hermione looked at him.  "Actually, yes."

Harry blinked.  "What?"

She handed him a stack of documents.  "Put these in chronological
order."

Harry stared.  "That's it?"

Hermione smiled.  "For now."

Harry grinned.  "Brilliant."

The three of them got to work.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

The next morning, Neville got an anonymous Owl.\
\
        Longbottom,

Speak to Edmund Wetherby in Slytherin.

His father works in the Department of Magical Health, and his mother is
a senior nurse at St.  Mungo's.  He knows how to get the correct forms
and which offices to send them to.

He's expecting you at lunch tomorrow.

Don't be late.

–Anonymous.

Neville Longbottom was staring at the letter.

Harry sat down beside him. "Morning."

Neville didn't answer.

Harry glanced at the parchment.  "What's that?"

Neville handed it to him.  Harry read.  Then read it again.

He smiled.  "Oh."

Neville scowled.  "Don't."

Harry blinked.  "Don't what?"

"Don't smile like that."

Harry tried to stop smiling.  He wasn't particularly successful.

Hermione leaned over.  "What is it?"

Harry handed her the letter.  She read it.  Her eyebrows rose.

"Oh!"

Neville groaned.  "Not you too."

Hermione looked toward the Slytherin table.  Dracena Malfoy was eating
breakfast.  She was chatting with Pansy Parkinson, looking perfectly
unconcerned with the affairs of Neville Longbottom.

Hermione smiled.  "Well, that's rather nice of her."

Neville looked horrified.  "Nice?"

Hermione blinked.  "Yes."

Neville stared.  "She called me a pathetic excuse for a wizard last
week!"

Harry shrugged.  "Well, you did call her Princess."

Neville glared.  "She deserved it!"

Harry considered this.  "Probably."

Neville blinked.

"But she was trying to help."

Neville looked down at the letter.  "That's what makes it worse."

Hermione frowned.  "Why?"

Neville stared at her.  "Because I need it!"  He looked toward Dracena.
 "Because she's right!"

Harry stopped smiling.

"I've spent three weeks trying to get someone at the Ministry to answer
my questions.  Three weeks!"  He gestured at the letter.  "And she
just..."  He shook it.  "She just sends a note to someone!"

Hermione looked at him.

Neville's voice tightened.  "One conversation.  That's all it took."

Harry said nothing.

Neville looked down.  "She probably didn't even have to ask twice."

Hermione hesitated.  "Neville..."

"She probably just said, 'Do this for me,' and he did!"

Harry considered that.  "Actually, that does sound like her."

Neville glared.

Harry raised his hands.  "Sorry."

Neville looked toward the Slytherin table again.  Dracena laughed at
something Pansy had said.  She didn't look toward them.

Neville's expression softened slightly.  Then hardened again.  "I don't
want to owe her anything."

Harry nodded.  "I know."

Neville looked at him.

"But you don't have to like her."

Neville frowned.  "What?"

"You don't have to like her to accept her help."

Neville looked down at the letter.

Harry shrugged.  "She doesn't like you much either."

Hermione shot him a look.  "Harry!"

"What?"

Neville almost smiled.

"And anyway, you're not doing it for her."

Neville looked at him.

"You're doing it for your parents."

Neville went quiet.  Harry watched him.  After a moment, Neville folded
the letter very carefully, then slipped it into his pocket.  "I
suppose."

Hermione smiled.  "Good."

Neville looked toward Dracena again.

"Stupid Malfoy." he muttered.

Harry grinned.  "Funny.  She says that about me all the time."

------------------------------------------------------------------------

"Longbottom?"  Neville looked up.

A tall Slytherin boy stood beside him.  "Edmund Wetherby."

Neville nodded.  "Hello."

Edmund sat down.  "Malfoy says you need help with some forms."

Neville stiffened.  "She told you?"

Edmund shrugged.  "More or less."

Neville frowned.  "What exactly did she say?"

Edmund sighed.  "'Wetherby, you remember those Transfiguration notes I
let you copy last term?'"

Neville blinked.

"'Well, now you can return the favor.'"

Neville stared.

Edmund shook his head.  "Honestly, I should've known better than to
borrow anything from that girl."

Neville couldn't help it.  He laughed.

Edmund took out a folder.  "Ok, so let's see what you have here."

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Later that week, Harry caught Dracena at lunch.  "Malfoy."

She looked up.  "Potter."

Harry sat down next to her.  "Neville got his paperwork sorted."

Dracena continued writing.  "How fascinating."

Harry smiled.  "He wanted me to thank you."

Dracena's quill stopped.  She looked up.  "Did he?"

Harry shrugged.  "Well, not exactly."

Dracena narrowed her eyes.

Harry grinned.  "He said you're an insufferable, arrogant, meddling
little princess."

Dracena's face hardened.  "How charming."

"But he also said his grandmother cried when she got the approval
letter."

Dracena went very still.

Harry watched her.

"Well."  She dipped her quill into the ink.  "Good."

Harry smiled.  "Yeah."

Dracena resumed writing.  "Now go away, Potter.  You're distracting me."

Harry stood.

"Thanks, Dracena."

She didn't look up.  "Stupid Potter."

------------------------------------------------------------------------

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# <span class="c9"></span>

# <span class="c9">Year 5</span>

## <span class="c12">The Hipogriff</span>

<div class="illustration">

![The Hipogriff](Images/The%20Hipogriff.jpg)

</div>

Harry Potter had been looking forward to Care of Magical Creatures all
week.  Mostly because Hagrid was teaching it.

Care of Magical Creatures also involved going outside, which was much
better than sitting in a classroom listening to Professor Binns explain
something that had happened four hundred years ago.  Harry was going to
enjoy the class, despite the fact that Dracena Malfoy, and many of her
Slytherin classmates shared the class with him.

"Potter."

Harry turned.

"Malfoy."

Dracena stood with Pansy Parkinson and two other Slytherin girls.  Her
pale hair was tied back with a dark green ribbon, and she was looking at
Harry with the expression she reserved for things she found beneath her
dignity.  

Which was most things.

"Looking forward to spending the afternoon with your enormous friend?"
She said, icily.

Harry smiled. "Yes, actually."

"How charming.  I do hope he remembers to wash his hands before touching
anything."

"Don't worry.  I'll remind him to keep them away from you."

Dracena's eyes narrowed.  "Meaning?"

"Wouldn't want him catching anything."

Ron snorted.

Dracena glared at Harry.  "You're revolting."

"Morning to you too."

She swept past.  Harry watched her go.

Ron shook his head.  "Honestly, why do you bother?"

Harry shrugged.  "She started it."

"She always starts it."

"Well, somebody has to finish it."

Hermione, walking beside them, sighed. "You're both ridiculous."

There was something about Hermione's tone he didn't quite follow.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Hagrid was waiting beside a fenced enclosure at the edge of the grounds.
 Behind him stood several enormous creatures.

Harry stopped.  "Blimey."

Ron stared. "What are those?"

Hermione was already reaching for her textbook.  "Uh, according to my
copy of 'Fantastic Beasts and Where to Find Them' that's a Hippogriff."

They were magnificent.  Each had the body and hindquarters of a horse,
but the front legs, wings and head of a great bird of prey.  Their
feathers shone in the afternoon sunlight, and their enormous claws dug
into the earth as they moved.

One was particularly striking.  Sparkling feathers that reminded Harry
of burnished steel.  A pale head with bright orange eyes.  Harry
couldn't stop looking at him.

"Beautiful, aren't they?" Hagrid said proudly.

Harry nodded.

Dracena, standing several feet away, said nothing.

Harry glanced at her.  She was staring at the creatures.

"What's wrong, Malfoy?"

"Nothing."  She gave him a poisonous look.

Harry grinned back at her.  Dracena rolled her eyes.

Hagrid clapped his enormous hands.  "Right!  Gather round!"

Most of the students moved closer.  Dracena did not.

"Now, these here are hippogriffs.  Very proud creatures.  Very
intelligent.  An' very particular about manners."

Dracena perked up slightly at that.

Hagrid continued.  "Yeh mustn't insult 'em.  Never approach one with
anger or malice in yer heart.  They can sense it, see?  An' they won't
tolerate disrespect."

Dracena frowned.  "How does one insult a hippogriff?"

Hagrid looked at her.  "Same way yeh insult anyone else, Miss Malfoy."

Ron coughed.  Harry looked at the ground.  Dracena glared at both of
them.

Hagrid continued. "Yeh need ter approach slowly.  Look 'em in the eye.
 Bow.  Then wait fer them ter bow back."

"And if they don't?" Hermione asked.

"Yeh back away.  Slowly.  Don't turn yer back.  Now, who wants ter try?"

Nobody moved.  Hagrid looked around.

"Harry?"

Harry swallowed.  "Me?"

"Go on.  Yeh'll be fine."

Harry looked at the enormous claws and blinked.  Hagrid was smiling at
him.  Harry stepped forward, slowly.

"This one's name is Buckbeak." Hagrid said.

Dracena watched, wide eyed.

Harry approached the steel grey hippogriff.  He bowed, not taking his
eyes off the creature.

It stared at him

Harry waited, then, slowly, the hippogriff lowered its head.

Hagrid beamed.  "Excellent, Harry!"

The class applauded.  Harry exhaled.  He hadn't realized he'd been
holding his breath.  Hagrid encouraged him to approach and stroke the
creature's neck.

The feathers were surprisingly soft.  He smiled at the creature.

"Hello, Buckbeak."

Behind him, Dracena continued staring.  "I was positive Potter was about
to get eaten." she said to a Slytherin girl next to her.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

The other students began taking turns.  Hermione managed beautifully, of
course.  Ron took a little longer, but eventually Buckbeak bowed to him
too.  Even Neville, after considerable encouragement, managed a nervous
bow and received one in return.

Dracena watched, cautiously.  She wasn't afraid of animals.  There were
horses at the Manor.  She'd had equestrian lessons for as long as she
could remember.  Equestrian lessons didn't prepare her for "horses" with
huge beaks.  Or horses with claws.  Or wings.

Most horses generally didn't stare at you as though deciding which part
to eat first.

"Miss Malfoy?"  Dracena jumped.

Hagrid smiled, oblivious.  "Yer turn."

She looked at Buckbeak.  "No, thank you."

Pansy stared.  "Dracena!?"

Dracena immediately regretted speaking.  "I mean, I don't see the
point."

Hagrid frowned.  "Point's ter learn how ter handle magical creatures."

"Yes, well, I already understand the instructions."

"Then give it a try."

Dracena glanced toward Harry.  He was watching.  Of **course**  he was
watching.  He had that stupid little smile on his face.

Her face turned hot

"Come on, Malfoy." Harry called. "It's easy."

That did it.  Dracena straightened.  "Of course it's easy, Potter." She
stepped forward.  Buckbeak raised his head, quickly, sharply.  Dracena
stopped.  He was enormous.  He looked far larger than any horse she'd
ever seen in her life.  She could see the sharp edges of his beak.  She
glanced quickly at his claws, and looked away quickly.

Her stomach tightened.   She took another step.

Buckbeak watched her.

"Bow, Miss Malfoy!" Hagrid instructed, excitedly.

Dracena did.  Barely.  Just a tiny inclination of her head.

Buckbeak didn't move.  She frowned.

"Well?"

"Wait." Hagrid said.

Buckbeak continued staring.  His head raised higher.

Dracena could hear people behind her.  She felt her face getting hotter.

"Honestly." she muttered.  Buckbeak shifted, his clawed foot pawing at
the ground.  Dracena jumped.  Someone laughed.  She turned redder.

"Oh, for Merlin's sake, you oversized chicken."

Hagrid's expression changed.  "Miss Malfoy..."  Hagrid said, cautiously.

Dracena took another step toward Buckbeak.  "Come on, you stupid
chicken.  I've bowed, haven't I?"  Buckbeak's wings spread.  Dracena
froze.  "Back away." Hagrid said sharply.

Dracena's eyes opened wide.  She couldn't move..  The enormous wings
filled her vision.  The creature shrieked.  Dracena screamed in
response.

Everything seemed to happen at once.

The beast's steel gray feathers blurred as it moved.  Dracena was aware
of claws flashing past her vision, then pain.  As something struck her
arm, she fell backward.

Hagrid shouted at Buckbeak, who moved back slightly.

Students screamed.

Dracena Malfoy, who had spent thirteen years learning how to look
composed under any circumstances, curled into a ball on the grass and
sobbed.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

The injury to her arm wasn't particularly serious.  That was what Madam
Pomfrey said.  Three deep scratches along Dracena's forearm, some
bruising, and a great deal of shock.  There would be no permanent
damage.

"I could have been killed!" Dracena shrieked.

Madam Pomfrey applied another dressing.  "Well.  You weren't."

"It attacked me!"

"Yes, dear."

"That creature is dangerous!"

"Most magical creatures are dangerous if mishandled.  That's part of why
we have these classes."

Dracena stared at her.  "Are you suggesting this was my fault?"

Madam Pomfrey looked at her over her spectacles.  "I'm suggesting you
rest your arm."

Dracena lay back against her pillows.  Her face was pale.  Her eyes were
red.  Worst of all, Harry Potter had seen everything.

She could still hear the laughter.  Not that anyone had really laughed
after Buckbeak attacked, but before.  They'd laughed before.

And Potter had been watching.  Dracena swallowed.  "Wait until my father
hears about this." she said.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Her father heard about it.  Dracena sent him a lengthy owl about the
incident.  Dracena's owl described an enormous, vicious creature that
had attacked her without warning during a dangerously mismanaged lesson
conducted by an incompetent groundskeeper.

Lucius Malfoy was furious.  Within days, owls arrived at Hogwarts.

Ministry officials quickly followed.

Hagrid was temporarily removed from teaching while the incident was
investigated.  Dracena received an owl from her father assuring her that
the matter would be handled appropriately.

She read it three times.  It made her feel considerably better.  That
was, until Harry Potter found her in the corridor.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

"Malfoy."

Dracena turned.  "Potter."

Harry looked at her bandaged arm.  "How's the terrible injury?"

Dracena lifted her chin.

"Painful."

"Really?"

"Excruciating."

"That's funny."

"What is?"

"Madam Pomfrey said you'd be fine in a few days."

Dracena glared.  "Madam Pomfrey is not the one who was nearly torn apart
by a vicious monster."

Harry folded his arms.  "Funny.  I thought Buckbeak was rather nice."

"Of course you did."

"He liked Hermione too."

"How wonderful for Granger."

"And Ron."

"Congratulations to Weasley."

"And Neville."

Dracena's eyes narrowed.  Harry smiled in response.

"Even Neville.  Your point, Potter?" Dracena said, icily.

Harry's smile faded.  "My point is that Buckbeak didn't attack anyone
else."

Dracena went still.  "Excuse me?"

" *Everyone else* followed Hagrid's instructions."

"I followed his instructions!"

"You called Buckbeak stupid."

"He was being stupid!"

"You called him an oversized chicken."

"Well, he looks like one!"

Harry stared at her.

Her face went red.

Harry sighed.  "Dracena."

She froze.

"Hagrid told you not to insult him."

"I know what Hagrid said!"

"Then why did you do it?"

Dracena opened her mouth.  Nothing came out.

Harry waited.  She looked away.

"I don't have to explain myself to you."

"No.  I guess you don't."  Harry continued.  "But Hagrid might lose his
job because of this."

Dracena's expression hardened.  "Then perhaps he shouldn't have brought
dangerous animals into a classroom."

"He's teaching Care of Magical Creatures!"

"That doesn't mean he should allow students to be attacked!"

"He didn't!"

Dracena recoiled slightly.

Harry took a breath.  "He told you exactly what to do.  You didn't
listen."

Dracena's eyes filled with angry tears.

She hated that.  She hated crying, especially in front of Potter.

"You weren't the one who got hurt!"

"No."  Harry admitted.

"Then you don't know what you're talking about!"

Harry looked at her.  For a moment he seemed about to say something
else, but stopped.

"Father is going to make certain that creature is destroyed."

Harry's expression changed.  "Destroyed?"

"Yes."

"You mean killed."

Dracena hesitated.  "That's generally what "destroyed" means, Potter."

Harry stared at her. "Because you were embarrassed?"

Dracena went scarlet. "How dare you!"

Harry shook his head.  "You're unbelievable."

He turned and walked away.  Dracena watched him go.

Her heart was hammering.

Somewhere beneath the anger, she remembered what Harry had said.  She
remembered Hagrid's voice.

*"Back away, Miss Malfoy."*

She hadn't.

*I called him stupid.*

Her face burned.

Potter was right.  Again.  And again, she absolutely hated him for it.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

The investigation continued for several weeks.  Dracena's injury healed,
but her bandage did not disappear.  In fact, it became considerably
larger.

She was excused from Care of Magical Creatures, then from certain
activities in Potions.  Then, somehow, from helping clean up after a
Charms lesson.

Professor Flitwick seemed rather puzzled by this.

"Your arm is still troubling you, Miss Malfoy?"

"Terribly, Professor."

"Oh, dear."

Dracena sighed theatrically.  "I'm managing.  Somehow."

Pansy carried her books.  Another Slytherin opened doors for her.
 Dracena discovered that an injured arm was remarkably convenient.  She
also discovered that people were less inclined to give her chores or
tasks when she looked tragically wounded.  There were advantages to
suffering.  Especially when one had suffered so terribly.  

Then Harry caught her.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

It happened on a Tuesday afternoon.  Nothing important should happen on
a Tuesday.  Harry was leaving the library when he heard a familiar
voice.

"Honestly, Pansy, I can manage."  Dracena stood near a window,
rearranging several books in her bag.  Her bandaged arm was hanging at
her side.

Her other hand was occupied, so she used the supposedly injured one to
lift a heavy textbook.  Then reached up and adjusted her hair.

Harry stopped, watched her, and smiled.  "Oh, that's miraculous."

Dracena jumped.  The book in her bandaged arm almost fell on the ground.

"Potter!"

Harry looked at her arm, then at the heavy book in her bandaged arm,
then back at her.

"Madam Pomfrey must be a genius."

Dracena's expression went blank.  "What?"

"Your arm."  She looked down, then quickly tucked her arm against her
chest.

Harry grinned.  "Remarkable recovery."

Dracena narrowed her eyes.  "I don't know what you're talking about."

"Of course not." Harry said, smiling.

"It's still injured."

"Naturally."

"Some movements are less painful than others."

"Like lifting enormous textbooks?"

Dracena flushed.  "Don't be ridiculous."

"Or fixing your hair?"

"Potter!"

Harry laughed.

Dracena glared at him, turning red.  "You're enjoying this."

"Immensely."

"You're a horrible person."

"Horrible people generally fake injuries to get out of classes."

Harry leaned against the wall.

"So, how long are you planning to keep this up?"

"Keep what up?"

"The tragic suffering."

Dracena looked offended. "I nearly lost my arm!"

Harry stared at her.  "Madam Pomfrey said you had three scratches."

"Deep scratches."

"Right." Harry said, sarcastically.

"Extremely deep."

"Practically down to the bone, I'm sure."

Dracena lifted her chin.

"You're mocking a wounded girl."

Harry grinned.  "You're using your injury to get out of homework."

"I am not!"

"And carrying books."

"I have a medical exemption!"

"And Care of Magical Creatures."

That did it.  Dracena's smile vanished.  Harry didn't notice
immediately.

"Honestly, Malfoy, Buckbeak probably remembers you as the girl who
called him a chicken."

Dracena went very still.  Harry continued.

"I bet if you went back and apologized, he'd..."

"Stop."

Harry blinked.

"What?"

"Stop."

Dracena started shaking.  Harry straightened.  Dracena was staring past
him.  Her fingers had curled against her chest.  She started breathing
heavier

Harry frowned.  "Dracena?"

She didn't answer.  Her eyes were wide.

Harry suddenly remembered the enormous wings.  Buckbeak's shriek.  His
 claws.  Dracena falling, then screaming.  He remembered how she'd
curled into a ball on the grass.  How she'd cried.

"Dracena?"

She flinched.

Harry stopped moving.  "Hey."

Her breathing was coming too quickly.

"It's all right."

She shook her head.  "Don't."

Harry looked around.  The corridor was empty.  Pansy had disappeared,
gone ahead at some point.

Harry stepped slightly closer.  Dracena recoiled.  He stopped
immediately.

"All right.  I won't."

She stared at him, eyes bulging in terror.

Harry lowered his voice.  "You're at Hogwarts.  You're in the corridor
outside the library."

Her eyes blinked slowly, then turned toward Harry

"There's no hippogriff here."

Dracena swallowed.

Harry waited.  He didn't know what else to do.  For once, when dealing
with Dracena, no clever remark came to his mind.  He just stood there,
watching her.

Dracena's breathing began to slow.

Her eyes filled.

"I hate that thing."  She said, voice twittering on the edge of crying.

Harry nodded.  "Yeah."

"I hate it."  Dracena wiped a tear away from her eye.

"I know."

Harry looked down at her hands.  They were shaking.

Harry's stomach tightened.  He'd thought she was simply being dramatic.
 And she was,  But...

But not about everything.

"Come on." he said quietly.

Dracena looked up.  "What?"

"There's a bench around the corner, outside"

"I don't need..."

"I know."  He smiled faintly.  "But I'm going to sit down for a minute.
 You can come if you want."

He started walking.  A moment later, he heard footsteps behind him.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

The bench overlooked one of the smaller courtyards.

Harry sat at one end, Dracena sat at the other.

They sat, neither of them speaking.  Dracena stared at her shoes.  Harry
watched a bird hopping along.

Dracena said, "You're not going to tell anyone?"

Harry looked at her.  "Tell them what?"

She gestured vaguely.  "About that."

Harry considered.  "No."

Dracena looked suspicious.  "Really?"

"Really."

"Not even Weasley?"

"No."

"Or Granger?"

"No."

She studied him.  Harry shrugged.  "It's not their business."

Dracena looked away.  "I wasn't afraid." Dracena said, unconvincingly.

Harry turned to look at her.  

Dracena was staring straight ahead.  "I wasn't."

Harry considered his answer. "All right."

She looked at him sharply.  "You don't believe me."

"No." Harry said, neutrally.

Her mouth fell open indignantly.

Harry smiled slightly. "Dracena, you were terrified."

She went red.  "How dare you..."

"It's all right."

She stopped.

"It's all right to be scared." Harry said, softly.

Dracena stared at him, and she gave a short, bitter laugh.  "Easy for
you to say."

Harry frowned.  "Why?"

"Because you're never scared."

Harry blinked.  "What?"

Dracena looked genuinely irritated.  "Oh, come on, Potter."

"What?"

"Everyone knows." she said, bitterly.

"Knows what?"

"You're Harry Potter."

Harry sighed.  "Here we go."

"You do ridiculous things all the time.  You fight monsters.  You fly
around on broomsticks chasing who knows what.  You go running toward
danger like some sort of lunatic."

Harry grinned.  "Thanks."

"And you're never afraid."

Harry stopped smiling.  "That's not true."

Dracena looked at him.

"Really?"

"Really." Harry said.

She seemed unconvinced.

Harry leaned back.  "Buckbeak scared the hell out of me."

Dracena blinked.  "What?"

"Buckbeak."

"But you walked straight up to him like it was nothing!"

"Yeah."

"And bowed!"

"Yeah."

"And touched him!"

"Yeah."

"Then you weren't afraid!"

Harry laughed.  "Dracena, have you seen the size of his claws?"

She hesitated.  "Yes."

"Well, so have I."

She stared..

"The thought HAD occurred to me that he might bite my head off."

"Then why did you do it?"

"Because Hagrid told me how." he said.

Dracena frowned.

Harry shrugged.  "I trust Hagrid.  He knows animals.  He told me what to
do, so I did it."

She considered this.  "You weren't frightened?"

"I just told you I was."

"But you didn't look frightened."

Harry thought about that.  "Well, I was."

Dracena looked down.

Harry watched her for a moment.  "I'm scared all the time."

She looked up.  "You're joking."

"No."

Harry leaned forward.  "When those Hogwarts letters started coming, I
was scared."

Dracena frowned. "Of letters?"

"Not the letters themselves."  He smiled faintly.  "My aunt and uncle
went completely mad.  They wouldn't let me read them.  They started
moving us around.  We ended up in this horrible little hut on a rock in
the sea."

Dracena stared.

"Then Hagrid turned up."  Harry smiled.  "He broke the door down."

Dracena blinked.  "Really?"

"Really."

Harry looked out into the courtyard.  "Suddenly this enormous stranger
was telling me I was a wizard, and that my parents were wizards, and
that everything my aunt and uncle had told me was a lie."

Dracena was silent.

"I was terrified."

"But you went with him."

"Yeah."

"Why?"

Harry thought.  "Because I wanted to know."

Dracena watched him.

"And because staying with the Dursleys was worse."  Harry looked at
Dracena.  "Not that I knew what would happen.  I didn't."

Dracena looked at him.

"Then there was King's Cross."

"The station?"

"Yeah."

Harry laughed.  "I had to run straight at a brick wall."

Dracena smiled despite herself.  "Platform Nine and Three-Quarters."

"Exactly."

"You didn't know how to get through?"

"No."

"Oh, Potter."

Harry grinned.  "Go on.  Laugh."

Dracena laughed.  Harry liked hearing it.  It was different from the
sharp, snobbish laugh she usually gave him.  He found himself smiling
too.

"Anyway." he continued, "I thought I was going to smash my face into the
wall."

"But you did it."

"Yeah."

"Why?"

"Because Ron's family told me how."

Dracena's smile faded slightly.  

Harry noticed.  He continued before she could say anything.  "And I
trusted them."

She looked down.

"I suppose that's the thing."  Harry said, thoughtfully.

"What?"

"Being scared doesn't always mean you shouldn't do something."

Dracena frowned.  "That sounds stupid."

"Probably."  Harry shrugged.  "But sometimes being scared tells you
something matters."

She looked at him.

Harry searched for the words.  He wasn't very good at this.  Not like
Hermione.  "Sometimes I'm scared because something's dangerous.  And
that's useful.  It makes me pay attention."

Dracena listened.

"Sometimes I'm scared because I don't know what's going to happen."  He
thought about Hagrid.  The letters.  The train.  The Sorting Hat.  "And
sometimes I'm scared because if I don't do something, I'll spend the
rest of my life wondering what would've happened if I had."

Dracena was very quiet.

"Sometimes I run toward what scares me because I'm afraid that if I stop
and think too long, I might freeze."

He looked at her.  "That doesn't mean I'm not frightened."

Dracena stared at him.  "I froze."

Harry nodded.

"I couldn't move."

"Yeah."

"Hagrid told me to back away."

Harry said nothing, looking at her, listening.

"I heard him."  She swallowed.  "I knew what he was saying, but I
couldn't..."  Her voice cracked.  "I couldn't make my legs move."

Harry felt something twist inside his chest.  He'd never heard Dracena
sound like this.

She looked down at her arm.  "I thought it was going to kill me."

Harry spoke quietly.  "I'm sorry."

Dracena looked up.

"I shouldn't have made fun of you for being scared."

She stared at him.

Harry shrugged.  "I didn't know."

Her eyes filled again.  She blinked furiously.

"Well."

Harry waited.  

Dracena cleared her throat.  "Thank you."

Harry smiled.  "You're welcome."

They sat in silence.

"But Hagrid still didn't do anything wrong." Harry said.

Dracena stiffened.

Harry continued.  "And Buckbeak doesn't deserve to die."

She looked away.  "...yeah."

Harry looked at her.

She swallowed.  "I shouldn't have called him stupid."

Harry said nothing.

"I was frightened.  And everyone was watching.  And you made it look so
easy."

Harry almost smiled.  "So you called him a chicken?"

Dracena's face reddened.  "Don't."

"Sorry."

She looked at him.  For a moment she thought he might laugh, but he
didn't.

She sighed.  "It was a very stupid thing to do."

Harry nodded.  "Yeah."

She glared.  "You don't have to agree so enthusiastically."

Harry grinned.

"Sorry."

Dracena's mouth twitched.  She looked down again.  "Father doesn't
know."

Harry frowned.  "Doesn't know what?"

"That I insulted Buckbeak."

"Oh."

"He thinks it attacked me for no reason."

Harry considered.  "Are you going to tell him?"

"I don't know."

Harry nodded.  "All right."

She looked at him.

"You're not going to tell me I have to?"

Harry shrugged.  "He's your father."

Dracena seemed surprised.

Harry continued.  "But Hagrid might lose his job."

She looked down.

"And Buckbeak might be killed."

"I know."

Harry waited.

Dracena sighed.  "I'll think about it."

Harry nodded.  "All right."

She looked at him suspiciously.

"That's it?"

"What?"

"You're not going to lecture me?"

Harry smiled.  "Would it help?"

"No." She smiled, despite herself.

"Then no."

Dracena stared at him.  She suddenly noticed how green his eyes were.
 She'd noticed before, of course.  In Madam Malkin's.  On the train.  In
corridors.  In classrooms.

She had always thought Potter had unusually green eyes.  But now?  Now
he was sitting beside her, looking at her without laughing, without
mocking, without expecting anything.

He'd seen her frightened.\
He'd seen her cry.\
He'd heard her admit she was wrong.\
And he hadn't used any of it against her.

He was being nice.

Genuinely nice.  To *her* .  Dracena felt her stomach do something
peculiar.

Harry smiled.  "You all right?"

She stared at him.  His hair was sticking up.  As usual.  

His glasses were slightly crooked.  As usual.

He looked ridiculous.

And...

Oh.   *Oh, no.*

Dracena looked away so quickly she nearly hurt her neck.

Harry frowned.

"Dracena?"

Her face was burning.  This was intolerable.  Absolutely intolerable.

Harry leaned forward.  "Are you feeling sick?"

That made it worse.  He was worried about her.

*Oh, for Merlin's sake.*

Dracena stood abruptly.

Harry blinked.  "What?"

She straightened her robes.

"Well."  she said, sourly.

Harry looked up at her.  "Well what?"

"I suppose you've enjoyed yourself."

Harry frowned.  "What?"

"Having a good laugh at my expense."

Harry stared.  "Dracena, I wasn't..."

"'Poor little Malfoy.  Frightened of a bird.  How terribly amusing.'"

Harry stood.  "I didn't say that!"

"Of course not."  Her voice sharpened.  Something changed in Dracena,
like a light went out.  "You simply decided to take pity on me."

Harry looked genuinely confused.  "Pity?"

"Yes, Potter.  Pity."

"I wasn't pitying you!"  Harry responded, confused.  Lost.

"Then what were you doing?"

Harry opened his mouth, but no words came out.  He'd seen someone
frightened.  He'd wanted to help.  That was all.  Wasn't it?

"I was trying to be nice."

Dracena's stomach twisted.  That was somehow worse.  "Well, don't."

Harry stared. "What?"

"I don't need your charity."

"Charity?"

"Or your sympathy."

Harry's expression changed.  "Dracena..."

"I'm perfectly capable of looking after myself."

"I didn't say you weren't." Harry said.

"I don't need Harry Potter rescuing me from my own feelings."

Harry took a step back.  "Fine."

Dracena felt a sudden, horrible pang.  She wanted to take it back.  She
was so confused.  She was angry at Harry, and angry at herself at the
same time.

Harry folded his arms.  "Fine, Malfoy."

The name stung.  She'd gotten used to him calling her Dracena.  Only for
a few minutes.  But she'd liked it.  More than she wanted to admit.

"Good." she said.

"Brilliant."

"Wonderful."

"Anything else?"

Dracena lifted her chin.

"Yes."

Harry waited.

"Your hair is still ridiculous."

Harry stared at her, then he gave a short, humorless laugh.  "Okay?"

Dracena turned and started walking.  For one terrible moment she wanted
to turn around, but she didn't.

"Goodbye, Potter." she said over her shoulder.

Harry looked at her.

"Bye, Malfoy."

She walked away.  Quickly, before he could see her crying.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Harry stood beside the bench.  He watched Dracena disappear around the
corner.  He sat down after she receded from view.

"What was THAT all about?" he asked nobody in particular.

The courtyard offered no explanation.  Harry stared at the wall.

He thought about the conversation.  She'd been frightened.  He'd
listened to her.

They'd talked.  Actually talked.  It had been...  Nice.  Really nice.
 Harry frowned.  He'd liked hearing her laugh.  He'd liked that she'd
trusted him enough to admit she was frightened.

He'd liked...

Harry stopped, and thought about how Hermione kept teasing him about how
he "liked" Dracena.  

No.  Absolutely not.  This was Malfoy.  Dracena Malfoy.  The girl who
insulted Ron's family.  Who called Hermione names.  Who treated everyone
as though they were beneath her.  Dracena, who was currently trying to
have Hagrid dismissed and Buckbeak killed.

Harry was supposed to be angry with her.  And he was.  Obviously.  He
was furious.

Harry tried to feel furious.  It wasn't working particularly well.

Instead he felt...  Hurt?  That was stupid.  Why should he care what
Dracena Malfoy thought of him?

Harry stood.  "She's horrible." he muttered.

The empty corridor did not disagree.  He started toward Gryffindor
Tower.

"Completely horrible."

He walked faster.

"Insufferable."

That sounded better.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Dracena made it back to the Slytherin common room before she started
crying properly.

She went straight to her dormitory, laid in her bed and buried her face
in her pillow.

She was crying because Harry Potter had been kind to her, and she'd
punished him for it.

She could still see his face.  The confusion, then the hurt.

*I was trying to be nice.*

Dracena squeezed her eyes shut.

"Idiot." she whispered.

She wasn't entirely sure if she meant him, or herself.

After she stopped crying, she rolled onto her back and stared at the
canopy.

Harry had been frightened too.  He'd told her that.  He'd admitted it
without embarrassment, without shame.

Harry acted as though being afraid was simply something people
experienced, as though it didn't make you weak.  as though you could be
afraid and still be brave.

Dracena thought about Buckbeak.\
About freezing.\
About Hagrid telling her to move.\
About Potter bowing to the enormous creature despite being frightened.

And about what he'd said.  " *Sometimes being scared tells you something
matters."*

She looked at her arm.  The scratches had almost completely healed.
 She'd been pretending otherwise for weeks.

Perhaps it was time to stop.  Dracena sighed.

Tomorrow she'd write to Father.  She wasn't sure what she'd say.  She
wasn't sure she could convince him to stop pursuing Hagrid.  She wasn't
even sure she wanted to admit everything.

She felt determined to go back to Care of Magical Creatures.

Eventually.  Not tomorrow.  Absolutely not tomorrow.

But someday.

She turned onto her side.  Annoyingly, she thought about Potter again.

His ridiculous hair.  His stupid glasses.  Those green eyes.  The way
he'd said her name.

*Dracena.  * She pulled the blanket over her head.

"Oh, shut up."  She said to herself.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

The matter of Buckbeak was eventually resolved.

The Hogwarts governors reviewed Hagrid's instructions, heard from the
students who had witnessed the incident, and concluded that the
hippogriff had reacted to provocation despite adequate warnings.

Hagrid was reinstated.  Buckbeak was spared.  Lucius Malfoy was furious.

Dracena received a lengthy owl expressing his disappointment with the
school's judgment.

She read it twice, then put it away.  For reasons she didn't entirely
understand, she felt relieved.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

A week later, Harry encountered her outside the Great Hall.

"Malfoy."

"Potter.

He glanced at her arm.  The bandage was gone.

"Miraculous recovery."

Dracena narrowed her eyes.  "Don't start."

Harry smiled.

"Going back to Care of Magical Creatures?"

She hesitated.

"Yes."

"Eventually."

"Right."

She glared.  "Don't look so pleased with yourself."

"Wouldn't dream of it."

They stood there.  For a moment neither seemed to know what to say.

Dracena lifted her chin.

"Your tie is crooked."

Harry looked down.  "No, it isn't."

"It is."

She stepped forward.  Without thinking, she reached up and straightened
it.

Harry froze.  Her fingers were inches from his face.

She could see his eyes.  He could see hers.  For one strange second, the
corridor seemed very quiet.

Dracena stepped back.  "There."

Harry swallowed.  "Thanks."

She looked away.  "Someone has to make you presentable."

Harry smiled. "Good thing you're here, then."

Dracena's cheeks went pink.

She glared at him.  "Don't push it, Potter."

Harry grinned.  "Wouldn't dream of it, Malfoy."

She walked away.  Harry watched her go.  This time, when she reached the
corner, she looked back.

Harry was still watching.  Dracena smiled.  Harry smiled back.  Then
they both turned away, thoroughly embarrassed.

Neither understood what had just happened.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## <span class="c12"></span>

## <span class="c12">The Broom Ride</span>

<div class="illustration">

![The Broom Ride](Images/The%20Broom%20Ride.jpg)

</div>

The wind was warm.  Dracena Malfoy had never particularly liked riding
on the back of someone else's broom.  She was the type of girl who
preferred being in control.  She preferred knowing exactly where she was
going, how fast she was getting there, and just how dangerous the flying
was going to be.

This was different.  Harry Potter was flying.  Dracena was sitting
behind him, her arms wrapped around his waist.  They soared over the
rolling green hills beyond Hogwarts, passing forests and sparkling
rivers.  Far below, sheep grazed in sunlit meadows, and little muggle
villages nestled between the hills.

The sky was impossibly blue.  Harry banked to the left, and pulled an
extra half gee.  Dracena tightened her grip.

She smiled.  She looked at the ordinary landscapes and smiled happily.
 She tightened her arms again.  Harry laughed.  Dracena rested her head
against his shoulder.  She could feel him laughing.

She liked that.  She liked the warmth of his back against her.  She
liked the way the wind tugged at her hair.  She liked that, for once,
neither of them had anywhere they needed to be.

Harry turned his head slightly.  "Are you all right?"

Dracena looked at him.  He was smiling, too.

She smiled back.  "Yes."

Harry turned forward again.

They flew low over a lake.  Sunlight glittered across the water, and
Dracena watched the reflection of the clouds moving beneath them.  She'd
flown over beautiful places before, but she'd never enjoyed flying quite
like this.

Harry knew where they were going.  She trusted him.  That was a strange
thought.  Dracena Malfoy trusted Harry Potter.  She smiled to herself,
then she moved a little closer and wrapped her arms more tightly around
him, resting her cheek against his back.

She could hear his heartbeat, or perhaps it was her own.  She wasn't
sure.  She closed her eyes.  For once, she didn't care.

Harry's voice came again.  "Dracena?"

She smiled.  "Hmm?"

"Are you all right?"

She nodded against his back.  "Perfectly."

The wind was warm.  The sunlight was beautiful.  Harry Potter was
carrying her somewhere wonderful.  She didn't know where.  She didn't
care.  She held him tighter, her eyes closed, savoring the moment.

Harry spoke again.  "Dracena?"  His voice sounded different.  "Dracena,
are you all right?"

She opened her eyes suddenly.  Harry Potter was standing over her.

Dracena stared.  He was looking down at her with a slightly worried
expression.  Behind him were bookshelves.  She was in the Hogwarts
library.  Dracena blinked.  

Harry leaned closer.  "Are you all right?"

Dracena looked around.  Her cheek had been resting on an open textbook.
 A small piece of parchment was stuck to her forehead, and fell to the
desk.  Her quill had rolled onto the floor.

Harry smiled down at her.  "You fell asleep."

Dracena stared at him.

"I know exams are tough, but I don't think Professor Flitwick will
accept drool on your notes as extra credit."

Harry frowned, because Dracena didn't answer.  "Dracena?"

She looked at him.  His ridiculous hair.  His tie was crooked.  AGAIN.
 Those stupid green eyes.  For one wonderful moment, she could still
feel his back against her cheek.  The way she'd held him.  She realized
none of it had happened.  He hadn't taken her anywhere.  He hadn't
smiled at her like that.  

He was just standing there, being Harry Potter.

She was sitting in the library, surrounded by textbooks, with an
enormous examination tomorrow.  Dracena felt something sting behind her
eyes.

She blinked.  No.  It was too late.

A single tear slipped down her cheek.

Harry's expression changed.  "Dracena?"

She stared at him.

"Hey, are you..."

**"STUPID POTTER!"**

Harry recoiled.  "What?"

Dracena sat upright.  Her face was bright red. ** "Stupid, stupid
Potter!"**

Harry stared.

"I..."

"Go away!"

He blinked.  "Dracena, I was just.."

**"I SAID GO AWAY!"**

Harry straightened.  "Right."  He raised both hands helplessly.  "Okay."

Dracena glared at him through watery eyes.

Harry took a step backward.  "Well."  He gestured vaguely toward her
textbooks.  "Good luck on exams."  Another helpless wave of his hands.
 "I guess."  He walked out of the library.

"Stupid potter." She said to herself, as she cried silently to herself.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# <span class="c9"></span>

# <span class="c9">Year 6</span>

## <span class="c12">The Yule Ball</span>

<div class="illustration">

![The Yule Ball](Images/The%20Yule%20Ball.jpg)

</div>

Harry Potter had faced enormous spiders, murderous wizards, and
creatures that could suck the happiness out of a person.  None of them
had been quite as frightening as asking Cho Chang to the Yule Ball.
 He'd spent three days working up the courage.\

He thought over the past three days of rehearsing what he would say.
 Three days listening to Ron offer advice that ranged from unhelpful to
actively disastrous.

Now?  He was finally standing in front of her.

"Cho?"

She smiled. "Hello, Harry."

Harry swallowed.  "Er.  Hello."

An excellent beginning, he thought.  Absolutely brilliant.

Cho waited.

Harry took a breath.  "I was wondering..."

"Yes?"

"About the Yule Ball."

Cho's expression softened.  "Oh."

Harry felt a brief surge of hope.  "Would you like to go with me?"

Cho looked genuinely disappointed.  "Oh, Harry."

His stomach sank.

"I'm sorry.  Someone already asked me."

"Oh." Harry's stomach continued its dive into the earth's core.

"Cedric Diggory.  I said yes."

Harry nodded.  "Oh!  Well then."  He smiled.  It was not a particularly
convincing smile, but he was proud of having managed one at all.
 "That's all right."

Cho looked apologetic. "I really am sorry."

"No, no.  It's fine."  He nodded again.  "Completely fine."

"Harry..."

"Hope you have a lovely time."

Cho smiled sadly.  "Thank you."

Harry turned away.

He made it about six steps before his smile collapsed.   *Brilliant  *
Three days.  Three bloody days, and somebody had beaten him to it.  Of
COURSE it had to be Diggory.

He stared at the floor.  Perhaps he could simply skip the ball.  Surely
there wasn't a rule requiring everyone to attend.  He could stay in
Gryffindor Tower.  Maybe read a book.  Get eaten by a dragon.

"Potter."  Harry closed his eyes.   *Oh, no.  * Not now.  Anyone but
her.

He turned.  Dracena Malfoy was leaning against a stone pillar, taking a
bite out of an apple, wearing the expression of someone who had just
discovered an exceptionally entertaining secret.

"Malfoy."

She smiled.

"Well, well."

Harry sighed.  "How long have you been standing there?"

Dracena tilted her head.  "Long enough."  She took another bite.

"Wonderful."

"Turned you down, did she?"

Harry looked away.  "Go away, Malfoy."

Dracena stepped closer.  "Honestly, Potter.  I almost feel sorry for
you."

Harry said nothing.

Dracena continued.  "But you're in luck."

Harry looked at her.

She smiled brightly.  "I'm feeling generous today."

Harry frowned.  "What?"

"I'll go with you."

Harry stared.

Dracena lifted her chin.  "I mean, somebody has to, right?"

Harry blinked.

She continued, gathering momentum.  "THE Harry Potter can't possibly go
stag to the Yule Ball."

"Dracena..." Harry started.  He felt anger rising in his ribcage.

"And my dress arrived this morning.  Mother had it made specially for
me."

Harry looked away.  He felt the angry fire inside him growing.

"It's easily the finest in the castle, so at least one of us would look
presentable."

"Malfoy..."  he said, voice low.

"And of course everyone would be watching us.  It would be quite the
occasion, wouldn't it?  Potter and Malfoy arriving  together.  I imagine
people would talk about it for weeks."  She smiled. "And really, Potter,
you should be grateful.  It's not every day someone like me offers
to..."

"SHUT UP!" Harry shouted, rounding on her.

Dracena stopped.  She dropped the apple she'd been eating.

Harry was staring at her.  His face was red.  His eyes were bright.
 "Just shut up, Malfoy!"

She blinked.  "Potter?"

"I've had enough of your stupid jokes!"

Dracena's smile disappeared.  "But I wasn't..."

"Can't you leave me alone for five minutes?"

She stared.

Harry's voice cracked.  "Just five minutes without you laughing at me?"

"Harry, I..."

"GO AND FIND SOMEONE ELSE TO LAUGH AT!"

Dracena recoiled.

Harry was breathing hard.  "AND STAY AWAY FROM ME!"

He turned, and walked away before she could see the tears forming in his
eyes.

Dracena stood motionless.  For several seconds she couldn't move.  She'd
been so pleased with herself.  She'd finally found a way to ask Potter
to the ball without actually having to ask him.

It had been perfect.  She could offer.  He could accept.  Neither would
have to admit anything embarrassing.  And now...

Dracena swallowed.

"But..."  Her voice was very small as Harry retreated out of sight.  "I
wasn't joking."  She stared at the empty corridor.  "I really wanted..."

She stopped.  Her eyes filled.  She looked down at her shoes.  "Oh.
 Stupid Potter."

But there was no anger in it.  Not really.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

<span class="c4 c14"></span>

Harry found Hermione in the library.  She was sitting beside a stack of
books, working on an essay.  "Hermione?"

She looked up.  "Harry!  What's wrong?"

"Nothing."

Hermione raised an eyebrow.

Harry sighed. "Right.  Something.  He sat opposite her.  "Cho's going
with someone else."

Hermione's expression softened.

"Oh, Harry."

"Cedric Diggory."

"Oh."

Harry looked at her.  "Yeah."

Hermione considered.  "Well, there are plenty of other girls."

Harry stared at the table.  "I Suppose."

Hermione smiled.  "Have you asked anyone else?"

Harry hesitated, then looked at her.  "Hermione?"

She blinked.  "Oh."

Harry swallowed.

"Would you..."

"Oh, Harry, I'm sorry."

Harry closed his eyes.  Of course.

"Ron already asked me."

Harry's eyes flew open.  "RON?"

Hermione went pink.  "Yes."

"Ron Weasley?"

Hermione laughed.  "How many Rons do you know?"

Harry stared.  "But..."

Hermione looked down at her book.  "He asked me yesterday."

"And you said yes?"

She smiled.  "Yes."

Harry blinked.  "Wow."

Hermione blushed more deeply.  "Honestly, Harry."

"No, I mean.."  He stopped.  He wasn't entirely sure what he meant.

Ron and Hermione?

<span class="c2 c4">Ron and Hermione?</span>

He considered.  Actually?  That made a strange sort of sense.  He'd
noticed them spending more time together lately.  Arguing, certainly.
 But they always argued.  Sometimes, when they thought nobody was
looking?  They smiled at one another in a way Harry didn't quite
understand.

"Oh."

Hermione looked at him.

Harry smiled.  "Well.  That's brilliant."  He actually meant it.

She smiled back.  "Thank you."

Harry leaned back.  "Guess I'm running out of options."

Hermione considered, then her eyes sparkled.

"Why don't you ask Dracena?"

Harry's expression changed instantly.  "Don't."

Hermione blinked.  "What?"

"Don't start."

She frowned.  "Harry..."

"I've already had enough of her today!"

Hermione stared.

Harry stood.  "Honestly, Hermione.  Of all people!"  He grabbed his bag.
 "Forget it."

"Harry!"

He walked away.  Hermione watched him go, then slowly closed her book.
 "Oh, dear."  She had a feeling she'd missed something important.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

The next morning, Harry was leaving breakfast when someone stepped into
his path.

"Potter."

He stopped.  Dracena stood before him.  She looked unusually
uncomfortable.

"Malfoy.  I thought I told you..."

Harry tried to walk around her.  She blocked him.

"Wait."

Harry sighed.  "What?"

Dracena took a breath.  "Look."

Harry waited.

She looked down, then back up.  "I'm sorry."

Harry blinked.  He tried to remember if Dracena Malfoy had ever
apologized to him before.  He couldn't recall any instances immediately.
 Harry looked at her again.  She looked genuinely miserable.

"I wasn't trying to make fun of you."

He looked away.  "Could've fooled me."

"I know."

Harry looked back.

She swallowed.  "I was trying to..."

She stopped.  Harry waited.

Dracena's cheeks warmed.  "I was trying to help."

Harry sighed. "Dracena..."  

"And I'm without a date too."

Harry frowned.  "What?"

She lifted her chin.  "I asked Cedric Diggory."

Harry stared.  "He's going with Cho."

Dracena looked at him.  "Yes.  I know.  He asked her after I asked him."
she said, bitterly.

Harry blinked.  "Oh."

Dracena looked away.  For once, she had nothing clever to say.

Harry studied her.  She'd asked Cedric Diggory.  He'd turned her down,
and then she'd found Harry, and offered to go with him.  Perhaps she
hadn't been joking.  Perhaps...

Harry took a breath.  "Look, Dracena..."

She turned.  "Yes."

Harry blinked.  

"What?"  She said, slightly annoyed.

"Yes.  I will."

She froze.  Harry stared.  Dracena stared back.  Her face went scarlet.
 "I mean..."

Harry smiled slowly.

Dracena lifted her chin.  "I'll show up with you."

Harry's smile widened.

"But you have to look presentable."  She said, sternly.

Harry laughed.

Dracena scowled.  "Don't laugh!"

"I'm not."

"You are!"

Harry grinned.  "All right."

Dracena blinked.  "All right?"

"Yeah."  He smiled.  "I'd like that."

Dracena felt her stomach flutter.  

"Oh."  Harry continued.

"And don't worry."  She frowned.

"About what?"

"I'll take you shopping."  Dracena stared, then she laughed.  "Potter,
you have absolutely no idea what you're getting yourself into."

Harry smiled.  "Probably not."

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Harry had never noticed the little boutique before.  It was tucked
between two much larger shops on a narrow street just off the main road
through Hogsmeade.  There was no sign, only a polished brass door and a
small window displaying a single set of exquisite dress robes.

Harry stopped.  "What's this place?"

Dracena smiled.  "A shop."

Harry looked at her.  "Yes, I gathered.  Very helpful."

"You're welcome."

He examined the door.  "Why doesn't it have a sign?"

Dracena looked faintly surprised.  "Because they don't need one."

Harry frowned.  "What?"

"They only admit people they know."

Harry stared.  "How does anyone find them?"

Dracena smiled.  "They don't."

Harry considered.  "That sounds like a terrible way to run a business."

Dracena laughed.  "Oh, Potter."

"What?"

"Some businesses don't need to LOOK for customers."

Harry stared.  "Then why are they businesses?"

Dracena sighed.  "Come along."

She opened the door.  A little bell chimed.  An impeccably dressed
attendant appeared almost immediately.

"Miss Malfoy!"  The man shouted theatrically, He positively sang her
name, spreading his hands in delight.

Dracena smiled.  "Good afternoon, Mr.  Vale."

"Oh, my *dear* , the pleasure is entirely mine."

His eyes moved toward Harry.  "And who." he asked, one eyebrow arching
magnificently, "is this charming young gentleman?"  His voice
practically sang as he spoke.

Dracena lifted her chin.  "Harry Potter."

The attendant's eyebrows rose.  "Ah."

Harry sighed.   *Here we go.*

Dracena continued.  "He needs dress robes."

Mr.  Vale clasped his hands together.  His eyes moved up and down,
examining Harry.

"Well, Mr.  Potter.  I believe we have our work cut out for us."

Dracena smiled.  "Don't worry, Potter.  We'll fix you up."

------------------------------------------------------------------------

The shop was enormous inside.  Much larger than it had appeared from the
street.  Rows of fine robes lined the walls.  Mirrors floated in the
air.  Soft lights illuminated displays of fabric that shimmered and
changed color as Harry walked past.

He stopped beside a rack.  "How about these?"

Dracena looked, then recoiled.  "Honestly, Potter."

Harry frowned.  "What?"

"Those are last year's styles."

Harry examined them.  "They look fine to me."

Dracena sighed.  "That's the problem."

Harry grinned.  "What's wrong with last year's styles?"

"Everything."

"They're clothes."

"Potter."

"What?"

"Do you want to look respectable?"

Harry considered.  "Not particularly."

Dracena glared.  Harry laughed.  "All right, all right."

She examined several racks, then stopped at a particular one.

"Oh."

Harry watched her.  She pulled out a set of dark dress robes.  They were
deep midnight blue, with subtle silver embroidery.  Elegant without
being ostentatious.  She held them up against Harry, slightly tilting
her head.

"Yes."

Harry looked down.  "These?"

"These."

"They look expensive."

Dracena smiled.  "They are."

Harry sighed.  "Naturally."

She handed them to him.  "Try them on."

Harry disappeared behind a dressing-room door.  Mr.  Vale followed to
assist him.  A moment later, Harry's ordinary robes came flying over the
top of the door.  Dracena caught them, and she stared, slowly smiling a
wicked smile.  She glanced toward the dressing room, and raised her
wand.

"Revel..."

The attendant's head appeared over the partition.  "Don't."

Dracena froze.  Mr.  Vale stared at her.

"Even."

Her cheeks warmed, and turned bright red.

"Try."

He raised an eyebrow.  "The fitting rooms are warded, Miss Malfoy.  Some
of us believe a gentleman deserves a little privacy."  he scolded with
exaggerated severity.

Dracena went scarlet.  "I wasn't..."

"Of course not."  Mr.  Vale grinned widely at her, and raised his
eyebrows, looking back at where Harry was changing.

He disappeared.  Then added from behind the door:  "Pervert."

Dracena nearly dropped her wand.  "I AM NOT!"

From inside, Harry called:  "What?"

"NOTHING, POTTER!"

Harry paused.  "Are you arguing with the shopkeeper?"

"NO!"

Harry laughed.

Dracena covered her face.   *Oh, for Merlin's sake.*

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Several minutes later, the door opened and Harry stepped out.  Dracena
turned, and forgot how to speak.  The robes fit him perfectly.  The
midnight-blue fabric complemented his dark hair and green eyes.  The
silver embroidery caught the light without drawing attention away from
him.  He looked older.  Taller.  Handsome.  Really, genuinely handsome.

Dracena stared.

Harry looked down.  "Well?"

She said nothing.

Harry frowned.  "Dracena?"

She blinked.  "Oh.  Wow."

Harry looked worried.  "Are they bad?"

Dracena swallowed.  "No."

Harry smiled.  "No?"

She straightened, lifting her chin.  "They'll do."

Harry laughed.  "They'll do?"

"Yes."

She looked away.  "Perfectly adequate."

Mr.  Vale clapped his hands gently, grinning widely.  "Excellent!" he
said.

Harry examined himself in the mirror.  "Well, I like them."

Dracena glanced back.  He was smiling.  That stupid smile.  His green
eyes shone.  She swallowed.  "Good."

Harry turned.  "Thanks, Dracena."  She looked at him.

For one moment she forgot to be difficult.  "You're welcome."  Then she
remembered herself.  "See you tonight, Potter."  She hurried toward the
door before he could notice how red her face had become.

<span class="c4 c14"></span>

Harry stood outside the entrance to the Slytherin common room.  Even Ron
had admitted how good his robes looked, although he'd complained
bitterly about how much better Harry's looked than his own.

Harry felt ridiculous because he was waiting for Dracena Malfoy.  As his
date.  Well.  Sort of.  She'd been very particular about the
terminology.  They were arriving together.  Nothing more.

Harry had agreed, but he couldn't quite stop smiling.  He knocked.  The
entrance opened, and Dracena stepped out.

Harry forgot how to breathe.  She wore a magnificent gown of deep
emerald green, embroidered with delicate silver patterns that shimmered
when she moved.  Her pale hair fell in soft waves around her bare
shoulders, held back by a small silver ornament.  Her eyes seemed
impossibly bright.  The dress was elegant.  Beautiful.

Harry stared.

Dracena smiled.  "Well?"

Harry opened his mouth, but nothing came out.

Dracena's smile widened.  "Pick your chin up off the floor, Potter.
 Honestly."

Harry blinked.  "Sorry."

She laughed.

Harry swallowed.  "You look amazing."

Dracena froze.  For once, Dracena Malfoy had absolutely no idea what to
say.  "Oh."

Harry smiled.  "Really."

Her cheeks warmed  "Well."  She cleared her throat.  "You look rather
presentable yourself."

Harry grinned. "High praise."

"Don't get used to it."

He offered his arm.

Dracena stared, smiled, and took it.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

The Great Hall was magnificent.  Enchanted snow drifted beneath the
ceiling.  Thousands of tiny lights floated in the air.  The walls
glittered with frost.  Music filled the room.  Students danced, laughing
and talking.

Harry and Dracena entered, and everything in the room stopped.  The
musicians faltered for a moment, and the conversations nearest the doors
fell silent.

Harry Potter and Dracena Malfoy.  Together.  Harry felt every eye turn
toward them.  His stomach tightened.

"Oh, brilliant." he muttered.

Dracena smiled.  She was in heaven.  Everyone was looking.  Everyone was
whispering.  Everyone was wondering.  She and Harry were the center of
attention.  She lifted her chin, and straightened her shoulders.  She
walked beside Harry as though they were royalty.

Harry leaned toward her.  "Dracena."

"Hmm?"

"Everyone's staring."

"I know."

"Doesn't that bother you?"

She smiled.  "Not in the slightest."

They reached the center of the room.

The music resumed.  Conversations slowly returned.

Dracena looked around, then released Harry's arm.

"Well."

Harry turned.  "Well?"

She smiled.  "See you later, Potter."

Harry blinked.  "What?"

She gestured toward the Slytherin tables.  "My friends."

Harry stared.  "But I thought..."

Dracena smiled sweetly.  "I said we'd show up together."

Harry opened his mouth, then closed it.

She laughed.  "Enjoy yourself." Then she walked away.

Harry stood motionless while he watched her go.

He looked toward the ceiling.  "Of course."  He sighed.  "Of course."

------------------------------------------------------------------------

<span class="c4 c14"></span>

Dracena was having a dreadful time.  This was particularly irritating
because she looked magnificent.  Everyone had said so.  Pansy had
admired her dress.  Several boys had complimented her.  Even a few
Gryffindor girls had stopped to ask where she'd bought it.  Everything
was going exactly as she'd expected.

Except she was bored.  Terribly bored.  Pansy was discussing someone
else's dress.  One of the other girls was explaining why a certain
Ravenclaw boy was unsuitable.  Another was gossiping about a couple who
had been caught kissing behind the greenhouses.

Dracena stared across the room.  Harry was sitting with Ron and
Hermione.  Ron looked extremely uncomfortable in his dress robes.
 Hermione looked beautiful.  The two of them were behaving very
strangely.  Ron kept offering Hermione things.  Food.  Drinks.  His
chair.  At one point he tried to pull her chair out for her after she'd
already sat down.  Hermione laughed.  Ron went red.  Hermione reached
across the table and straightened his collar.  Ron looked as though he'd
been struck by lightning.

Dracena smiled faintly.   *Oh, those two.*

Harry, meanwhile, looked thoroughly abandoned.  He said something.
 Neither Ron nor Hermione heard him.  He tried again.  Hermione was
laughing at something Ron had said.  Harry sighed, then stood and walked
away.  Dracena watched him walk toward the edge of the room.  He found
an empty chair and sat down.

He stared at the dancers.  Dracena's smile faded.  She looked back at
her friends.  Pansy was still talking.  "...and honestly, the color was
dreadful."

Dracena sighed.  She'd spent the entire evening waiting for something
interesting to happen.  The most interesting person in the room was
sitting alone.  She looked at her glass.  The punch was rather good.
 She'd already had several.  Perhaps another wouldn't hurt.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

<span class="c4 c14"></span>

Harry had decided that the Yule Ball was overrated.  The decorations
were lovely.  The food was excellent.  The music was pleasant.  Ron and
Hermione were behaving like two people who had suddenly discovered that

Harry was happy for them, but it made him feel rather unnecessary.  He
watched them dancing.  Badly.  Ron stepped on Hermione's foot.  She
laughed, and he apologized.  Then she stepped on his.  They both
laughed.  Harry smiled.

*Good for them.  * Then he sighed.  The musicians announced the final
