"Potter!"

He looked up.  Dracena Malfoy stood before him.  She was smiling
broadly.

Harry blinked.  "Dracena?"

She held out her hand.  "Come on!"

Harry frowned.  "Where?"

She laughed.  "To dance, you idiot!"

Harry stared.  "With you?"

"No, Potter.  With the dementors!"

Harry smiled.  "All Right."

Harry noticed  Dracena was swaying slightly.  Her cheeks were flushed.
 Her eyes were unusually bright.

And she smelled...

Harry frowned.  "Dracena?"

"Hmm?"

"Are you all right?"

She beamed.  "I'm fiiiiine, Potter!"

Harry leaned closer and sniffed. "Blimey."

Dracena giggled.

Harry stared.  "You smell like a brewery."

She looked offended.  "Don't be ridiculous."

"Have you been drinking?"

Dracena waved a hand.  "Punch."

Harry frowned.  "Punch?"

"Delicious punch."  She smiled.  "Lots of punch."

Harry stared.

Dracena grabbed his hand.  "COME ON!"

"Dracena..."  

"LET'S DANCE!"  She pulled him toward the floor.

Harry stumbled as he tugged his arm.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Harry had never been particularly good at dancing.  Fortunately, Dracena
had.  Unfortunately, Dracena was currently having some difficulty
remembering which direction was forward.

"Potter."

"Yes?"

"Your hand."

Harry looked down.  "What about it?"

"Here."  She took his hand and placed it against her waist.

Harry went red.  "Oh."

Dracena smiled.  "Honestly.  Have you never danced before?"

"Not really."

"Well, you're learning tonight!"  She placed her other hand on his
shoulder.

"Now.  Follow me."  Harry tried, and stepped on her foot.

Dracena laughed. "Potter!"

"Sorry!"

"Other foot!"

"Right!"

"No, your *other*  other foot!"

Harry laughed.  Dracena laughed too.  They turned.  Slowly and
awkwardly.  Eventually, Harry began to catch on.  He found the rhythm
and adjusted his steps.

Dracena smiled.  "There."

Harry grinned.  "Not so difficult."

"Of course not."

They moved together.  The music was slow and gentle.  Harry relaxed.

Dracena leaned a little closer.  Her head was beginning to feel
pleasantly light.  The room seemed warmer.

Harry Potter was holding her.  That was nice.  Very nice.

She smiled.  "You're not terrible, Potter."

Harry laughed.  "High praise."

"Don't get used to it."

He smiled.

Dracena rested her head briefly against his shoulder.

Harry blinked.

They continued dancing.

"Harry?" Dracena said.

Harry looked at her

"Yeah?"

"I'm lonely."

Harry frowned.  "What?"

Dracena looked at him, her smile fading

"I'm lonely."

Harry stared.  "Dracena..."

She shook her head.  "Everyone thinks I'm so lucky."

Harry considered.  "Well..."

Dracena looked at him.  "Don't."

Harry smiled faintly.  "All right."

She continued.  "I have everything."

Harry nodded.  "Pretty much."

"Father gives me anything I want."

"I noticed."

She sighed.  "And everyone wants to be my friend."

Harry looked at her.

Dracena's eyes were shining.  "But nobody..."  She stopped, and Harry
waited.

"Nobody **knows**  me."

Harry frowned.  "What do you mean?"

Dracena looked away.  "They know Dracena **Malfoy** ."  She said,
emphasis on her last name.

Harry said nothing.

"They know Father's daughter."  She swallowed.  "They know what I'm
supposed to be.  They don't know **me** ."

Harry watched her.

"But sometimes..."  She looked down.  "Sometimes I don't know if that's
who I am."

Harry felt something tighten in his chest.  He tried to remember the
last time he heard her talk like this.  Was it back when the hippogriff
had terrified her?

"Dracena."

She looked up.

Harry smiled gently.  "I'm glad I've got my friends."

Dracena blinked.  "What?"

"Ron.  Hermione.  Neville."  He looked across the room.

Ron and Hermione were still dancing.  Quite Badly.

Harry smiled.  "They know me."

Dracena watched him.

"They don't care about the famous Harry Potter."  He laughed.  "Well,
Ron did at first."

Dracena smiled faintly.

"But now?"  Harry shook his head. "To them?  I'm just Harry."  He looked
at her.  "And that's brilliant."

Dracena stared.  Her eyes filled.  "I don't have anyone like that,
Harry."

Harry stopped smiling.  "Not Pansy?"

Dracena shook her head.

"Nobody in Slytherin?"

She looked down.  "They all expect me to be something."

Harry waited.

"And I don't know if I'm that."  Her voice cracked.  "I never got a
chance to really find out."

Harry didn't know what to say, so he simply held her a little closer.

Dracena closed her eyes.  For a moment, she let herself enjoy it.  The
warmth.  The music.  Harry's hand against her back.

It reminded her of something, a dream.  She was flying over beautiful
countryside, her arms around his waist.  Her cheek against his back.
 She smiled sadly.  "You're nice, Harry."

Harry blinked.  "What?"

Dracena looked up.  "Sometimes."

Harry laughed softly.  "Thanks.  I think."

She smiled.  "Don't tell anyone." She said, in an exaggerated whisper.

"Wouldn't dream of it."

They continued dancing.

"You know, I've always been a bit jealous of you." Harry said, idly.

Dracena blinked.  "Me?"

Harry nodded.  "Yeah."

She laughed.  "Potter, you have no idea how ridiculous that sounds."

Harry smiled.  "Probably."

Dracena looked at him.  "Why?"

"Your father."

Her expression changed.  "Father?"

Harry nodded.  "You talk about him all the time."

Dracena smiled.  "He's wonderful."

Harry looked down.  "I never knew mine."

Dracena's smile faded.

Harry continued.  "My parents, I mean."

She watched him.  They separated for a moment.  Harry reached inside his
robes.  He carried a small pouch beneath them.  From it, he carefully
withdrew an old photograph, and held it out.  "This is all I've got of

Dracena stared.  The photograph showed a young man and woman.  It was a
tattered, muggle style photograph.  James and Lily Potter.  They were
smiling.  Happy.

Dracena took the photograph.  Very carefully, she looked at it.  Really
looked.  The woman had Harry's eyes.  The man had his hair.  She stared
for a long time.  Something about how his mother looked at his father
caught her attention.

Harry smiled sadly.  "That's my mum."

Dracena said nothing.

"And my dad."

She looked up.  Harry was watching her.  For a moment, she felt
something enormous and complicated.  Harry had trusted her with this.
 Not just shown her.  Trusted her.  She looked back at the photograph.
 Her thoughts became pleasantly tangled.

James and Lily Potter smiled up at her, looking impossibly young.  His
mother had his eyes.  His father had that same untidy hair.

Dracena swallowed.  She'd never seen Harry look quite so vulnerable.
 She'd never FELT so vulnerable.  She'd spent the evening telling him
things she'd never told anyone.  Things about Father.  About the people
she knew.  About how lonely she sometimes felt.

And Harry had listened.  He hadn't laughed.  Hadn't made one of his
infuriating remarks.  Hadn't even called her a snob.  He'd simply
listened.  Now he'd shown her this.

Dracena looked up at him.  Harry smiled a small, sad smile.

Suddenly she desperately wanted him to smile properly, the way he did
when they were arguing.  The way he did when they were flying against
each other, laughing and shouting insults across the Quidditch pitch.
 That was much easier.  Much more comfortable.  She could do that.

Him smiling at her like this felt way too different.  She didn't know
how to handle it.

She looked up. "Harry."

He blinked.  "Yeah?"

"You shouldn't keep things like this just lying around." she smiled
widely.

Harry frowned.  "What?"

Dracena lifted the photograph.  "This."

Harry reached for it.  "Well, I keep it in my pouch..."

"That tatty little thing?"

Harry looked down.  "What's wrong with my pouch?"

Dracena smiled.  "Oh, nothing.  If you're a house-elf."

Harry laughed.  "There she is."

Dracena beamed.  There.  That was better.  She was much more comfortable
with this.

"Honestly, Potter, I don't know how you manage."

Harry grinned.  "Manage what?"

"Anything."

"Very funny."

"Thank you."

She looked at the photograph again, holding it just beyond his reach.

"I think I'll keep this."

Harry blinked.

"What?"

Dracena smiled mischievously.

She smiled.  "I'm keeping it."

Harry blinked.  "What?"  The smile on his face disappeared immediately.

"I'm con..." She frowned. "Consif..."

Harry stared.  

Dracena tried again.  "Confis... cating."  She smiled triumphantly.
 "Confiscating."

Harry's expression changed.  "Dracena."

She nodded. "Yes?"

"Give it back." Harry said, flatly.

She shook her head.  "No."

Harry stared.  "That's my parents."

Dracena smiled.  "I know."

"Then give it back."

She tucked the photograph against her chest.  "For chafe..."  She
frowned. "Safe."

Harry's jaw tightened.  "Dracena."

"Safekeeping."  She smiled.  "There."

Harry stared.  For a moment he couldn't believe what was happening.
 They'd been having such a nice evening.  They'd talked.  Really talked.
 He'd seen a side of Dracena he'd never seen before.  He'd shown her the
only photograph he had of his parents.  And now?  Now she was playing
another one of her stupid games.

"Give it back."

Dracena shook her head.  "Oh, Harry.  Honestly."

Harry's face hardened.  "Dracena, I'm serious."

"So am I."  She looked at the photograph.  "Very serious."

Harry stared.  Then he said quietly:  "...and I was having such a nice
time."

Dracena looked up.  Her face brightened.  "I'M STILL HAVING A GREAT
TIME!"

Harry recoiled.  Several nearby dancers glanced toward them.

Dracena laughed.

Harry's expression closed.  Of course.  Of course she'd do this.  He
should have known.

"Fine."  He said, flatly.

Dracena blinked, confused.  "What?"

"Whatever."  Harry stepped back.

She frowned.  "Harry?"

He looked at her.  "Have a good night, Malfoy."

Dracena's smile faltered.  "Harry?"

He turned and walked away without looking back.

Dracena stood in the middle of the dance floor, holding the photograph
and watched him go.

For a moment she didn't understand, then something twisted in her chest.
 Oh.  She looked down.  The photograph was still in her hand.  James and
Lily Potter smiled up at her.

Dracena swallowed.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

<span class="c4 c14"></span>

"WHO IS RESPONSIBLE FOR THIS?"  Professor McGonagall's voice cut through
the Great Hall.  Several students froze.  McGonagall stood beside the
refreshments table, holding a glass of punch.  Her expression was
thunderous.  "WHO HAS BEEN ADDING FIREWHISKY TO THE PUNCH?"

A group of older students abruptly discovered urgent business elsewhere.

McGonagall glared.  "COME BACK HERE!"

Dracena blinked.  Firewhisky?  She looked at her empty glass, then at
the punch bowl.  Then at the photograph in her hand.  "Oh."  Her stomach
lurched.  She belched, loudly.

A nearby Ravenclaw stared.

Dracena covered her mouth.  "Oh, Merlin."  She looked toward the doors.

Harry was gone.  She looked at the photograph again.

*What have I done?  * The room seemed to tilt.  Dracena swallowed.

She suddenly felt terribly sick.  She hurried toward the exit.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Dracena woke with a headache.  A dreadful headache.  Her mouth was dry.
 Her stomach felt peculiar, and the world seemed unnecessarily bright.

She groaned.  "Oh, Merlin."  She was in her bed, still wearing part of
her dress.  Someone had helped her out of her shoes.  She vaguely
remembered stumbling into the dormitory.  Pansy laughing at her.
 Someone telling her to drink water.

Dracena sat up.  Her head throbbed, as she looked around.  The other
girls were still awake, sitting together and  gossiping about the night.

Dracena looked down.  Something lay beside her pillow.  A photograph.
 James and Lily Potter.  She stared.  Then she remembered everything.

Harry smiling.  Harry dancing with her.  Harry holding her.  Harry
telling her about his parents.  Harry trusting her.

And then...

*I'm confiscating this.*

Dracena closed her eyes.  "Oh, no."  She remembered his face.  The
anger.  The disappointment.

*"And I was having such a nice time."*

Her eyes filled.  She'd ruined it.  She'd ruined everything.

Again.  

She picked up the photograph, holding it carefully, pressing it against
her chest.

"Idiot." she whispered.

Pansy looked over. "Dracena?"

Dracena stood.

"Where are you going?"  

"Bathroom."

Pansy frowned.  "Are you all right?"

Dracena nodded too quickly.  "Fine."

She hurried toward the door.

"Dracena..."

"I'm fine!"

She left before anyone could see her crying.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

The girls' bathroom was empty.

Well, almost empty.  Dracena pushed open the door.  The room was cold
and quiet.

Moonlight spilled through the windows.  She walked toward the sinks,
staring as her reflection stared back at her.

Her pale hair was tangled.  Her eyes red and bloodshot.  Her makeup was
smudged.  The magnificent dress Mother had commissioned was wrinkled.

Dracena looked dreadful.  She laughed bitterly.  "So much for the finest
dress in the castle."

A toilet flushed.  Dracena jumped.  A pale, ghostly figure rose slowly
from one of the stalls.

"Who's there?"  Dracena wiped her eyes.  

"Nobody." an ethereal voice responded.  Moaning Myrtle floated closer.
 "Oh."

She looked delighted.  "Someone's crying."

Dracena glared.  "Go away."

Myrtle drifted nearer.  "What's wrong?"

"Nothing."

------------------------------------------------------------------------

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## <span class="c12"></span>

## <span class="c12">The Bathroom</span>

<div class="illustration">

![The Bathroom](Images/The%20Bathroom.jpg)

</div>

Harry had several excellent reasons for being inside the Slytherin
girls' washroom after midnight.  None of them would have sounded nearly
as good if he got caught.

Dracena Malfoy had stolen his photograph.  Technically, according to
her, she had not stolen it; she had "confiscated" it.  It was an old
Muggle photograph of his parents, taken before he was born.  His mother
was laughing at something outside the frame while his father looked at
her instead of the camera.  Malfoy had stared at it for longer than
Potter expected.  Then she'd taken it.

That had been several hours ago at the party.  Which explained why Harry
was now creeping through the girls' washroom near the Slytherin dorms,
wearing his invisibility cloak.  He knew Malfoy secretly kept a little
silver case in the cabinet beside the sinks.  He knew this because she
had once complained at considerable length that someone had moved it.

Harry had called her insane.

Dracena had informed him that Gryffindors probably stored their
belongings in piles on the floor like livestock.

Harry had asked how she knew what his dormitory looked like.  She had
gone scarlet.

Harry smiled at the memory.

He didn't know what she kept in that case, but he remembered her
reaction when it went missing.  He could teach her a lesson, too.

He heard someone crying.  Harry stopped smiling.  This wasn't ordinary
crying, it was what Hermione once called "Ugly crying."  Someone was
absolutely sobbing.  Potter froze.  The sound came from around the
corner.

He should leave.  Every sensible part of him knew this immediately.  He
was invisible, after midnight, inside a girls' washroom where he
absolutely did not belong.  Leaving was the only remotely decent option.

"Oh, stop wailing.  You're making me depressed.  More than usual, I
mean."

Potter recognized the voice.  Moaning Myrtle.  The crying stopped for
several seconds, and another voice answered.  "Shut up, Myrtle."

Potter's heart stopped.  It was Dracena Malfoy.  He should absolutely
leave.  Immediately, if not sooner.  He took one silent step backward to
find the door, trying as hard as he could to insure the invisibility
cloak didn't move and expose him.

"I still don't see what the problem is." Myrtle said.

"You wouldn't."

"Of course I would.  I know all about boys."

"You died when you were fourteen." Malfoy said, matter of factly.

"Exactly.  I've had decades to study them."

Despite himself, Harry almost laughed.  Dracena did not.

"From the Bathroom?  Please.  You don't understand."

"Then explain it again." Myrtle said

"I've explained it three times."

"And you've cried four."

"Thank you, Myrtle."

"You're welcome."

Pregnant silence.  Harry was afraid to move, in case his footsteps
revealed him.

Harry knew he should leave, but was also terrified of accidentally
making a noise that could reveal him, so he remained exactly where he
was.

Malfoy sniffed.  "It's Potter."

Harry's stomach dropped.

"What about him?"

Harry heard a lonely drip from one of the washbasins.  It echoed
impossibly loud.

Malfoy said something so quietly Potter almost didn't hear it.

"I think I'm in love with him."

Harry forgot how legs worked.

Myrtle made a delighted squeal.  "Oh, I KNEW IT!"

"Shhh!"

"You LOVE Harry Potter!" Myrtle said, squealing in delight.

"Myrtle!"

"Malfoy and Potter, sitting in a..."

"If you finish that, I swear I will find out whether ghosts can be
exorcised."

Myrtle giggled.  Harry leaned against the stone wall, desperately trying
to remember how to walk.  His heart was pounding so loudly he became
genuinely concerned they might hear it.

"You've kissed him, haven't you?"

"No!" Malfoy screamed.  Harry's ears rang from the shout.

"You want to."

Malfoy looked directly at Myrtle, and said nothing.

"Ooooooh."  Myrtle squealed, delightfully.

"Myrtle."

"You do!"

"Of course I do!"  The words exploded out of her.  When she spoke again,
her voice cracked.  "That's the problem."

Harry's smile disappeared.

"I don't understand." Myrtle said.

"No.  You wouldn't."

"You keep saying that."

"You don't understand because your parents were Muggles!" Malfoy
snapped.

Myrtle went very still.  The wounded look on her face was clear.
 Dracena saw it.

"Oh, for...  No.  Myrtle, that isn't what I meant.  I said it badly."

Myrtle folded her arms.

"Very badly." Dracena said.

"Yes.  Fine.  Very badly."  Myrtle said.

Dracena took a breath.  "What I mean is... your parents didn't know
about any of this.  About our world.  Our history.  Our customs.  You
couldn't grow up with something they couldn't teach you."

Myrtle's expression softened slightly, although her arms remained
folded.

"That doesn't mean there was anything wrong with them." Dracena added
quickly. "Or with you.  I don't mean that."

"Then what do you mean?"

"I mean my mother sang wizarding songs to me when I was little.  My
grandfather taught me our family history before I could properly read.
 I knew the names of the old families before I knew half the names of
the countries in Europe."

Dracena sniffed.

"I grew up hearing stories about Hogwarts.  About Slytherin.  About
people whose portraits are hanging in my house.  There are things we do
at Christmas because Malfoys have done them for hundreds of years.
 There are things Mother owns that belonged to her grandmother, and
someday they'll belong to me."

Her voice became quieter.  "This isn't some club I joined, Myrtle."

Myrtle unfolded her arms.  

"It's where I came from." Dracena said, silently.

"Oh."

"That's what I mean when I say you wouldn't understand.  Not because
you're..." Dracena waved her hand helplessly. "Not because your blood is
wrong or any of that."

She grimaced and sighed, loudly

"I'm trying.  Myrtle.  I really am.  It's hard to change... something
you thought your  entire life."

"I know."

"And Potter makes it sound so easy.  Just decide this tradition is
stupid.  Decide that one is unfair.  Throw this away.  Stop believing
that.  And sometimes he's right, which is the absolutely intolerable
part."

Dracena sniffed again.

"People like Potter think I'm just supposed to decide Father is wrong
about everything.  That my family is wrong.  That everything I've been
taught since I could speak is stupid and evil and I'm supposed to throw
it away because Potter has those beautiful eyes and stupid hair and
smiles at me like an idiot."

Harry instinctively touched his hair.

"And his hair is stupid." Dracena added bitterly.

Harry  frowned.

Myrtle said, "I like his hair."

"Nobody asked you."

"I think it's romantic." She said, dreamily.

"It's ridiculous."

"You touch it."

"I do not." Malfoy said, stubbornly.

"I've seen you."

"When?"

"I float through walls, Dracena"

"That's horrifying."

Myrtle giggled again.

"My family matters to me." Dracena continued.  Something about her voice
made Potter straighten.

"My name matters to me.  Our history matters.  My grandfather used to
tell me stories about our family when I was little.  About things we've
accomplished.  Things we've survived.  Traditions that are hundreds of
years old."  Her voice trembled.  "Potter hears 'tradition' and thinks
it means 'I hate Granger.'"

Harry looked at the floor.

"Do you?"

"No!"  The answer came immediately.

"I don't know."

Potter frowned.

"I don't hate her.  She's irritating and smug and correct about things
far too often, but I don't hate her."

"So you're friends now?  Great!."

"Shut up."

"Do you think you're better than her?"

Another long silence.

"Father would say yes."

Harry stopped breathing.

"But me?  I don't know anymore."

Dracena's voice had become small.

"I don't know what I think anymore.  Do you have any idea how scary that
is, Myrtle?"

Myrtle was silent now.

"I know things Potter doesn't." Malfoy continued. "He acts as though
everything old is just some stupid rule somebody invented because they
were bored.  He doesn't understand what it's like to grow up in a
magical family.  He doesn't know the stories.  He doesn't know the
customs.  He doesn't know why some things matter."

Harry felt an unexpected sting, because she was right.  He'd entered
their world as a stranger and sometimes treated the parts he disliked as
though their age alone made them ridiculous.

Dracena sniffed.

"But then he asks me why.  And I hate him for it."  Myrtle said nothing.
 Potter's heart sank.  "Because sometimes I don't have an answer."

His heart rose again.

"Father says something, and I've always believed it because Father said
it.  Then Potter looks at me with those stupid beautiful eyes and asks,
'Why?'"

A tiny laugh escaped her through the tears.  "And sometimes I want to
hex him."

"Sometimes?"

"Most of the time."

"Ah."

"And sometimes I want to kiss him until he shuts up."

Harry's entire face turned bright red, and suddenly felt incredibly hot.

Myrtle sighed dreamily.  "That's much more romantic."

"It is NOT romantic!"

"Sounds romantic."

"It's horrible!"

Dracena started crying again, and suddenly Potter didn't find any of
this funny.

"I don't want to lose my family."  Dracena sniffled.  "I don't want
Father to look at me differently.  I don't want Mother ashamed of me.  I
don't want to become some cautionary story they whisper about at
Christmas."

Her breathing skipped.

"And I don't want to lose myself.  I like being a Malfoy."  She sounded
apologetic, lost.

"I like our stupid enormous house.  I like Mother's ridiculous formal
dinners.  I like knowing which fork to use.  I like the old songs.  I
like hearing Grandfather's stories.  I like our Christmas traditions.  I
like knowing my family has been part of this world for hundreds of
years.  I like knowing how we fit into the world."

Her voice broke.  "Why does everyone act as though being friendly with
 Potter means I'm supposed to hate all of that?"

Harry's chest hurt.

Myrtle, surprisingly, didn't answer immediately.  "Does Potter ask you
to?"

Dracena was silent.

"Has he ever asked you to?" Myrtle repeated

"No."  Dracena whispered.

"Then perhaps you're arguing with a ghost."

Dracena sniffed. "You're funny."

"What I mean is, perhaps you're arguing with something that isn't real.
 You think you know what's going to happen, but you really don't."

"What?"

"You keep telling me what Potter thinks you have to become." Myrtle
said.  Her voice had lost its usual theatrical whine.  "But have you
ever asked him?"

The washbasin faucet dripped somewhere in the background again

"No."

"Why not?"

"Because he'll say something noble and infuriating."

Harry smiled under the cloak.

"And then I'll love him more."

Harry's smile disappeared again.  He stood absolutely motionless, afraid
to breathe.

Dracena began crying again, exhausted little sobs  "I've tried not
talking to him.  "That lasted two days."

"Thirty-seven hours." Myrtle said.

"I remember.  "I tried being horrible to him."

"You gave him a Christmas present."

"It was a book."

"You wrapped it."

"Mother says presentation matters."

"You put a little silver ribbon on it."

"I LIKE RIBBONS, MYRTLE."  Dracena shouted.

Potter covered his mouth.  Myrtle was laughing, too.  Dracena was trying
not to.

"I stole his photograph."  she whispered, shame dripping from her voice.

Harry's eyebrows rose.

"Why?" asked Myrtle.

"I wanted to look at it."  Harry's anger evaporated so quickly it
embarrassed him.

"It's his parents."

"I know."

"They're dead."

"I know, Myrtle."

"That's rather cruel."

"I KNOW."  Dracena sounded miserable.

"I was going to give it back tomorrow."

"Why did you take it?"

Dracena was quiet.  "Because I wanted to understand him."

Potter's throat tightened.

"He looks like his father.  Everyone says so."  Another sniff.  "But he
looks at people like his mother."  

Harry's hand dropped from his mouth.

"I noticed it in the photograph."

Dracena's voice softened.

"She looks at his father the way Potter looks at people when he thinks
nobody's watching."

"How?"

"Like they're important."

Harry stared into nothing.

"He does it with Weasley.  Granger.  Longbottom.  I never would have
thought of them as important until Harry did."  She paused for a moment.

"Sometimes he looks at me that way, too." Dracena said, quietly.

Myrtle replied, just as quietly, "Maybe you are."

Dracena broke.  Harry heard her sob into her sleeves.  "I don't know
what to do."

For several moments there was nothing but crying.

"You could tell him."  Myrtle said.

"Absolutely not!"

"Why?"

"Because I'd rather die."

"I did that.  I don't recommend it."

Despite everything, Dracena laughed.  A broken, watery little laugh.

Harry looked toward the exit.  He could leave.  Perhaps he should.  He
needed to.  This wasn't meant for him.  He'd heard something Dracena
Malfoy would rather throw herself into the Black Lake than let him hear.

"What if he loves you too?"  Myrtle said, as if it was the simplest
thing in the world.

Dracena became completely silent.

"He doesn't."

"How do you know?"

"Because I'm horrible to him."

"Yes."

"Thank you."

"You're welcome."

"And because he thinks I'm a snob."

"You are a snob."

"Myrtle!"

"You are."

"SIGH.  Fine."

"And because you insult his friends."

"I've been trying not to."

Harry blinked.  He thought back.  He couldn't remember the last time
Dracena called Ron "poor."  It must have been several weeks.  Similarly
he couldn't remember the last time she called Hermione "the Muggle-born
mudblood."  Harry never would have noticed.

"And because." Dracena continued, "even if he does..."  She couldn't
finish.

Myrtle waited.

Dracena finally whispered:  "What if loving each other isn't enough?
 We're so different!"

Harry felt that one, because for the first time that night, she had
asked the question he'd been avoiding himself.  Attraction was easy.
 Flirting was easy.  Arguing in corridors and pretending not to enjoy it
was easy.  Even kissing her (something he had spent an increasingly
unreasonable amount of time imagining) would be easy.  Everything
afterward was difficult.  It wasn't like the movies.

Their friends.\
Their families.\
Their houses.\
Their ideas about the world.\
The parts of Dracena Malfoy that Harry would have actually wanted her to
change.\
The parts he slowly realized that she shouldn't have to.\
And, perhaps most frighteningly, the possibility that she might ask the
same of him.

"Maybe it isn't." Myrtle said.

Dracena sniffed.  "You're terrible at this."

"I've never had a boyfriend."  Myrtle said, quietly.

"Despite studying boys from the bathroom?  Clearly."

"But maybe..."

Myrtle hesitated.  "Maybe loving somebody doesn't solve the problem."

Dracena was silent.  Myrtle continued, "Maybe it just makes the problem
worth solving."

Harry stared at the stone floor.

For once in her afterlife, Moaning Myrtle had said something
astonishingly wise.  

"That's disgustingly sentimental." Dracena said.

"I thought it was rather good."

"It was awful."

"You're going to remember it forever."

"I hate you."

"No, you don't."

"No."  Malfoy sighed.  "I don't."

Harry quietly backed toward the door.  He had forgotten entirely about
the photograph.  He didn't want it back anymore.  He had reached the
doorway.

"Myrtle?"

"Yes?"

"If you ever tell Potter any of this, I'll deny everything."

"Of course."

"And if he somehow happened to be standing under a cloak of
invisibility, in the washroom, listening to us?" Myrtle looked right at
him.  Harry froze.

There was a terrible silence.  Dracena stopped breathing.  Harry stopped
breathing.  Then Myrtle shrieked with laughter.  "I'm joking!"

"Myrtle!"

Dracena threw something.  It passed directly through the ghost and
shattered against the wall.

Harry slipped through the doorway before his heart could explode.  He
made it halfway down the corridor before his head felt like it would
shatter.  Slowly, Harry reached beneath the cloak and touched his
crooked tie.  For months, he realized he'd been trying to decide whether
Dracena was someone he could love.  Despite his anger, earlier.  Maybe
his anger came from the fact he knew the same things, and refused to
face them.  He had never seriously considered the other question.   He
suddenly realized how brave Dracena was for admitting it to Myrtle.

Could he love Dracena without requiring her to stop being Malfoy?

Potter looked back toward the washroom.

"Oh, no." he whispered.  He realized exactly what his thoughts meant.
 This was much worse than he'd thought.  He was in love with her, too.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## <span class="c12"></span>

## <span class="c12">The Apology</span>

<div class="illustration">

![The Apology](Images/The%20Apology.jpg)

</div>

Hermione knew something was wrong when Dracena Malfoy asked if she could
sit down.  Dracena usually did not ask permission to sit down.  She sat,
and somehow the furniture understood that was how it was going to be.

Hermione lowered her book.  "What do you want?"

Dracena remained standing.  "I want to talk to you."

"That sounds fun."

Dracena sighed.

"Usually when you want to talk to me, you begin by explaining something
I already know." Hermione said, coldly.

Dracena's mouth tightened.  Hermione waited for the inevitable retort,
but it never came.  Instead Dracena pulled out the chair opposite her
and sat.  Properly, of course.  Dracena could sit properly during an
earthquake.

"I owe you an apology."

Hermione blinked.  "I'm sorry?"

"I said I owe you an apology."

"No, I heard you.  I just thought perhaps I'd suffered a head injury."

Dracena's eyes narrowed.  "Granger..."

"There she is." Hermione said, sharply.

"I am trying."

Hermione closed her book, and looked at Dracena.  "All right."

Dracena placed both hands in her lap.  For several seconds she said
nothing.  Hermione realized that this wasn't just difficult for Dracena
in the ordinary sense.  It was agonizing.

"I've said things about you." Dracena began.

"Yes."

"And about your family."

"Yes."

"And Muggles."

"Yes."

"You needn't make an inventory."

"You brought it up."

Dracena inhaled through her nose.

"Yes.  Fine."  She stared at her hands.  "I was wrong."

Hermione said nothing.

"Not about everything."

Hermione's eyebrow rose.

Dracena winced.  "That came out badly."

"Remarkably."

"I mean I'm not repudiating everything I've ever believed simply
because..."

"Because of Harry?"

Dracena's head snapped up.

"This has nothing to do with Potter."

"Of course."

"It doesn't."

"Dracena."

The use of her first name stopped her.  Hermione rarely used it.

Dracena looked away.  "It isn't **only**  because of Potter."

That, Hermione thought, was probably as much honesty as she was going to
get.  "All right."

Dracena took another breath.  "I've been thinking about things."

"Ooh, that's dangerous."

"Would you please stop doing that?"

"Doing what?"

"Making this harder."

Hermione saw something unexpectedly vulnerable in her expression.  She
softened.  "Sorry."

Dracena nodded.

"I realized I don't actually know very much about Muggles."

Hermione nearly laughed.  

Dracena glared at her.  "I said I was sorry."

This was almost as hard for Hermione as it was for Dracena.  She would
take that to the grave, though.

"I know the important things, obviously."

Hermione's smile vanished.  "The important things." she repeated,
coldly.

Dracena apparently didn't notice.

"Some history.  Muggle governments.  Their relationship with wizarding
society.  Obviously Muggles don't have magic, so technologically they're
rather..."

Dracena stopped.  Hermione stared at her.

"Rather what?" she repeated, sharply.

Dracena knew she'd stepped into something, but didn't know what.  She
continued, knowing she was already in trouble.

"Limited."

Hermione slowly put her book on the table.

"Limited."

"I don't mean stupid."

"Oh, well thank you.  How generous."

"I mean there are things Muggles simply can't do."

"You mean, like Magic."

"Exactly."

"And therefore we're backward."

"I didn't say backward!"

"You were about to."

"I said limited."

"Dracena, how did you get to France last summer?"

The change of subject bewildered her.

"What?"

"France.  Your family got there somehow.  How did you travel there?"

"Portkey."

"How long did it take?"

"I don't know.  Several seconds."

"Fine.  How would a Muggle get there?"

Dracena shrugged.  "A ship?"

Hermione stared at her.

"A ship?"

Dracena's uncertainty increased.

"Yes?"

"Dracena, Muggles routinely fly across oceans."

Silence.

Dracena smiled.  It was a tiny, condescending smile.  Hermione
recognized it immediately.

"No, they don't."

"Yes, they do."

"Without magic?" Dracena said, skeptically.

"Yes."

Dracena looked almost offended by the proposition.

"How?"

"Airplanes."

"What is an airplane?"

Hermione stared at her.  Dracena stared back.  Suddenly Hermione
understood the scale of the problem.

"You genuinely don't know, do you?"

"Know what?"

Hermione leaned forward.

"We build machines that fly."

Dracena frowned.

"Like balloons?  I've seen..."

"No.  Hundreds of people at once.  Hundreds of miles an hour.  Miles
above the ground."

Dracena's expression went flat..

"No.  I don't..."

"Yes."

"Without magic?"

"Without magic."

"How?"

"Engines.  Aerodynamics.  Lift.  It's hard to explain.  It's almost its
own type of magic."

Dracena stared.  "Muggle magic?"

"Yes." Hermione said.  "It's called 'science.'"

Dracena was silent.  Hermione could actually see her trying to fit this
information into her understanding of reality.

"How many?"

"How many what?  Airplanes?"

"Yes."

"Thousands in the air every day."

Dracena stared at her.

"Thousands?"

"Yes."

"At the same time?"

"Yes."

"How do they not crash into one another?"

Hermione smiled despite herself.  "We muggles are quite clever."

Dracena looked down.  The remark had landed harder than Hermione
intended.  

"I didn't mean..."  Hermione started.

Dracena swallowed.

She felt suddenly guilty.  Dracena stared at the table.  "What else?"

"What?"

"What else can Muggles do that I don't know about?"

Hermione considered her.  "Quite a lot."

"Such as?"

"Computers."

"I've heard of those."

"What do you think they do?"

"Accounting."

Hermione laughed.

Dracena went pink.

"Some of them do a lot more."

"What else?"

Hermione thought about it.

"We've been to the Moon."

Dracena blinked.  "What?"

"The Moon."

"Yes, I heard you."

"Muggles have walked on it."

Dracena stared at her.

Hermione watched disbelief become suspicion, then astonishment.

"You're lying."

"No."

"People have stood on the Moon."

"Yes."

"When?"

"1969.  Before we were born."

"That's absurd."

"It happened."

"How did they breathe?"

"They brought air with them."

"How did they get there?"

"Rockets."

"What is a..."  Dracena stopped herself.  She looked exhausted.  "Never
mind.  Continue."

Hermione almost smiled.  "Nuclear power."

Dracena's brow furrowed.

"We learned how to release energy stored inside atoms."

Dracena's expression became very still.

"Inside matter?"

"Yes."

"How much energy?"

Hermione hesitated.  "Enough that explaining the other thing we did with
it probably isn't going to improve your opinion of us."

Dracena considered that. "War?"

"War."

"I think I heard about that.  Japan."

For the first time, they both smiled.  It vanished quickly.

Dracena looked toward the window.

"I didn't know."

"No."

"I thought..."  She stopped.

Hermione waited.

"I thought Muggles were still using candles.  Like we do.  Only without
magic."

Hermione's mouth fell open.

"You thought what?"

"Well, not candles exclusively."

"Oh, splendid."

"Granger..."

"Did you think my parents churned butter before sending me to Hogwarts?"

"No!"

"Perhaps we gathered berries in the forest?"

"I said I didn't know!"

Dracena stood.  Hermione immediately regretted her outburst.  Dracena's
face turned red.  "I'm trying to apologize to you!"

"You're doing an astonishing job."

"I KNOW!"

Her voice cracked.

Hermione stopped.

"I know." Dracena said, softly.  "I don't know how to do this."
 Dracenia looked horrified.  Her voice was suddenly tiny.  "I know I'm
doing this badly."

Hermione said nothing.

Dracena sat again.  The perfect posture was gone.

"I don't know how to do this."  she said, softly.  Hermione saw
vulnerability for just a moment.  It was quickly gone.  She wiped
angrily beneath one eye.

"I've never had to question any of it before."

"Dracena..."

"No.  Please."

Hermione stopped.

"If you say something now, I'll lose my nerve."

Dracena's hands were trembling.

"I grew up knowing exactly how the world worked.  Wizards had magic.
 Muggles didn't.  We protected ourselves from them.  We had knowledge
they couldn't possibly understand.  We had traditions older than their
countries.  And everyone I trusted told me what that meant."

A tear slipped down her cheek.  She looked furious about it.

"Then I came here."

Hermione watched her.

"And **you**  knew things I didn't."

More tears.

"You were better at spells than I was."  Dracena laughed miserably. "Do
you have any idea how much I hated you for that?"

"I had some indication." Hermione said, neutrally.

"I thought there had to be some reason it didn't count."

Hermione's expression changed.  Dracena couldn't look at her.

"Because if it counted, then maybe other things counted too."

She wiped her face.

"And then Potter..."

"There it is."

"Please don't."

Hermione immediately fell silent, again, regretting the outburst.

Dracena's composure finally collapsed.   She began weeping.

"He keeps asking me why."

Hermione knew exactly what she meant.

"And I don't know anymore.  I don't know which things my family taught
me because they're true and which things they taught me because their
parents taught them.  I don't know which traditions are beautiful and
which ones are cruel.  I don't know what I'm supposed to keep."

Hermione felt the ice inside her melt.  Just slightly.

"And every time I try to talk to you, I say something awful."

"That is something of a talent."  Hermione instantly regretted it.  Old
habits are hard to break.

Dracena gave her a watery glare.

Hermione smiled faintly.

Malfoy's face crumpled again.

"Please."

Hermione had never heard Malfoy say that word quite like this.

"Please, Granger."  She reached across the table, stopped herself, then
pulled her hand back.  "Give me another chance."

Hermione stared at her.

"I know I don't deserve one."

"Dracena..."

"I am sorry."  She could barely get the words out.  "I am so terribly
sorry."

Hermione's anger evaporated.

"I'm going to say stupid things again."

That surprised Hermione.

Dracena laughed through her tears.  "I know myself well enough to
promise you that.  I'm going to assume something.  Or say something
Father taught me.  Or ask you a question that's insulting and not
realize it until you've already hexed me."

"I probably won't hex you."

"Thank you."

"Probably."

Dracena almost laughed.

"Just... tell me."

Hermione frowned.  "Tell you?"

"When I'm doing it."

"You want me to correct you?"

"I will almost certainly hate it."

"That sounds healthy."

"I'll probably argue."

"Even healthier."

"And perhaps afterward I'll think about it."

Hermione looked at her for a long moment.  "All right."

Dracena stared at her.  "All right?"

"One more chance."

The relief on Dracena's face was startling. "Thank you."  Dracena said,
relief swimming over her.

Hermione nodded.

Dracena wiped her face and began reconstructing her dignity one piece at
a time.  It was almost impressive.

"So what are these wonderful traditions you're so worried about losing?"
 Hermione opened her mouth, and immediately regretted it again.

Dracena stopped.

"What?"

"I mean, you've mentioned them often enough.  Your family traditions.
 Ancient wizarding culture."  Hermione knew she made a mistake, but
didn't know how to stop.

"Yes."

"I suppose I've always assumed it was mostly..." Hermione waved her
hand.

"Mostly what?"

"Old families congratulating themselves for being old families."  She
internally winced.  She had an idea of what Dracena was going through
now.

Dracena became completely motionless.

Hermione heard her own words a second time, and winced.

"Oh."

"Yes."

"I didn't mean..."

"Didn't you?"

The tears were gone.  That somehow made it worse.

Dracena stood.

"Dracena, wait."

"No.  It's fine.  I just spent half an hour apologizing for assuming an
entire civilization was primitive because I knew nothing about it."

Hermione flushed.

"That isn't the same."

Dracena flinched.  Hermione saw it.  She actually saw the instant those
words struck her.  Dracena's jaw tightened.

Hermione prepared herself.

*Here comes the explosion.*  she thought.

But none came.  Dracena closed her eyes.  Her fists clenched at her
sides.  She breathed in.

Once.\
Twice.\
When she opened her eyes, they were wet again. "No." she said carefully.
"It isn't exactly the same."

Hermione relaxed slightly.

"But perhaps it's close enough that you could listen before deciding."

Hermione's mouth opened.  The sharp response was already there.  But
this time she stopped.

Dracena was shaking.  Just enough that Hermione could see how hard she
was working not to become the person she'd been five minutes earlier.

Hermione closed her mouth.  "All right."

Dracena blinked.  "What?"

"I said all right.  Tell me."   Hermione gestured toward the chair.
 "Tell me."

Dracena didn't sit.

"About what?"

"Something I don't know."

That stopped Dracena.

Hermione leaned back.  "You said I don't understand your culture."

"You don't."

There was no malice in it this time.

Hermione nodded.  "Then explain it."

Dracena slowly sat down.  For a while she said nothing.

"Do you know why old wizarding houses have family tapestries?"

Hermione shrugged.

"Genealogy.  Obsession with bloodlines."

Dracena winced.  Hermione did, too.

"Partly." Dracena said.

Hermione caught herself.  "Sorry."

Dracena looked surprised, then continued.

"They're also memorials."

Hermione frowned. "To whom?"

"Everyone."  Dracena's voice softened.  "Not merely important people.
 Children who only lived three days.  Cousins nobody remembers.
 Squibs."

Hermione looked surprised.  "Squibs are included?"

"In ours."  Dracena hesitated.  "Some families removed them.  Mine
doesn't."  Something in her tone suggested this distinction mattered
greatly to her.  "When someone dies, their thread changes.  There are
charms woven into it.  You can touch their name and sometimes hear their
voice."

Hermione sat forward.  "What?"

"Only a few words.  Usually."

Dracena was smiling now.

Not the Malfoy smirk.  An actual smile.

"My great-great-grandmother laughs."

Hermione said nothing.

"I don't know what she's laughing at.  Nobody does anymore.  But if you
touch her name, she laughs."

Dracena looked down at her hands.  "When I was little, I used to sneak
downstairs and touch it."

Hermione found herself smiling.  "Why?"

"I liked her laugh."

Something passed between them.  Hermione thought of photographs.
 Recordings.  All the ways Muggles desperately tried to keep pieces of
the dead.

"That isn't what I thought the tapestries were."

"I know."

Dracena looked at her.

"Are there other things like that?"

Dracena hesitated.  "Yes."

"Would you show me?"

The question plainly startled her.  "Why?"

"Because I don't know."  Hermione gave her a rueful smile.  "And
apparently I've been forming opinions anyway."

Dracena stared at her.

Then the corner of her mouth lifted.

"That does sound familiar."

"Don't push it."

"I wouldn't dream of it."

"So." Dracena said, "you really went to the Moon?"

"We did."

"How?"

Hermione smiled.

"That may take a while."

"I have time."

"And I want to know how that tapestry works."

"That may also take a while."

"I, too, have time."

They looked at one another.

It had been, by any reasonable measure, a disaster.

Dracena stood.  "I should go."

"Yes."

She gathered her things.

At the door, Hermione spoke.

"Dracena?"

Dracena turned.

"Next time you don't know something about Muggles..."

"Yes?"

"Ask me."

Dracena nodded.

"Next time you don't know something about us..."

Hermione understood.

"I'll ask you."

Dracena nodded again.

"Goodnight, Granger."

"Goodnight, Malfoy."

Dracena reached the door.

"Dracena?"

She looked back.

Hermione smiled.

"We don't use candles."

Dracena's eyes narrowed.

"I hate you."

"I know."  

They grinned at each other.

The door closed.

Hermione opened her book.  She read the same sentence four times without
understanding a word of it.  Finally she closed it again.

"Voices in tapestries." Hermione murmured.

She desperately wanted to know how that worked.

Halfway down the corridor, Dracena stopped beneath a torch.  She looked
up at the flame.

"Airplanes." Dracena whispered.  "Hundreds of people.  Flying.  Without
a single spell."

She desperately wanted to know how that worked.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## <span class="c12"></span>

## <span class="c12">The Bruise</span>

<div class="illustration">

![The Bruise](Images/The%20Bruise.jpg)

</div>

The three of them were laughing.  That, by itself, would have been
unimaginable months earlier.  Hermione was sprawled sideways in one of
the armchairs near the fire, an enormous book forgotten open across her
lap.  Harry sat on the rug with his back against the sofa.  Dracena
occupied the sofa itself in a position she would have described as
dignified, despite having one foot tucked beneath her and tears
streaming down her face.

"I did not say that."

"You absolutely did." Hermione said.

"I said they were aesthetically offensive."

"You said automobiles were 'hideous little Muggle carriages that smell
of explosions.'"

"They do!"

Potter was laughing too hard to speak.  Dracena pointed accusingly at
him.

"And you agreed with me!"

"I said some of them are ugly."

"You showed me the one with the wood stuck to the sides!"

"The station wagon?"

"Yes!"

Hermione dissolved into laughter again.  Dracena tried to maintain her
outrage for perhaps three seconds before losing the battle and laughing
with them.

"I hate both of you."

"No, you don't." Harry said.

"I am reconsidering."

She wiped the tears from beneath her eyes.  Hermione stopped laughing.
 Harry noticed Hermione first.  He followed her gaze.  The laughter died
in his throat.  Dracena was still smiling.

"What?"  Dracena's smile faded.  "What?"

Hermione slowly closed the book.

"Dracena."

Something in her voice made Dracena sit straighter.

"What?"

"How did you get that bruise?"

Dracena's hand rose automatically toward her face.  Her fingers found
the place where she'd wiped away the makeup.  She froze.  Harry could
see it now, a dark purple mark beneath her left eye, extending across
the top of her cheek.

Dracena stood abruptly.  "It's nothing."

Hermione's expression hardened.  "That isn't nothing."

"I walked into something."

"Dracena."

"I said it's nothing."

She reached for her bag.

Harry stood.

"Dracena."

"I'm tired.  I'm going to bed."

"Who hit you?"  Harry blurted out.

Nobody moved.  Dracena's face became utterly blank.  Harry had seen that
expression before.  It was the "Malfoy face."  The one she wore when she
wanted the entire world locked outside.

Hermione rose.  "Dracena..."

"Hermione." Dracena said, coldly.

Harry looked at her.  Hermione looked back.

"Could you give us a minute?"

"Harry, this..."

"Please." Harry said, desperately.

Hermione's eyes went to Dracena.  Dracena was staring at the floor.
 Hermione hesitated, then she walked over to her.  "You know where I'll
be."

Dracena nodded once.  Hermione squeezed her arm.  Dracena didn't
respond.  Hermione collected her books and left.

The door closed.

Harry didn't move toward Dracena.  He desperately wanted to.  Instead he
waited.  Dracena remained standing beside the sofa.

"Father hit me."

Harry felt something hot and violent ignite behind his ribs.  Quashed it
as best he could.  He knew anger wasn't what Dracena needed.  He
desperately worked to display the calm he didn't feel.

"Why?"

She gave a tiny, bitter laugh.  "Muggles."

Harry waited, and listened, desperately working his anger into his feet.

"I was telling him what Hermione taught me."

"About what?"

"Airplanes."

Harry blinked.  "You didn't know about airplanes?"

Dracena closed her eyes.  "Harry, please."

"Sorry."

She sat.  Harry sat beside her, leaving space between them.

"I was telling him about them.  And computers.  And that Muggles went to
the Moon."  Her mouth twitched.  "I still think Granger may be lying
about that."

"She's not."

"Yeah, I know."  The smile vanished.  "I told Father I'd been wrong
about them."

Harry's stomach tightened.

"He asked where I'd learned all this."  She swallowed.  "I told him."

Harry knew what came next, but stayed silent.

"He became angry."  Dracena touched her cheek.  "He said hanging around
with Mudbloods was beneath a Malfoy."

Potter's fists clenched, and Dracena noticed.

"Don't."

"I'm not doing anything."

"You're thinking very loudly."

Potter forced his hands open. "Then he hit you." Harry said, with a
neutrality he didn't feel.

"Yes."

"Has he done it before?"

"No.  No.  Never.  He was furious though." Dracena said. "I've never
seen him like that."

Harry stared into the fire, working overtime to maintain the calm
composure he didn't feel.  There were perhaps a hundred things he wanted
to say.  Most of them were terrible.

*Your father's a bastard* .\
No.\
*You can't go home* .\
Absolutely not.\
*You have to tell someone.\*
Maybe.  Eventually.

He remembered a washroom and a girl crying because everyone seemed to
think Harry required her to hate everything she'd ever loved before him.
 He remembered Hermione and Dracena sitting together for hours
afterward, alternately arguing and explaining things to one another.  He
remembered Dracena trying to explain that family wasn't simply a
collection of people she happened to share blood with.

It was her history.\
Her culture.\
Her place in the world.\
And her father was part of that.\
Harry chose his words carefully.  "There's a Muggle term for this."

Dracena looked at him.  "Of course there is."

"Domestic abuse."

Her brow furrowed.  "What does that mean?"

"Someone hurting someone in their family.  A husband hitting his wife.
 A parent hurting a child.  Severe mistreatment.  Things like that."

Dracena's expression changed.  "I'm not a child."

"You're sixteen."

"That's old enough to..."

"Dracena."

She stopped.  "In the Muggle world, what your father did can be a
crime."

Her eyes widened.  "A crime?"

"Yes."

"For striking his own daughter?"

"Especially his own daughter."

Dracena looked genuinely bewildered.  "It's not... acceptable for us
either."

Potter waited.  "But?"

She looked uncomfortable.  "It's private."

"No."

Her eyes flashed.

"Harry..."

"No."

"Don't tell me..."

"I'm not."  His voice remained quiet.  "I'm telling you what *I*
 believe."

Dracena looked away. "In old families, these things are handled within
the family."

"And what happens if the person hurting you is the head of the family?"
 Harry asked, his anger slowly seeping into his voice.

She didn't answer.  Harry immediately wished he'd phrased it
differently.  Dracena's eyes filled again.

"I never thought he'd hit me."

Harry's anger disappeared beneath something much sadder.  "That's
unfortunately how it works sometimes."

She looked at him.  "How do you know?"

Harry hesitated.

"Did your Aunt and Uncle...?"

"Sort of."

"How..."

"They kept me in a cupboard under the stairs."

Dracena looked horrified.

"They gave Dudley, their son, two rooms.  One for all his toys.  Another
for his bedroom."

"Oh Merlin."

"They never hit me, or anything like that." Harry added, quickly.  "But
they...  It's difficult.  They had a difficult relationship with my
parents.  And I think it spilled out onto me."

Dracena looked at Harry.

"It's the reason why I left with Hagrid, despite being terrified."

"So you think I should leave my family?" Dracena asked, fear in her
voice.

Harry didn't know what to say.  Exactly the place Potter had desperately
hoped not to end up.

"No."

She looked at him sharply.

"No?"

"I think you're sixteen."

"But you just said..."

"I know what I said."

Harry rubbed his forehead.  "I'm making a mess of this."

"You're doing better than I did with Hermione." Dracena said, bitterly.

That almost made him laugh.  Almost.

Dracena's voice broke.  "But Harry, I'm only sixteen."

"I know."

"What am I supposed to do?  Get a job?  Find a house?  Disown my own
father?"

"No."

"Then what?"

"I don't know."

She stared at him.  Harry hated those words, but they were true.  "I
don't know yet."

Dracena looked down.  Harry stared into the fire.

"I know your family is important to you."

She didn't answer.

"Your heritage is important to you."

Dracena remained silent.

"And I've been trying to understand that."

"I know."

"I really am."

"I know."

"But..."

He stopped.

Every version of the sentence sounded wrong.

*But your father...\
But your traditions...\
But your family...*

All of them turned this into a choice.  Harry didn't want to make her
choose.  Then he looked at the bruise again.  He realized there was one
thing he could say.

"But you're important to me."

Dracena looked up.  Harry swallowed.  "Merlin knows why." he muttered.

"What?"

"Nothing."

"I heard you."

"Of course you did."

Despite herself, her mouth twitched.

Harry became serious again.  "I want to understand your family.  Your
traditions.  All of it.  I want you to teach me."

Dracena stared at him.

"And there are things I'm probably never going to understand."  He took
a breath.  "But some things aren't complicated for me."

Her eyes flicked toward him.

"A father hurting his daughter because he's angry is one of them."

"Harry..."

"Dracena, please.  Let me finish first."  His voice wasn't loud.  It
didn't need to be.  "I can't let that go."

Dracena's expression hardened defensively.

"He's still my father."

"I know."

"I love him."

"I know."

"He has been good to me my entire life."

"I believe you."

"You don't sound like you do."

"I do."

Harry turned toward her.

"And he hit you."

Dracena flinched.  Harry immediately softened.

"I'm sorry."

She wiped her eyes.

"He made a mistake."

"Maybe."

"Maybe?"

"Maybe it was one terrible mistake he'll regret for the rest of his
life."  Harry hoped so.

"Then what do you want?"

"I want to make sure it never happens again."

"How?"  Dracena pleaded.

"I don't know."

Again.  Those horrible, honest words.  Dracena began crying.

"I don't know either."

Harry moved closer, almost touching her.  "I care about you, Dracena."
 he spat out, unexpectedly.  He immediately regretted it.  Almost.  She
looked at him.

"I wouldn't want this to happen to anyone.  Hermione.  Ron.  Neville.
 Someone I've never met."

His voice caught.  "But you?"

Dracena's lower lip trembled.

"Especially you."

That did it.  Whatever remained of Dracena's dignity simply
disintegrated.  She made an awful little choking noise and threw herself
at him, wrapping her arms around him as she wept.  Harry barely caught
her.

Dracena buried her face against his chest and sobbed.  Not the
picturesque single tear she would have tolerated in public.  She
ugly-cried.  Her shoulders shook.  Her breathing came in ragged gasps.
 She grabbed fistfuls of Potter's jumper and held on as though someone
might pull her away.  Harry wrapped both arms around her and held her.

"It's all right."

"No, it isn't."

"No"  Harry corrected himself.  "No, it isn't."

She cried harder.

Harry rested his cheek against her hair.  After a while, Dracena
whispered:

"Why do you care so much?"

Harry closed his eyes.  There were moments when a person could still
retreat.  This was one of them.

He could make a joke.\
He could say, *Because we're friends.* \
He could tell her *this is what anyone would do* .\
All technically true.\
All cowardly.\
Harry decided he wasn't going to be a coward.

"Because I'm in love with you."

Dracena stopped crying.  Instantly.  Harry immediately wished for death.

She slowly pulled away.

"What?"

"I said..."

"I heard you."

"Oh."

"You love me."

"Apparently."

"Apparently?"

"I'm having a difficult evening."

Dracena stared at him.

"How long?" she said, simply.

"What?"

"How long have you known?"

Harry considered it. "I don't know."

"Those words are helpful."

"I think I've been doing it for longer than I've known I was doing it."

Dracena's eyes filled again.

"Oh, don't cry again."

"I'm not!"

"You are."

"Shut up."

Harry smiled a bit, and  Dracena punched him weakly in the shoulder.
 Then she grabbed the front of his jumper again.  

She looked directly into his eyes.  "I love you too."

Potter forgot every intelligent thought he'd ever had.

"What?"

"Oh, don't make me say it again."

"I think you should."

"Harry."

"Just to make sure."

She glared at him through smeared mascara.

"I love you."

Harry smiled at her.  Dracena's face crumpled again.

"Oh, damn you."

They kissed each other.  It wasn't elegant.  It was messy and awkward.
 Their noses bumped.  Potter's hand went somewhere completely useless
before he figured out where to put it.

Dracena was still crying.  Then Harry was laughing.  Then she was
laughing and crying simultaneously.  When they separated, their
foreheads remained together.

"This is an absolute disaster." Dracena whispered.

"Yeah."

"My father is going to hate this."

Potter's smile disappeared.  Dracena saw it.  Reality came flooding
back.

Her family.

His friends.

The things she believed.

The things he couldn't accept.

The war everyone could feel somewhere beyond the castle walls, still
distant but getting closer.

Dracena looked frightened again.

"And if we can't make this work?"

"Dracena, don't."

"Seriously.  If we can't?"  Her voice was smaller now.  "I don't want to
lose you."

Harry opened his mouth.  He desperately wanted to say, "You won't."  The
words were right there.  Easy.  Two words, and the fear would leave her
face.  He'd said things like that before.  He meant them, too.  As if
wanting something badly enough made it a promise.  But it never worked.

He closed his mouth.  Dracena noticed.

"You can't say it."

"I want to."

Somehow that seemed to hurt her more.

"That's not what I asked."

"I know."

She looked away.  Potter wanted to take it back.  He wanted to say the
easy thing and deal with whether it was true later.

"I don't know."

She looked at him again.

"I don't know what's going to happen.  I don't know what you're going to
do.  I don't know what I'm going to have to do."

Her jaw tightened.

"You mean my father.  You mean..."

Harry didn't answer.

"Say it."

"Your father."

For a moment she looked furious, then her face dropped.  The fear was
evident

"I love him."

"I know."

"He's wrong."

"I know."

"Those can both be true, Potter."

"I know."

"Stop saying that."

"Then stop saying things I know."

A tiny, furious laugh escaped her before she could stop it.  Then it was
gone.

"I don't know what I'll do."

Harry swallowed.  "Neither do I."

"That's a bloody awful foundation for a relationship."

"It could be worse."

That almost got another laugh.  Almost.

"So that's it?" she asked. "We don't know?"

Harry shook his head.

"No."

"No?"

"We don't lie about not knowing."

Dracena stared at him.  Harry took her hand.  "I can't promise you that
everything will work out."  She looked down at their fingers.

"I can't promise your father will accept me.  Or Hermione.  Or that
you'll agree with me about everything someday."

"God forbid."

"Exactly."

That earned him the faintest smile.

"And I can't promise I'm never going to ask something from you that you
can't give me."

Her smile disappeared.

"Neither can I."

"I know."

She squeezed his hand.

Potter looked at the bruise on her face.

"But I can promise you something else."

"What?"

"If I don't know, I'll tell you I don't know."

Dracena's eyes filled.

"If I'm frightened, I'll tell you I'm frightened.  If I'm angry, I'll
try to tell you why before I do something stupid."

"Try?"

"I'm Harry Potter, The Boy Who Lived, not a miracle worker."

She snorted.

"And if something you believe matters to you, I'll listen before I
decide it's stupid."

"That's an improvement."

"And if I think it's wrong, I'll tell you."

"I would expect nothing less."

Harry nodded.

"And I need the same from you."

Dracena was quiet for a moment.  "You have it."

He touched her cheek very gently, careful to stay far away from the
bruise.

"And this?"

Dracena went still.

"This is one of the things I can't compromise about."

She looked at him.

"I am not asking you to stop loving your father."

Her eyes widened slightly.

"I'm not asking you to stop being a Malfoy."

That one hit harder.  Potter could tell.

"I'm asking you to let me help make sure nobody gets to hurt you because
they love you."

Dracena closed her eyes.  Another tear escaped.  "Even him?"

Harry hesitated.  "Especially him."

She opened her eyes.

"Why especially?"

"Because if some stranger hit you, you'd know exactly what to do."

Dracena stared at him.  Harry saw that she understood.

"Oh."

"Yeah."

She leaned against him again, resting her head against his shoulder.
 Harry put his arm around her.

"Hermione is waiting outside, isn't she?"

"Probably."

"Listening at the door?"

"Definitely."

From the other side of the door came an indignant:  "I AM NOT."

Dracena stared at it.  Harry stared at it.  Then Dracena laughed.  A
real laugh.  Harry joined her.

"Come in, Granger." Dracena called.

The door opened almost immediately.  Hermione stood there holding her
enormous book.

"I couldn't hear much anyway.  Besides, I was reading."

"In the corridor?"

"The lighting is excellent."

Dracena rolled her eyes.

Hermione saw them holding hands, and stopped.

She looked at Dracena.  She Looked at Harry, staring at their hands
clasped together.

"Oh." Hermione grinned widely.

Dracena immediately became defensive.  "Don't."

"I didn't say anything."

"Your face did."

Harry started laughing.  Hermione sat beside Dracena.  For a moment,
none of them spoke.  Then Dracena leaned against her too.  Hermione
stiffened in surprise, then put an arm around her.

Her father was still her father.  Her family was still her family.
 Harry was still Harry.  Hermione was still an insufferable Muggle-born
know-it-all who had somehow become one of the people Dracena trusted
most in the world.  Nothing had been solved.

Tomorrow there would be professors to tell, decisions to make, arguments
to have, and consequences none of them could predict.

But tonight Dracena sat between two people who loved her.  For the
moment, that was enough.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## <span class="c12">The Boundary</span>

<div class="illustration">

![The Boundary](Images/The%20Boundary.jpg)

</div>

Snow covered the grounds of Hogwarts.

Harry stood beside Dracena near the carriages, hands shoved into his
pockets while students hurried around them with trunks, owls, parcels,
and the particular chaos produced by several hundred teenagers
simultaneously trying to go home for Christmas.

Dracena looked immaculate.  Of course she did.  Harry had discovered
that Dracena Malfoy could survive examinations, windstorms, emotional
breakdowns, and at least one catastrophic encounter with a self-inking
quill while remaining more presentable than he looked immediately after
getting dressed.

"You could stay."  Harry said, simply.

Dracena looked at him.  "We've discussed this."

"I know."

"Three times."

"I know."

"Four, now."

Harry frowned.  "You're sure?"

Her expression softened.  "Yes."

"You don't have to go home."

"I know.  I've spoken to Dumbledore."

Potter stopped.  "When?"

"Yesterday."

"And?"

"And that conversation was between Professor Dumbledore and me."

"Dracena."

She sighed.  "I have arrangements."

"What kind of arrangements?"

"The kind which mean you needn't spend Christmas imagining me locked in
a tower."

"I wasn't going to imagine a tower."

"You were absolutely going to imagine a tower."

Harry  conceded this with a shrug.  Dracena stepped closer.  "If I
decide I shouldn't remain at home, I have somewhere to go."

"Where?"

"Somewhere safe."

"Dracena."

"Harry."  She looked directly into his eyes.  That stopped him.  She
rarely used his first name.  "I have thought about this."

Harry searched her face.  She wasn't frightened.  Nervous, yes, but
underneath it was something he'd been seeing more often lately.  It was
resolve.

"There are things I need to do."

"At home."

"Yes."

"With your father."

She paused, silently.  "Among other things."

Harry thought he understood,  at least partially.

"You don't have to prove anything."

"I'm not."

"You don't have to go back there just to prove you're not afraid."

"I'm not doing that either."

"Then why?"

Dracena looked toward Castle Hogwarts.  For a moment she seemed to
search for the words.  "Because it's my home."

Harry remained silent.

"My room is there.  My mother is there.  My family's history is there.
 My great-great-grandmother who laughs when you touch her name on the
family tapestry is there."

Potter smiled faintly.

"I still want to see that."

"You will."

The certainty of it warmed his heart.

"I'm not surrendering my home because something terrible happened in
it."

Harry's smile disappeared.

"And I'm not surrendering my father either."

"I know."

"But I'm also not going home and pretending nothing happened."

Potter nodded.

"Okay."

Dracena blinked.

"Okay?"

"Okay."

"That's all?"

"What do you want me to do?"

"I don't know.  Argue."

"I'm trying this new thing where I respect your decisions." Harry said,
flatly.

"How awful." Dracena said, smiling

"I hate it."  Harry said, smiling back

Potter took her hand.

"If anything happens..."

"I'll send an owl."

"Immediately."

"Immediately."

"Not three hours later after you've decided whether it's sufficiently
serious."

Dracena looked offended.

"I would not do that."

"You absolutely would."

"I'll send an owl." she said, finally.

"Promise?"

She squeezed his hand.

"I promise."

Potter relaxed slightly.

"Good."

"And you?"

"What about me?" Harry asked.

"You're really spending Christmas with your aunt and uncle?"

"Yeah."

Dracena's eyebrows rose.  "Voluntarily."

"Mostly."

"These are the people you spent half the year describing as
intolerable."

"They're complicated."

Dracena stared at him.

Potter stared back.  Dracena's mouth curled upward.

"Oh, don't." Harry said, smiling.

"Potter."

"Don't."

" *Your*  family is complicated."

"Shut up." Harry grinned.

" *You*  are returning to  them despite having serious disagreements
with them."

"Dracena."

"And *you've*  decided that perhaps preserving a relationship doesn't
require approving  of everything they've ever..."

Harry kissed her.  It was an extremely effective method of ending the
conversation.  When they separated, Dracena looked insufferably pleased.

"You know I'm right."

"I know you're impossible."

"You love me."

"Against my better judgment."

"Your judgment has always been suspect."

Harry kissed her again.

When they finally pulled apart, the noise of the station seemed to
return around them.

Dracena rested her forehead against his.

"I'll miss you."

Potter smiled.

"I'll miss you too."

"I'll write."

"You'd better."

"And Potter?"

"Yeah?"

"I'm going to be all right."

Harry looked into her eyes.  Months ago he would have answered
automatically.  "I know."  This time he didn't.  Instead, he said, "I
believe you."

Dracena smiled.  That was better.

She kissed his cheek, picked up her bag, and walked toward the carriage
waiting for her.  Halfway there she turned.  Harry was still watching.
 Dracena raised one eyebrow.

He waved.  She rolled her eyes, then waved back.

They went home.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Malfoy Manor had never seemed particularly imposing to Dracena.  Other
people found it so.  Today, she understood why.  The long drive up to
the iron gates.  The ancient stone.  The portraits.  The enormous
entrance hall designed by some distant ancestor who believed ceilings
were a sign of weakness unless they were at least thirty feet high.

To Dracena it was simply home.\
The clock in the west hall still ran four minutes slow.\
The third stair still creaked.\
Someone had decorated the banister with winter holly.  Her mother's
decorations.

Dracena touched one of the leaves as she passed.  She had missed this
place.  That realization brought an unexpected lump to her throat.  She
had missed it terribly.  Nothing about what happened had changed that.

Her mother embraced her.  Dracena held on longer than usual.  Neither
commented upon it.

Dinner was polite.  Painfully polite.  Her father asked about
examinations.  Dracena answered.  Her mother discussed Christmas plans.

Nobody mentioned Potter.\
Nobody mentioned Granger.\
Nobody mentioned the bruise, now faded to a faint yellow shadow that
cosmetics could easily hide.  Dracena did not hide it.

Her father noticed.\
She knew he noticed.\
Neither said anything.\
Not yet.  It came shortly after dinner.

A house-elf found her in the drawing room.

"Miss Dracena's father requests her presence in his study."

Of course he did.  Dracena put down her book.  Her hands were cold.  She
stood outside the study door for several seconds.

I'm going to be all right.  She had told Harry that.  Now she had to
decide whether she'd believed herself.  Dracena opened the door.  Her
father sat behind his desk.  

Lucius Malfoy looked exactly as he always had.  That was almost worse.
 Instead of the monster many people wanted to imagine, there was her
father.  The man who'd taught her to ride a broom.  The man who had
carried her through this very house when she'd fallen asleep at
Christmas parties.  The man who'd taught her their family history.  The
man whose approval had mattered to her for as long as she could
remember.

The man who had hit her.

"Close the door, Dracena."  She did.

"Sit."

She remained standing.  Lucius noticed.  His expression tightened.

"I understand." he began, "that there are several matters concerning
your behavior at school which we need to..."

"Father."

He stopped.  Dracena had interrupted Lucius Malfoy.  There were probably
ancestral portraits throughout the house fainting in horror.

"Excuse me?"

Her heart was pounding.

She could hear Harry's voice in her head.  "If I'm frightened, I'll tell
you I'm frightened."

Fine.  She was frightened.  Terrified, actually.  She continued anyway.

"You will never hit me again."  Lucius stared at her.  Silence filled
the study.  Dracena's knees felt weak.

Her father slowly put down the parchment he'd been holding.  "What did
you say?"

"You heard me."

"Dracena."

"You will never raise your hand against me in anger, again."

Lucius stood.  Dracena's entire body screamed at her to step backward.
 She didn't.  Her father looked genuinely astonished.  Not enraged.  Not
yet, anyway.  Just unable to believe what he was hearing.  "And if I
do?"

Dracena hadn't expected that.  She had rehearsed this conversation a
dozen times.  Lucius had never said that.

"What?"

"You've apparently decided to issue your father an ultimatum."  His
voice remained eerily calm. "So I should  like to understand it." He
walked around the desk toward her.  "If I strike you again, then what?"
 His voice wasn't angry.  He sounded genuinely confused.  This made it
far more terrifying.

Dracena opened her mouth.  Nothing came out.  Lucius waited.

She could lie.  She could invent something impressive.  She could
threaten to go to Dumbledore.  Threaten to leave.  Threaten to expose
him.  She had backup plans.  She had people who would help her.  But
none of those things was the reason she'd come into this room.

"I don't know."

Something flickered across Lucius's face.

Dracena swallowed.  "I don't know what I'll do."

"Then this is a rather empty threat."

"It isn't a threat."

"What is it?"

"A boundary."

Lucius's expression suggested the word offended him personally.

Dracena continued.  "I don't know what happens if you cross it."  Her
voice trembled.  She hated that.  "But I know I won't accept it."

"Accept it?"

"No."

"You are my daughter."

"Yes."

"You are sixteen years old."

"Yes."

"You live beneath my roof."

"Yes."

"Everything you possess..."

"Father."

Lucius's eyes flashed.

Dracena nearly stopped.  Nearly.  "If you have a problem with me, tell
me."

He stared at her.

"If I embarrass you, tell me.  If I disgrace the family, tell me.  If
you think my friends are beneath me, tell me."  Her voice grew stronger.
 "You may shout if you absolutely must."

One of Lucius's eyebrows rose.

"But you will use your words."  Dracena's eyes burned.  "You will
**not**  use your hands."

Lucius said nothing.

"Have I made myself clear?"

The instant the words left her mouth, Dracena realized she'd just asked
Lucius Malfoy whether *she*  had made *herself*  clear.  There was a
peculiar ringing in her ears.

Her father stared at her.  Dracena stared back.  Something moved behind
his eyes.  Anger?  Certainly anger.  Indignation?  Absolutely.  But
there was something else.  Something Dracena couldn't identify.  Lucius
walked toward her.  Every instinct told her to move.

She stayed.

He stopped directly in front of her.  Dracena had forgotten how tall he
was.

"Potter."

Of course.  Dracena's fear transformed instantly into irritation.  "This
isn't just about Potter."

"I find that difficult to believe."

"Hermione helped."

Lucius's mouth tightened at the name.  "That..."

"Don't."

His eyes returned to hers.

"Don't what?"

"Don't say it.  Don't call her that word."

"I haven't said anything."

"You were about to."

Lucius's eyebrows rose.  Dracena realized she'd done it again.  If she
was going to be executed, she might as well make the evening worthwhile.

"Her name is Hermione Granger."

"I am aware."

"She's my friend."

Lucius's jaw tightened.  Dracena's heart hammered.

"And Potter?"  She hesitated.  This was perhaps not the ideal moment.
 Then again, there was probably never going to be an ideal moment.
 "He's more than that."

Lucius closed his eyes.  For one brief, glorious second, Dracena thought
he looked as though he had developed a headache.  She almost smiled.

"Of course he is."

Dracena said nothing.  Lucius turned away.  He walked toward the
fireplace.  For several seconds, he merely stared into it.

"You've changed."

Dracena felt unexpectedly wounded. "No.  I am still a Malfoy."

Her father looked over his shoulder.  She hadn't intended to say it so
desperately.

"I am."

Lucius studied her.

"I love this family."

Silence.

"I love Mother."

Silence.

"I love you."

That one changed something.  Barely, but she saw it.

Dracena's voice trembled.

"I don't want another family."

Lucius turned fully toward her.

"I don't want another name.  I don't want to become someone else."

She wiped angrily at one eye.

"But I am not going to believe something merely because you believe it
anymore."

Lucius's face hardened.

"I see."  Lucius said, icily.

"No." Dracena shook her head.  "I don't think you do."  She drew herself
upright.  "You taught me what our name means."

Something flickered in his expression.  Something unreadable.

"You taught me that a Malfoy does not grovel.  That we do not allow
ourselves to be intimidated.  That we know our history and defend what
belongs to us."

Lucius stared at her.  Dracena's voice became quieter.

"I belong to me."

Lucius didn't move.  Neither did she.  For the first time in her life,
Dracena looked at her father and realized he didn't know what to say.
 It was terrifying.  It was also exhilarating.  Somewhere beneath his
anger, beneath the affront to his dignity and the absolute certainty
that his daughter had become intolerably insolent...  Dracena thought
she saw it.  Pride?  Not approval.  Certainly not approval.  But pride.

That might have been worse.

Lucius drew a slow breath.  "Go to your room."

Dracena's stomach dropped.  "Father..."

"I said go to your room."  His voice gave her nothing.  

Dracena wanted to ask.\
*Are we all right?\
Do you understand?\
Do you still love me?*

She asked none of them.  "Goodnight, Father."

She turned toward the door.

"Dracena."

Her hand stopped on the handle.  She looked back.

Lucius stood beside the fire.  He seemed older than he had when she'd
entered.  For several seconds neither spoke.  Then his eyes moved toward
the fading bruise beneath hers.

Something tightened in his face.  Again, something unreadable.

"Goodnight."

Dracena opened the door.  She stepped into the corridor, then closed it
behind her.  She leaned against the wood and released a breath she'd
apparently been holding since September.

Her hands were shaking.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Inside the study, Lucius Malfoy remained beside the fire.  His daughter
had defied him.  In his own study.  Under his own roof.  She had
interrupted him, challenged him, questioned his judgment, defended a
Muggle-born, admitted an attachment to Harry bloody Potter, and
concluded by informing him of the proper conduct expected inside the
Malfoy family.

The insolence was breathtaking.  Lucius stared into the fire.  Then,
despite himself, the faintest trace of something appeared at the corner
of his mouth.

He suppressed it immediately.  The girl had stood her ground.  He had
taught her that.  Perhaps that was the trouble.

Or perhaps...

Lucius's eyes fell upon an old family tapestry hanging opposite his
desk.  Generations of Malfoys stared silently back at him.  He looked
