toward the closed door.  His expression hardened again.

"Potter." he muttered.

The fire cracked.

Somewhere upstairs, Dracena Malfoy began writing an owl.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## <span class="c12"></span>

## <span class="c12">The Late Night Discussion</span>

<div class="illustration">

![The Late Night Discussion](Images/The%20Late%20Night%20Discussion.jpg)

</div>

The fire turned green precisely as the clock in his study struck the
first chime at eleven.  Lucius Malfoy was waiting.  He stood alone in
his study, one hand resting upon the silver head of his cane, watching
ordinary flames transform.  Green fire climbed the grate.  A face
appeared within it.  Pale.  Serpentine.

"Lucius."

"My Lord."

Lucius bowed.

"I trust preparations are proceeding."

"They are."

"The Ministry?"

"Three additional members have agreed to cooperate.  Two others are
sympathetic but unwilling to commit themselves openly."

"Cowards."

"Politicians."

Voldemort's mouth twitched in a vile smile.

"Is there a difference?"

"Occasionally."

Lucius allowed himself the faintest smile in return.  Voldemort did not
return it.

"The funds we discussed?"

"Transferred through Gringotts."

"And our friends in America?"

"I have spoken with them."

"They will support us?"

"They will support *me* ."

Voldemort's eyes narrowed.

Lucius continued before the distinction could become an issue.

"And I have reminded them that supporting *me*  means supporting *you*
."

The green flames shifted.

Lucius waited.  He had been doing a great deal of that lately.

Waiting.  Watching.  Thinking.  All extremely dangerous habits.

When this had begun, years ago, the arrangement had seemed almost
elegant.  Voldemort possessed power.  Lucius possessed money, influence,
connections and a name that opened doors which even the threat of
violence could not.

Their interests aligned.  The old families had been losing influence for
generations.  The Ministry increasingly concerned itself with regulating
ancient institutions it barely understood.  Customs that had survived
centuries were suddenly treated as embarrassing relics.  Overseas
wizards, and Muggle influence seeped farther into their world every
year.  Voldemort promised restoration.  Order.  Power returned to those
Lucius believed had earned the right to wield it.

Lucius had never imagined himself a fanatic.  Fanatics were tiresome.
 He had imagined himself a patron.

Money here.\
An introduction there.\
A Ministry official persuaded.\
A newspaper influenced .\
An inconvenient investigation made to disappear.\
Politics.

Then Voldemort had begun asking for other things.\
Names.\
Information.\
Places.\
People.

Lucius had provided them.  Then came threats.  Then disappearances.
 Then bodies.  By then, Lucius's hands were dirty enough that objecting
to the mud seemed rather pointless.

And besides?  He still agreed with much of it.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Dracena couldn't sleep.  This was becoming an irritating feature of this
Christmas vacation.

She had gone to bed at ten.  She had read until ten thirty.  She had put
out the lamp, turned it back on, read another chapter, turned it out
again, rearranged her pillows, decided the room was too warm, decided
five minutes later that it was too cold, and spent an unreasonable
amount of time staring at the ceiling while thinking about Potter.

Mostly Potter.\
Hermione, too.\
Her father.

Quite a lot of her father, actually.  That conversation still bothered
her.   *You will never hit me again.  * She'd said it.  To Lucius
Malfoy.  More astonishingly, he had listened.  Well.  He had stood there
looking like someone had force-fed him a lemon and then sent her to her
room, which wasn't quite the same thing as listening.

But he hadn't shouted.  Hadn't threatened her.  Definitely hadn't struck
her again.  He'd said goodnight.  Dracena still didn't know what any of
that meant.

At ten till eleven, she finally surrendered.  If she couldn't sleep, she
could at least be unable to sleep while eating something.  She pulled on
a dressing gown and slippers and slipped into the corridor.

Malfoy Manor was strange at night.  The house was never truly dark.
 Tiny enchanted lamps glowed near the floor, just bright enough to keep
someone from breaking their neck on the stairs.  Moonlight came through
the enormous windows.  Portraits snored softly.

Dracena descended toward the kitchen.  She was halfway down the main
staircase when she noticed the light beneath her father's study door.

She stopped.  Father was awake.  That wasn't particularly interesting.
 Father was frequently awake at ridiculous hours doing whatever it was
important men did at ridiculous hours.

Dracena continued downstairs until she heard a voice.  Was it her
father's?

Father might have had visitors.  Father always had visitors.
 Politicians.  Ministry officials.  Businessmen.  Foreign wizards with
expensive robes and unpleasant handshakes.  People who seemed to exist
primarily to drink Father's brandy and discuss things they didn't want
children hearing.

Dracena was sixteen.  Naturally, she considered " *things adults didn't
want children hearing"*  to be one of the most interesting categories of
information in existence.

She glanced toward the kitchen.  Then toward the study.

Snack.\
Study.\
Snack.\
Study.

Dracena sighed.  The snack could wait.  She padded toward the door,
noticing the flickering glow of the study's fireplace spilling through
the narrow gap beneath it.  She suddenly realized the fireplace glow was
green, not orange.  A Floo connection?

The atmosphere changed.  Dracena couldn't have explained it afterward.
 Every instinct she possessed suddenly told her that she should go
upstairs.  Now.

She didn't.  Of course she didn't.  She leaned closer.  Her father's
voice reached her.   He was talking to someone.  Someone whose voice was
unnaturally high and cold, with a soft, snake-like hiss that made the
little hairs on her arms stand on end.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

"Lucius."

He returned his attention to the fire.

"My apologies, my Lord"

"You seem distracted."

"My daughter returned home for Christmas."

Something changed in Voldemort's expression.  Lucius noticed.

"Ah.  Dracena."

"Yes."

"I have heard interesting things about your daughter."

Lucius's grip tightened around the cane.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

*My Lord.  * Dracena froze.  There were not many people Lucius Malfoy
called *my Lord.*

She brought her head down to stare through the crack.  She saw HIS FACE.

*Oh.  * Voldemort.  Her father was talking to Voldemort.

Dracena's first reaction was not exactly surprise .  She had always
assumed Father knew Voldemort.  Why wouldn't he?  Father knew everyone.
 He knew Ministers and former Ministers and people who wanted to become
Ministers.  He knew people at Gringotts, people in France, people in
America, people whose names appeared in the Prophet and people whose
names absolutely did not.

Everybody knew the stories about the Malfoys during the war.  Father
didn't talk about them.  Mother didn't talk about them.  Whenever
Dracena had asked questions as a child, the answers had consisted mostly
of variations on *those were complicated times.  * So she'd filled in
the blanks herself.

There were uglier stories, of course.  Everybody had heard them.
 Disappearances.  Torture.  Murder.  Dracena had never been certain how
much of that to believe.  People said horrible things about important
families.  People particularly enjoyed saying horrible things about the
Malfoys.

Some of them were even true.

But...

*"My Lord."\*
Not *Lord Voldemort.\*
Not even *Voldemort.\
"My Lord."*

Dracena suddenly became considerably more interested.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

"Teenagers are inexhaustible sources of gossip."  Lucius continued.

"This is more than gossip."  Voldemort smiled a reptilian smile. "She
has become close to Harry Potter."

There it was.  Lucius said nothing.

"Very close, according to what I've been told."

"Yes."

The admission tasted unpleasant.

"You approve?"

"No."

Voldemort smiled again.

"But you permit it."

"Dracena is sixteen."

"And?"

"Meaning she is at an age when forbidding something is an excellent way
of ensuring she does it more enthusiastically."

Voldemort laughed.  "Practical as always."

"I try, my Lord."

"Perhaps her adolescent infatuation can be made useful."  Voldemort
said, coldly.

Lucius became very still.  "How?"

Voldemort's expression changed.  Lucius knew that expression.  He'd seen
it before someone disappeared

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Dracena nearly opened the door purely to inform her father that her
"adolescent infatuation" bloody well was not something to be used.
 Fortunately, some surviving instinct for self-preservation prevented
this.

Something tightened in Dracena's stomach as she listened to her father
and Voldemort discuss her.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

"Potter trusts her."

Lucius said nothing.

"He allows her close to him.  Closer than any of us could get."

Lucius knew where this was going.  Some desperate part of him hoped he
was wrong.

"Give her something." Voldemort said.

Lucius stared into the flames, his jaw moving slowly.

"Something?"

"A potion.  A cursed object.  Whatever method you think she could
conceal."

Lucius's hand tightened around the cane.

"And?"

Voldemort looked almost amused.

"Surely I needn't explain everything to you." Voldemort was going to,
anyway. "She gets close to Potter.  She waits until they're alone."

Lucius remembered his daughter in his study earlier that night.   *He's
more than that,*  she said.  Lucius had known.  He hated knowing.

Voldemort continued.  "Then she kills him."

------------------------------------------------------------------------

For several seconds Dracena genuinely didn't understand.  

Then she *did* .

Her stomach turned.  Voldemort was talking about **killing Harry.  **
And he wanted...   *HER.  * To do it.

Dracena put a hand over her mouth.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

"No." The word escaped before Lucius had decided to say it.

The green flames hissed.  Voldemort's expression didn't change.

"What did you say?"

Lucius felt his heartbeat in his throat.  He could soften it.

*It would be impractical.*  he could say.\
*She's unreliable.\
There are better methods.\*
Politics.  Always politics.  His jaw tightened.

"I said no."

Voldemort's eyes narrowed.  Lucius drew himself upright.  "I will not
ask my daughter to murder Harry Potter."

"Because she's fond of him?"

"Because she is MY daughter."

Voldemort stared at him.  Lucius stared back.

"How sentimental."

"Perhaps."

"You disappoint me."

"I have survived greater tragedies.  I will survive this one."

That was dangerous.  Lucius knew it the moment he said it.  The green
flames seemed to grow colder.  Voldemort's voice became soft.

"Careful, Lucius."

Every instinct told him to yield.  Lucius did not.  Voldemort watched
him for several seconds through the fire.

"Very well." Voldemort said.

Lucius almost relaxed.  Almost.

"She needn't kill him.  Have her bring Potter to me."

"No."  This time there was no hesitation.

Voldemort's face twisted.

"Lucius."

"I said No, my Lord."

"She need only to arrange..."

"I understood you."

"Then perhaps you misunderstand your position."

Lucius felt something inside him settle.  Strangely, that made this
easier.

"No, my Lord."  He idly adjusted his cufflinks.  "I think perhaps you
misunderstand **mine** ."

"Explain." Voldemort said, coldly

Lucius walked toward the fireplace.  "Dracena is not a Death Eater."

"Not yet she isn't."

Lucius ignored that.

"I will do what you ask of me." Lucius continued.  "The people who have
pledged themselves to our cause will do what is required of them."

"Our cause."

"Yes.  But my daughter is not one of them."

"Your daughter is a resource uniquely positioned to..."

"She is **not a resource.** " The words came harder than Lucius
intended.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Dracena knew her father's anger.  She heard it clearly.  She knew the
quiet, poisonous voice that had terrified servants, Ministry officials,
shopkeepers and, occasionally, Dracena herself.

Dracena listened as her father pushed back against The Dark Lord.

She thought back to Voldemort's statement calling her *a resource.*  
Was he pleading?  It almost sounded like he was.

She remembered what she told her father earlier that night: *I belong to
me.  * He **had**  listened.  The stubborn, arrogant, impossible ass had
actually **listened.  ** Dracena closed her eyes.  She loved him.
 Merlin help her, she loved that man.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Voldemort went silent.

Lucius heard Dracena's voice in his memory.   *I belong to me* .  He had
been furious when she'd said it.  He had considered it adolescent
insolence.  Now another man was discussing his daughter as though she
were a useful piece on a chessboard.  Lucius discovered that he despised
it.

"Dracena has made no oath to you." he said.

"Her father has."

"Yes, he has"

"You would deny me victory because your daughter has developed a
schoolgirl fascination with Potter?"

"No."  Lucius surprised himself with the answer.  "I would deny you my
daughter, until she decides to ally herself with you of her own right."

Voldemort's face hardened.  "You forget yourself."

Lucius almost laughed.  Perhaps he did.

"No.  I remember myself perfectly."  He walked closer to the flames.
 "My name is Lucius Malfoy."

Voldemort's lip curled.  "I know your name."

"Then remember what comes with it."  Lucius felt the old instincts
returning.  He remembered how his father always talked about
negotiation.  This was the language he understood best.

"You need me."

Voldemort's eyes flashed.  "Do I?"

"Yes."  Lucius said it calmly.  "Very badly, in fact."

"Be careful."

"Oh, now wait."  Lucius raised one hand.

Voldemort looked stunned by the audacity.

Lucius continued.  "You need my money.  You need my connections inside
the Ministry.  You need my contacts abroad.  You need the families who
joined you because I told them you were worth supporting."

"Do you imagine yourself indispensable?"

"No."  That answer seemed to surprise Voldemort.  "I imagine myself
extremely inconvenient to lose."  Lucius allowed that to sit between
them.  "Kill me if you like."

Voldemort's eyes narrowed.

"Threaten my wife.  My Family.  Burn my house down.  You are certainly
capable of all of this."  Lucius's voice remained conversational.  "But
then tomorrow morning several very wealthy people are going to ask why
Lucius Malfoy disappeared immediately after speaking with you."

Voldemort said nothing.

"And if instead I simply withdraw my support?"  Lucius shrugged.  "I
make several Floo calls."  His heart was hammering now.  He refused to
let it show.  "I tell people I've reconsidered."

"You wouldn't."

"Wouldn't I?"

"You would sacrifice everything you've worked for?"

Lucius considered that.

"No.  But I would sacrifice *you* ."

The green flames surged.  Voldemort's face contorted with rage.

Lucius gripped his cane.  For one insane second he wondered whether one
could cast a Killing Curse through a Floo connection.  He sincerely
hoped not.

"You dare threaten me?"

"I am not threatening you."

Lucius heard Dracena again.   *It's a boundary.*   Merlin.  He almost
laughed.  His daughter was going to be the death of him.

"I am explaining the arrangement."

"Arrangement?"

"Yes."

Lucius's voice hardened.

"I support you."  He ticked the points off on one hand calmly.  "I help
fund you.  I persuade others to fund you.  I give you access to people
and institutions you could otherwise reach only through violence."

Voldemort's face flared.

"I will continue doing those things."  Lucius leaned slightly closer.
 "But Dracena is not part of the arrangement."  He took a breath, and
stared directly at the Dark Lord.  "She does not carry messages for
you."  Lucius's voice became colder.  "She does not spy for you.  She
does not lure Potter anywhere.  And she does not kill for you.  At
least, not until she decides to join you on her own."

Voldemort stared at him.  Lucius returned the stare.

"If you want Harry Potter dead, kill him yourself."

"Lucius..."

"Or get someone else.  Leave my daughter out of it."

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Dracena's heart hammered in her chest.  She suddenly realized she should
not be standing here.

She backed away slowly, then ran.  She hurried toward the kitchen.  The
snack suddenly seemed extremely important.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

For several seconds neither man spoke.  Then Voldemort smiled.  It was
not pleasant.

"Interesting."

Lucius waited.

"You've grown a spine."

"I've always had one."

"You've simply preferred not to use it."  Lucius couldn't argue with
that.

Voldemort's eyes seemed to look through him.

"Does she know?"

"Know what?"

"What her father has done for me."

Lucius felt cold.  "She knows enough."

"Does she know everything?"

"No."

"Perhaps she should."

There was the threat.  Lucius's voice dropped. "Do not."

Voldemort smiled wider.

"Ah."

Lucius hated himself for reacting.

"You fear losing her."

Lucius said nothing.

"You fear she'll discover what her father really is."  Voldemort laughed
softly. "How touching."

Lucius stared into the flames.  He thought of Dracena standing in this
room from earlier.  Terrified.  Defiant.   *I love you.* she said.  She
had said it after telling him he was wrong.  Those two things could
apparently coexist.  Lucius still wasn't entirely sure how.

"Perhaps." he said.

Voldemort's smile disappeared.

"But if you intend to use that fear to control me, I suggest you think
very carefully."

"Is that another threat?"

"No."

Lucius smiled faintly.

"Consider it financial advice, instead."  Lucius faked a grin.

Voldemort stared at him.  And then, he laughed.  There was no warmth in
it.

"Very well, Lucius."

Lucius did not relax.

"Dracena remains uninvolved."

"Yes."

"I will find another way to Potter."

"I assumed you would."

"But understand something."

Voldemort leaned toward the flames.

"This indulgence will not be forgotten."

Lucius looked directly into his eyes.

"I didn't expect it to be."

Voldemort's face disappeared.  The fire returned to a bright, warm
orange.  Lucius still felt cold.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Dracena had found bread, cheese, some leftover Christmas ham, and a
slice of cake that she was certain Mother had specifically told everyone
not to touch because she was saving it.  Dracena ate it anyway.

She stood alone in the kitchen in her dressing gown, eating stolen cake
and grinning like an idiot.

**Father had listened.**

When Voldemort himself had told Lucius Malfoy to use his daughter?
 Father had said no.

Dracena returned upstairs feeling better than she had in days.  Perhaps
things were going to be all right.

She slept peacefully.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Lucius Malfoy's knees felt surprisingly unreliable.

He walked to the drinks cabinet and poured himself considerably more
brandy than good judgment recommended.  His hands were shaking.  He
stared at them.

"Bloody girl." he muttered.  He drank.  On the table beside him lay
several rectangular plastic cases Dracena had left there several nights
earlier.

She had called it a "DVD."  Lucius picked it up.  Two Muggle films.  One
about an Empire striking back.  The other about some Jedi returning from
wherever Jedi apparently went.

Lucius poured another drink.

He would never tell Dracena he'd acquired a Muggle television and
watched them after she'd gone to bed.  He certainly would never tell her
he'd enjoyed them.  He would take that fact to his grave before
admitting that he'd understood the man in the black mask.  A servant of
enormous power.  A loyal lieutenant.  A man who had done monstrous
things because he'd convinced himself there was no longer any meaningful
difference between one compromise and the next.  Until his master
demanded one thing too many.

His son.

Lucius stared into the fire.

"Overwrought Muggle rubbish."  He tried to convince himself.  It wasn't
working.

He took another drink.  Then he looked toward the door.  Somewhere
upstairs, Dracena was probably writing to Potter.

Lucius grimaced.

He still disliked the boy.  Intensely.

He disliked Granger.\
He disliked airplanes.\
He disliked the Moon, largely because he had recently learned Muggles
had apparently been walking about on it without anyone in the Ministry
bothering to mention this extraordinary breach of good sense.

He disliked nearly everything that had happened since his daughter had
met Harry Potter.

But she was laughing again.  He'd heard it yesterday.  Really laughing.
 Lucius hadn't realized until then how long it had been.

He stared into his brandy.  Perhaps Potter would break her heart.
 Perhaps she would eventually discover that the enormous gulf between
their worlds could not be crossed merely because two teenagers
desperately wished it could.

Perhaps Lucius would eventually have to pick up the pieces.  If so, he
would.

But they would be *her*  pieces to break.\
*Her*  mistake to make.\
*Her*  life.

Lucius closed his eyes.

" *I belong to me.* "  He could still hear her saying it.  At the time,
he'd thought it the most infuriating thing his daughter had ever said.
 Now Lucius understood something he had absolutely no intention of
telling her.  

She had been right.  

He finished the brandy.  He glanced at the Muggle film again.

"Still should have thrown the Emperor down the bloody hole sooner."
Lucius muttered.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## <span class="c12"></span>

## <span class="c12">The Realization</span>

<div class="illustration">

![The Realization](Images/The%20Realization.jpg)

</div>

The next morning ruined everything.

Narcissa was preparing breakfast when Dracena wandered into the dining
room considerably later than was respectable.

"Good morning."

"Morning."

"Sleep well?"

"Eventually."

Dracena kissed her mother's cheek and sat.

The Daily Prophet lay folded beside Mother's tea.  Dracena picked it up.

Politics.\
Politics.\
Some incomprehensible argument at the Ministry.\
Politics.\
Goblin banking financial outlooks.\
Politics.\
She turned several pages looking for something interesting.

There had been an ongoing scandal involving a famous Quidditch player,
his wife, his wife's sister, and a bewitched wardrobe.  Dracena had
absolutely no idea what was happening anymore, which naturally meant she
was desperate for the latest developments.  She never found them.
 Halfway through the paper, a photograph caught her attention.  She
recognized the man, but wasn't sure where from.

**MISSING MINISTRY OFFICIAL FOUND DEAD**

Dracena frowned.  She unfolded the paper.  The photograph showed a
round-faced wizard in his late forties or early fifties.  He was
smiling.  His picture moved, and he laughed.  Dracena knew that smile.
 That laugh.

Not well, but she knew it.

She read the story below the photograph.

<span class="c2 c4">Edwin Pritchard</span> *, Deputy Director in the
Ministry's Office of Muggle Liaisons, had been missing for six days
before his body was discovered shortly before midnight outside Gimbel's
Meadow.*  

The article went on to describe him as an advocate for "expanded
cooperative relations between magical and non-magical communities" and a
supporter of reforms intended to reduce unnecessary restrictions on
contact between the two populations.

Dracena skimmed.  Then stopped.  Pritchard.  Of course.  His son had
been a Hufflepuff.

Tommy Pritchard.  He'd graduated last year.  Dracena hadn't really known
him well.  Everyone had remembered Tommy.  He was one of those
irritating people who seemed capable of talking to absolutely anyone.
 Dracena remembered his father coming to Hogwarts for some school event.
 Tommy had been mortified because his father had arrived wearing an
enormous yellow-and-black scarf.  Not a Hufflepuff scarf.  An actual
badger.  Or something charmed to look like one.  Dracena remembered Mr.
 Pritchard walking through the entrance hall while the thing's head
moved around his neck.  Tommy's face had turned bright red.  "Father,
take it off."

"But I'm supporting your House!"

"You're humiliating me!"

"That's also a father's responsibility."

Even Dracena had laughed.  She remembered Mr.  Pritchard laughing too.
 Even Tommy laughed, while red from embarrassment.

His father had one of those laughs that made everyone around him laugh
harder.  She looked at the photograph again.  He was laughing, but
Dracena didn't feel the mirth.

The Prophet had included another photograph beneath the first.  The
night sky.  Trees.  And hanging above them?  A skull, with a serpent
emerging from its mouth.

The Dark Mark.  Dracena stopped reading.

"Mother?"

"Hm?"  Narcissa was buttering toast.

"Did you know Edwin Pritchard?"  The knife stopped briefly.

"Yes."

"Did you know him well?"

"No."  Mother resumed buttering.  "Your father knew him better."

Dracena looked down at the paper.

*Your father knew him better.*

Last night came back all at once.

Green fire.\
*My Lord.\*
Dracena's breakfast suddenly didn't look very appealing.

"Was he a bad person?" Dracena asked.

Mother looked at her sideways.  "Edwin?  No."

Dracena looked back at his photograph.  He was laughing again.

"Then why would somebody kill him?"

Narcissa's face changed.

"Dracena."

"What?"

"This is not appropriate breakfast conversation."

That was an extremely "Narcissa Malfoy" answer.  Normally Dracena would
have accepted it.  Today she couldn't.

She folded the Prophet and put it under her arm.

"Dracena..."

"I'm going outside."

"You haven't eaten."

"I'm not hungry."

------------------------------------------------------------------------

She carried the newspaper with her.  Dracena walked the grounds without
any particular destination.  The winter air hurt her face.

Good.  She needed that.

Mr.  Pritchard's photograph kept smiling and laughing at her from
beneath her arm.

Father knew him.  Father worked for Voldemort.  Death Eaters had killed
him.

Those were three facts.  They were not necessarily connected.  Dracena
knew that.  Hermione would insist upon that distinction.

*Correlation is not causation.  * Dracena had heard that phrase enough
times to want to strangle her with it.  Father might have had nothing
whatsoever to do with Edwin Pritchard's death.  He might not even have
known it was going to happen.  There were lots of Death Eaters.

But Father was important.  Powerful.  Connected.  Voldemort spoke to him
directly.

*My Lord.*

Her father wasn't merely some idiot on the edge of the organization.
 What if he'd known?  What if he'd given Voldemort Pritchard's name?  

What if...

Dracena stopped walking.  What if Father had him killed?

"No."  She said it aloud.  A crow somewhere in the trees objected.
 "No."  She couldn't believe that.  She **wouldn't** .

She accidentally dropped the newspaper.  The Prophet had fallen open to
the photograph of the Dark Mark.

She remembered Tommy Pritchard.

*Father, take it off.\
But I'm supporting your House!\
You're humiliating me!\
That's also a father's responsibility.*

That stupid moving badger.  Dracena could still hear the man's laugh.

Tommy didn't have a father anymore.  Someone had taken him away.  And
last night Dracena had gone to bed happy because **her**  father had
protected **her** .

The thought suddenly made her feel ashamed.  Father had stood up to
Voldemort.  For her.  That mattered.

But...  Why only her?

Dracena stared at the Dark Mark.  Why hadn't someone stood up for Edwin
Pritchard?  Who stood up for the next person?  And the next?  How many
people had Father's money helped hurt?  How many doors had Father's name
opened?  How many people had died while powerful families told their
children that these were " *complicated times* ?"

Dracena felt sick.  She wished Potter were here.  The realization
annoyed her immediately.  Potter would know what to do.

No.  That wasn't true.  Potter would know that **something had to be
done.  ** Those were very different things.  She could practically hear
him.

*"We have to do something!"  * Then he would grab his wand, run directly
toward whatever appeared most dangerous, and trust that courage, luck
and Hermione's genius would somehow keep him alive.

Dracena loved Harry.  She really did.  He was also a bit of an idiot
sometimes.  A brave idiot, but an idiot.

She smiled despite herself.  Harry was brave.  Braver than anyone she
knew.  Dracena had once thought bravery meant not being frightened.
 Harry had disabused her of that.  She had seen him frightened.  She had
watched him do things anyway.  Hermione too.  Even Longbottom, damn him.
 There was something admirable in that.  Something Dracena didn't
naturally possess.

She was frightened now.  More frightened than she could remember being.
 Her father was involved with Voldemort.  Perhaps deeply.  People were
dying.

Voldemort wanted Harry dead.  And Voldemort knew Dracena's name.  Every
sensible instinct told her to return to the house, burn the newspaper,
write Potter a letter, and allow someone older and more qualified to
deal with it.

Harry wouldn't.  Harry would say: " *We have to do something."  *
Dracena stared at the Dark Mark again.  "Fine, Potter."

Her voice trembled.  "Something..."  Running toward Voldemort with her
wand drawn would accomplish exactly one thing: there would be a
considerably shorter obituary in tomorrow's Prophet.

Dracena Malfoy was not a Gryffindor.  She was a Slytherin.

She didn't charge.  She wasn't brave.  Not naturally, anyway.  She was
cunning.

She thought about what she remembered Voldemort had said.

*Your daughter is uniquely positioned...*

Dracena's eyes moved back to the photograph of the Dark Mark.

"...Uniquely positioned..."

She hated those words, but he wasn't wrong.

She was Lucius Malfoy's daughter.  People trusted her name.  The wrong
people, perhaps, but they trusted it.  She had grown up around families
other people feared.  She knew their children.  Their houses.  Their
manners.  And their manors.  Their assumptions.

She knew how they spoke when outsiders weren't listening.  And Voldemort
already knew who she was.

More than that...  He already wanted her involved.

Dracena became very still.  A thought had appeared.  A horrible thought.
 An absurd thought.  The sort of thought Potter would have immediately
announced to Hermione, at which point Hermione would explain seventeen
reasons it was impossible.

Dracena didn't announce it.  She examined it.  Turned it around in her
head.  She looked for weaknesses.  There were many.  She iterated on her
plan.  One particular weakness was that it might get her killed.  That
seemed significant.

Dracena stared at the image of the  skull and serpent.  Her hands were
shaking.  She folded them together until they stopped.  Harry could be
brave.  That didn't mean she had to be stupid.  Perhaps there was a way.
 Perhaps courage didn't always mean charging toward the monster.

Perhaps sometimes...

Dracena looked toward Malfoy Manor.  Toward the window to her father's
study, Then back at the Dark Mark.

She thought for a very long time, and then Dracena Malfoy smiled.

Fear, she was beginning to understand, did not necessarily mean no.  She
remembered something Harry had told her a while ago.  Sometimes being
scared tells you something matters.

Her smile broadened.  Voldemort had made a rather interesting mistake.
 He had told Lucius Malfoy exactly how useful his daughter could be.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## <span class="c12">The Plan</span>

<div class="illustration">

![The Plan](Images/The%20Plan.jpg)

</div>

Hogwarts was wrong when it was empty.  Dracena had never noticed that
before.  There were always footsteps somewhere.  Students shouting
across corridors, suits of armour complaining about being bumped, Peeves
doing something that would eventually require a mop, and portraits
gossiping about everyone who passed.

Now there was only the sound of Dracena's shoes against stone.

Some Christmas decorations still hung in the Great Hall.  Filch was
taking them down, slowly.  The enchanted ceiling was pale and grey,
threatening snow.  A handful of staff had already returned, along with a
few students who had remained over the holiday, but the castle otherwise
felt abandoned.

Dracena had told her mother she wanted to return early to prepare for
term.  Narcissa had stared at her for several seconds. "Of course." she
finally said.  Dracena suspected her mother knew perfectly well that her
daughter had never voluntarily returned to school early in order to
*prepare for term*  in her life.  Fortunately, Mother had not asked for
any details.

Dracena's trunk was upstairs.  Dracena was not.  She stood outside
Professor Snape's office.  She had been standing there for almost ten
minutes.  This was becoming embarrassing.

She raised her hand.\
Stopped.\
Lowered it.  "This is ridiculous." she said to herself.

The door opened suddenly.  Professor Snape stood on the other side.
 Dracena nearly jumped out of her skin.  He looked at her.  She looked
at him.  

"Miss Malfoy."  he said, in his monotone voice.

"Professor."

"Have you developed an objection to doors?"

"No."

"Then either enter, or cease haunting mine."

He turned away and went inside his office.  Dracena quickly followed,
and gently closed the door behind her.

Snape's office smelled exactly as unpleasant as she remembered: old
stone, dried ingredients, things suspended in jars that Dracena strongly
suspected had once possessed opinions.

Snape returned to his desk.  There was a stack of essays before him.  He
picked up his quill.  "Term begins tomorrow." he said, in his icy
monotone voice.

"I know."

"Your presence suggests otherwise."

"I came back early."

"So I had gathered."  He made a mark on an essay.  Dracena waited.
 Snape did not look up.  She suddenly understood why Potter found him so
infuriating.

"I need to speak to you."  She said, voice shaking.

"I had assumed you did not travel halfway across Britain for the
pleasure of watching me mark essays."

"It's important."

"Everything is important at sixteen."

Dracena bristled.  Snape finally looked up.  His eyes moved over her
face.

The sarcasm receded.  Just a bit.

"Sit."

Dracena sat.  Snape put down his quill.

"Has Potter done something?"

"What?"

"Potter.  Has he..."

"No."

"Miss Granger?"

"No."

"Your father?"

Dracena hesitated.  Snape noticed.  Of course he did.  Snape noticed
everything.  Another infuriating thing about him.

"Ah."

"It's not what you think."

"I assure you, Miss Malfoy, I have made no attempt to imagine the
domestic affairs of your family."

Dracena took a breath.  "I need to know how someone becomes a Death
Eater."

Snape stared at her.  "Get out." Snape said, angrily.

Dracena blinked.  "What?"

"You heard me."  His icy monotone voice grew angrier.

"But..."

"Whatever adolescent melodrama has inspired this astonishing display of
stupidity, I have neither the time nor inclination to participate in
it."

"It isn't.."

"Out!" Professor Snape shouted, a bit louder this time.  She heard more
emotion from his voice than she ever noticed before.

"I heard my father talking to Voldemort."

Snape stopped.  Dracena had never seen anyone become so completely
still.

"Sit down."

"I am sitting."

"Then continue to do so."

The icy, cold monotone returned to his voice.

"When?"

"Three nights ago."

"Where?"

"At home.  Father's study.  Through the Floo."

"You saw him?"

"A little.  I peeked under the door."

"You heard him?"

"Yes."

"What precisely did you hear?"

Dracena swallowed.  "He wanted Father to use me."

Snape's face revealed nothing.  "To do what?"

"Kill Harry."

"Continue." Snape said, coldly.

"He said Harry trusted me.  That I could give him something.  A potion,
I think.  Father refused."

Snape leaned back.

"He refused?" Snape asked, surprised.

Dracena couldn't keep the pride from her voice.  "Yes."

"Your father told the Dark Lord 'no?'"

"Several times."

Snape's eyebrows rose slightly.

"He said I wasn't a Death Eater."

"An accurate observation.  Now, unfortunately, it seems your father
appears to have developed an unexpected enthusiasm for suicide."

Despite herself, Dracena laughed.  It came out strangely.  She hadn't
realized how frightened she was until that moment.  Snape didn't laugh.

"What happened then?"

"Voldemort kept pushing.  He said I was uniquely positioned.  That Harry
trusted me.  Father told him I hadn't sworn anything to him.  Voldemort
called me a resource."  Her mouth tightened.  "Father said I wasn't
one."

Something unreadable flickered in Snape's expression.

"And?"

"He told Voldemort if he wanted Harry dead, he could kill him himself.
 Or find someone else.  But he was to leave me out of it."

"And the Dark Lord agreed?"

"Eventually."

"No."

Dracena frowned.  "He did."

"The Dark Lord does not agree to abandon useful ideas, Miss Malfoy.  He
finds other methods."  The warmth she'd carried since overhearing her
father suddenly felt much smaller. "Your father bought you time."

Dracena looked at the floor.  "I thought that might be the case."

"And therefore you've decided to reward his efforts by doing precisely
what he risked his life to prevent."

"No." Dracena said, quietly.

"You've travelled here a day early to ask a former Death Eater how to
become a Death Eater."

"That's not what I mean."

"It is precisely what you mean."

"No.  I want him to think I've become one."

Snape's eyes narrowed.  She had his attention now.  Dracena leaned
forward.

"He already wants me in his circle."

"Yes."

"He already thinks I'm useful."

"Yes."

"He thinks Father is preventing me from being useful."

"Apparently."

"So let him."

Snape looked at Dracena, but said nothing.

"Let him think Father was wrong.  Let him think Potter confused me.  Let
him think Hermione filled my head with Muggle nonsense.  I went home,
remembered who I was, remembered I'm a Malfoy, and decided I'd had
enough."

"That sounds remarkably like several things you've actually believed."

Dracena flinched.

"I know."

"Good."

She hadn't expected that.  Snape folded his hands.

"Continue."

Dracena gathered herself.

"I tell Voldemort I want to prove myself."

"By doing what?"

"Whatever makes sense."

"An inspiring, detailed plan."

"I haven't got that far!"

"Clearly."

"That's why I'm here!"

Snape's eyes narrowed.  Dracena forced herself to lower her voice.

"I don't know how this works."

"Obviously."

"You do."

Snape said nothing, his face inscrutable.

"I know you were one of them."

"Many people know that."

"Dumbledore trusts you."

"Many people question his judgment."

"I don't."

That seemed to surprise Snape.

"I don't know what you did.  I don't know why Dumbledore trusts you.
 And I'm not asking."

"How generous of you, Miss Malfoy."

"But if there's a way to get close enough to Voldemort to hear things
without actually belonging to him..."

"You are making assumptions about matters far beyond your
understanding."

"Probably."

That stopped him.  Dracena swallowed.

"That's why I'm asking you."

Snape studied her.  "Suppose I tell you no."

"I'll go back to my dormitory."

"And?"

"I don't know."

"An unsatisfactory answer."

"It's a truthful one."

Snape watched her.  "What exactly do you imagine accomplishing?"

"I don't know that either."

Snape sighed.  "Then this conversation has been enormously productive."
Snape said, icy sarcasm dripping from his voice.

"I could hear things."

"Things." Snape said, sarcastically.

"Names.  Plans.  People they're targeting.  Where they're going.  What
they're doing.  I don't know."

"And then?"

"I tell someone."

"Whom do you think would listen to a Death Eater?"

Dracena looked at him.  Snape's expression changed almost imperceptibly.

"Oh, no."

"What?"

"You imagine *I*  would be this someone."

Dracena suddenly felt much less clever.

"Well..."

"Extraordinary."

"You know what you're doing!"

"And you demonstrably do not."

"Then teach me."

"No."

"Professor..."

"No." Snape said, sharply.

Dracena stood.

"You haven't even heard me out."

"I have heard more than enough.  You are sixteen years old.  You
overheard one conversation which frightened you, discovered that the
Dark Lord wishes to exploit your relationship with Potter, and have
responded by concocting a fantasy in which you infiltrate his
organization and report his plans to me."

"It's not a fantasy."

"It is considerably worse.  Fantasies are generally somewhat
believable."

"I'm serious."

"So am I."

Snape stood.  "The people you are discussing torture children, Miss
Malfoy."

Dracena's anger vanished, replaced by terror.

"They murder families.  They make people disappear.  They will kill you
if they suspect you.  They may kill you merely to determine whether
someone else is loyal.  Like your Father.  They may do the same to him,
to test your loyalty.  Or your mother."

Dracena said nothing.

"You appear to imagine becoming a Death Eater involves attending
sinister meetings, nodding gravely while villains discuss their plans,
then slipping away afterward to send Potter a helpful owl."

"I don't..."

"Do you know what the Dark Lord does to people who lie to him?"

"No."

"I do."

The words landed like a bird hitting a window.  Dracena looked at him.
 For the first time, she understood that Snape wasn't trying to frighten
her.

Or rather?  He was.  But he also wasn't exaggerating.

"Sit down."

Dracena sat.  Snape remained standing.

"If we continue this conversation...  And I have not agreed that we
shall...  You will first abandon several childish assumptions."

"All right." Dracena said.

"First: Potter cannot know."

Dracena nodded.

"I assumed..."

"No.  You haven't."

His voice sharpened.

"You think you understand that sentence.  You do not."

Dracena went still.

"If Potter knows, Potter must lie."

"He can."

Snape actually looked offended.

"Potter?"

Dracena nearly smiled.

"He's not *that*  bad of a liar."

"He is a catastrophically poor liar when emotionally invested."

"He's gotten better."

"Yes.  Largely because you've spent time informing him when he's being
an idiot."

That almost made her smile again.  Snape didn't let her.

"If Potter knows, every glance becomes a performance.  Every argument
becomes a performance.  Every moment the two of you occupy the same room
becomes an opportunity for someone to notice that Harry Potter does not
behave like a young man betrayed by the girl he loves."

Dracena's smile vanished.

"He has to believe it."

"Yes."

"Completely?"

"Completely."

Dracena looked down.

"No messages." Snape continued. "No hidden notes.  No coded assurances.
 No secret meetings.  No touching his hand beneath a table when nobody
appears to be watching.  No clever little phrase only the two of you
understand."

Dracena's throat tightened.

"Nothing?" Dracena repeated.

"Nothing."  Snape said, coldly.

"But if.."

" **Nothing** ." Snape said, sharply.

Snape leaned toward her.

"Potter must not *act*  heartbroken, Miss Malfoy."

She looked up.  

"He must **be**  heartbroken."

That hurt.  Snape saw it.  He continued anyway.

"The Dark Lord will not merely watch you.  He will watch Potter.  Others
will watch Potter.  And Harry Potter, for all his many faults, wears
most of his emotions on his shirtsleeves with the subtlety of a
Gryffindor banner."

Dracena whispered, "I know."

"No.  I don't believe you do."

"I said I know."

"You will have to make him hate you."

Dracena flinched.

"Maybe.  Not..."

"Yes."

"He doesn't have to hate me."

"He has to despise you."

"No." Dracena whimpered.

"He must believe you a coward."

Her eyes burned.

"Stop." she pleaded, forcing the tears back.

"He must believe you used him."

"Professor, I know!" She said, wiping a tear from her face.

"Believe every conversation in which you questioned your family's
beliefs was a temporary adolescent rebellion."

"Stop." She said, a hair's breadth from tears.

"Believe you chose your name, your status and your father over him."

She held back the tears with nearly superhuman strength.

"Believe that when you told him you loved him..."

" **Enough!** " She screamed.  Her voice echoed through the dungeon.

Snape stopped.

Dracena was breathing hard.  Her eyes were wet.

Snape's expression remained infuriatingly calm.

"That." he said quietly, "is what you are proposing."

Dracena wiped her face angrily.  "You're being cruel."

"Yes.  If cruelty in this room prevents you from discovering the reality
of your proposal while standing before the Dark Lord, I will consider it
time well spent."

Dracena hated him.  For perhaps thirty seconds, she genuinely hated him.
 Then she realized something worse.  He was right.  She took a deep
breath, and gathered herself.  She put on the "Malfoy face" she wore
when she wanted to lock the world behind a door.  

"Enough." She said, with a calmness she didn't feel.

"Granger cannot know either."

Dracena shut her eyes.

"Hermione..."

"No."

" **She**  can lie."

"Better than Potter."

"Much better."

"Which is irrelevant."

Dracena opened her eyes. "Why?"

"Because Miss Granger will attempt to help you."

"That's one of her better qualities."

"Not here."  Snape returned to his chair.  "She will research.  She will
plan.  She will worry.  She will watch you.  She will watch Potter
watching you.  She will attempt to anticipate problems.  Eventually she
will do something intelligent."

Dracena frowned.

"That sounds useful."

"Which is precisely what will make it dangerous."

Dracena understood.

"And Dumbledore?"

"No."

She stared at him.

"Dumbledore has to know."

"He does not."

"You're serious?"

"Entirely."

"But you trust him."

Snape's expression became unreadable. "With my life."

"Then why..."

"Because the Headmaster is one of the most perceptive men the Dark Lord
knows."

Dracena waited.

"And if Albus Dumbledore believes Lucius Malfoy's daughter has finally
embraced her father's cause, the Dark Lord will find that fact extremely
persuasive."

Dracena felt sick.

"And I have to let him think...?"

"Yes."

Dracena stared at Snape.

"Who knows, then?"

"If this were to be done?"

"Yes."

"Me."

"And?"

"No one else."

Dracena blinked.

"No one?"

"No one."

"What if something happens to you?"

"Then you have discovered one of the many reasons this is a terrible
idea."

She stared at him.

"That's insane."

"At last, we agree."

Dracena stood again and began pacing.

"This isn't what I thought."

"You thought?"

"I thought..."

"I know what you thought."

"No, you don't."

"I assure you..."

"I thought I'd lose Harry."

Snape stopped.

Dracena turned toward him.

"I knew that."

Her voice shook.

"I knew if I did this, Harry and I couldn't stay together.  Voldemort
wants me because Harry loves me.  That's the whole point.  If I'm still
with Harry, the first thing Voldemort will do is ask me to use him.  To
hurt him."

Snape said nothing.

"So I knew."

"Did you?"

"Yes."

"And your solution?"

"I leave him."

"Potter will not believe you."

"I'll make him."

"How?"

Dracena didn't answer.

Snape waited.

"He knows you." Snape said.

"I know."

"He knows how you speak when you're angry.  He knows how you behave when
you're frightened.  He knows when you're lying badly."

"I know."

"He will ask questions."

"I know."

"He will follow you."

"I know."

"He will probably conclude that your father has threatened you."

"I know."

"He may decide to confront Lucius."

"Oh, Merlin."

"Quite."

Dracena sank back into the chair.  Snape watched her.

"You have promised Potter things, I assume."

Dracena looked at him sharply.  "That is none of your business."

"Then I shall take that as confirmation."

She looked away.  " *We don't lie about not knowing." s* he remembered
him saying.  Harry had promised her that.  So had she.

Snape's voice came quietly.  "Can you do it?"

Dracena didn't answer.

"Can you look at Harry Potter and convince him that whatever exists
between you is finished?"

She heard Harry's voice, again in her head.

*"And if we can't?"*

*"Dracena, don't."*

*"Seriously.  If we can't?"*

Then another memory.

*"You can't say it."*

*"I want to."*

*"That's not what I asked."*

*"I know."*

And then she remembered:

*"We don't lie about not knowing."*

Dracena swallowed.

"I don't know."

Snape's mouth tightened.

"'I don't know' is not sufficient."

"It has to be."

"No."

"It **has to be.** " Dracena said, sharply.

Snape went silent.  Dracena looked at him through wet eyes.

"I love Harry."  Her voice broke.  She didn't care.  "I really, truly
love him."

Snape looked away.  

"And it's because I love him that I have to be willing to do this."  She
wiped her face.  "For him.  And more importantly, for me."

Snape's eyes returned to her.

Dracena continued before she lost her nerve.  "This isn't just about
Harry."

"Then what is it about?" Snape asked.

"I don't know how to explain it."

"You should really try, Miss Malfoy.  It's your best hope to convince
me."

Dracena stared at her hands.  "I read about Edwin Pritchard."

Snape knew the name.  She could tell.

"He was found dead."

"Yes."

"Death Mark over the body."

"Dark Mark."

"Yeah, that.  Whatever."

She rubbed her palms against her robes.  "I knew his son.  Not well.  He
was a Hufflepuff.  Graduated last year."

Snape listened.

"His father came to Hogwarts once wearing this ridiculous badger scarf.
 It moved.  Like some kind of silly badger."

Snape continued listening.

"He was funny."

"I remember Mr.  Pritchard."

"You do?"

"I taught his son for seven years."

"Oh."

Dracena looked down.  "Father knew him."

Snape continued to listen, saying nothing.

"I don't know if Father had anything to do with what happened.  I don't
want you to tell me."

"Why?"  Snape said, genuinely curious.

"Because I'm not asking you to make this easy."

Snape studied her.

"I don't know what my father has done."

She spoke slowly now.

"I know he's involved.  I know he's involved more deeply than I wanted
to believe.  I know he gives Voldemort money.  Contacts.  Influence."

She looked down at her hands, thinking intently.

"I know he protected me."  Her voice softened.  "He stood up to him for
me."

Snape said nothing, watching and listening.

"I'm proud of him for that."  Her eyes filled again.  "And I'm furious
with him."  She hadn't intended to say that, but once it was out, more
followed.  "Because if he can stand there and tell Voldemort no for me,
then he knows how to say no."

Snape remained perfectly still.

"So why doesn't he?"  Her voice cracked.  "For everybody else?"

A single tear fell down her cheek.

"I don't understand."  She shook her head.  "Maybe there are things I
don't know.  There probably are.  Maybe Father thinks he's protecting
us.  Maybe he thinks Voldemort is necessary.  Maybe he thinks whatever
they're doing is worth it.  Maybe he's trapped.  Maybe he's frightened."
 She laughed bitterly.  "Maybe I'm still making excuses for him."

Snape didn't rescue her from that thought.  Dracena was grateful.

"I don't know."

Again.  Those three words, but Harry had taught her something about
them.  Sometimes *I don't know*  was the most honest thing a person
could say.

"What I do know is that people are dying."

Snape's face remained unreadable.

"And I know it's wrong."  Dracena looked directly at him.  "And if I can
do something that helps stop it, I think I have to."

"You think."

"I do."

"Those are different statements."

"Fine."  She breathed.  "I have to." she said, with a bravery she didn't
quite feel.

Snape leaned back.  "You will lose Potter."

"I know."

"You may lose your father."

Dracena's face tightened.  "I know."

"Your mother."

"I know."

"Your home."

"I know."

"Your inheritance."

"I know."

"Your name."

That one hurt unexpectedly.  Dracena looked at him.  "My name is mine."

Snape's eyes sharpened.  She hadn't meant it defiantly, but suddenly she
heard herself saying it to her father.

*"I belong to me."*

"My name is mine." she repeated.  "Even if my family decides otherwise."

Snape said nothing.

Dracena's hands were trembling again.  "I could lose everything."

"Yes."

"I could die."

"Yes."

"I'm frightened."

"Good."

Dracena stared at him.

"Good?"

"Only fools are not frightened."

"I'm terrified."

"Better."

"You're awful at comforting people."

"It is a skill I have lacked after many years at Hogwarts."

Despite everything, Dracena laughed.  It lasted only a second.  Then she
looked down at her hands.

"I'm going to be a mess."

Snape didn't contradict her.

"I know that."  Her voice became very quiet.  "If I do this, I'm going
to hurt Harry.  I'm going to hurt Hermione.  Dumbledore will think I'm
horrible.  My father might think he's won, which somehow makes this even
worse."  She swallowed.  "And I'm going to hate myself sometimes."

Snape watched her.

"But if I don't do anything..."  She stopped.  That possibility
frightened her differently.  "If I go back to school tomorrow and
pretend I never heard that conversation, and somebody else ends up like
Mr.  Pritchard..."  She looked up at Snape, pleading  "I'll always
wonder."

Snape said nothing.

"For the rest of my life, I'll wonder whether there was something I
could have done."  Her eyes met his.  "I think that would make a bigger
mess of me."

For the first time since she'd entered the office, Severus Snape looked
at Dracena Malfoy without sarcasm, irritation or contempt.  He simply
looked at her, for an uncomfortably long time.

"You are not ready."

Dracena's heart sank.  "But..." Dracena began

I did not say no." Snape said, a touch of warmth in his voice he never
heard before.

She stopped.  Snape rose.  "You came here with an idea."  He walked
around the desk.  "A dangerous idea.  Poorly conceived, sentimentally
motivated, strategically incomplete and based upon an almost insulting
ignorance of the organization you propose to infiltrate."  The ice
returned to Snape's voice

Dracena glared at him.  "Thank you."

"You are welcome."

He stopped in front of her.  "But not **entirely**  without merit."

Dracena stared.  "Really?"

"Do not look so pleased.  It is unbecoming."

She immediately stopped looking pleased.

"The Dark Lord already wants you involved.  That matters."

Dracena nodded.

"Your father has explicitly denied him access to you.  That matters
more."

She hadn't considered that.  "Why?"

"Because things being forbidden makes things more valuable."

"Oh."

"You are Lucius Malfoy's daughter.  You are a Slytherin.  You are
connected to families the Dark Lord wishes to control.  You have been
publicly close to Potter and Granger.  And, if presented correctly, your
apparent repudiation of them could be interpreted as evidence of
commitment."

Dracena listened.

"But the greatest obstacle remains Potter."

"I know."

"No, I still don't think you do."

Snape stepped closer.

"Understand this now."  His voice was very quiet.  "If you proceed,
Harry Potter is not your accomplice."

Dracena held his gaze.

"He is not your confidant."

Her throat tightened.

"He is not your secret lover."

She said nothing.

"He is not waiting for you at the end of this."

That one landed.  Dracena's eyes burned.

"You do not know what he will be."  Snape said.

"No."  Dracena whispered  "Neither do you."

Snape's expression was merciless.  "You will not permit yourself to
imagine that Potter owes you forgiveness because you did this partly for
him."

"I wouldn't."

"You will."

Dracena started to object.  Snape cut her off.

"You will imagine the explanation.  You will imagine his face when he
learns the truth.  You will imagine him understanding.  You will imagine
everything returning to what it was."

Dracena couldn't look at him anymore.

"You will use that fantasy to comfort yourself."  He paused.  "And if
you do, it will make you weak."

A tear slipped down Dracena's cheek.  She wiped it away.

"So what am I supposed to believe?"

"The truth."

She looked up.

Snape's face had changed.  Only slightly.  Suddenly Dracena wondered how
much of this conversation was actually about her.

"You do not know whether Harry Potter will forgive you."

There it was again.   *"I don't know."*

"You do not know whether he will still love you."

Her chest hurt.

"You do not know whether your father will survive this war.  You do not
know whether your mother will forgive you.  You do not know what
Dumbledore will think of you when this is finished."

Snape's voice became quieter.

"You control **none**  of those things."

Dracena stared at him.

"You control Dracena Malfoy."

Something inside her shifted.

"Nothing else."

Dracena breathed.  Once.  Twice.  Then she nodded.

Snape returned to his desk and sat, picked up his quill and returned to
marking essays.

Dracena stared at him.

"That's it?"

"For today."

"What am I supposed to do?"

"Go to your dormitory."

"And?"

"You need to think."

"Professor Snape..."

His quill scratched across parchment.

"Think."

"I've been thinking."

"Then attempt it competently."

Dracena stood.  She made it halfway to the door.

"Miss Malfoy."

She stopped.  Snape still wasn't looking at her.

"Tell no one about this conversation."

Dracena swallowed.

"Not Harry.  Not Hermione.  Not Dumbledore."

Dracena nodded as she reached for the door.

"And Miss Malfoy?"

She turned.   Snape's expression was unreadable again.

"Return tonight at eleven and tell me if you've changed your mind.  If
so, I will think no less of you."

Dracena blinked.  That might have been the kindest thing Severus Snape
had said to her in six years, which was depressing.

"And if I haven't?"

Snape regarded her for a long moment.

"Then we begin by teaching you how to lie.  I will try and teach you to
become a proper spy in the limited amount of time we have."

Dracena's hand tightened around the door handle.

"I already know how to lie."

Snape's mouth twitched.  It wasn't a smile.  It was worse.  "No, Miss
Malfoy."

He lowered his eyes to the essay.

"You know how to lie to your father."  The quill scratched across the
parchment.  "Tomorrow, we discover whether you can lie to Harry Potter.
 And then, you'll become a Death Eater."

------------------------------------------------------------------------

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## <span class="c12"></span>

## <span class="c12">The Return</span>

<div class="illustration">

![The Return](Images/The%20Return.jpg)

</div>

The Hogwarts Express, and the rest of the Hogwarts students had arrived
the previous evening.  She had said "hi" to her friends when they
arrived.  Pansy complained about her Christmas presents, while the other
girls discussed their holidays.

She had thought about Harry.  She had thought about him rather a lot.

This was a big problem.

Harry had sent two owls over Christmas.  She had answered the first.
 The second was still folded inside her trunk, tucked beneath a stack of
winter robes where she wouldn't have to look at it.

She knew its contents by heart.  It wasn't a particularly remarkable
letter.  Harry had written about his aunt and uncle, about Christmas
dinner and  a disastrous attempt to help his aunt Petuna in the kitchen.
 He'd asked whether she was enjoying herself at home.  

And he'd written that he missed her.

I wish you were here.  Harry had written.

Five words.  Five perfectly ordinary words that had made her cry so hard
she'd been unable to finish reading the letter.

She'd read it another eleven times since.  Now she was back at Hogwarts,
and she had a problem.  Harry Potter was somewhere in this castle.
 Sooner or later, he was going to find her.

She was crossing the entrance hall the following afternoon when she
heard him.

"Dracena!"

Her heart leapt.  For one glorious, treacherous instant, she wanted
nothing more than to turn around, run to him, and throw her arms around
his neck.  Instead, she stopped walking.  She took a breath.  Another.

*You control Dracena Malfoy.*

She turned.  "Potter."

Harry was approaching with that ridiculous, untidy hair and that equally
ridiculous smile.  He looked happy to see her.  That was going to make
this difficult.

"Hi!"

"Hello."

He slowed.  Something in her expression must have warned him.

"Everything all right?"

"Of course."

"Oh.  Good."

He smiled again, although not quite as broadly.

"I was looking for you last night."

"How flattering."

Harry blinked.

Dracena adjusted the strap of her schoolbag.  "Was there something you
wanted?"

"Well, I just..." He hesitated. "I wanted to see you."

She looked at him.  Her insides already hurt

"I see."

Harry shifted his weight.

"Did you get my owl?"

"Yes."

"Oh."

He waited.  She said nothing.

"Well, I was wondering why you didn't answer."

Dracena sighed.  "I was busy, Potter."

Harry blinked.  "Busy?"

"Yes.  Busy.  I had things to do, you know."

"I know, I just..."

"I can't spend my entire holiday sitting beside a window waiting for
your letters."

Harry's smile disappeared.  Dracena felt it happen.  It was almost
physical, like watching someone slam a door.

"Oh."  He looked down.  "Sorry."

There it was.  One small word.   *Sorry.  * She had made Harry Potter
apologize for missing her.  Something inside her twisted.

"Honestly, Potter." she continued, "you're becoming rather obsessed."

Harry looked up.  "What?"

"Following me about.  Sending letters.  Demanding explanations when I
don't answer them."

"I wasn't demanding..."

"It's unbecoming."

He stared at her.

Dracena raised an eyebrow.  "Surely you have other things to occupy your
time."

Harry's face reddened.  "Yeah."  He swallowed.  "Yeah, of course."

She waited.  He looked as though he wanted to say something else.  For a
moment, she was terrified he would, then he nodded.

"Sorry.  I didn't mean to bother you."  He turned away.

Dracena watched him go.  He didn't look back.

She stood in the entrance hall until he disappeared around the corner.
 Only then did she realize she had been holding her breath.  She let it
out slowly.  

*Well done,*  she thought.   *Very convincing.  * She hated herself.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Professor Snape's office smelled of dried herbs, old parchment, and
something faintly medicinal.  Dracena had never particularly liked the
smell.  Tonight was different.  She somehow found it comforting.  It was
familiar.  Predictable.  Nothing in this room was likely to ask her why
she hadn't answered an owl.  She wasn't going to have to break anyone's
heart in here.

Snape sat behind his desk, reading a piece of parchment.  Dracena stood
before him.  He had been silent for nearly a minute.

Finally, he looked up.  "Again."

Dracena closed her eyes.  "Professor..."

"Again, Miss Malfoy."

She opened her eyes.  

He watched her.

She straightened.  Her shoulders settled.  Her chin lifted.  The
expression she wore was one she'd practiced since childhood.

*"The Malfoy face."*

Her father wore it when dealing with people he considered beneath him.
 Her mother wore a gentler variation when entertaining guests she
privately despised.  Dracena had learned it before she'd learned long
division.

Snape studied her.  "Better."

She said nothing.

"Your difficulty." he continued, "is not that you will not lie."

Dracena looked at him.

"You are an accomplished liar, Miss Malfoy.  I suspect you have been
lying to your father about your academic pursuits for years."

"I have excellent marks."

"Yes.  Which makes your dishonesty all the more irritating."

She almost smiled.  Almost.

Snape leaned back.  "Your difficulty is that you wish to be understood."

Dracena's smile vanished.

"You wish Potter to recognize that you are concealing something.  You
wish him to see through your performance."

"That's not true."

Snape raised an eyebrow.  She looked away.

"Isn't it?"

Snape set down the parchment.  "Miss Malfoy, you must understand
something.  Potter cannot suspect that you are acting."

"I know."

"No.  I do not believe you do."  His voice remained quiet.  "Potter must
believe that the young woman he thought he knew was a fabrication."

Dracena stared at the floor.

"He must believe that everything you told him was a lie."

She swallowed.  "Professor..."

"And you must allow him to believe it."

Her eyes stung.  She blinked rapidly.

Snape watched her.  "You have spent sixteen years learning to conceal
your feelings.  Your mother and father have taught you to smile at
people you dislike, flatter people you despise, and conceal your
opinions when expressing them would be inconvenient."

Dracena looked up.  "Yes, sir."

"Then I suggest you put that education to use."

She nodded.

Snape studied her for another moment.  "How did your conversation with
Potter go?"

Dracena's stomach tightened.  "Well."

"Explain."

"I told him I was busy over Christmas."

"And?"

"That I couldn't be expected to answer every letter."

Snape waited.

Dracena looked down.

"I told him he was becoming obsessed."

Something changed in Snape's expression.  Not much, but enough.

"And how did he respond?"

"He apologized."

Snape was silent.

Dracena stared at her hands.  "He apologized." she repeated.

Her voice cracked.  She hated that.  She hated it more than anything.

Snape rose.  He walked toward the shelves behind his desk, examining the
contents as though searching for something.

When he spoke again, his voice was quieter.

"You will have to do worse."

Dracena shut her eyes.  "I know."

"Much worse."

"I know."

He turned.  "Look at me.  You have not yet received the Mark.  Until you
do, your role is to create distance.  Nothing more."

Dracena nodded.

"Once you have taken it, however, there can be no ambiguity.  Your
allegiance must be unmistakable."

"I understand."

"Do you?"

She met his eyes.  "Yes, Professor."

Snape studied her.

Then he reached into a drawer and withdrew a small black box.  He placed
it on the desk.

Dracena stared.  "What's that?"

"An ointment."

She blinked.  "An ointment?"

"The Mark is not applied painlessly."

Dracena looked at the box.

Snape continued.  "You will be expected to endure the ceremony without
complaint.  You will be watched."

She nodded.  "The ointment will help afterward."

Dracena looked at him.  "Thank you."

Snape's expression hardened.  "Do not thank me yet."

She lowered her eyes.  He returned to his chair.

"Now.  Again."

Dracena straightened.  She arranged her face.  Professor Snape began
teaching her how to break Harry Potter's heart.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## <span class="c12"></span>

## <span class="c12">The Mark</span>

The room was colder than Dracena had expected.  She wasn't certain why
that surprised her.  The manor was enormous, and many of its rooms were
difficult to heat in winter.

But this wasn't an ordinary cold.  It seemed to settle beneath her skin.
 The chamber was lit by candles arranged along the walls.  Their flames
burned steadily, without flickering, despite the occasional movement of
robes.

There were twelve people present.  Several wore masks.  She recognized
some of the others.

Avery.\
Nott.\
Her aunt Bellatrix.\
And her father.

Lucius Malfoy stood near the far wall, his silver-headed cane resting
against his leg.  He had been unusually quiet throughout the evening.

Dracena had expected him to be pleased.  After all, she was finally
joining the cause he'd served for so many years.  She was proving
herself worthy of the family name.  Yet when she'd approached him
earlier, he'd barely spoken.  He'd simply adjusted the collar of her
robes.  A strange gesture.  Almost tender.

"Stand straight." he'd murmured.

She did so.

He'd looked at her for a long moment, then he'd stepped away.  Now she
stood in the center of the chamber, waiting.

The air changed.  Dracena felt it before she saw him.  The conversations
stopped.  A figure emerged from the darkness at the far end of the room.

Tall.  Thin.  Almost skeletal.  His skin was unnaturally pale, stretched
tightly over a face that seemed to have forgotten what it meant to be
human.

His eyes were red.  His nose...

Dracena stopped herself from staring, and she lowered her head.

"Miss Malfoy."

The voice was high and cold.  It carried a faint hiss.  She had heard it
before, through the study door a few days ago.  It seemed like forever
ago.

"Your Lordship."  Someone nearby shifted.  Dracena wondered whether
she'd used the wrong form of address.  Then the figure laughed softly.

"Such exquisite manners."

She kept her head lowered.

"Your father has raised you well."

"Thank you, my Lord."

"Look at me."

She obeyed.

Voldemort studied her.

His eyes moved across her face.  Dracena had the unpleasant sensation
that he was examining something he intended to purchase.

"Sixteen years old."

"Yes, my Lord."

"And already so accomplished."

She said nothing.

"Your father speaks highly of you."

Dracena's heart lifted despite herself.  She glanced toward Lucius.  He
didn't smile.

Voldemort noticed.

"Does that please you?"

"Of course, my Lord.  Of course."

He moved closer.

"Your family has served me for many years."

"Yes, my Lord."

"Your father has made great sacrifices."

Dracena looked toward Lucius again.  Something in his expression made
her uneasy.

"Yes, my Lord."

"And now his daughter wishes to follow him."

She straightened.  "I would be honored."

Voldemort smiled.  It was a terrible thing to see.

"Would you?"

"Yes, my Lord."

"Then let us see whether you are worthy."

He extended his hand.  "Your arm."

Dracena hesitated for only a fraction of a second, then she offered her
left arm.

Voldemort tilted his head.  "The other."

She switched arms.  He took her right wrist.  His fingers were cold.
 Not merely cool, but cold in a way living fingers shouldn't be.  He
turned her arm upward.

Dracena stared at the pale skin of her forearm.  She had seen the Mark
before, on her father.\
On others.\
She knew what was coming.  Knowing didn't help.

Voldemort raised his wand.  "Do you swear loyalty to me?"

"I do, my Lord."

"Above all others?"

Dracena felt her father's eyes on her.  "Yes, my Lord."

"Above family?"

Her heart stumbled.  She looked toward Lucius.  He stood perfectly
still.  Dracena returned her gaze to Voldemort.

"Yes, my Lord."

"Above friendship?"

Harry's face appeared in her mind.  She forced it away.

"Yes, my Lord."

Voldemort's smile widened.

"Above love?"

For one terrible instant, she couldn't speak.  Harry.  His smile.  His
ridiculous hair.  The way he'd held her hand beneath the table when he
thought nobody was watching.  The way he'd kissed her.

The way he'd said...   *I love you.*

Dracena swallowed.  "Yes, my Lord."

Voldemort watched her, then he pressed his wand against her arm.

Pain exploded through her.  Dracena had never experienced anything like
it.  It was as though something had seized the bones of her arm and
begun twisting them apart.  Her knees threatened to buckle.  She locked
them.

Her vision blurred.  The room tilted.  She could hear someone breathing.
 A strange, ragged sound.  It took her a moment to realize it was her.

She would not scream.  She would **not.**

The pain spread upward, into her shoulder, across her chest.  For a
moment she thought she might faint.  Abruptly, it stopped.

Voldemort released her wrist.  Dracena staggered.  She caught herself
before she fell.  Her arm burned.

She looked down.  The Dark Mark lay against her skin.

Black.\
Ugly.\
Unmistakable.

A skull.  A serpent emerging from its mouth.

She stared.  It was real.  She had done it.

She was now a Death Eater.

Voldemort watched her.  "Well?"

Dracena raised her head.  She smiled.

Not broadly.  Not foolishly.  A small, proud smile.  The smile of a
young aristocrat who had just received a tremendous honor.

"Thank you, my Lord."

Voldemort laughed.  "Excellent."

He turned toward the others.  "Behold.  Another Malfoy."

There was applause.  Dracena bowed her head.  Her arm was still
trembling.  She concealed it beneath her sleeve.

Across the room, Lucius Malfoy began to applaud.  Slowly.  Deliberately.
 He watched his daughter smile.

She looked magnificent.  Composed.  Proud.  Every inch a Malfoy.

He should have been pleased.  He had spent sixteen years preparing her
for precisely this kind of moment.

Yet something was wrong.  He knew these people.  Not the way Dracena
knew them.  He knew what they did when the masks came off.  He knew what
happened to people who disappointed them.  He knew how quickly loyalty
became a demand for sacrifice, and now his daughter stood among them.

His little girl was wearing their Mark.

He looked at Voldemort.  For the first time in many years, Lucius Malfoy
wondered whether he had made a terrible mistake.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Dracena made it back to Hogwarts without crying.  She made it through
the entrance hall.\
Up the stairs.\
Along the corridor.\
Past a pair of laughing Ravenclaws.\
She even managed to smile politely at Professor Flitwick.

She reached her dormitory.  Closed the door and secured it

She pulled up her sleeve.

The Mark was still there.  Of course it was.

She stared at it.  The ointment Snape had given her sat unopened on her
bedside table.  She didn't want to touch it.

She didn't want to touch anything.

She sat on the bed.  She thought of her father.  She thought of
Voldemort.  She thought of the people who had applauded.

And then she thought of Harry.  Harry, who had apologized to her in the
entrance hall.  Harry, who still believed she was merely being
difficult.  Harry, who would probably try to talk to her again tomorrow.

She imagined his face when he saw the Mark .\
She imagined him asking why.\
She imagined telling him.   *It was all a lie.*

*Her breath caught.  She bent forward.  For several seconds she couldn't
move, then a sound escaped her.*

A small, broken noise.

She clapped a hand over her mouth, but it didn't help.

She began to cry.

She cried until she couldn't breathe properly, until her throat hurt,
until her entire body shook.

She wanted Harry.  That was the worst part.  She wanted him to come
through the door.\
To sit beside her.\
To put his arms around her.\
To tell her everything would be all right.\
And she knew that if he did, she would tell him everything.

She would tell him about Voldemort.  About Snape.   About the Mark .
 About what she was trying to do.  And then all of it would have been
for nothing.

She curled onto her side.  Her right arm lay across the pillow.   The
Mark  stared back at her.  She pulled her sleeve down.

Tomorrow, she would have to show it to Harry.\
Tomorrow, she would have to make him hate her.

Dracena buried her face in the pillow.  "Stupid Potter." she whispered.
 But there was no anger in it.

Not anymore.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## <span class="c12"></span>

## <span class="c12">The Performance</span>

<div class="illustration">

![The Performance](Images/The%20Performance.jpg)

</div>

Pansy Parkinson had always possessed a remarkable ability to make
breakfast unpleasant.  It was, Dracena thought, almost admirable.

Dracena hated it.

She sat at the Slytherin table with her back straight, her hair
immaculate, and her face arranged into the expression generations of
Malfoys had developed specifically for occasions when they wished the
rest of humanity would stop existing.

Professor Snape had called it useful.  He had also called it
"insufferable."  Dracena had chosen to interpret that as praise.

She hadn't seen Harry yet this morning.  She remembered earlier in the
week when she called him "Obsessed."  She remembered a few days ago when
he tried to kiss her, and she stopped him.  "My makeup!" she said.  "I
don't want it smudged.  Honestly, Potter..."

Harry hadn't seen her.  Dracena had been relieved, then disappointed.
 She was ashamed of being relieved.

Hermione approached.  Dracena saw her before Hermione saw her.  Her
stomach tightened.  Hermione was carrying three books because breakfast
was an insufficient reason to stop studying.

Dracena looked back at her plate.

Pansy noticed Hermione.  Unfortunately, Pansy noticed everything that
might potentially become someone else's problem.

"Well, look who's back."

Hermione stopped.  "Pansy."

"Granger."

Hermione's eyes moved past her, to Dracena.  Dracena continued buttering
her toast, ignoring what she knew was coming.

Pansy smiled.  "Did you have a nice Muggle Christmas?"

"Yes, actually."

"How quaint."

Hermione rolled her eyes, and started to walk away.

Pansy wasn't finished.  "Did they give you books?"

Hermione stopped.  "Yes."

"How exciting.  Perhaps one of them can teach you how to stop being such
a stupid Mudblood."

Several nearby conversations died.  Dracena's knife stopped.  Hermione
looked at Pansy, then at Dracena.  She didn't ask.  She didn't need to.
 Dracena knew exactly what Hermione expected.

Two weeks ago she might have reminded her that Hermione had beaten both
of them on the last Transfiguration examination.  Earlier in the week?
 She spent the last few nights with Professor Snape learning how to lie.

*"You control Dracena Malfoy."*

She put down her knife.  "Oh, come on, Pansy."

Hermione relaxed.  Dracena saw it.  That made what was coming next feel
much worse.

"Hermione is one of the best students in the school."  Pansy frowned.
 Dracena looked directly at Hermione.  "She's certainly not stupid."
 Hermione's mouth almost became a smile.  Dracena waited. "Even if she
**is**  a  Mudblood."

Hermione's expression went blank.  Dracena felt something inside herself
tear.  She dared not show it.  "And when things finally change around
here, being clever isn't going to make people like her matter."

Silence fell over the entire cafeteria.  Dracena could feel people
listening.  Good.  They needed to, for this performance to work.

Hermione stared at her.  "What?"

Dracena shrugged.  "You heard me."

"Dracena."  The hurt in Hermione's voice was almost enough.

Almost.  Dracena heard Snape again.

*No hidden messages.  No clever little phrase only the two of you
understand.  * <span class="c2 c4">Nothing.</span>

So she smiled.  "I had a rather educational Christmas."

Hermione stared at her.  Pansy was staring too.  Everyone was.  Dracena
could feel the weight of it.  This was the moment.  She had known it was
coming.  Her left hand moved to her right sleeve.  She hesitated, then
pulled it upward.  The Dark Mark lay against the pale skin of her
