forearm.  Black.  Ugly.  Unmistakable.

Hermione's mouth opened.  Had the laws of anatomy permitted her jaw to
hit the floor, Dracena suspected it would have.

Across the hall, Harry Potter heard the slur Mudblood.  He hadn't been
paying attention.  Ron was explaining something about a Chudley Cannons
trade that Harry didn't follow and didn't particularly care about.
 Harry had been nodding at intervals and wondering where Dracena was.
 Something seemed different about her after Christmas.  He wondered what
he might have done wrong.  Still...  He'd expected her to find him.  Or
he'd expected to find her.  Then he'd heard  the slur, "Mudblood."
 Harry turned.  Pansy Parkinson.

Of course.  Then, he saw Dracena.

Harry got up, and noticed  Hermione's face.  He realized Pansy hadn't
said it.  Dracena had.  He stopped.  She was holding up her sleeve.
 Then Harry saw the tattoo.  All the colour left his face.

Ron followed his stare.  "What the ...?"

Harry didn't hear him.  The world was dissolving around him.  He was
already moving.  Dracena saw him coming.  Her heart began pounding so
hard she wondered whether anyone else could hear it.  Harry stopped
several feet away.  His eyes were fixed on her arm.  Then her face.
 Then her arm again.

"Dracena..."

*Don't...*  she said to herself.   *Please don't.  Not here.  Not like
this.  I'm not ready for this...*

"I thought..." Harry said, voice unsure, trailing off.

Dracena smiled.  "What, Potter?"  He flinched.  Good.  No.  Not good.
 Necessary.

She stood.  "You thought what?"

Harry looked completely lost.  Dracena had seen Harry frightened.  She
had seen him angry.  She had seen him embarrassed, jealous, reckless,
arrogant, exhausted and once so happy he'd picked her up and spun her
around until she'd threatened to hex him.  She had never seen him look
like this.  "You thought I could actually have feelings for someone like
**you** ?"

His mouth opened.  Nothing came out.  Dracena wanted to die.  Instead,
she laughed.  A small, dismissive laugh.  Perfect.  "That's cute."

Hermione whispered, "Dracena, what are you doing?"  Dracena ignored her.

Harry's eyes hadn't left hers.  "What about..."  He stopped.  Dracena
knew exactly what he had almost said.

*What about everything you told me?\
What about the common room?\
What about her father?\
What about "I love you too?"\
What about...\
We don't lie about not knowing.*

Dracena couldn't let him finish.  "It was a test, Potter."

Harry stared at her.  "A test?"

"Of course."

She raised her marked arm slightly.  "And as you can see..."  She
smiled.  She felt dirty.  Filthy.  She wanted to sit in the shower and
scrub her skin away until it bled, she felt so dirty.  "I passed with
flying colours."

Harry's face crumpled, then he turned.

"Harry..."  Ron said something she didn't catch.  Harry ran.  He made it
six steps.  His foot caught the end of a bench.  He went down
spectacularly.  Someone gasped.  A few Slytherins laughed.

Dracena didn't.

Harry scrambled upright so quickly that he nearly fell again.  Ron went
after him.

"Harry!"

Harry shoved through the doors and disappeared.

Dracena stood motionless.

*You control Dracena Malfoy.*

Her fingernails were cutting into her palm.

*You control Dracena Malfoy.*

Hermione was still there.  Dracena looked at her.  Hermione's eyes were
wet.  That was somehow worse than Harry running.  Hermione looked at the
Mark on her arm, Then Dracena.  Then the Mark again.

"You..."

Dracena raised an eyebrow.

Hermione swallowed.  "You Slytherin cunt."

The Great Hall went silent.  Even Pansy looked impressed.

Hermione picked up the book she'd dropped.  She gathered her things
carefully, turned, and slowly walked out of the Great Hall without
looking back.  Dracena watched her go, then sat down.  Pansy stared at
her.

"Malfoy."

"What?"

"That was..."  Pansy searched for the word.  She didn't find it.

Dracena picked up her toast.  "Pass the marmalade."

------------------------------------------------------------------------

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## <span class="c12"></span>

## <span class="c12">The Depression</span>

<div class="illustration">

![The Depression](Images/The%20Depression.jpg)

</div>

Harry stopped going to breakfast.  He still went to lunch.  Usually.
 Dinner depended on whether Ron or Hermione physically dragged him
there.

For the first week, everyone tried talking to him.  That was a mistake.

Ron tried anger .  "She's a bloody Death Eater, mate.  Forget her."  \
Harry had left the room.

Hermione tried reason .  "What she did was horrible, Harry, but you
cannot let it destroy you."\
Harry had left the room.

Ginny tried distraction.\
Harry had left the room.

Neville eventually discovered the most effective strategy.  He sat
beside Harry and said nothing.  So they did that sometimes.  Neville
would sit there, in complete silence.  Sometimes, he'd be working with a
plant.  He just sat next to Harry.  That seemed to help, somehow.

Harry's schoolwork deteriorated.  Now, Harry had never been Hermione,
but assignments became late.  Then incomplete.  Professor McGonagall
returned one Transfiguration essay with a grade Harry had not received
since second year.  She asked him to remain after class.

"Mr Potter."

"I'm fine."

"I did not ask."

Harry looked away.

McGonagall regarded him over her spectacles.

"Whatever has happened, you are still expected to perform to the best of
your ability."

"Yes, Professor."

"You are presently not doing so."

"No, Professor."

Her expression softened.

"See that you begin."

Harry nodded.

Quidditch went next.  Harry missed a catch he should have made
blindfolded.  During the following practice, he flew directly past the
Snitch because he'd been staring at the Slytherin stands.  Dracena
wasn't even there.  The captain landed beside him afterward.

"Harry."

"What"

"Harry!"

"I'm fine."

"You keep saying that."

"I am."

"You're off Saturday's roster."

"What?"

"You're now Alternate."

"You can't..."

"I can.  And I just did."

Harry stared at him.

"For how long?"

"Until I get my Seeker back."

Harry wanted to argue.  He didn't have the energy.  "Fine."

------------------------------------------------------------------------

"I'm worried about Harry."  Ron was sitting on the floor of the
Gryffindor common room.  Hermione looked up from her book.  Neville was
repotting something that hissed whenever he touched its roots.

"We're all worried about Harry." Hermione said.

"No, I mean really worried."

Hermione closed the book.

Ron stared into the fire.

"He was never this bad over Cho."

"Cho didn't tell him she loved him and then ran off to join the Death
Eaters." Hermione said.

Ron grimaced.  "Yeah."

Neville carefully pressed soil around the plant.  "He'll pull through."

Ron looked at him.  "How do you know?"

Neville shrugged.  "Because I refuse to believe anything else."

Hermione stared at him, then smiled faintly. "That's not very logical."

"No." Neville said, while carefully placing more soil around the hissing
plant.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Harry did pull through.  Slowly.  There was no morning when he woke and
discovered Dracena Malfoy no longer mattered.  He almost wished there
had been.  Instead, he simply became tired of being miserable.  The
first assignment he completed properly was Potions.  That annoyed him.
 Snape returned it with:  Acceptable.  At last.  Harry nearly tore it in
half.  Instead he kept it.

His marks improved.  Slowly.

He returned to Quidditch practice.

The first time he caught the Snitch again, nobody made a great fuss.
 The captain simply nodded.

"Again."  

Harry flew again.  He caught it easily.

Life resumed.  Mostly.

A Ravenclaw girl named Amelia Foster asked him out in March.  She caught
him outside Charms.

"Harry?"

"Yeah?"

She was pretty.  Harry noticed that objectively.  Dark hair.  Nice
smile.  Under other circumstances...

"Would you maybe want to go to Hogsmeade with me Saturday?"

Harry stared at her.  She blushed.  "I mean, just us."  There was an
awkward pause.

"No."

Her face fell.  Harry immediately realized he'd said it too quickly.

"It's fine." she said, walking away too quickly.

"I'm just not..."

"You don't have to explain."  Harry stood there feeling like a jerk.

Ron appeared from around the corner.

"What was that?"

"Nothing."

"Did Amelia Foster just ask you out?"

Harry picked up his bag.

"No."

"She absolutely did."

"Then why'd you ask?"

Ron stared after Amelia, then at Harry.

"She's pretty."

"I know."

"She's smart."

"I know."

"She likes Quidditch."

"Yeah."

"Harry." Ron said.

"What?"

Ron hesitated.  "Nothing."

Harry walked away.  He wasn't interested.  That was all.  Maybe someday.
 Just not now.  He didn't know for how long.  The thought hurt less than
it used to.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Dracena watched him from across the Great Hall.  She had become very
good at not watching Harry Potter.  She knew where he sat without
looking.  She knew when he entered a room  from the sound of Ron's
voice.  Knew when he laughed.  That had taken the longest to return.
 His laugh.

The first time she'd heard it after everything, she'd nearly cried.  She
hadn't.  Dracena had also become very good at not crying.  She watched
him now only because he wasn't looking.  Harry stood near the end of the
Gryffindor table, eating an apple while Neville told him something.

Harry laughed.

Dracena looked down.\
There were things Harry didn't know.\
Things Hermione didn't know.\
Names.\
Dates.\
Places.\
Conversations overheard.\
A Ministry office that had secretly been evacuated before something
terrible could happen.\
Someone who had been warned not to take their usual route home.\
Other things.\
Small things.\
A few large things.\
Things Dracena herself didn't entirely understand.

She passed them to Snape.  Snape never praised her.  Of course he
didn't.

Occasionally he said:  "This is useful."  Dracena had learned this was
equivalent to anyone else leaping onto a table and applauding.  It
mattered.

People were alive who might not otherwise have been.  She reminded
herself of that whenever Harry laughed.   Whenever Hermione walked past
without looking at her.  Whenever Father wrote to tell her how proud he
was that she'd finally remembered who she was.  That one hurt
differently.

Dracena looked across the Great Hall again.  Harry was smiling.

*"That's why."*

She repeated it silently.

*"That's why."*

------------------------------------------------------------------------

It happened on a Tuesday.  Nothing important should happen on a Tuesday.
 Dracena was leaving Ancient Runes.  Harry was coming the other way.
 Neither saw the other around the corner.

They collided.  Books went everywhere.

"Bloody hell."

"Sorry."

They both crouched automatically.  Harry gathered his things.

Dracena reached for a textbook.  Harry picked up one of hers.

"Here, you..."

He looked up, into her eyes.

Everything stopped.  Dracena froze.  They were very close.  Closer than
they'd been since January.  Harry's hand was still resting on her book,
inches away from her hand.

For one terrible second, neither moved.  Dracena forgot every lesson
Snape had taught her.

Forgot Voldemort.\
Forgot her father.\
Forgot the Mark hidden beneath her sleeve.

It was just Harry.  Messy hair.  Beautiful hair.  She missed running her
hands through it.  Green eyes.  She missed staring into them right
before he...

*STOP THAT*  she thought to herself.

He looked older.  That frightened her.  Something behind his eyes had
changed.   *She'd*  done that.  Dracena's mouth moved before her mind
caught it.

"Harry..."

His face changed.  Dracena knew immediately that she'd made a mistake.
 Not operationally.  Nobody was around.  Something worse.  She'd hurt
him again.

Harry looked at her.  For a moment she thought he might say something.
 Demand an explanation.  Instead he put her book down, and stood up.

"Fuck you, Dracena."  He turned and walked away, slowly.

Dracena remained on the floor.  She listened to his footsteps disappear
around the corner.  One of her pieces of parchment had landed several
feet away.  She stared at it.

Her vision blurred. "No.  Not here." she demanded of herself.  Dracena
closed her eyes.  Breathed.

*"You control Dracena Malfoy."*

She wanted to cry.

*"You control Dracena Malfoy."*

She wanted to run after him.  Tell him everything.  Tell him she loved
him.  Tell him she'd never stopped.  Tell him that every horrible thing
she'd said in the Great Hall had been rehearsed with Snape until she'd
been able to say it without shaking.

She needed to tell him the Mark on her arm made her sick.\
Tell him she'd watched him fall apart and had been forbidden to help.\
Tell him people were alive because of what she was doing.\
Tell him she was sorry.\
Tell him to wait for her.\
Tell him she didn't know whether she had any right to ask.

Dracena opened her eyes.  She did none of those things, because Harry's
hatred hurt.

But Harry being dead would hurt more.\
Hermione being dead would hurt more.\
Another Edwin Pritchard being dead would hurt more.

And somewhere, Voldemort believed Harry Potter had learned to hate
Dracena Malfoy.

Good.  Let him.  

Dracena picked up the last piece of parchment.  She stood.  Straightened
her robes, lifted her chin, and walked to class.

Pansy Parkinson, under the effects of a Disillusionment Charm, watched
Dracena.  Pansy was seconds away from sneaking into Professor Flitwick's
office to steal the answer key to tomorrow's exam, when she happened to
notice the exchange.  

"Huh.  Interesting..." she said to herself.  She tapped the Mark on her
forearm.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## <span class="c12"></span>

## <span class="c12">The Capture</span>

<div class="illustration">

![The Capture](Images/The%20Capture.jpg)

</div>

Dracena woke because someone was touching her shoulder.  She made an
irritated noise and pulled the blankets higher.  The hand shook her
again.

"Dracena."  the voice called out, quietly.

"Mfff." she responded, sleepily.

"Dracena."

"I'm sleeping.  Whatever it is, it can wait until morning."

A giggle.  Dracena opened one eye.

Pansy Parkinson stood beside her bed.  She was smiling.  She was smiling
far too widely.  Dracena opened the other eye.

"What's up, Sleepyhead?" Pansy asked.

Dracena faked a yawn.  Then she noticed the two girls standing behind
Pansy.  Both were Slytherins.  Both played Quidditch.  Both were built
like wardrobes.  They were also smiling far too widely.

Dracena's sleepiness evaporated suddenly.  There was a fourth girl
behind them.  Maribel Fawley.  Sixth year.  Spectacles.  Always carried
at least four books.  Had once corrected Professor Binns on the date of
a seventeenth-century goblin uprising and then produced three sources
proving she was right.  Dracena privately thought of her as Hermione
Granger if Hermione had been born into an old pure-blood family and used
her brain for sinister things.

Maribel wasn't smiling.  Somehow that was worse.

Pansy held something up.  "What's this?" she asked.

Dracena looked.  Her heart stopped.  An old iPhone.  Cracked along one
corner of the screen, many generations out of date.  And unmistakably
hers.  Or, unmistakably the one Snape gave to her.  Dracena's brain
suddenly woke up fully.  She faked another yawn. "Oh.  That."

Pansy's smile widened.

"It's just a weird Muggle thing I found." Dracena said.

"Really?"

"Yes."

Pansy turned it over in her hand.

"Interesting."

Dracena shrugged.

"It is, actually.  Muggles have come up with some rather clever things."

"I know." Pansy said, grinning that unnerving grin.  That wasn't the
answer Dracena expected.

Pansy leaned closer.  "It's an iPhone."

Dracena said nothing.

"I'm not as ignorant about all things Muggle as your family is."  Pansy
was enjoying this, far too much.

Dracena looked at the phone, then Pansy, then the two teenage siege
engines behind her, then Maribel.

*Well.  Crap.  * She was screwed, but panic was information, and Dracena
had no intention of providing any.  She sat up and brushed her hair away
from her face.

"Fine.  It's an iPhone."

"And you have one."

"Apparently."

"Why?"

"I told you.  I found it interesting."

"In your locked trunk?"

"I have many interesting things in my locked trunk."

"Under a false bottom?"

Dracena shrugged.  "I didn't particularly want Father finding  it."

Pansy laughed softly.  "Uh huh." Sarcasm dripped from her voice.  Pansy
looked down at the phone, and Dracena watched her.  There was still a
chance.  The thing was locked.  She'd been careful about that.  And...

"I wonder who's numbers you have in it?"

"Numbers?" Dracena said, unconvincingly.

Pansy's grin became almost feral. "Come now.  You want me to believe you
don't know how to use this?  Even though it's charged up and locked?
 There isn't any electricity in Hogwarts, you know.  It's 90% charged."

"Crap, crap, crap, crap, crap, crap."  Dracena said to herself.

"How about you show us what you have in here ?"  Pansy said, grinning.

"Certainly."  Dracena held out her hand.  "Give it here."

Pansy laughed.  "Uh, No.  I have a much safer idea."

Behind her, Maribel stepped forward.  Dracena looked at her.  Maribel
produced a small vial.  Dracena's stomach dropped.

Oh No.  No no no no no.

Maribel uncorked it, and she drank.

Dracena threw off the blankets.

One of the Quidditch girls moved, and pushed Dracena back.  Maribel
doubled over.  Her body shifted.  Her short, dark hair lengthened and
turned pale blonde.  Her face twisted and reformed.  Seconds later,
Dracena Malfoy was standing beside Dracena Malfoy's bed.

The other Dracena adjusted her nightdress.  "Merlin." she said in
Dracena's voice. "Your hair is ridiculous."  Under other circumstances
Dracena would have been offended.

Pansy handed her the phone.  The false Dracena pressed her thumb against
it.  The screen unlocked.  Pansy looked down as Maribel began touching
the screen, and scrolling.

Dracena could hear her own heartbeat.

*"You control Dracena Malfoy."  * Dracena concentrated on controlling
Dracena Malfoy.

They had the phone.  They managed to unlock it.  Still, that didn't mean
they understood it.  That didn't mean...

Maribel stopped scrolling.  "Oh." she said, theatrically.

Pansy looked over her shoulder.

"Oh, **dear.** "

Dracena worked furiously to keep her face blank.  Pansy looked at her.

"That's quite a lot of messages.  All of them rather  recent."

"It's a phone.  That's generally what they're for."

"Uh huh.  And why are you actually using this one?"

Pansy stepped closer to Maribel and looked over at the screen.  Maribel
grinned and showed her what she found.

"Names."

Dracena said nothing.

"Dates.  Places."

Dracena's mouth had gone dry.

"Things the Dark Lord apparently intends to do."

There was no point denying what was on the screen, so Dracena didn't.
 She kept quiet.

Pansy's smile disappeared.  That frightened Dracena considerably more
than the smile had.

"And all of them." Pansy said softly, "being sent to someone."

Dracena looked at Maribel wearing her face.\
At the phone in "her" hand.\
At the two behemoths.\
She considered screaming.  Bad plan.\
Her wand.  On the bedside table.  No, too far away..\
Escape through the window.  No.  Also too far.\
Four against one.  Worse, she was in bed, being blocked by human
Quidditch dummies.

Pansy tilted her head.  "So."

Dracena looked at her.

"Who have you been talking to?"

Dracena smiled.  It wasn't much of one, but it was a smile.

"Pansy."

"Yes?"

"Go kiss a Blast-Ended Skrewt."

Pansy's smile returned.  "Thought so."  Her wand was already in her
hand.  Dracena moved, but it was too late.

" **Somnum dormientis.** "  Pansy's wand flicked sideways, then tapped
Dracena on her forehead.

The room tilted as the spell hit her full on.  Dracena reached for the
bedside table.  Her arm suddenly weighed a hundred pounds.  She tried to
shout.  Nothing coherent came out.  She felt the drowsiness slowly
descend on her, like a theater curtain.  Her vision blurred.  Her limbs
stopped obeying her.  She fell sideways onto the mattress.   *Snape.  *
She had to...

<span class="c2 c4">Harry.</span>

No.  Don't think about Harry.  Don't...\
\
Someone rolled her onto her stomach.  Dracena felt her wrists pulled
together.  Rope tightened around them.  She tried to fight.  Her body
barely moved.  She fought to stay awake.  Voices seemed to come from
very far away as sleep slowly claimed her.

"Hurry."

"I am!"

"Get her legs."

"Where's the..."

Darkness descended over her face.

For one confused instant Dracena thought the spell had finally taken
her.  Then she smelled laundry soap.  A pillowcase.  They were pulling a
bloody pillowcase over her head.  Some distant, absurd part of her mind
was offended by the indignity.

Her hands were bound behind her back.  She tried one last time to move.
 Couldn't.

The darkness became warm.  Heavy.  Dracena heard Pansy's voice.

"Sleep tight."

Then sleep claimed her.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## <span class="c12"></span>

## <span class="c12">The Concequences</span>

"I told him."

Dracena's voice barely sounded like hers anymore.

Someone stood in front of her.  She couldn't quite make out who.  Her
vision kept going soft around the edges.

"What?"

"I told him."

A hand caught her hair and pulled her head back.  Dracena made a small
squeal of pain.

"Everything?"

"Yes."

"Everything, Malfoy?"

"Yes."

"Who did you tell?" a voice demanded.

"Snape."

The hand released her.

Dracena's head fell forward.

"Professor Snape."

"How long?"

"Since January."

"And Dumbledore?"

"Didn't know."

"Your father?"

Dracena's eyes moved.  Lucius stood against the far wall.  He had been
standing there for most of it.  She couldn't remember exactly when
they'd brought him in.  Before the second Cruciatus, perhaps.  Maybe
after.  Time had become unreliable.

Father looked immaculate except for his face.  His face looked terrible.
 Dracena looked away.

"He didn't know."

Someone laughed.  "No." another voice said. "He certainly didn't."

Dracena closed her eyes.

"What did you give Snape?"

"Names."

"Whose?"

She told them.

"Locations?"

She told them.

"Which operations?"

She told them.

There was no heroic resistance.  No clever lie.  No brave declaration
that they could kill her before she betrayed anyone.  Dracena Malfoy had
imagined, once, that perhaps she would be capable of something like
that.

She wasn't.  They had hurt her.  She had tried not to talk.  Then they
had hurt her more.  Then she had talked.  It had not taken particularly
long.

Snape had warned her.  " *Do you know what the Dark Lord does to people
who lie to him?"\
"No."\
"I do."\*
Now she did too.

"Who did you warn about Selwyn?"

"Snape."

"The Ministry raid in February?"

"Snape."

"Yaxley's contact?"

"Snape."

"The safe house in York?"

"Snape."

"The shipment from Albania?"

"Snape."

Each answer made someone angrier.  That was when Dracena began to
understand.  They already knew.  Maybe not everything, but enough.  This
wasn't an interrogation anymore.  Perhaps it hadn't been for some time.
 It was revenge.  They were angry.  Dracena had cost them people.\
Money.\
Plans.\
Safe houses.\
Months of work.

People had escaped because of her.  People who were supposed to die, had
lived.  Death Eaters who were supposed to disappear quietly into
respectable society had instead found Aurors waiting for them.  She had
done more damage than she'd known.  That realization should have made
her proud.  At the moment it mostly made her terrified.

One of the Death Eaters crouched in front of her.  "Do you know how many
friends you cost me?"  Dracena breathed through her mouth.

"No."

"Three."

"I'm..."

She stopped herself.

*"Sorry"*  seemed inappropriate.  Besides, she wasn't.

The man smiled.  "Let's see whether you can remember anything else."

Dracena's eyes widened.

"I told you everything."

"I know."

That was the worst answer possible.

"No."

His wand rose.  "No, please.  I told you..."

" **Crucio** ."

There were no thoughts after that.  Only pain.  When it stopped, Dracena
realized she was on the floor.  She didn't remember falling.  Someone
was crying.  It took her a moment to realize it was her.  She tasted
blood.  A boot nudged her onto her back.

Dracena stared at the ceiling.  "I told you." she whispered.

"I know."

Another laugh.  Dracena turned her head.  Father was still there.  His
fists were clenched.  His face was white.

And he was furious.\
At them.\
At her.\
At himself.

"You've established your point." Lucius said.  Nobody answered him.
 "You have the information."  

"We're deciding whether she has more."

"She doesn't."

"How would you know?"

Lucius's jaw tightened.

"Apparently I wouldn't."

That hurt more than Dracena expected.  Father looked at her.  There were
a thousand things Dracena wanted to say.

*I'm sorry.\
I had to.\
You were wrong.\
I still love you.\
Please don't hate me.*

Instead she whispered:

"Father, please..."

His face hardened.  Dracena looked away.  A Death Eater laughed.
 "Perhaps another round will improve her memory."

Lucius moved.  Only half a step.  Several wands immediately pointed
toward him.  He stopped.

"Enough."  The door opened.  Everyone became still.  Dracena didn't need
to look.    She knew it was the Dark Lord.  

Voldemort entered.  He regarded Dracena on the floor with mild interest.
 "How disappointing."

Dracena hated that voice.

"All that promise."  He walked around her.  "All that pride."

Dracena tried to push herself upright.  Her arms shook with fatigue and
pain.  She fell down on her back again.

Voldemort looked toward Lucius.  "And all that confidence in your
daughter."

Lucius said nothing.

"You insisted she was not a resource."  His smile was thin.  "In
hindsight, I should have listened." he smiled, that awful, terrible
smile.

Lucius' eyes moved to Dracena.  Something ugly passed through his
expression.

"You betrayed us."

Dracena closed her eyes.

"You betrayed **me** ."  She opened her eyes again and tried to speak.
 Couldn't.

"Why?"

Dracena almost answered, Then Voldemort spoke.  "Does it matter?"

Lucius looked at him.  "It matters to me."

"How touching."

Voldemort turned toward Dracena.  "Perhaps she can explain after we've
finished."

One of the Death Eaters raised his wand again.  Lucius's voice became
very quiet.  " **Do not** ."

The man paused.  Voldemort smiled.

"Lucius."

"She has told you everything."

"Perhaps."

"You have made your point."

"I don't believe I have."

Voldemort looked at Dracena.

"Again."

The man's wand moved, then the world disappeared.

This time, it wasn't with pain.  Everything just went dark

Dracena blinked.  She was still awake.  Still alive.  She just couldn't
see.  Everything was absolute black.  Someone shouted.

"What the.."

"LIGHT!"

"Lumos!"

Nothing happened.

**"AURORS!  DROP YOUR WANDS!"**

Chaos.  Dracena heard an explosion.  Someone screamed.  A door
shattered.  Spells cracked through the darkness.

"Peruvian Darkness Powder!"

"Clear the room!"

"Destroy everything!"

"Move!"

Footsteps went in every direction.  Someone crashed into a table.  Glass
shattered.  Dracena curled instinctively against the floor.

Hands touched her.  She screamed.

"Dracena."  

Father.  She froze.

"It's me."

Something pulled at the ropes around her wrists.  

A blade?  No.  A spell.  She couldn't see.

"Father?"

"Quiet."

The ropes loosened.  Dracena pulled one hand free, then the other.  Her
wrists burned.  Lucius found her face in the darkness.  His hands were
shaking.  She had never felt her father's hands shake before.

"I love you, Dracena."

She stopped breathing.

"I'm sorry."

"Father..."

Something slammed into him.  Lucius disappeared.  Dracena heard bodies
hit the floor.

"Father!"

"RUN!"

She didn't know whether the voice was his.

A spell struck the wall beside her.  Stone exploded across her face.
 Dracena crawled.

Someone stepped on her hand.  She screamed.  Kept moving.

She found a wall.  Followed it.

Someone grabbed her robes.  She twisted away.

Another explosion.

For a few seconds she saw light through the darkness.

Red.\
Green.\
Gold.\
Figures moving.

Aurors.  Death Eaters.  Blackness swallowed everything again.  Dracena
stumbled through a doorway.

"Father!"

No answer.  She kept moving.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

The Aurors had surprise .  For perhaps three minutes, it was enough.

Until it wasn't.

The Death Eaters knew the building.  They knew the exits.  They knew
which rooms mattered and which evidence had to burn.  By the time the
Darkness Powder began to thin, half the documents in the hideout were
already on fire.

A masked Death Eater blasted an Auror through a doorway.  Another
collapsed the staircase.  Someone shouted for retreat.

The raid had failed.  Not completely.  Nothing involving that much fire
could be considered completely unsuccessful, but the Aurors were
withdrawing.

Dracena realized this when two of them ran past her.

"Go!"

One grabbed her arm.  Dracena tried to follow, but her leg buckled.  The
Auror caught her, then a curse struck him in the back.  He fell.

Dracena hit the floor beside him.  "No." she whispered.  She crawled
toward him.

A hand caught the back of her robes.  Dracena kicked.

"LET GO!"

Someone dragged her backward.  The last Auror disappeared through the
doorway.

A spell hit the wall.  The doorway collapsed.  Dracena screamed.

"No!"

Dust filled the corridor.  The hand holding her tightened.  Dracena
turned.  A grotesque face.

Voldemort.

The darkness had mostly cleared now.  He looked almost amused.

"So close."  He said, in a serpentine voice.

Dracena stopped struggling. "No."

"So very close."  He looked toward the ruined doorway.  "Your rescuers
appear to have abandoned you."

Dracena glared at him.  Voldemort smiled.

"Where is Lucius?"  Nobody answered.  That smile disappeared.  "Find
him."

They did.  Or, Lucius found them first.  He stepped through the smoke
and dust.  His robes were torn and filthy, with black soot and dirt.
 His hair had come loose.  There was blood along one side of his face.
 His wand was already raised.  Dracena had never seen her father look
less like Lucius Malfoy.  Or more like him.

"Let her go."

Voldemort turned.  "Ah."

Dracena stared.

"Father."

Lucius didn't look at her.  "Leave my daughter alone."

"Lucius."

"If you have a grievance, you will address it to me."

Voldemort's expression became almost curious.  "Your daughter betrayed
you."

"Yes."

"She betrayed everything you claim to believe."

"Yes."

"She has cost us lives."

Lucius's jaw tightened.  "I am aware."

"And still you defend her?"

Lucius finally looked at Dracena.  She couldn't read his face.  "She is
my daughter."

Voldemort laughed.  It wasn't loud.  That made it worse.

"Oh, Lucius."  He stepped closer.  "You still misunderstand."

Lucius raised his wand slightly.  "I understand perfectly."

Voldemort glanced at Dracena.  "Killing her would punish her."

Dracena went cold.  "But pain is so much more instructive when applied
to someone else's flesh."  Voldemort laughed.

Lucius's face changed.  Voldemort smiled.

"Every scream she makes tells me something about **you.** "

Something broke.  Dracena saw it happen.  Lucius Malfoy had spent years
compromising.\
Negotiating.\
Rationalizing.\
Drawing lines and moving them.\
Giving Voldemort money.\
Influence.\
Names.\
Contacts.\
Telling himself that this concession prevented a worse one.

That cooperation preserved the family.  That survival required
unpleasant choices.  Then Voldemort had put his daughter on the floor
and tortured her so Lucius could watch.  There were no more lines left
to move.

Lucius raised his wand.  " **Expelliarmus** !"

Voldemort deflected it.  " **Avada Kedavra!** "

Lucius threw himself aside.  Green light exploded against the wall.

The room erupted.  Dracena rolled beneath a table.  A red curse crossed
above her.  Lucius fired back.  Voldemort twisted aside.  Stone
shattered.

" **Stupefy** !"\
" **Protego** !"\
" **Confringo** !"

Lucius ducked.  The wall behind him exploded.  He answered with
something Dracena didn't recognize.  Voldemort barely avoided it.

For the first time, Voldemort's expression changed.  Surprise.  Lucius
pressed him.  Spell after spell.  Not elegant anymore.  Not restrained.
 Furious.  

Voldemort gave ground slightly.

Once.\
Twice.\
Dracena stared.

Her father was **GOOD** .  She had known that, abstractly.  Lucius
Malfoy had been duelling since before she was born, but she'd never seen
it.  He moved with frightening precision.  A flick of his wand sent a
curse toward Voldemort's knees.

Voldemort jumped it and answered before landing.  Lucius spun behind a
stone pillar.

Green light carved a chunk from it.  

"Father!"

"STAY DOWN!" Lucius hissed.

Dracena stayed down.  For once in her life, she obeyed immediately.

Voldemort laughed.  "You cannot win."

Lucius stepped from behind the pillar.  "We'll see about that."

Another curse.  Voldemort blocked.  Lucius fired another.  And another.
 Voldemort stopped smiling.  Their spells collided.  Light filled the
room.

For several seconds neither gave ground, then the spells broke apart.
 Both men staggered backward.  

Lucius was breathing hard.  So was Voldemort.

Dracena realized something extraordinary.  Father was losing.  Voldemort
had expected to crush him.  Instead Lucius Malfoy was making him work
for it.  Voldemort realized it too.

His face twisted.  "Enough."

Lucius raised his wand.  So did Voldemort.  Dracena saw what was coming.

"No!"

Voldemort's wand moved.  " **AVADA KEDAVRA!** "

Lucius shouted at the same instant.  " **EXPELLIARMUS!** "

Green.  Red.  And then?  Black.

The world vanished again.  A second cloud of Peruvian Darkness Powder
exploded through the room.  

**"SECOND TEAM!  MOVE!"**

Aurors.  More explosions.  Someone grabbed Dracena.

She fought.  

"Malfoy!"  An unfamiliar voice.  "WE'RE AURORS!"

Dracena stopped.

"My father!"

"We have to move!"

"My father!"

An auror hauled her upright.  Her injured leg nearly folded.  Dracena
screamed.  He caught her around the waist.

"Can you walk?"

"Yes." She lied.

Behind them, the room had become chaos again.  Spells flashed through
absolute darkness.

Red.\
Green.\
Blue.

Dracena twisted around.  "FATHER!"

No answer.  She tried to pull away.  The Auror held her.

"He's back there!"

"We can't stay!"

"LUCIUS!"

Nothing.

Dracena stumbled.  Her hand struck the wall.  She pushed herself
forward.

One step.\
Another.\
Her entire body hurt.\
Her face was wet.\
Blood or tears.  Probably both.

Behind her came another explosion.  The floor shook.  Dracena turned one
last time.

Darkness.  Only darkness.

She didn't know whether her father's spell had landed.  She didn't know
whether Voldemort's had.  She didn't know whether Lucius Malfoy was
alive.

*I don't know.*

Harry's voice came back to her.

*"We don't lie about not knowing."*

Dracena limped into the darkness with the aurors.

And for once, not knowing was unbearable.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## <span class="c12"></span>

## <span class="c12">The End</span>

Harry woke to someone pounding on the dormitory door.

"UP!"

He rolled over.  For one confused moment, he had absolutely no idea
where he was.

Professor McGonagall stood in the doorway in a tartan dressing gown,
wand  already in her hand.  "Everyone up.  Immediately.  Robes on.
 Shoes.  Wands."

Ron bolted upright.  "What happened?"

"Great Hall.  Now."  Something in her voice ensured nobody asked another
question.

Five minutes later, Harry was descending the stairs with several dozen
frightened Gryffindors.

The corridors were full.  Hufflepuffs came from one direction.
 Ravenclaws from another.  Slytherins emerged from the dungeons.  Nobody
seemed to know what was happening.

Harry saw frightened first-years clutching one another's hands.  A
Ravenclaw girl was crying.  One of the prefects kept insisting
everything was all right in a voice that made it perfectly clear
everything was not all right.

Hermione found Harry and Ron near the entrance to the Great Hall.

"Do you know anything?"

"No."

"McGonagall wouldn't tell us."

"Me either."

They entered.  Every student in Hogwarts seemed to be there.  The doors
slammed shut.  Dumbledore stood before the staff table.  He wasn't
smiling.  The room became silent.

"Shortly after midnight." he said, "we received credible information
that Voldemort intends to attack Hogwarts."  The silence broke into
hundreds of frightened whispers.  Harry felt Ron move closer beside him.
 Dumbledore raised his hand.  The room became quiet again.  "We believe
the attack is imminent."

Harry's fingers tightened around his wand.

"Messages have been sent throughout Great Britain.  Aurors, Ministry
personnel and others willing to defend this school are already on their
way."  Dumbledore paused.  "But Hogwarts is some distance from many of
them."

Harry understood before Dumbledore said it.

"We may not have that time."

Hermione inhaled sharply.

"Students in their fifth year and below will be evacuated to a secure
location.  Your Heads of House will provide instructions."

Someone shouted:  "What about us?"

Dumbledore looked toward the older students. "Sixth and seventh years
will assist the staff."

The room erupted.  McGonagall shouted for silence.  Dumbledore waited.
 "I am not asking any of you to fight."

Harry frowned.

"You will be placed in groups around the castle and grounds.  You are
observers.  Nothing more."

His expression hardened.

"I cannot emphasize this sufficiently: **do not be heroes.** "

Several people looked at Harry.  Harry noticed.  "Why is everyone
looking at me?"

Ron rolled his eyes and muttered:  "Can't imagine."

Dumbledore continued.  "If you see anything suspicious, you **will**
 cast Periculum directly upward.  A red signal will alert the staff.
 You will then withdraw toward the castle."

His eyes swept the room.

"You will not investigate.  You will not pursue .  You will not attempt
to engage."  Dumbledore's gaze landed directly upon Harry.

Harry looked offended.

Hermione whispered:  "He's definitely talking to you."

Harry rolled his eyes.

A set of side doors clanged open.  Another voice cut through the room.
 A dull, but strong monotone. "We are not entirely unprepared."  Snape
stepped forward.  Dumbledore turned.  Something about his expression
changed.

"Severus?"  

"We know their approximate numbers."  Dumbledore stared at him.  "We
know their intended points of entry.  We know which groups have been
assigned to each objective.  We know several of their fallback
positions, the methods they've been training to breach the outer
protections, and which attacks are intended merely as diversions.
 Unfortunately, our spy was caught, but managed to barely escape with
her life.  Unfortunately, I believe this is why the attack is happening
now."

Now Dumbledore looked genuinely dumbfounded.  "How?"

Snape was silent for a moment.  "As I said, I have a spy."

Harry's head snapped up.  Something happened inside his chest.  No.
 Impossible.  Harry knew he shouldn't hope.  He did, anyway.

Snape continued.  "Someone successfully infiltrated the Death Eaters and
has been providing me with intelligence for some time."

Harry couldn't breathe.  Hermione looked at him.  She'd realized it too.

Ron hadn't.  "Blimey." he whispered. "Who?"

Harry didn't answer.  Hope was a dangerous thing.  Suddenly his heart
was hammering.

*Please.*

Snape's expression was unreadable.  "The information provided by this
person has disrupted attacks, exposed safe houses, identified
collaborators and allowed potential targets to be moved before the Dark
Lord's forces reached them."  He paused.  "The number of lives saved is
difficult to calculate."

Harry barely heard him.   *Please.*

Snape looked toward the doors he entered from.  "That intelligence was
obtained at considerable personal cost."

The door opened.  Someone limped through.

Harry stopped breathing.

He saw her platinum-blonde hair, first.  Her filthy robes.  One sleeve
torn, and dried blood ran from her hairline down the side of her face.
 She was walking badly, favoring one leg.

There were bruises Harry could see even from across the Great Hall.  She
looked exhausted.  She looked terrible.  She looked beautiful.

She looked alive.

"Dracena!" Harry whispered.

Ron stared.  "No bloody way."

Hermione covered her mouth.

Dracena stopped, standing beside Snape.

For the first time in months, Harry Potter looked at Dracena Malfoy...

...and she looked back.

Everything disappeared from her face.

The Great Hall.\
Dumbledore.\
Snape.\
Hundreds of students.

For one second there was only Harry.   Harry was already moving.  He
didn't remember deciding to.

"Dracena!"  She barely had time to turn before he reached her.

Harry stopped inches away.\
He wanted to grab her.\
He wanted to scream at her.\
He wanted to ask what had happened to her face.\
He wanted to kiss her.\
He wanted to know why she'd destroyed him.

Instead, what came out was:  "Why didn't you tell me?"

Dracena stared at him, dumbfounded.  "Because I'm a spy, Potter."

Harry blinked.

"Being a Spy generally works better when nobody knows she's a spy." she
said, matter of factly.

"You could've told *me!* "

"No, I couldn't!"

"Why?"

"Because you're a terrible liar!"

"I am not!"

Dracena stared at him.

Harry thought about it.  "All right, sometimes."

"Harry, you once tried to conceal something from McGonagall by standing
in front of it."

"It nearly worked."

"It was on fire."

Ron made a strangled noise behind them.

Dracena's mouth twitched.  Harry stared at her.

That expression.\
That voice. * * It was her.  Actually her.  His eyes filled before he
could stop them.

"Dracena..."

Her expression softened.  "Harry."

He stepped toward her.\
She stepped toward him.\
Harry stopped.  Something inside him wouldn't move.  Months of grief
didn't simply disappear because he'd been given an explanation.

Dracena saw it.  The hope on her face collapsed.  "Oh."

Harry hated himself immediately.  "No.  Dracena, I..."  he tried to
lurch forward.

"It's all right."  She said.

"It isn't."

"I understand."

"No, you don't."

Her face closed.  Harry recognized that too.  The Malfoy mask.

"Later." she said.

"Dracena..."  "Later, Potter."

Then she looked toward Dumbledore.  "We're going to stop Voldemort
tonight."

She glanced back at Harry.  "Would you like to help, or are you planning
to spend the evening moping?"

Harry stared, then, despite everything, laughed.

Dracena smiled.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

They were assigned the eastern grounds.  Harry thought this was
ridiculous.

"It's completely lit."

"That's presumably the idea." Hermione said.

Lumos charms floated above the grounds at regular intervals,
illuminating the lawn almost to the forest.  Nobody could approach
unseen.  Harry, Ron, Hermione and Dracena stood together near an old
stone wall.

Dracena leaned against it.

Harry noticed.  "You're hurt."

"I'm observant too, Potter."

"Seriously."

"So am I."

"Should you even be out here?"

"No."  She said.

Harry frowned.

Dracena sighed.  "I told Madam Pomfrey I was going to bed."

Hermione stared.  "You lied to Madam Pomfrey?"

"Granger, I spent several months lying to Voldemort.  I can manage the
school nurse."

Ron snorted.

Hermione didn't.  She was looking at Dracena.

Dracena noticed.  "What?"

Hermione folded her arms.  "You called me Mudblood."  The humor
vanished.

Dracena looked away.  "I know."

"You said people like me wouldn't matter when things changed."

"I know."

"You showed me the Mark."

Dracena touched her sleeve.

"I know."

Hermione's voice trembled.  "I thought you meant it.  I really thought
you meant it."

"I needed you to."  Dracena said, shakily.

"Well, congratulations."

Dracena flinched.

Hermione immediately looked guilty, But Dracena nodded.  

"No.  That's fair."

They were silent for a long moment.

"I hated it." She said, looking away toward the forest.

Hermione looked at her.

"Every.  Time."  Dracena swallowed.  "I kept telling myself they were
only words."  She looked down.  "They weren't."

"I had to use things that were true."

"What does that mean?" Ron asked.

"Professor Snape taught me that the best lies are built around truths."

Harry listened.

"I really had been angry with Hermione.  Jealous, really.  I really
looked down on Muggles.  I really thought Potter was insufferable
sometimes."

Harry almost smiled.  "Thanks."

"You're welcome."  Dracena grinned at him for a moment, then continued.

"I used all of it.  Every ugly thought I'd ever had.  Every argument.
 Every moment someone made me feel ashamed of my family."  She looked at
Hermione. "And I turned them into something worse."

Hermione's anger had softened.

Dracena stared at the grass.  "I felt filthy."

Harry said quietly:  "Then why did you do it?"

Dracena looked at him.  For a moment Harry thought she'd become angry.
 Instead she looked tired.  "My father."

Harry stiffened.  Dracena noticed.

"No.  Listen."

Harry listened.

"I overheard him speaking to Voldemort."

Harry's hand tightened around his wand.

"Voldemort wanted Father to use me against you."

"What?"

"He wanted me to get close to you.  Poison you, or curse you.  Lure you
somewhere."

Harry stared.

"Father said no."

Everyone was shocked.  Dracena smiled sadly.

"He told Voldemort I wasn't a resource.  That I hadn't taken any oath.
 That if Voldemort wanted Harry Potter dead, he could do his own
killing."

Harry didn't know what to say.

"I was proud of him." Dracena's voice cracked.  "God, Harry, I was so
proud of him."  She wiped her face angrily.  "And then the next morning
I read about a man who'd been murdered by Death Eaters."

Hermione listened silently.

"My father knew him."  Dracena swallowed.  "And I started thinking."
 Her voice cracked.  She looked toward Harry.  "If Father could stand in
front of Voldemort and say no for *me* ..."

Dracena barely held back her tears  "...then why couldn't he say no for
anyone else?"

Harry understood.

"I still don't know the answer."  Dracena looked at him.  "I still love
him."

"I know."

"I still think there are things he's right about."

"I know."

"I still think you're wrong about him sometimes."

Harry nodded.

"Probably."

That surprised her.

"But I knew people were dying."  She looked at all three of them.  "And
I knew I was in a position to do something."

Harry's chest hurt.  "So you did."

"I tried."

"You did more than try."

Dracena looked away.  Harry stepped closer.

"You wish you could have told me."

"I couldn't."

"I know that.  Still."

"I hurt you."

"Yes." he said.  She flinched.

Harry continued.  "But I understand why."

"That's not the same as forgiving me."

"No."

Dracena nodded.

"We don't lie about not knowing."

Harry stared at her.

Their promise.

"I don't know yet." he said.

"All right."  She said.

"But..."

Harry stepped closer.  Dracena looked up.

"I think I want to try."

Her eyes filled.  "I'd like that."

Harry reached for her hand.  She let him take it.

"I'm sorry too."

"For what?"

"For thinking the worst of you."

"You were supposed to."

"I know."

"For hating me."

"You were supposed to."

"I know."

Dracena smiled through tears.

"Stop saying that."

"Then stop saying things I know."

She laughed.  Harry did too.

Dracena looked at him.

Harry noticed.

Harry looked at her.

She noticed.

"Oh." Ron muttered.

Hermione elbowed him.

Dracena leaned forward towards Harry...

Every light went out.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Darkness swallowed Hogwarts.

Hermione immediately shouted: **"PERICULUM!"**

Red light shot upward.

Then the night exploded.

Shouts.\
Spells.\
Screaming.

Green.\
Red.\
Blue.\
Gold.

The grounds filled  with people.

Hundreds.

Then THOUSANDS.

Harry couldn't understand where they'd come from.

Dracena could.  "Disillusionment charms!"

The outer field erupted with figures.

Death Eaters.  Far more than anyone had expected.  The first Hogwarts
defenders were overwhelmed almost immediately.

Harry saw students disarmed.\
Staff surrounded.\
Somebody shouted for everyone to fall back.\
They couldn't.  They were surrounded.  Within minutes, the grounds
belonged to Voldemort.  Harry and Dracena had been separated from Ron
and Hermione.

The crowd parted.  Voldemort walked through.  Several Death Eaters
followed.  Harry raised his wand.  Dracena stood beside him.

Voldemort smiled.

"Harry Potter."

Harry said nothing.

"How appropriate."

He looked around at Hogwarts.

"Do you see it?"

Harry glared.

"The old world ending.  The new world rising.  My world."

"Do you practice these speeches?" Harry interrupted.

Voldemort stopped.  Dracena made a tiny choking noise.

Harry shrugged.  "You always seem to have one."

Voldemort's smile vanished.  "The world will remember tonight."

"Over my dead body."

The smile returned.  "Oh, Harry."  Voldemort raised his wand. "I'm *so*
 glad you said that."

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Harry attacked first.  Of course he did.

Dracena swore.

Voldemort was faster.  Harry blocked, and then countered.

Voldemort moved

Harry dodged and moved

He fought bravely.  And stupidly.

Voldemort kept giving ground.

Harry followed.  Dracena saw it before Harry did.

"Harry!"

Too late.

Voldemort smiled.  Harry realized.   *Trap.  * Three Death Eaters moved
behind him.

Harry spun.  For one terrible second his instinct screamed:  
**Attack.**

And suddenly he thought of Dracena.   *Potter, 'do something' is not a
plan.*

Harry smiled.  Voldemort's expression changed.

Harry retreated, and moved sideways.

One Death Eater fired a spell.  Harry ducked.

The Killing Curse glanced off Voldemort's wards and struck another Death
Eater.

Harry shouted, " **CONFRIGO** !"

The trap collapsed into confusion.

Dracena saw it.  Despite everything, she grinned.  "About bloody time,
Potter." she said to herself.

Then Voldemort attacked.  The duel became something else.  Harry
couldn't beat him.  He knew it.  Voldemort knew it.  Voldemort drove him
backward.  Harry stumbled.  His wand came up.

**"AVADA KEDAVRA!"**

**"EXPELLIARMUS!"**

Harry's spell met Voldemorts.  The spells collided.  Harry screamed.
 The force nearly tore the wand from his hand.

Voldemort pushed.  Harry pushed back.

The point where the spells met began moving toward Harry.  Slowly.  Inch
by inch.

Harry planted both feet.  His arms shook.

"No."  

Voldemort smiled.  The green light of the Killing Curse inched closer to
Harry.

Harry's vision blurred.  "No."  he whispered.

Closer.  He could feel heat on his face.

His knees buckled.  He thought of Ron.\
Hermione.\
Hagrid.\
His parents.\
Hogwarts.

Dracena.

Out of nowhere, a hand touched his.  Harry gasped in surprise.  Another
hand closed around the wand.  Platinum blonde hair brushed his cheek.

Dracena stood beside him.  "Move over, Potter."

Harry almost laughed. "Little busy."

"I noticed."

She pressed herself against his side.  Her fingers wrapped around his.

For one strange instant, the battle disappeared.\
Harry could feel her breathing.\
Her heart hammering.\
Her hand trembling.\
She was terrified.\
Dracena Malfoy was absolutely terrified, and she was here anyway.

She turned her face and kissed his cheek.

"Together?"

Harry looked at her.  "Together."

They pushed.  Harry's spell stopped retreating.

Voldemort's smile disappeared.

Harry felt Dracena's hand tighten.  He didn't know where his magic ended
and hers began.\
Didn't know whose strength he was feeling.\
Didn't care.\
Everything outside them dimmed.

The screaming disappeared.\
The battlefield disappeared.\
There was only Dracena beside him.\
Her shoulder against his.\
Her fingers around his.\
Her breath against his cheek.

Six years.\
A robe shop.\
A train.\
A duel.\
A broom.\
A hippogriff.\
A photograph.\
A bathroom.\
A Christmas present.\
A thousand stupid arguments.

Harry pushed.  Dracena pushed with him.

Harry's spell lurched forward.

Voldemort's eyes widened.  "No."

A little closer...

"No!"

Dracena screamed.  Harry screamed with her.  " **EXPELLIARMUS** !"

Voldemort's spell shattered, and his wand ripped from his hand.  It flew
high into the air.

For one perfect second, nobody moved.

The horizon lit up.  Hundreds of spells streaked across the grounds.

Aurors.  Ministry forces.  Witches and wizards from across Britain.

The reinforcements had arrived.  The Death Eaters broke into a run.
 Harry barely noticed.  His wand fell from his hand.  Dracena caught
him.  Or Harry caught her.  They ended up holding each other.

Harry laughed.  Dracena started crying.  Then Harry did too.

"You're horrible." Harry whispered.

Dracena laughed through her tears. "I still love you."

Dracena stopped.  Harry looked at her.  Dracena touched his face.

"I love you, Harry Potter."

Harry smiled.  "Yeah."

Dracena frowned.  " *Yeah?* "

Harry laughed.  There she was.

"I love you too, Dracena Malfoy."

"Better."

She kissed him.  This time Harry didn't pull away.

The battle continued around them.

People shouted.\
Spells flashed.\
Somewhere behind them, Ron yelled something Harry couldn't hear and
Hermione told him to shut up.

Harry didn't care.\
For six years, Harry Potter and Dracena Malfoy had argued about nearly
everything.

They would undoubtedly continue doing so, but that could wait until
morning.  For now, Harry kissed the Slytherin girl he'd once been
absolutely certain he couldn't possibly like.

<div class="illustration">

![The End](Images/The%20End.jpg)

</div>

------------------------------------------------------------------------

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## <span class="c12"></span>

## <span class="c12">The Epilogue</span>

**THREE YEARS LATER**

<div class="illustration">

![The Epilogue](Images/The%20Epilogue.jpg)

</div>

Lucius Malfoy had endured many unpleasant things in his life.  He had
endured Ministry investigations, political reversals, financial crises,
and the indignity of watching men of questionable ancestry rise to
positions of considerable influence.

He had endured Lord Voldemort.

He had endured his daughter's adolescence.

He had even endured several Muggle films, although he would deny
enjoying *The Empire Strikes Back*  until his dying breath.

But there were limits.  And Harry Potter was sitting at his dinner
table.  Again.

Worse, Dracena appeared to be enjoying herself.

Lucius looked across the table.  Potter was laughing at something his
daughter had said.  Not the polite laughter of a guest attempting to
ingratiate himself with his hosts.  Genuine laughter.  Dracena was
laughing too.  Lucius found this particularly irritating.  He had spent
years attempting to raise a daughter who understood the dignity of the
Malfoy name.  He had provided the finest education, the best tutors, a
magnificent home, and every opportunity to develop the poise and
sophistication appropriate to her station.

And somehow she had developed a taste for Harry Potter.  .

Dracena looked at him.

"Father."

"What?"

"You're staring."

"I am observing."

Harry grinned.  Lucius narrowed his eyes.

"Something amusing, Potter?"

"No, sir."

"Then kindly refrain from looking amused."

Harry made an effort.  It was not successful.  Dracena sighed.

"This is going to be a very long dinner."

------------------------------------------------------------------------

The argument began with a newspaper.  It usually did.  Lucius had
mentioned a Ministry proposal concerning several collections of
privately held magical artifacts.  The proposed legislation would grant
the Ministry authority to acquire certain historically significant
magical objects and research materials for preservation and public
study.

Harry thought this sounded reasonable.  Lucius thought it sounded like
theft.  Dracena had initially hoped the discussion would end there.
 Unfortunately, she had fallen in love with a Gryffindor.

"What exactly is wrong with making old magical knowledge available to
people?" Harry asked.

Lucius put down his fork.  "Nothing."

Harry blinked.  "Nothing?"

"Provided its owners consent."

"Well, that's the problem, isn't it?"

"Is it?"

"If they won't consent, nobody else gets to learn from it."

Lucius regarded him.

"Yes, Potter.  That is generally how ownership works."

Harry sighed.  "You're talking about hoarding knowledge."

Lucius's expression changed.  Dracena recognized it.

*Oh, here we go.*

"Hoarding." Lucius repeated.

"Yes."

"An interesting choice of words."

"Well, what would you call it?"

"Stewardship."

Harry frowned.  "That's a rather convenient word."

"Words frequently become inconvenient when one understands what they
mean."

"Father." Dracena warned.  She idly fingered the band on her left ring
finger, feeling the comfort of the enormous cluster of gems.

Lucius waved a hand.  "No, no.  I am genuinely interested."  He turned
back to Harry.  "Tell me, Potter.  Where do you suppose new magic comes
from?"

Harry blinked.  "What?"

"New spells.  New enchantments.  New magical techniques.  Where do you
suppose they originate?"

"People invent them."

"Astounding."

"Father."  Dracena said, with a warning tone.

Lucius ignored her.  "Who, Potter?  Who invents them?"

"Researchers, I suppose."

"Employed by whom?"

Harry hesitated.  "Hogwarts?  The Ministry?  Private businesses?"

"Some.  Certainly."  Lucius leaned back.  "But historically, much
magical research has been conducted by individuals and families.
 Sometimes over generations."

Harry considered this.  "All right."

"I have learned..." Lucius began, "...your Muggle world has enormous
institutions devoted to research.  Universities.  Corporations.
 Government laboratories.  Thousands of people collaborating, sharing
facilities, funding experiments."

Harry nodded.  "Yes."

"We have relatively little of that."

"You have Hogwarts."

"A school."

"The Ministry."

"A bureaucracy."

"St Mungo's."

"A hospital."

Harry smiled faintly.  "You're very difficult to satisfy."

"So I've been told."

Dracena made a small noise into her glass.

Lucius continued.  "Imagine a family discovers a useful enchantment.
 Perhaps something as simple as a charm that prevents wood from rotting.
 They teach it to their children.  Their children improve it.  A century
later, a descendant discovers that a variation can preserve certain
medicinal ingredients."

Harry nodded.  "All right."

"Another generation discovers that the same principle can preserve
delicate magical instruments."  Lucius spread his hands.  "Three hundred
years of experimentation.  Failures.  Improvements.  Discoveries.  All
preserved within one family."

"All right." Harry repeated.

"Now imagine the Ministry arrives and declares that this knowledge
represents an unacceptable concentration of magical resources."

Harry frowned.  "I don't think that's quite what they're saying."

"Then what are they saying?"

"That something useful shouldn't necessarily be kept away from everyone
else."

"By what right?"

Harry thought.

Lucius continued before he could answer.  "Because other people wish to
use it?"

"Well..."

"Because the family is wealthy?"

"That's not..."

"Because some Ministry committee has decided that possessing something
valuable is inherently suspicious?"

Harry shook his head.  "No.  I don't agree with that."

Lucius stopped.  "You don't?"

"No."

Dracena looked up.

Harry continued.  "If someone invents something, they should be allowed
to benefit from it."

Lucius regarded him with renewed interest.  "Indeed."

"Fred and George, for example."

Lucius closed his eyes.  "Must we?"

Dracena grinned.  "Oh, Father, this should be good."

Harry leaned forward.  "They've invented all sorts of things.  Joke
products, mostly, but some are genuinely useful."

"I am aware."

"Their Darkness Powder helped the Aurors during the war."

Lucius's jaw tightened.  "I am painfully aware."

Harry caught the expression.  For a moment, the memory of that war
passed between them.  Neither pursued it.

Lucius continued.  "An excellent example, actually.  The Weasley twins
developed those products through their own ingenuity.  They invested
their own money.  They took risks.  They experimented."

"And blew things up." Harry added.

"Frequently, I imagine."

Dracena laughed.

"Sometimes intentionally."

"Indeed."

Lucius turned back to Harry.

"Would you approve of the Ministry confiscating their inventions?"

"No."

"Would you approve of a committee declaring that the twins had
accumulated too much valuable magical knowledge and therefore must
surrender it for the public good?"

"No."

"Would you approve of compelling them to share their methods with
competitors?"

Harry considered.  "Probably not."

"Why?"

"Because they invented them."  

"Precisely."  Lucius took a sip of wine.  "Now suppose the inventions
are three hundred years old."

Harry frowned.  "What?"

"The twins are long dead.  Their descendants maintain the business.
 Perhaps they've improved the original inventions.  Perhaps they've
discovered entirely new applications."

"All right."

"At what point does that knowledge stop belonging to their family?"

Harry opened his mouth, then stopped.  Dracena smiled.

Lucius noticed.  "Something amusing, my dear?"

"Nothing, Father."

Harry thought for a moment.  "I don't know."

Lucius inclined his head.  "A perfectly respectable answer."

Harry looked up.  "Really?"

"Yes.  I wish more people would give it."

Dracena almost laughed.  Harry grinned.  They grinned at each other.

"Consider, Potter, that inheritance is not merely the transfer of
wealth.  It can also be the transfer of obligations."

Harry frowned.  "Obligations?"

"Certainly.  To preserve.  To maintain.  To improve when possible.  To
avoid squandering what previous generations created."

Lucius glanced toward Dracena.  "My daughter stands to inherit a great
many things.  Not merely property."

Dracena looked at her father.

He continued.  "She inherits a history.  Traditions.  Knowledge.
 Responsibilities."

Harry nodded slowly.  "I think I understand that better than I used to."

Lucius looked surprised.  "Do you?"

"Yes."

Harry glanced at Dracena  "She showed me some of it."

Dracena smiled.  "The tapestries.  Among other things."

Harry looked back at Lucius.  "I used to think all those old family
traditions were just ways for rich wizards to congratulate themselves
for being rich."

Lucius looked offended.  "How predictably vulgar."

"Yes, well."  Harry shrugged.  "I was wrong about some of it."

Dracena's smile widened.

Lucius noticed.  Something in his expression softened.  Only slightly.

Harry continued.  "But there's something I still don't understand."

"Naturally."

"If a family discovers something useful, why keep it secret?"

Lucius considered.  "Sometimes because they wish to profit from it."

Harry nodded.  "Right.  That makes sense, I guess."

"Sometimes because they believe others would misuse it."

Harry nodded thoughtfully.

"And sometimes, Potter, because the discovery should never have been
made in the first place."

Harry stopped.

Dracena looked at her father.  "What do you mean?"

Lucius put down his wineglass.  "Have you ever heard of the Vesper
Curse?"

Harry shook his head.  Dracena shook hers too.

"Good."  Lucius leaned back.  "Approximately two centuries ago, a member
of an old wizarding family discovered a method of transferring
enchantments from one object to another."

Harry frowned.  "That sounds useful."

"It was intended to be."

"Intended?"

"The researcher discovered that certain protective charms could be
transferred between objects without being recast.  A remarkable
achievement."

Dracena leaned forward.  "That would have tremendous applications."

"Indeed."

"What happened?"

Lucius looked at her.  "The enchantment did not distinguish reliably
between protective magic and curses."

Harry frowned.  "So it transferred curses too?"

"Yes."

"That's unfortunate."

"Extremely."

Lucius paused.  "Then the researcher discovered something considerably
worse."

Neither Harry nor Dracena spoke.

"Under certain circumstances, an enchanted object could transfer its
properties to another without the caster's intervention.  That object
could then transfer them again."

Harry stared.  "Like an infection."

"Precisely."

Dracena's expression changed.  "Oh."

Lucius nodded.  "Imagine a curse capable of propagating through other
enchanted objects.  A cursed door infecting the protective charms on a
house.  The house infecting everything inside it.  An enchanted carriage
carrying the curse into another household."

Harry stopped smiling.  "What happened?"

"The family destroyed the research."

"All of it?"

"As much as they could find."

Harry looked thoughtful.  "Did they tell the Ministry?"

"No."

He frowned.  "Why not?"

Lucius looked at him for a long moment.  "Which Ministry?"

"What?"

"Which particular Ministry would you entrust with such knowledge?"

Harry shrugged.  "The Ministry at the time, I suppose."

"And the Ministry twenty years later?"

Harry hesitated.

Lucius continued.  "Suppose a responsible Minister establishes a secure
research facility.  Appoints competent people.  Establishes safeguards."

"That sounds reasonable."

"It does."

Lucius took a sip of wine.  "Then the Minister dies."

Harry said nothing.  

"His successor appoints different people.  A researcher retires.
 Another copies the documents.  Someone decides the original
restrictions were excessive.  Perhaps the research is rediscovered by
someone who believes himself cleverer than his predecessors."  Lucius
spread his hands.  "And suddenly a discovery that one family had the
sense to destroy becomes the foundation of a catastrophe."

Harry considered this.  "But what if the family was wrong?"

Lucius nodded.  "A legitimate question."

Dracena glanced between them.

Harry continued.  "What if someone eventually figured out how to make it
safe?"

"Then a potentially valuable discovery has been lost."

"You're comfortable with that?"

"Yes."

Harry blinked.

Lucius leaned forward.  "Sometimes one must choose between preserving
something valuable and preventing something terrible."  He paused.  "I
would rather lose a useful enchantment than preserve a dangerous one
merely because I cannot bear the thought of destroying knowledge."

Harry thought about that.  Dracena looked at her father with an
expression Harry couldn't quite identify.

"All right." Harry said finally.

Lucius raised an eyebrow.  "All right?"

"I can see your point."

"How extraordinary."

"But."

"Ah."

Dracena sighed.

Harry leaned forward.

"How do we know the family actually destroyed the research?"

Lucius said nothing.

"How do we know they didn't keep it for themselves?"

The silence changed.

Dracena slowly put down her fork.

Harry continued.

"You're saying we shouldn't trust the Ministry because eventually
someone might misuse the knowledge."

"Yes."

"But families change too."

Lucius's expression hardened.

"Explain."

"People die.  Children inherit things.  Sometimes the next generation
isn't as responsible as the last."

Lucius glanced briefly at Dracena.  She stared pointedly at her plate.

Harry continued.  "Sometimes a family that promises to protect dangerous
knowledge might decide to use it."

Lucius's eyes narrowed.  "Choose your words carefully, Potter."

Harry met his gaze.  "I'm not accusing you of keeping that particular
curse."

"How reassuring."

"I'm saying the argument works both ways."

Lucius said nothing.

"You don't trust the Ministry because the wrong people might eventually
get control of something dangerous."

"Correct."

"Neither do I."

That surprised him.

Harry continued.  "But I don't automatically trust a family because
their great-grandfather was trustworthy."

Silence.  Lucius looked at Harry for a long time.

Then he nodded.  Very slightly.  "A legitimate objection."

Dracena nearly dropped her fork.

Harry stared.  "Really?"

"Yes, Potter.  Really."

Lucius looked irritated.  "Do you imagine I have never considered the
possibility that hereditary stewardship might fail?"

Harry smiled faintly.  "Well, you don't usually advertise it."

"Because recognizing a danger is not the same thing as accepting someone
else's proposed solution."

Harry considered that.

Lucius continued.  "And who, precisely, would decide which families were
sufficiently responsible to retain their knowledge?"

"I don't know."

"Ministry officials?"

"Not necessarily."

"Independent scholars?"

"Maybe."

"Appointed by whom?"

Harry frowned.  "That's the problem, isn't it?"

"Precisely."

Harry thought for a moment.  "Maybe you need some kind of independent
review.  Not necessarily access to all the knowledge, but enough to know
what's being kept and whether it's dangerous."

Lucius looked skeptical.  "And what prevents the reviewers themselves
from becoming a danger?"

"I don't know."

"Then your solution has merely moved the problem."

Harry nodded.  "Possibly."

Lucius seemed surprised by the admission.

Harry continued.  "But your solution leaves it entirely in the hands of
whoever inherited the family vault."

"True."

"And you don't know whether their grandchildren will be responsible
either."

Lucius considered.  "No."

A long silence followed.  Dracena watched her father.

He wasn't angry.  He was thinking.  That was new.  Not that Father
didn't think, obviously.  She had rarely seen him seriously consider an
argument from someone he regarded as socially inferior.  Especially
Potter.  

Finally, Lucius spoke.  "There is a distinction, Potter, between
acknowledging that a system is imperfect and concluding that its
replacement would be better."

Harry nodded.  "That's fair."

"And there is a distinction between private ownership and arbitrary
exclusion."

Harry blinked.  "That's what I've been saying."

"Yes."  Lucius looked faintly annoyed.  "I am aware."

Dracena smiled.

Lucius continued.  "I am prepared to concede that some families have
confused the preservation of knowledge with the preservation of
privilege."

Harry looked at him.  "Really?"

"Do stop sounding astonished."

"Sorry."

" **Some**  families." Lucius repeated pointedly.

Harry grinned.  "Of course."

"But I am not prepared to concede that the Ministry has an inherent
right to seize whatever it decides would be useful to the public."

Harry nodded.  "Neither am I."

Lucius stopped.  "What?"

Harry shrugged.  "I said I don't think the Ministry should simply be
able to take everything."

"Then what have we been arguing about for the last half hour?"

Dracena burst out laughing.

Harry smiled.  "The details."

Lucius stared at him.  Then, to Dracena's absolute astonishment, he
laughed.  "Merlin help me."

Dracena wiped her eyes.

"Oh, this is wonderful."

Both men looked at her.

"What?" Harry asked.

"You've spent half an hour arguing and discovered you actually agree
about something."

Lucius sniffed.  "Do not exaggerate, Dracena."

Harry laughed.  "Wouldn't dream of it."

------------------------------------------------------------------------

The conversation continued through dessert.

The discussion somehow led to an argument about patenting spells, which
led to an argument about whether magical techniques should expire into
public use after a certain number of years, which led to Lucius
explaining several ancient disputes between wizarding families.

Harry discovered that some of these disputes had continued for
centuries.

"You're joking."

"I assure you I am not."

"Three hundred years?"

"Approximately."

"Over a charm for keeping soup warm?"

"A very sophisticated charm."

"It's soup."

"Potter, you are missing the point."

Dracena laughed so hard she had to put down her spoon.  At one point
Lucius admitted that the Weasley twins were talented inventors.  This
produced such an expression of astonishment on Harry's face that Lucius
immediately regretted saying it.

"I said talented, Potter.  Not respectable."

"Right."

"Nor tasteful."

"Of course."

"Nor particularly well-bred."

"Naturally."

Dracena grinned.

"But talented."

Lucius glared at his daughter.

"Yes, Dracena.  Talented."

Harry looked delighted.

"I can't wait to tell George."

"You will do no such thing."

"Oh, I absolutely will."

"Potter."

"He's going to frame it."

Dracena dissolved into laughter again.

Lucius wondered, not for the first time, whether allowing Harry Potter
into his home had been a terrible mistake.

Then he noticed something.  Harry was listening.  Not merely waiting for
his opportunity to argue, but listening.\
Asking questions.\
Occasionally disagreeing.\
Occasionally conceding a point.\
Lucius had expected Potter to be reckless, stubborn, sentimental, and
thoroughly convinced of his own righteousness.

He was, admittedly, all of those things.  But there was something else.
 The boy could change his mind.  Not easily, not completely, but he
could do it.

When Lucius explained something Harry hadn't considered, Harry didn't
immediately dismiss it merely because Lucius Malfoy had said it.  Lucius
found that unexpectedly agreeable.  He would never have admitted it
during dinner.

Naturally.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Later, Dracena excused herself from the table.  She slowly worked
herself out of her chair, carefully protecting her swollen belly as she
sat up to help her mother with something upstairs.  

Harry suspected this was a conspiracy.  He suspected it even more
strongly when she kissed his cheek and whispered:  "Behave."

Harry smiled.  "Me?"

"Especially you."

She looked toward her father.  "And you."

Lucius raised an eyebrow.  "I beg your pardon?"

"Behave, Father."

"Dracena."

She smiled sweetly.

"Love you."

Then she disappeared with her mother.

Lucius watched her go.  "Extraordinary child."

Harry smiled.  "Yes."

"Stubborn."

"Very."

"Argumentative."

"Absolutely."

"Entirely too convinced of her own intelligence."

Harry grinned.  "Reminds me of someone."

Lucius looked at him.  "Be careful, Potter."

Harry laughed.  Lucius poured himself a brandy.  He offered Harry one.
 Harry declined.

For several minutes they sat in comfortable silence.  It was an entirely
new experience.  Lucius considered the young man across from him.

Harry Potter.  The boy who had become famous for surviving the Dark
Lord.  The boy who had repeatedly interfered with plans Lucius had
considered essential.  The boy whose convictions Lucius found naive,
whose manners he found occasionally appalling, and whose choice of
friends he continued to regard with considerable skepticism.

The boy his daughter loved.

Lucius looked toward the doorway.

He remembered Dracena at sixteen, standing in his study.

Terrified.\
Defiant.\
Telling him he would never strike her again.\
Telling him she loved her family.\
Telling him she didn't want another name.

*"I belong to me."*

He remembered her lying on the floor.\
Blood on her face.\
Barely conscious.\
He remembered Voldemort smiling.\
Lucius closed his eyes briefly.\
There were things he would never forgive.\
Things he would never forget.\
And things he would never apologize for.

He opened his eyes.  Potter was looking at the fire.

Lucius cleared his throat.  "Mr Potter."

Harry turned.  "Yes, sir?"

"I must confess something."

Harry raised an eyebrow.  "That sounds ominous."

"Don't be impertinent."

"Sorry."

Lucius considered his brandy.  "I unexpectedly enjoyed dinner this
evening."

Harry looked surprised.  "Really?"

"Yes."

Lucius paused.  "I expected..."

Harry smiled.  "You expected an argument."

Lucius raised an eyebrow.  "Oh, yes, Mr Potter.  I certainly expected
one of those."

Harry laughed.

Lucius allowed himself a small smile.  "What I didn't expect was to
*enjoy*  it."

Harry looked sheepish.  "Yeah, Mr Malfoy.  I did too."

"Lucius."

Harry blinked.  "What?"

"You may call me Lucius."  Harry stared at him.

Lucius sighed.  "It appears you have become a part of this family,
despite my previous wishes to the contrary."

Harry's mouth twitched.  "Previous wishes?"

Lucius narrowed his eyes.  "Don't push it, Potter."

Harry smiled.  "Harry."

For a moment, Lucius simply stared at him.  Then, very slowly, the
corner of his mouth rose.  "Goodnight, Harry."

"Goodnight, Lucius."

Lucius rose and left the room.  

Harry sat alone for a moment, smiling faintly at the fire.

He had not suddenly come to agree with the man's politics, his
prejudices, or his conception of the wizarding world.  But Harry had
learned something that evening.

Lucius had too.  And perhaps that was enough.  For tonight.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

# <span class="c9">About the Author</span>

<div class="illustration">

![Catgirl](Images/Profile%20Angry.png)

</div>

Sensurround Q. Catgirl is the nom de plume of @ShamashAran on X, who
values her privacy, largely because it allows her to write about
whatever she pleases without having to explain herself to people who
might recognize her at the grocery store, or at work.

She writes about a wide variety of subjects, many of which are virtually
guaranteed to make someone angry. This is not necessarily her intention,
although she admits it is sometimes a pleasant side effect.

Her career in Harry Potter fan fiction began entirely by accident. What
started as a one-shot scene titled The Bathroom, set during Harry
Potter’s sixth year at Hogwarts, received an unexpectedly enthusiastic
reception on X. The scene explored an alternate version of the Harry
Potter universe in which Draco Malfoy was instead Dracena Malfoy, and a
rather different relationship developed between the young Malfoy and
Harry Potter.

The response was sufficiently encouraging that she decided to write
another scene. And then another. Eventually, the whole thing got rather
out of hand, and she found herself writing an entire novel.

Thus was born Harry Potter and the Serpent’s Heart.

“It’s a lot of fun to write something outside of politics and culture
that far fewer people will get angry about,” she explains. “I really
enjoyed the whole ‘Malfoid’ trend because it’s just wholesome fun.
There’s something refreshing about writing a story where the biggest
controversy is whether two fictional teenagers should kiss.”

When not writing political commentary, engaging in recreational ragebait
on X, or inventing increasingly complicated romantic problems for
fictional witches and wizards, Sensurround enjoys amateur radio,
automotive repair, electronics, programming, and various other hobbies
involving the disassembly of expensive equipment.

She lives with her husband (Who co-wrote and inspired many scenes) as
well as two dogs, and two cats, the pets whom have thus far demonstrated
remarkably little interest in her literary accomplishments.
